# SECTION:  Management of Care

### Question 1
The registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a client who requires assistance with activities of daily living. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's ability to swallow before providing a meal.
B. Showing the client how to use the prescribed incentive spirometer.
C. Assisting the client with a partial bed bath and oral care.
D. Evaluating the client's response to scheduled pain medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) are trained to assist clients with basic activities of daily living (ADLs) such as bathing, grooming, and oral care. Assessing (A), teaching (B), and evaluating (D) are all within the scope of practice of the RN and cannot legally or safely be delegated to a UAP. 
</details>

---

### Question 2
A client is scheduled for an elective surgical procedure. The surgeon has explained the risks and benefits, and the client is ready to sign the consent form. What is the primary role of the nurse during the informed consent process?

A. Explaining the surgical risks and alternative treatments to the client.
B. Witnessing the client's voluntary signature on the informed consent document.
C. Determining if the client has the financial means to pay for the surgery.
D. Answering the client's detailed questions about the surgical technique.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The nurse's primary role in informed consent is to act as a witness to the client's signature, ensuring the client is signing voluntarily and appears competent to do so. Explaining risks, benefits, alternatives, and detailed surgical techniques (A, D) is the legal responsibility of the healthcare provider performing the procedure. Financial determination (C) is not part of the clinical informed consent process.
</details>

---

### Question 3
The nurse is providing education to a client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "My durable power of attorney for health care will make decisions for me only if I become unable to do so."
B. "Once I complete an advance directive, I am legally unable to change my mind."
C. "My family can legally override my advance directive if they disagree with my choices."
D. "I must have a lawyer present to sign my living will for it to be legally binding."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A durable power of attorney for health care (or healthcare proxy) only goes into effect when the client is deemed incapacitated and unable to make their own medical decisions. Advance directives can be changed or revoked by the competent client at any time (B). Family members cannot override a legally valid advance directive (C). While state laws vary regarding witnesses or notarization, a lawyer is not required to be present to create a valid advance directive (D).
</details>

---

### Question 4
A client recently diagnosed with a terminal illness tells the nurse, "I don't know how to tell my family about my diagnosis. I don't want to upset them." Which response by the nurse demonstrates appropriate therapeutic communication?

A. "You shouldn't worry about upsetting them; they need to know the truth."
B. "I can tell your family for you if that would make it easier on you."
C. "It sounds like you are feeling anxious about sharing this news with your family."
D. "Most people find that their families are very supportive once they know."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Reflecting the client's feelings ("It sounds like you are feeling anxious...") is a therapeutic communication technique that acknowledges the client's emotions, builds trust, and encourages further dialogue. Telling the client not to worry (A) is dismissive. Offering to tell the family (B) takes away the client's autonomy and crosses professional boundaries. Generalizing about "most people" (D) provides false reassurance and invalidates the client's specific concerns.
</details>

---

### Question 5
The nurse is working at the nurses' station on a busy medical-surgical unit. Which action by a healthcare team member presents the greatest risk for a breach of client confidentiality?

A. Disposing of a client's printed lab results in a designated secure shredder bin.
B. Logging off the electronic health record (EHR) before leaving the computer terminal.
C. Discussing a client's diagnosis with the covering provider in a private room.
D. Leaving a computer screen displaying a client's medical history open and unattended.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Leaving an electronic health record open and unattended on a computer screen creates a high risk for a breach of confidentiality (a HIPAA violation), as unauthorized individuals could view the client's protected health information. Disposing of documents in a shredder bin (A), logging off the computer (B), and having clinical discussions in a private area (C) are all correct actions that protect client privacy.
</details>
### Question 6
A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a stable client and working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the UAP?

A) Evaluating the client's response to pain medication.
B) Obtaining a clean-catch urine specimen.
C) Educating the client on how to use a walker.
D) Assessing the client's skin integrity during a bed bath.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Obtaining a clean-catch urine specimen.

**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)

**Rationale:** The RN can safely delegate standard, routine, and unchanging tasks to the UAP, such as obtaining basic urine or stool specimens for a stable client. Evaluating pain (A), providing initial education (C), and performing nursing assessments (D) all require the specialized knowledge and judgment of a licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.

</details>

---

### Question 7
A client's neighbor approaches the nurse's station and asks for an update on the client's condition. The client has not signed a release of information form for the neighbor. What is the correct action by the nurse?

A) Ask the neighbor to step into a private hallway before talking.
B) Confirm the client is admitted but state the diagnosis is private.
C) Provide a vague, general update to politely satisfy the neighbor.
D) State that privacy laws prevent sharing any client information.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) State that privacy laws prevent sharing any client information.

**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)

**Rationale:** Under HIPAA and client confidentiality laws, healthcare providers cannot disclose any protected health information (PHI) to unauthorized individuals. This includes confirming whether the client is even admitted to the facility. The nurse must firmly but politely protect the client's privacy by stating that laws prevent the sharing of any information.

</details>

---

### Question 8
The nurse is providing education to a newly admitted client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I must have a hospital lawyer present to make this document legally binding."
B) "Once I sign this document, I am no longer allowed to change my mind."
C) "This document outlines my wishes for care if I become unable to speak for myself."
D) "This document allows my healthcare proxy to override my decisions while I am alert."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "This document outlines my wishes for care if I become unable to speak for myself."

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

**Rationale:** An advance directive is a legal document that allows clients to specify their healthcare wishes and decisions in advance, ensuring those wishes are honored if they become incapacitated and unable to communicate. Clients can change their advance directive at any time, they do not require a lawyer to sign (usually just witnesses or a notary), and a proxy cannot override an alert, competent client's decisions.

</details>

---

### Question 9
A client is scheduled for a surgical procedure. The provider has just explained the risks and benefits, and left the room. The client holds the consent form and says to the nurse, "I'm not sure if I actually want to go through with this." What is the nurse's best response?

A) "Tell me more about your concerns regarding the surgery."
B) "Your surgeon is very skilled; there is nothing to worry about."
C) "You should sign it now, and you can cancel the surgery later if you want."
D) "I will call the surgeon back right now to explain the procedure again."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "Tell me more about your concerns regarding the surgery."

**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)

**Rationale:** The nurse acts as an advocate and uses therapeutic communication (an open-ended statement) to explore the client's feelings and clarify their concerns before taking further action. Providing false reassurance (B) is non-therapeutic. Pressuring the client to sign (C) violates informed consent protocols. While the surgeon may need to return to answer medical questions (D), the nurse's *best first* response is to actively listen and assess the client's specific worries.

</details>

---

### Question 10
A client is being admitted to the medical-surgical unit and brings a large envelope containing cash and several pieces of jewelry. The client's family is not present to take the items home. What is the nurse's appropriate physical action regarding these items?

A) Instruct the client to keep the envelope hidden inside their pillowcase.
B) Hide the envelope at the bottom of the client's personal luggage.
C) Lock the valuables in the client's bedside drawer.
D) Document the items objectively and secure them in the facility's safe.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Document the items objectively and secure them in the facility's safe.

**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)

**Rationale:** When a client brings valuables to the hospital and cannot send them home with family, facility protocol mandates that the nurse objectively document the items (e.g., "yellow metal ring with clear stone" rather than "gold diamond ring") and secure them in the designated hospital safe. Leaving valuables in the room (luggage, bedside drawer, or bed) puts them at high risk for loss or theft.

</details>
### Question 11
The registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a stable client who is 2 days post-operative. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the client's incision for redness.
B) Assisting the client with a daily bed bath.
C) Explaining the new discharge instructions.
D) Administering a scheduled oral analgesic.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B 
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
**Rationale:** Assisting with activities of daily living (ADLs), such as bathing, is a standard and safe task to delegate to a UAP for a stable client. Evaluating incisions requires nursing assessment (RN only), teaching requires nursing knowledge (RN only), and medication administration is reserved for licensed personnel (RN or LPN/LVN).
</details>

---

### Question 12
A visitor approaches the nurses' station and asks for the room number and diagnosis of a newly admitted client. The client has not authorized the release of any information. Which action by the nurse is legally required?

A) Provide the room number but withhold the medical diagnosis.
B) Ask the visitor to provide a photo ID before sharing the information.
C) Confirm the client is admitted but refuse to provide the room number.
D) State that no information can be provided without the client's permission.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)
**Rationale:** Under HIPAA (Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act) guidelines, the nurse must protect client confidentiality. If a client has not authorized the release of information, the nurse cannot confirm their presence in the facility, share their room number, or disclose their diagnosis to unauthorized individuals. 
</details>

---

### Question 13
A client tells the nurse, "I really don't want to be kept alive on machines if my heart stops, but my daughter says I have to." Which response by the nurse demonstrates therapeutic communication?

A) "It sounds like you have some concerns about your end-of-life care."
B) "You should listen to your daughter since she will be making decisions."
C) "I will tell the doctor to write a Do Not Resuscitate order right away."
D) "Why do you think your daughter wants you to stay on machines?"

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)
**Rationale:** Option A uses the therapeutic technique of reflection and validation, encouraging the client to elaborate on their feelings regarding their own healthcare wishes. Option B gives unsolicited advice and ignores the client's autonomy. Option C jumps to action without fully exploring the client's wishes or providing education on advance directives. Option D uses a non-therapeutic "why" question, which can make the client feel defensive.
</details>

---

### Question 14
The nurse is teaching a client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of a durable power of attorney for healthcare?

A) "This document lets my doctor make all medical decisions for me."
B) "My family can use this to access my bank accounts to pay medical bills."
C) "This document names someone to make medical decisions for me if I cannot."
D) "I cannot change my mind once I sign this legal document."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Rationale:** A durable power of attorney for healthcare (or healthcare proxy) is a legal document that designates a specific person to make healthcare decisions on the client's behalf *only* if the client becomes unable to do so themselves. It does not grant access to finances, does not assign decision-making to the physician, and can be revoked or changed by the client at any time as long as they are competent.
</details>

---

### Question 15
The nurse is charting in a client's electronic health record (EHR) at a mobile workstation. The nurse is called away to assist another staff member. What is the required physical step the nurse must take before walking away from the computer?

A) Turn the computer monitor to face the wall.
B) Minimize the charting application window.
C) Log out of the electronic health record system.
D) Lock the wheels on the mobile workstation.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)
**Rationale:** To maintain strict client confidentiality and comply with legal requirements (HIPAA), the nurse must physically log off or lock the screen of the EHR system whenever leaving a workstation unattended. Simply turning the monitor or minimizing the screen leaves the system vulnerable to unauthorized access and charting under the nurse's credentials. 
</details>
### Question 16
A registered nurse (RN) is working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) on a medical-surgical unit. Which task is safe and appropriate for the RN to delegate to the UAP?

A. Assessing a client's wound for signs of infection.
B. Administering oral pain medication to a client.
C. Assisting a stable client with ambulation to the bathroom.
D. Teaching a client about a newly prescribed low-sodium diet.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Delegation (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
**Category:** Management of Care
UAPs can perform basic, routine care tasks such as assisting stable clients with ambulation, feeding, and hygiene. Assessing wounds, administering medications, and providing initial client teaching are strictly within the scope of practice of a licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 17
A client is scheduled for an elective surgical procedure. The surgeon previously explained the risks, benefits, and alternatives to the client. The nurse brings the informed consent form to the client to sign. The client states, "I forgot what the doctor said about the risks, but I'll sign it anyway." What is the appropriate legal and ethical action by the nurse?

A. Have the client sign the form and place it in the chart immediately.
B. Notify the surgeon that the client needs further explanation.
C. Explain the surgical risks to the client and then obtain the signature.
D. Ask a family member to co-sign and witness the signature.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Legal/Ethical (Informed consent/Confidentiality)
**Category:** Management of Care
Informed consent requires the client to fully understand the procedure, including its risks, benefits, and alternatives. If the client states they do not remember or understand, it is the provider's (surgeon's) responsibility to explain it again. The nurse advocates for the client by notifying the provider; the nurse should not step out of their scope to explain the surgical risks themselves. 
</details>

---

### Question 18
A client who was recently diagnosed with a chronic illness tells the nurse, "I just can't believe this is happening to me. It feels like my life is over." Which response by the nurse demonstrates therapeutic communication?

A. "This must be incredibly difficult for you. Can you tell me more about how you are feeling?"
B. "You need to stay positive and strong for your family during this difficult time."
C. "Everything happens for a reason, and there are excellent treatments available now."
D. "I know exactly how you feel; my family member had the exact same diagnosis."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Therapeutic Communication (Active listening/Empathy)
**Category:** Management of Care
This response uses empathy and an open-ended question to encourage the client to express their feelings, which is the core of therapeutic communication. Telling the client to stay strong, offering cliches ("everything happens for a reason"), or shifting the focus to the nurse's own personal life all block therapeutic communication and minimize the client's current emotional experience.
</details>

---

### Question 19
The nurse is teaching a client about advance directives upon admission to the hospital. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "My healthcare proxy can override my decisions while I am still awake and alert."
B. "My personal lawyer must be present in the room for my advance directive to be valid."
C. "I can change my mind and cancel my living will at any time."
D. "Only the hospital's ethics committee is legally allowed to view my living will."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Client Teaching (Do they understand the basics?)
**Category:** Management of Care
A client has the legal right to change or revoke an advance directive (such as a living will) at any time, as long as they are competent. A healthcare proxy only makes decisions when the client is incapacitated and unable to do so. A lawyer is not required to make the document valid, and the document should be readily shared with the healthcare team to guide care.
</details>

---

### Question 20
An older adult client is admitted to the medical unit from the emergency department. The client is wearing a gold wedding band and has $100 in cash. What is the most appropriate initial procedural step the nurse should take to protect the client's valuables?

A. Tape the wedding band to the client's finger and put the money in the bedside drawer.
B. Ask the client to sign a waiver of liability for lost items to protect the hospital.
C. Send the items down to the hospital security office without notifying the client.
D. Encourage the client to send the valuables home with a trusted family member.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Procedural Step (What is the first physical step in a skill?)
**Category:** Management of Care
The safest and preferred step for managing a client's valuables upon admission is to send them home with a trusted family member. If this is not possible, the items should be counted, inventoried with another nurse, and stored in the hospital safe according to facility policy. Leaving cash in a bedside drawer puts it at high risk for theft.
</details>
### Question 21
The nurse is caring for a client recently diagnosed with a terminal illness. The client begins to cry and says, "I just don't know how I am going to tell my family about this." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "Don't worry, your family will understand and support you."
B. "Would you like me to call the hospital chaplain to talk with you?"
C. "It must be very overwhelming to think about sharing this news."
D. "You should wait until you feel a little stronger before telling them."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION

**Rationale:** Option C uses the therapeutic communication technique of empathy and reflection. It acknowledges and validates the client's current feelings, leaving the door open for further communication and expression of emotions. Option A offers false reassurance. Option B passes the responsibility to another team member without first exploring the client's feelings. Option D gives unwarranted advice. 
</details>

---

### Question 22
A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a stable client who is on strict intake and output (I&O) monitoring. Which task is safe and appropriate for the RN to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Administering a prescribed oral stool softener.
B. Emptying the client's urinary catheter drainage bag and recording the amount.
C. Evaluating the client's hydration status based on the morning urine output.
D. Assessing the client's peripheral IV site for signs of infiltration.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** DELEGATION

**Rationale:** Emptying a urinary catheter bag and recording the output is a routine, standard task that falls well within the scope of practice for a UAP. Administering medication (Option A), evaluating clinical status (Option C), and assessing an IV site (Option D) all require nursing judgment and must be performed by a licensed nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 23
The nurse is evaluating a client who recently received a unit of packed red blood cells for symptomatic anemia. Which client statement indicates a positive, expected outcome of the intervention?

A. "I feel like I have more energy and I'm not breathing as hard."
B. "I feel a slight ache in my lower back that just started."
C. "I feel much warmer now and I have the chills."
D. "I feel dizzy when I try to sit up in bed."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** EVALUATION

**Rationale:** A decrease in fatigue and shortness of breath indicates improved oxygen-carrying capacity, which is the expected therapeutic outcome of a blood transfusion for anemia. Lower back pain (Option B) and chills (Option C) are unexpected signs of a potential hemolytic or febrile transfusion reaction. Dizziness (Option D) indicates ongoing instability.
</details>

---

### Question 24
A visitor approaches the nurse's station and asks for the room number of a specific client. The client has explicitly requested that no information be shared with any visitors. What is the nurse's best action to maintain confidentiality?

A. Inform the visitor that the client has asked for no visitors today.
B. Tell the visitor to call the client's family members to get the room number.
C. Provide the room number but ask the visitor to only stay for five minutes.
D. State that no information can be confirmed or provided about that individual.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL

**Rationale:** Under HIPAA and standard confidentiality guidelines, if a client opts out of the facility directory or requests total privacy, the nurse cannot even confirm that the client is admitted to the facility. Option D safely protects the client's privacy. Options A and B violate privacy by confirming the client is currently in the hospital. Option C directly violates the client's explicit request.
</details>

---

### Question 25
A nurse is preparing to transfer a client from the bed to a wheelchair using a transfer (gait) belt. What is the very first physical safety step the nurse should take before assisting the client to a standing position?

A. Ensure the wheel locks are securely engaged on both the bed and wheelchair.
B. Place the transfer belt loosely around the client's upper chest area.
C. Instruct the client to firmly wrap their arms around the nurse's neck.
D. Have the client place their bare feet flat on the smooth hospital floor.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP

**Rationale:** The most critical first physical step in safe patient transfer is ensuring that the bed and wheelchair wheels are locked to prevent the equipment from sliding, which could cause a severe fall. The gait belt goes snugly around the waist, not the chest (Option B). Clients should never pull on the nurse's neck (Option C), and they should wear non-slip footwear, not be barefoot (Option D).
</details>
### Question 26
A nurse is working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) on a medical-surgical unit. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the UAP?

A. Assessing a client's wound for signs of infection  
B. Assisting a stable client with ambulation down the hallway  
C. Administering an oral pain medication to a client  
D. Educating a client on how to use an incentive spirometer  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong> (Lens 7: Delegation)<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> 
- <strong>Option B is correct:</strong> Assisting a stable client with ambulation is a routine, standard-care task that falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. It does not require nursing judgment.<br>
- <strong>Option A is incorrect:</strong> Assessment is solely the responsibility of the registered nurse (RN) and cannot be delegated.<br>
- <strong>Option C is incorrect:</strong> Medication administration requires a nursing license (RN or LPN/LVN) and cannot be delegated to a UAP.<br>
- <strong>Option D is incorrect:</strong> Initial client education and teaching are the responsibility of the RN and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 27
A nurse receives a phone call from a person claiming to be a client's sister, asking for an update on the client's condition. What is the nurse's appropriate action to maintain client confidentiality?

A. Provide a brief, general update to ease the sister's anxiety  
B. Tell the sister to come to the hospital to get the information in person  
C. Check the client's medical record for the designated authorized contact list and privacy code  
D. Ask the sister to verify the client's date of birth before providing the update  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong> (Lens 9: Legal/Ethical)<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> 
- <strong>Option C is correct:</strong> Under HIPAA, nurses must ensure they are only sharing protected health information (PHI) with authorized individuals. Checking the record for an authorized contact list or a specific privacy code ensures compliance with legal and ethical standards.<br>
- <strong>Option A is incorrect:</strong> Providing any information, even general, to an unauthorized person is a HIPAA violation.<br>
- <strong>Option B is incorrect:</strong> Coming to the hospital does not bypass HIPAA rules; the person must still be an authorized contact.<br>
- <strong>Option D is incorrect:</strong> Knowing the client's date of birth does not prove the caller is an authorized contact to receive medical updates.
</details>

---

### Question 28
A client who is scheduled for a surgical biopsy tomorrow tells the nurse, "I am so scared that they are going to find cancer." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "It sounds like you are very worried about the biopsy results."  
B. "Most of these biopsies come back completely benign, so try not to worry."  
C. "I will ask your doctor to come give you medication to help you sleep."  
D. "Why do you think the results will show that you have cancer?"  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong> (Lens 6: Therapeutic Communication)<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> 
- <strong>Option A is correct:</strong> This response uses the therapeutic technique of reflection and active listening, acknowledging the client's feelings and encouraging them to express their concerns.<br>
- <strong>Option B is incorrect:</strong> This provides false reassurance, which minimizes the client's feelings and shuts down further communication.<br>
- <strong>Option C is incorrect:</strong> Offering medication avoids addressing the client's emotional distress and shuts down the conversation.<br>
- <strong>Option D is incorrect:</strong> Asking "why" questions can make the client feel defensive and is considered a non-therapeutic communication technique.
</details>

---

### Question 29
The nurse is teaching a client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "My advance directive means I cannot change my mind about my treatment later."  
B. "I must have a lawyer present to sign my advance directive."  
C. "This document allows my doctor to make financial decisions for me."  
D. "My living will outlines my wishes regarding life-sustaining treatments if I cannot speak for myself."  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong> (Lens 4: Client Teaching)<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> 
- <strong>Option D is correct:</strong> A living will is a type of advance directive that clearly states the client's wishes regarding healthcare and life-sustaining measures in the event they become incapacitated.<br>
- <strong>Option A is incorrect:</strong> A client can revoke or change their advance directive at any time, as long as they are competent to do so.<br>
- <strong>Option B is incorrect:</strong> While rules vary by state, a lawyer is not strictly required to complete an advance directive; witnesses or a notary are usually sufficient.<br>
- <strong>Option C is incorrect:</strong> An advance directive pertains to healthcare decisions, not financial decisions. A durable power of attorney for finances is a separate legal document.
</details>

---

### Question 30
A nurse is preparing to administer routine morning care and medications to a newly admitted client. What is the very first procedural step the nurse must take upon entering the client's room?

A. Document the time of interaction in the electronic health record  
B. Perform hand hygiene and verify the client's identity using two identifiers  
C. Explain the planned schedule for the day to the client  
D. Check the client's vital signs and oxygen saturation  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong> (Lens 10: Procedural Step)<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> 
- <strong>Option B is correct:</strong> The foundational first step in any client encounter, especially before providing care or medications, is to perform hand hygiene (for infection control) and correctly identify the client using two acceptable identifiers (such as name and date of birth) to ensure safe management of care.<br>
- <strong>Option A is incorrect:</strong> Documentation occurs after care is provided, not as the first step upon entering the room.<br>
- <strong>Option C is incorrect:</strong> Explaining the schedule is important, but only after the nurse has ensured they are speaking to the correct client.<br>
- <strong>Option D is incorrect:</strong> Taking vital signs is a nursing action that must be preceded by confirming the client's identity.
</details>
### Question 31
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
A registered nurse (RN) is planning morning care for a group of clients. Which of the following tasks is safe and appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating a client's ability to swallow during breakfast.
B) Providing a client with discharge instructions about bathing.
C) Assisting a stable client with a bed bath and routine oral care.
D) Administering a prescribed topical antibiotic ointment to a wound.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) are trained to assist with standard activities of daily living (ADLs), such as bathing, grooming, and routine oral care for stable clients. 
**Why other options are incorrect:**
- **A:** Evaluating swallowing is an assessment, which falls under the RN's scope of practice.
- **B:** Client teaching and discharge instructions are the responsibility of the RN and cannot be delegated.
- **D:** Administering medications, including topical prescription ointments, is generally outside the scope of practice for a UAP and must be performed by a licensed nurse (RN or LPN/LVN).
</details>

---

### Question 32
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)
A nurse receives a phone call from an individual claiming to be a client's employer. The caller asks for a general update on the client's medical condition. The client has not signed a release of information form for this individual. What is the nurse's best action?

A) Inform the caller that no information can be provided without the client's permission.
B) Provide a brief update stating only that the client is currently in stable condition.
C) Transfer the phone call directly to the client's room so the employer can ask them.
D) Ask the caller to provide their employee ID number to verify their identity before speaking.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), a nurse cannot disclose any protected health information (PHI) to unauthorized individuals, including employers, without the explicit consent of the client. 
**Why other options are incorrect:**
- **B:** Even providing a "general update" or confirming the client is stable is a violation of HIPAA if the caller is not authorized.
- **C:** Transferring the call confirms the client's presence in the facility, which is a breach of confidentiality.
- **D:** Verifying the caller's identity does not bypass the legal requirement for the client's explicit authorization to share medical information.
</details>

---

### Question 33
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)
A client scheduled for an elective surgical procedure suddenly tells the nurse, "I changed my mind. I do not want to have this surgery." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "You have already signed the consent form, so we really should proceed."
B) "I will cancel the surgery immediately and prepare your discharge papers."
C) "The surgeon is an expert, and you really need this procedure to get better."
D) "Can you tell me more about your concerns regarding the surgery?"

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** The nurse should use therapeutic communication to explore the client's feelings and concerns. Using an open-ended question ("Can you tell me more...") allows the client to express their fears or lack of understanding, which the nurse can then address or relay to the provider.
**Why other options are incorrect:**
- **A:** A client has the legal right to revoke informed consent at any time, even after signing the form.
- **B:** While the client has the right to refuse, canceling immediately without exploring their feelings is premature and unsupportive. 
- **C:** Giving personal opinions or advice blocks therapeutic communication and dismisses the client's autonomy and feelings.
</details>

---

### Question 34
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/use?)
A nurse is preparing to transfer a client from the bed to a chair using a mechanical lift. Which of the following findings makes it unsafe to proceed with the transfer?

A) The client is completely non-weight-bearing on both lower extremities.
B) The mechanical lift sling has a small tear near the weight-bearing seam.
C) The client requires the physical assistance of two nursing staff members.
D) The client currently weighs 250 lbs (113 kg).

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Equipment safety is a critical component of care management. A tear in the fabric of a mechanical lift sling compromises the integrity of the equipment, creating an immediate risk of the client falling. The sling must not be used and should be removed from service.
**Why other options are incorrect:**
- **A:** Being non-weight-bearing is a primary indication for using a mechanical lift, not a contraindication.
- **C:** Using a mechanical lift safely often requires the presence and assistance of two staff members according to facility policy.
- **D:** Most standard mechanical lifts have weight capacities far exceeding 250 lbs (often 400-600 lbs). The nurse should verify the lift's limit, but this weight alone is not a contraindication.
</details>

---

### Question 35
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill/process?)
A mentally competent adult client expresses the desire to establish an advance directive to outline their future healthcare wishes. What is the nurse's first action to facilitate this process?

A) Provide the client with written, objective information about advance directives.
B) Call the hospital's legal department to have them draft the specific document.
C) Ask the client's family members to make healthcare decisions on their behalf.
D) Place a Do Not Resuscitate (DNR) bracelet immediately on the client's wrist.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Under the Patient Self-Determination Act (PSDA), healthcare facilities must provide clients with written information about their right to make medical decisions and formulate advance directives. Providing this information is the first step in helping the client understand their options.
**Why other options are incorrect:**
- **B:** The nurse does not need to call the legal department to draft the document; standard forms are generally provided by the facility or social services for the client to complete.
- **C:** An advance directive allows the client to make their *own* choices or appoint a specific proxy. Asking the family to make decisions prematurely bypasses the client's current autonomy.
- **D:** Wanting to complete an advance directive does not automatically mean the client wants a DNR order. The specific wishes must be formalized first.
</details>
### Question 36
A nurse is working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) on a medical-surgical unit. Which of the following tasks is safe and appropriate to delegate to the UAP?

A. Providing discharge instructions to a client going home.
B. Evaluating a client's response to pain medication.
C. Assisting a stable client with a routine bed bath.
D. Showing a client how to properly use an incentive spirometer.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
**Reasoning:** Assisting a stable client with activities of daily living (ADLs), such as a bed bath, is a routine task with a predictable outcome that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. 
**Incorrect Options:**
A, B, D: Teaching (discharge instructions, incentive spirometer) and evaluating (response to pain medication) require nursing judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP. These are strictly the responsibility of the registered nurse (RN).
</details>

---

### Question 37
A client is scheduled for an elective surgical procedure. The healthcare provider has just finished explaining the risks, benefits, and alternatives of the surgery to the client. What is the nurse's role in the informed consent process?

A. Witnessing the client's voluntary signature on the consent form.
B. Explaining the surgical risks again if the client forgets them.
C. Convincing the client that the surgery is their best possible option.
D. Selecting alternative, non-surgical treatments for the client to consider.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)
**Reasoning:** The nurse's primary role in informed consent is to act as a witness. This ensures the client is signing the form voluntarily, is competent to sign, and that the signature belongs to the client.
**Incorrect Options:**
B, D: It is the provider's legal responsibility to explain risks, benefits, and alternatives. If the client has further questions about the procedure itself, the nurse must call the provider back.
C: The nurse must act as an advocate and remain objective; convincing or coercing a client into a procedure is unethical.
</details>

---

### Question 38
A client becomes tearful and tells the nurse, "I am so afraid of having to go to a nursing home after I leave the hospital." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "Most people do very well in nursing homes once they get used to the routine."
B. "You seem very worried about going to a nursing home. Tell me more about that."
C. "Why are you so afraid of going to the nursing home?"
D. "The social worker will make sure you go to a really good and safe facility."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)
**Reasoning:** This response uses the therapeutic techniques of reflection and broad opening. It validates the client's feelings ("You seem very worried") and encourages them to openly share their concerns ("Tell me more about that"). 
**Incorrect Options:**
A, D: These statements offer false reassurance, which invalidates the client's feelings and minimizes their valid concerns.
C: Asking "Why" questions can make the client feel defensive and shut down communication.
</details>

---

### Question 39
The nurse is providing education to a newly admitted client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "My advance directive can only be changed or canceled by my doctor."
B. "I must have a lawyer present in the room to sign my advance directive."
C. "Signing this means I am giving up my right to refuse treatment right now."
D. "This document tells the healthcare team my wishes if I cannot speak for myself."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Reasoning:** An advance directive (such as a living will or healthcare proxy) is a legal document that outlines a client's healthcare wishes or designates an individual to make decisions if the client becomes incapacitated and unable to communicate.
**Incorrect Options:**
A: A client can change or revoke their advance directive at any time as long as they are competent; the doctor does not do this.
B: A lawyer is not required to complete or sign an advance directive, though witnesses or a notary may be required depending on state laws.
C: An advance directive only takes effect when the client loses the capacity to make decisions. The client retains full right to refuse treatment while competent.
</details>

---

### Question 40
A nurse is preparing to transfer a client from the medical-surgical unit to a physical rehabilitation facility. What is an essential procedural step the nurse must take to ensure safe continuity of care?

A. Leave a handwritten note outlining the client's needs on the bedside table.
B. Ask the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) to call the facility and give the report.
C. Provide a standardized hand-off report directly to the receiving nurse.
D. Delete the client's historical data from the electronic health record to protect privacy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)
**Reasoning:** Providing a structured, standardized hand-off report (e.g., using SBAR) to the receiving nurse is an essential step in care management. It ensures that critical information regarding the client's condition, medications, and care plan is safely accurately transferred to the next provider.
**Incorrect Options:**
A: Leaving a note is unsafe and does not guarantee that the receiving nurse will receive or understand the information.
B: Giving a hand-off report requires nursing knowledge and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
D: A client's historical data is a permanent part of the medical record and should never be deleted.
</details>
### Question 41
The registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a stable client who requires assistance with activities of daily living. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Evaluating the client's response to recently administered pain medication.
B. Assisting the client with a bed bath and oral hygiene.
C. Teaching the client about a newly prescribed low-sodium diet.
D. Assessing the client's skin integrity over bony prominences.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Assisting the client with a bed bath and oral hygiene.

**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)

**Rationale:** The UAP can safely perform basic, routine tasks for stable clients, such as hygiene (bathing, oral care), feeding, and ambulating. Evaluating, teaching, and assessing are core nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 42
A client is scheduled for an elective surgical procedure. The surgeon has explained the risks, benefits, and alternatives to the client. What is the nurse's primary role regarding the informed consent process?

A. Explaining the specific surgical steps in detail to the client.
B. Persuading the client to undergo the procedure for their health.
C. Witnessing the client's signature on the consent form.
D. Deciding which family member should sign the form for the client.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Witnessing the client's signature on the consent form.

**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)

**Rationale:** The nurse's primary role in informed consent is to witness the client's signature, ensuring that the client is signing voluntarily and appears competent to do so. It is the healthcare provider's responsibility to explain the procedure, risks, and benefits. The nurse should never persuade a client, and a competent adult must sign their own consent.
</details>

---

### Question 43
A client tells the nurse, "I am so scared about the biopsy results tomorrow. I don't know what I'll do if it's cancer." Which response by the nurse demonstrates the best therapeutic communication?

A. "Everything will be fine; most biopsies come back benign."
B. "Why do you think the results will show cancer?"
C. "Don't worry, we have excellent cancer treatments now."
D. "It sounds like you are feeling very anxious about the results."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. "It sounds like you are feeling very anxious about the results."

**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)

**Rationale:** Option D uses the therapeutic techniques of reflection and empathy, acknowledging the client's feelings and leaving the door open for further communication. Options A and C offer false reassurance, which invalidates the client's feelings. Option B uses a "why" question, which can make the client feel defensive and is considered non-therapeutic.
</details>

---

### Question 44
The nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client who will be using a walker for the first time. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the basic teaching?

A. "I will move the walker forward, then step forward with my weaker leg."
B. "I should carry the walker up the stairs instead of using the handrail."
C. "I will place my full weight on the walker while pulling myself up from a chair."
D. "I need to look straight down at my feet the whole time I am walking."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. "I will move the walker forward, then step forward with my weaker leg."

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

**Rationale:** The correct basic sequence for using a standard walker is to move the walker forward, step with the weaker leg into the walker, and then follow with the stronger leg. Using a walker to pull up from a chair is a safety hazard as it can easily tip over. Walkers are not safe to use on stairs. Looking straight down at the feet causes poor posture and can lead to a loss of balance.
</details>

---

### Question 45
The nurse is conducting a safety assessment on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for developing a healthcare-associated infection (HAI)?

A. A client recovering from a mild concussion with no invasive lines.
B. A client receiving intravenous fluids through a peripheral line placed 12 hours ago.
C. A client prescribed oral antibiotics for a community-acquired pneumonia.
D. A client with an indwelling urinary catheter that was placed three days ago.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. A client with an indwelling urinary catheter that was placed three days ago.

**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

**Rationale:** Indwelling urinary catheters are a major risk factor for healthcare-associated infections, specifically catheter-associated urinary tract infections (CAUTIs). The longer the catheter remains in place, the higher the risk of infection. While peripheral IVs carry a slight risk, a catheter in place for three days presents a significantly higher and more common risk for an HAI compared to the other options.
</details>
### Question 46
**Lens: DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)**

A nurse is caring for a stable client who is 2 days post-operative. Which task is appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assess the client's surgical incision for signs of redness.
B) Administer the client's scheduled oral pain medication.
C) Assist the client with a morning bed bath.
D) Teach the client about the signs and symptoms of infection.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client with hygiene needs, such as a bed bath, is a standard, unchanging procedure that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing incisions, administering medications, and providing client teaching all require nursing judgment and must be performed by a licensed nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 47
**Lens: LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)**

A client is scheduled for a surgical procedure. The surgeon has explained the risks, benefits, and alternatives to the client. What is the primary role of the nurse when the client signs the informed consent form?

A) To explain the specific surgical risks to the client.
B) To witness that the client's signature is given voluntarily.
C) To ensure the client's family agrees with the medical decision.
D) To suggest safer alternative treatments to the client.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** The nurse's role in informed consent is to act as a witness to the client's signature, ensuring that the client is signing voluntarily, is competent, and appears to understand what they have been told. Explaining risks, benefits, and alternatives is the legal responsibility of the healthcare provider performing the procedure.
</details>

---

### Question 48
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

The nurse is providing information about advance directives to a newly admitted client. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I can change my mind about my advance directive at any time."
B) "Only my lawyer can write or alter my advance directive."
C) "This document means I am giving up my right to receive any medical care."
D) "My family can override my advance directive if they disagree with it."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** A client retains the right to revoke or change their advance directive at any time, as long as they are mentally competent to do so. Advance directives do not require a lawyer, they do not mean the client gives up all medical care (they just specify what care is desired), and family members cannot legally override a valid advance directive.
</details>

---

### Question 49
**Lens: THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)**

A client tells the nurse, "I don't want to take this medication anymore, but the doctor insists." What is the most therapeutic response by the nurse to advocate for the client?

A) "You really need to follow the doctor's orders to get better."
B) "Why do you want to stop taking your medication?"
C) "It sounds like you have concerns about taking this medication. Can you tell me more?"
D) "I will just document in your chart that you refused your medication today."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Reflecting the client's feelings and encouraging an open dialogue ("It sounds like you have concerns...") is highly therapeutic. It validates the client's feelings and allows the nurse to explore the root of the refusal to better advocate for them. "Why" questions can make the client defensive, and simply telling them to follow orders ignores their right to self-determination. 
</details>

---

### Question 50
**Lens: EVALUATION (Is the patient stable after care?)**

A nurse implements a new turning and repositioning schedule for a bedbound client to prevent skin breakdown. Which assessment finding best indicates that the care plan is effective?

A) The client's heels display non-blanchable redness.
B) The client reports discomfort when turned to the right side.
C) The client requires a friction-reducing sheet for movement.
D) The client's skin over all bony prominences remains intact.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Evaluating the effectiveness of a care plan involves looking at the expected outcomes. The goal of a turning schedule is to prevent pressure injuries. Intact skin over bony prominences demonstrates that the nursing interventions are successfully preventing skin breakdown. Non-blanchable redness (Option A) would indicate the interventions are failing and a Stage 1 pressure injury has developed. 
</details>
### Question 51
**Lens:** DELEGATION
A registered nurse (RN) is assigning tasks for a client who is 2 days postoperative following a total hip replacement. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Assess the surgical incision for signs of redness and infection.
B) Teach the client how to properly use the incentive spirometer.
C) Administer the client's scheduled oral pain medication.
D) Assist the client with a morning bed bath and daily hygiene.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client with activities of daily living (ADLs), such as a bed bath and daily hygiene, is a routine task that falls within the scope of practice for Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP). 
**Why other options are wrong:**
- **A:** Assessment of a surgical site requires nursing judgment and must be performed by the RN.
- **B:** Client education and teaching are solely the responsibility of the RN and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
- **C:** Medication administration requires a licensed nurse (RN or LPN/LVN, depending on state practice acts) and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 52
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
A client is scheduled for an elective cholecystectomy. The surgeon has just explained the risks, benefits, and alternatives to the surgery. The nurse brings the consent form to the client to sign. The client states, "I'm still not sure what happens if they find a gallstone in my bile duct." What is the appropriate legal and ethical action by the nurse?

A) Ask the surgeon to return to the client's room to answer their questions.
B) Explain the bile duct exploration procedure to the client before they sign.
C) Have the client sign the form and note their question in the medical record.
D) Tell the client this is a routine surgery and there is nothing to worry about.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Informed consent requires that the client fully understands the procedure, including risks, benefits, and alternatives. The provider performing the procedure (the surgeon) is legally responsible for answering medical questions and ensuring understanding. If the client has lingering questions about the procedure itself, the nurse must advocate for the client by asking the surgeon to return.
**Why other options are wrong:**
- **B:** It is outside the nurse's scope of practice to explain the medical/surgical details of the procedure for the purpose of informed consent.
- **C:** The client cannot legally sign the consent form if they do not fully understand the procedure; having them sign anyway violates informed consent principles.
- **D:** Telling the client not to worry is false reassurance, which is non-therapeutic and unethical.
</details>

---

### Question 53
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client about their newly completed advance directive. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "My doctor will make all the medical decisions if I become unable to speak for myself."
B) "This document tells my healthcare team what treatments I want if I cannot speak for myself."
C) "I cannot change my mind about this document once it is placed in my medical record."
D) "My family members can override my advance directive if they disagree with my choices."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** An advance directive is a legal document that outlines a client's healthcare wishes (such as a living will) or designates a surrogate decision-maker (healthcare proxy) if the client loses the capacity to make or communicate decisions. 
**Why other options are wrong:**
- **A:** The advance directive (or a designated healthcare proxy), not the physician, guides decision-making if the client is incapacitated.
- **C:** A client can update, change, or revoke their advance directive at any time as long as they are competent to do so.
- **D:** Family members cannot legally override a valid advance directive; the document is specifically designed to ensure the client's own wishes are honored.
</details>

---

### Question 54
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
The nurse is participating in an interdisciplinary care conference for a client recovering from a stroke. The team is discussing the expected roles of each discipline. Which outcome represents an expected goal specifically for the physical therapist's role on the care team?

A) The client will safely swallow mechanical soft foods without aspirating.
B) The client will independently administer their daily insulin injections.
C) The client will safely transfer from the bed to a chair using a walker.
D) The client will express their feelings regarding the loss of independence.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Physical therapy (PT) focuses on gross motor skills, mobility, strength, and safe transfers (e.g., bed to chair using assistive devices). This is an expected goal for the PT.
**Why other options are wrong:**
- **A:** Swallowing and preventing aspiration are managed by the Speech-Language Pathologist (SLP).
- **B:** Administering insulin involves fine motor skills and health management, which are typically addressed by Occupational Therapy (OT) and Nursing.
- **C:** Addressing emotional coping and loss of independence is the expected domain of Social Work, Psychology, or Nursing.
</details>
