### Question 55
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
A client who recently received a terminal diagnosis tells the nurse, "I don't see the point in filling out these advance directives. I am just going to die anyway." Which response by the nurse demonstrates therapeutic communication?

A) "It sounds like you are feeling overwhelmed right now. Tell me more about what you are thinking."
B) "You should fill them out because it is hospital policy for all admitted patients."
C) "Don't worry, your family can make all the decisions for you when the time comes."
D) "Why would you want to leave such important decisions up to chance?"

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** This response uses the therapeutic communication techniques of acknowledging feelings ("It sounds like you are feeling overwhelmed") and using an open-ended prompt ("Tell me more") to encourage the client to express their emotions and concerns regarding their diagnosis and care.
**Why other options are wrong:**
- **B:** Citing hospital policy is dismissive of the client's feelings and blocks further communication.
- **C:** Saying "Don't worry" provides false reassurance and minimizes the client's valid emotional response to a terminal diagnosis.
- **D:** Asking "Why" questions can make the client feel defensive and judged, which halts the therapeutic process.
</details>
### Question 56
**Lens:** DELEGATION 
**Category:** Management of Care
**Scenario:** A registered nurse (RN) is delegating tasks to an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) for a client who is preparing for discharge. Which task is appropriate to delegate to the UAP?

A. Helping the client pack their personal belongings.
B. Reviewing the discharge medication list with the client.
C. Assessing the client's readiness to learn discharge instructions.
D. Answering the client's questions about follow-up appointments.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** Packing belongings is a standard, routine task with a predictable outcome that does not require nursing judgment, making it safe and appropriate to delegate to a UAP.
- **B is incorrect:** Reviewing medications requires nursing knowledge and patient teaching, which falls outside the UAP's scope of practice.
- **C is incorrect:** Assessment is a core function of the RN and can never be delegated to a UAP.
- **D is incorrect:** Providing medical or logistical information regarding follow-up care involves education and nursing judgment, which cannot be delegated.

</details>

---

### Question 57
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL 
**Category:** Management of Care
**Scenario:** The nurse is caring for a high-profile client in the medical-surgical unit. A person claiming to be the client's sibling calls the nursing station asking for an update on the client's condition. The client has not provided a passcode or consent to share medical information. Which action should the nurse take?

A. Transfer the call to the client's attending physician.
B. Confirm the client is admitted but state no details can be shared.
C. State that no information can be provided about any client.
D. Ask the caller for their date of birth to verify their identity.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** 
- **C is correct:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), a nurse cannot acknowledge a client's presence in the facility or share any information without the client's explicit consent. The nurse should give a neutral response stating that no information can be given out.
- **A is incorrect:** Transferring the call to the physician still violates HIPAA, as it implicitly confirms the client is admitted.
- **B is incorrect:** Confirming the client's admission violates the client's privacy and HIPAA laws.
- **D is incorrect:** Verifying the caller's identity does not bypass the legal requirement to have the client's documented consent before sharing information.

</details>

---

### Question 58
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION 
**Category:** Management of Care
**Scenario:** A client who was admitted for pneumonia abruptly states, "I am tired of being in the hospital and I want to go home right now." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "You cannot leave until the doctor writes your discharge orders."
B. "Tell me more about what is making you want to leave the hospital."
C. "If you leave now, your insurance might not pay for your stay."
D. "I will go get the Against Medical Advice forms for you to sign."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** 
- **B is correct:** Using an open-ended statement like "Tell me more" encourages the client to express their feelings, allowing the nurse to identify and potentially resolve the underlying issue before the client leaves Against Medical Advice (AMA).
- **A is incorrect:** This is an authoritarian and legally false statement. Clients have the right to leave AMA.
- **C is incorrect:** While leaving AMA can have financial implications, threatening or coercing the client with financial consequences is non-therapeutic and blocks communication.
- **D is incorrect:** While getting the AMA forms may eventually be necessary, the nurse should first attempt to explore the client's concerns and advocate for their needs.

</details>

---

### Question 59
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING 
**Category:** Management of Care
**Scenario:** The nurse is providing information to a client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I must hire a lawyer to make my advance directive legally binding."
B. "My healthcare proxy can override my decisions while I am awake and alert."
C. "This document will only be used if I become unable to speak for myself."
D. "Once I sign this document, I am no longer allowed to change my mind."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** 
- **C is correct:** An advance directive (such as a living will or durable power of attorney for healthcare) only goes into effect when the client becomes incapacitated and loses the ability to make or communicate their own medical decisions.
- **A is incorrect:** A lawyer is not required to create an advance directive. It generally only requires a notary or witnesses, depending on state laws.
- **B is incorrect:** As long as the client is competent and alert, they retain complete control over their healthcare decisions. A proxy cannot override them.
- **D is incorrect:** A client can update, change, or revoke their advance directive at any time as long as they are competent to do so.

</details>

---

### Question 60
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION 
**Category:** Management of Care
**Scenario:** The nurse is reviewing unit events from the shift to determine if an incident report (variance report) needs to be filed. Which situation requires the completion of an incident report?

A. A client received their prescribed antibiotic 15 minutes late.
B. A client refused to take their scheduled morning daily vitamin.
C. A nurse requested a translator for a client who speaks limited English.
D. A visitor slipped on a wet spot in the hallway but was not injured.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** 
- **D is correct:** An incident report must be completed for any unexpected event that affects the safety of a client, staff member, or visitor. Even if the visitor was not injured, the slip is a variance from normal safe operations and must be reported for risk management and quality improvement.
- **A is incorrect:** Most facilities allow a 30- to 60-minute window before or after the scheduled time for medication administration; 15 minutes is within standard limits and does not require a report.
- **B is incorrect:** Clients have the right to refuse medication. This should be documented in the medical record, but it does not generally require a separate incident report.
- **C is incorrect:** Requesting an interpreter is standard nursing practice and legally required for clients with limited English proficiency. It is not an adverse incident.

</details>
### Question 61
A client is scheduled for a surgical procedure and the healthcare provider has just finished explaining the risks, benefits, and alternatives. What is the nurse's primary role in the informed consent process?

A) To explain the specific details of the surgical technique to the client.
B) To witness the client's signature on the informed consent document.
C) To determine if the client should choose a different treatment option.
D) To outline the potential complications of the anesthesia.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<em>Lens: LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)</em><br>
The nurse's primary role in informed consent is to act as a witness to the client's signature, ensuring the client is competent, acting voluntarily, and has had their questions answered by the provider. Explaining risks, benefits, surgical techniques, and complications is the responsibility of the healthcare provider performing the procedure.
</details>

---

### Question 62
A registered nurse (RN) is working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Which of the following tasks is appropriate and safe for the RN to delegate to the UAP?

A) Assessing a client's newly formed surgical incision.
B) Providing discharge instructions to a client going home.
C) Administering oral pain medication to a client.
D) Assisting a stable client with a routine bed bath.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<em>Lens: DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)</em><br>
Assisting a stable client with activities of daily living (ADLs), such as a bed bath, is a routine, standard task that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing incisions, providing teaching, and administering medications require a nursing license and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 63
A client diagnosed with a chronic illness tells the nurse, "I am so overwhelmed by all these new treatment choices. I just don't know what to do." Which response by the nurse demonstrates active listening and empathy?

A) "You seem to be feeling very overwhelmed by all the options right now."
B) "You should just pick the option that your doctor recommended to you."
C) "Don't worry, most people feel this way when they are in the hospital."
D) "I will ask the doctor to come back and make the decision for you."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<em>Lens: THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)</em><br>
Option A uses the therapeutic communication technique of reflection, which validates the client's feelings and encourages them to express their thoughts further. Giving advice (Option B), minimizing feelings (Option C), and taking away the client's autonomy (Option D) are non-therapeutic communication blocks.
</details>

---

### Question 64
The nurse is providing education to a client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "My advance directive can only be changed by my primary care doctor."
B) "I must have a lawyer present with me to fill out an advance directive."
C) "This document tells my healthcare team my wishes if I cannot speak for myself."
D) "Once I sign this document, I can never change my mind about my care."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<em>Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)</em><br>
An advance directive is a legal document that allows a client to outline their preferences for medical care (or appoint a proxy) in the event they become incapacitated and unable to communicate. Clients do not need a lawyer to complete one, and they have the right to change or revoke their advance directive at any time.
</details>

---

### Question 65
The nurse is completing an admission assessment on a client. Which of the following findings would most strongly indicate that the client is at a high risk for falls?

A) The client wears reading glasses to look at the daily food menu.
B) The client has a history of falling at home two weeks ago.
C) The client is currently receiving a daily oral vitamin supplement.
D) The client requests help from the nurse when completing their admission paperwork.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<em>Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)</em><br>
A recent history of falls is one of the strongest recognized predictors and risk factors for future falls in the healthcare setting. Wearing reading glasses for close-up tasks, taking daily vitamins, and asking for help with paperwork do not inherently place a client at a high risk for physical falls.
</details>
### Question 66
The nurse answers a phone call at the nurse's station from an individual claiming to be the client's sister. The caller asks for an update on the client's condition. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate?

A. Give a brief, generalized update without sharing specific medical details.
B. Check the client's chart for an authorized information-sharing code or list.
C. Transfer the call immediately to the attending physician.
D. Ask the caller to provide their date of birth to verify their identity.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Check the client's chart for an authorized information-sharing code or list.

**Rationale:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), nurses must protect client confidentiality. Before sharing any health information, the nurse must verify that the caller is authorized by the client to receive updates, which is typically done by checking the chart for an approved list of contacts or asking the caller for a pre-established privacy code. Giving a "generalized" update still violates HIPAA. 

**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
</details>

---

### Question 67
The registered nurse (RN) is assigning tasks for the shift. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Assisting a stable client with a bed bath and routine oral care.
B. Evaluating the effectiveness of a client's prescribed pain medication.
C. Showing a client how to properly use a walker for the first time.
D. Explaining the side effects of a newly prescribed blood pressure medication.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Assisting a stable client with a bed bath and routine oral care.

**Rationale:** The RN can safely delegate routine, standard tasks that do not require nursing judgment to the UAP, such as assisting with activities of daily living (ADLs) like hygiene, bathing, and oral care for stable clients. Evaluating effectiveness of meds, initial teaching, and explaining medication side effects are all responsibilities that require nursing judgment and must be performed by the RN.

**Lens:** DELEGATION
</details>

---

### Question 68
A client scheduled for a diagnostic biopsy tomorrow is pacing the room and states, "I am so scared they are going to find cancer." What is the best therapeutic response by the nurse?

A. "Try not to worry; most biopsies come back completely normal."
B. "Your doctor is excellent, so you are in very good hands."
C. "It sounds like you are feeling very anxious about the results."
D. "Why do you think the results will show that you have cancer?"

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. "It sounds like you are feeling very anxious about the results."

**Rationale:** This response uses the therapeutic communication techniques of reflection and validation. It acknowledges the client's feelings and opens the door for further discussion without minimizing their fears. Options A and B offer false reassurance. Option D uses a "why" question, which can make the client feel defensive.

**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
</details>

---

### Question 69
The nurse is providing teaching about advance directives to a client upon admission to the hospital. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I must have a lawyer present to sign my advance directive."
B. "My advance directive can only be changed by my primary healthcare provider."
C. "My spouse will automatically make all my decisions, even if I am awake and alert."
D. "I can change my mind and update my advance directive at any time."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. "I can change my mind and update my advance directive at any time."

**Rationale:** Clients have the right to update, change, or revoke an advance directive at any time as long as they are competent. A lawyer is not required to complete an advance directive. The client retains all decision-making capacity as long as they are alert, oriented, and competent; the proxy only steps in if the client is unable to make decisions. 

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
</details>

---

### Question 70
The nurse is admitting four clients to the medical-surgical unit. Which client should the nurse identify as having the highest risk for a fall?

A. A 45-year-old client with pneumonia receiving intravenous antibiotics.
B. A 78-year-old client with nocturia who is taking a prescribed diuretic.
C. A 60-year-old client with a cast on their left arm for a fractured radius.
D. A 30-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. A 78-year-old client with nocturia who is taking a prescribed diuretic.

**Rationale:** Advanced age combined with nocturia (waking up at night to urinate) and the use of a diuretic increases the urgency and frequency of nighttime trips to the bathroom. This combination significantly increases the client's risk of tripping and falling, especially in an unfamiliar hospital environment.

**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
</details>
### Question 71
A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a group of clients and working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the UAP?

A. Assessing a new admission’s skin integrity and risk for pressure injuries.
B. Providing discharge instructions to a client regarding proper wound care.
C. Assisting a medically stable client with a routine morning bed bath.
D. Evaluating a client’s response thirty minutes after administering pain medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Assisting a medically stable client with a routine morning bed bath.

**Rationale:** 
- **Lens:** Delegation
- **Explanation:** Assisting a stable client with activities of daily living (ADLs), such as a bed bath, falls within the standard scope of practice for an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Options A, B, and D all require the specialized skill, knowledge, and clinical judgment of a registered nurse (assessing, teaching, and evaluating) and cannot be legally delegated to a UAP under the principles of safe delegation.
</details>

---

### Question 72
A nurse receives a phone call from an individual claiming to be the cousin of a client admitted to the medical-surgical unit. The caller asks for an update on the client's condition. The client has not provided authorization to share medical information with this individual. What is the most appropriate action by the nurse?

A. Provide a brief, general update but avoid sharing specific laboratory results.
B. State that privacy laws prevent confirming or denying the client's presence on the unit.
C. Tell the caller to contact the healthcare provider directly for any medical updates.
D. Ask the caller to prove their relationship to the client before speaking about the care.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. State that privacy laws prevent confirming or denying the client's presence on the unit.

**Rationale:** 
- **Lens:** Legal/Ethical
- **Explanation:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), healthcare workers must protect client confidentiality. If a client has not given explicit permission to share information, the nurse cannot disclose any details, including whether the client is even admitted to the facility. Answering questions, referring them to the doctor, or verifying identity without consent all violate HIPAA regulations.
</details>

---

### Question 73
A nurse is receiving a telephone prescription from a healthcare provider for a client's new antibiotic. The nurse writes the prescription down on the client's chart. What is the immediate next step the nurse must take?

A. Administer the first dose of the medication to the client.
B. Call the hospital pharmacy to verify the medication is currently in stock.
C. Have another registered nurse co-sign the written order immediately.
D. Read the complete prescription back to the healthcare provider.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Read the complete prescription back to the healthcare provider.

**Rationale:** 
- **Lens:** Procedural Step
- **Explanation:** When receiving a verbal or telephone order, the Joint Commission strictly requires the nurse to write the order down and then "read back" the complete prescription to the prescriber. This critical procedural step ensures accuracy and prevents potentially fatal medication errors before any further action is taken.
</details>

---

### Question 74
A client who was admitted for severe pneumonia tells the nurse, "I am tired of being in this hospital. I want to go home right now." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "Can you tell me more about why you feel like leaving today?"
B. "You cannot leave until the doctor formally writes your discharge order."
C. "If you leave right now, your insurance will not pay for your hospital stay."
D. "I will go get the Against Medical Advice (AMA) forms for you to sign."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. "Can you tell me more about why you feel like leaving today?"

**Rationale:** 
- **Lens:** Therapeutic Communication
- **Explanation:** Using open-ended questions encourages the client to express their feelings and allows the nurse to identify the root cause of the client's frustration. This demonstrates active listening and advocacy. Option B is threatening/false imprisonment. Option C is non-therapeutic and often factually incorrect. Option D prematurely jumps to paperwork without first exploring the client's concerns. 
</details>

---

### Question 75
A nurse is educating a client about advance directives during the admission process. Which statement by the client indicates an accurate understanding of the teaching?

A. "Signing this document means I am giving up my right to receive pain medication."
B. "My advance directive can only be changed by my primary healthcare provider."
C. "This document allows me to state my wishes for medical care if I cannot speak for myself."
D. "I must have an attorney present when I sign this document for it to be legal."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. "This document allows me to state my wishes for medical care if I cannot speak for myself."

**Rationale:** 
- **Lens:** Client Teaching
- **Explanation:** An advance directive (such as a living will) is a legal document that dictates a client's specific medical preferences (e.g., mechanical ventilation, CPR) in the event they lose the capacity to make or communicate those decisions. Clients do not give up their right to comfort care/pain management, they can change the document at any time themselves, and an attorney is not required to be present for it to be valid.
</details>
### Question 76
**Lens:** DELEGATION
A nurse is assigning tasks to an Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP) on a medical-surgical unit. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the UAP?

A) Evaluating the skin integrity of a bedbound client.
B) Administering a scheduled oral stool softener.
C) Emptying and recording the output of a Foley catheter.
D) Providing discharge instructions about a new walker.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Emptying a Foley catheter and recording intake and output (I&O) is a standard, routine procedure that falls safely within the scope of practice for a UAP. Evaluating skin integrity requires nursing assessment (A), administering medications requires a nursing license (B), and providing discharge instructions involves client education (D), all of which must be performed by the registered nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 77
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
A client is scheduled for an elective cholecystectomy. The surgeon has explained the procedure and asked the client to sign the consent form. What is the primary role of the nurse when witnessing the client's signature?

A) Ensuring the client voluntarily signs the consent form and appears competent.
B) Explaining the potential risks and complications of the surgery.
C) Describing alternative medical treatments to the surgical procedure.
D) Answering the client's specific questions regarding the surgical technique.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** The nurse's role as a witness to informed consent is to ensure that the client is signing the form voluntarily, is competent to do so, and that the signature belongs to the client. Explaining risks, describing alternative treatments, and answering technical questions about the procedure are the legal responsibilities of the provider performing the procedure.
</details>

---

### Question 78
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
The nurse is providing education to a client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I must have a lawyer present at the hospital to sign my living will."
B) "If I create an advance directive, it is permanent and I can never change it."
C) "The hospital will automatically make decisions for me if I don't have this form."
D) "My healthcare proxy can only make decisions for me if I am unable to do so."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** A healthcare proxy (durable power of attorney for healthcare) only goes into effect when the client becomes incapacitated and is unable to make or communicate their own healthcare decisions. A lawyer is not required to complete an advance directive (A). Advance directives can be updated or revoked by the client at any time (B). If a client lacks an advance directive, decisions usually fall to the legal next of kin, not the hospital automatically (C).
</details>

---

### Question 79
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
A client newly diagnosed with terminal cancer tells the nurse, "I don't want to go through chemotherapy, but my family is begging me to do it. I don't know what to do." Which response by the nurse uses a therapeutic communication technique?

A) "You should listen to your family since they love you and want what is best."
B) "It sounds like you are feeling torn between your own wishes and your family's."
C) "Why don't you want to try the chemotherapy your doctor recommended?"
D) "I will call the doctor so they can explain the benefits of chemotherapy again."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** "It sounds like you are feeling torn..." is an example of reflection and active listening. It validates the client's feelings and encourages them to explore their thoughts further without judgment. Telling the client what to do (A) gives unwanted advice. Asking "why" (C) can make the client feel defensive. Deferring to the doctor (D) ignores the client's emotional distress and fails to address their immediate need to talk.
</details>

---

### Question 80
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
The charge nurse is observing staff behavior on a busy medical-surgical unit. Which action by a staff member requires immediate intervention due to a risk of breaching client confidentiality?

A) A nursing assistant discusses a client's specific diagnosis in the crowded hospital elevator.
B) A nurse logs off the electronic health record before walking away from the nurses' station.
C) A nurse places a client's printed laboratory results in the secure unit shredding bin.
D) A nurse gives a bedside handoff report with the client's explicit permission.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Discussing client information in a public place, such as an elevator or cafeteria, is a direct violation of HIPAA and poses a significant risk to client confidentiality. The charge nurse must intervene immediately. Logging off the computer (B), shredding secure documents (C), and providing bedside report with the client's permission (D) are all safe, expected behaviors that protect client privacy.
</details>
### Question 81
A registered nurse (RN) is working on a medical-surgical unit with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the UAP?

A. Instructing the client on how to properly use a rolling walker.  
B. Assessing a postoperative client's incision site for signs of infection.  
C. Assisting a clinically stable client with ambulation down the hallway.  
D. Administering an oral stool softener to a client with constipation.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client with ambulation is a basic care task that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing incisions (Option B), providing initial teaching (Option A), and administering medications (Option D) require nursing judgment and a license, so they cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 82
A client is preparing for an elective surgery. The healthcare provider has just finished explaining the procedure, risks, and benefits. The provider leaves the room, and the nurse enters with the consent form. What is the nurse's primary role in the informed consent process?

A. Witnessing the client's signature on the consent form.  
B. Explaining the potential risks and complications of the surgery.  
C. Answering specific questions about the surgical techniques to be used.  
D. Persuading the client to sign the form quickly so the schedule is maintained.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
**Rationale:** The nurse's primary role in informed consent is to act as a witness to the client's signature, ensuring the client signed it voluntarily and appears competent. It is the responsibility of the healthcare provider performing the procedure to explain the risks, benefits, and surgical techniques (Options B and C). Persuading or coercing a client (Option D) is unethical and invalidates the consent.
</details>

---

### Question 83
The nurse is reviewing advance directives with a client upon admission. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of a durable power of attorney for healthcare (healthcare proxy)?

A. "This document lists exactly which medications I am allowed to take."  
B. "This allows me to name someone to make medical decisions for me if I cannot."  
C. "My doctor will automatically become my decision-maker if I am unconscious."  
D. "I am legally required to appoint my spouse as my healthcare proxy."  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Rationale:** A durable power of attorney for healthcare designates a surrogate decision-maker (proxy) to make medical choices on the client's behalf if they become incapacitated. It does not dictate specific medications (Option A). The doctor does not automatically become the proxy (Option C), and the client can choose anyone they trust, not just a spouse (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 84
A client recently diagnosed with a chronic illness says to the nurse, "I don't know how I am going to afford all these new medications and arrange for my home care." What is the most therapeutic response by the nurse?

A. "Don't worry, everything usually works out fine in the end."  
B. "You should ask your extended family for financial help."  
C. "Why are you so worried about home care right now?"  
D. "It sounds like you are feeling overwhelmed; let's talk about involving a social worker."  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
**Rationale:** Option D uses active listening and empathy to validate the client's feelings ("feeling overwhelmed") while offering a constructive management of care solution (involving a social worker or case manager). Option A offers false reassurance. Option B gives unsolicited advice. Option C uses "Why," which can make the client feel defensive.
</details>

---

### Question 85
The nurse has implemented a fall-prevention care plan for a newly admitted older adult client. Which observation best evaluates that the nursing interventions for safety are currently effective?

A. The client uses the call bell to ask for assistance before getting out of bed.  
B. The client attempts to walk to the bathroom independently in the dark.  
C. The client leaves their non-skid socks folded on the bedside table.  
D. The client lowers the bed rails themselves to reach items on the floor.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** EVALUATION
**Rationale:** Using the call bell to ask for help demonstrates that the client understands the safety plan and that the intervention is effective in preventing unassisted movement, which reduces fall risk. Walking in the dark (Option B), not wearing non-skid socks (Option C), and reaching for items on the floor (Option D) all indicate that the safety plan is not being followed and the client remains at high risk for falls.
</details>
### Question 86
A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a group of clients. Which task is appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assisting a client with a fractured radius with their lunch tray.
B) Evaluating a client's swallowing ability after a recent stroke.
C) Educating a client on a newly prescribed low-sodium diet.
D) Administering a scheduled oral vitamin supplement.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Delegation (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
**Section:** Management of Care

**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client with meals is a routine task that falls within the scope of practice for an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Evaluating swallowing (B) requires nursing assessment. Educating a client (C) requires nursing judgment and teaching skills. Administering medication (D) is outside the UAP's scope of practice and must be performed by a licensed nurse (LPN or RN).
</details>

---

### Question 87
A hospitalized client requests to read their own medical record. What is the most appropriate response by the nurse?

A) "You cannot view your record while you are currently admitted to the hospital."
B) "I will leave the computer logged in so you can read the notes on the screen."
C) "You have the right to view your record, and I will contact the appropriate department to arrange this."
D) "Only your primary healthcare provider can give you permission to read your chart."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Legal/Ethical (Informed consent/Confidentiality)
**Section:** Management of Care

**Rationale:** Under HIPAA regulations, clients have a legal right to view and request copies of their own medical records. The nurse should acknowledge this right and follow the facility's policy (such as contacting medical records) to facilitate the request. Leaving a computer logged in (B) violates security policies. Denying the request (A) or stating only the provider can grant permission (D) violates the client's legal rights.
</details>

---

### Question 88
A nurse is teaching a client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of a living will?

A) "Once I sign this document, I can never change my mind about my care."
B) "I must have a lawyer present at the hospital to sign this document legally."
C) "This document transfers all my financial assets to my healthcare proxy."
D) "This document guides the medical team on my treatment wishes if I cannot speak for myself."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Client Teaching (Do they understand the basics?)
**Section:** Management of Care

**Rationale:** A living will is a type of advance directive that outlines the client's specific wishes regarding medical treatments (such as life support or feeding tubes) if they become incapacitated and unable to communicate their choices. Advance directives can be changed at any time by the client (A). A lawyer is not required to complete an advance directive (B). Advance directives handle healthcare decisions, not financial assets (C).
</details>

---

### Question 89
A client who is scheduled for physical therapy refuses to go, stating, "I am just so tired of trying. Nothing seems to be getting better." Which response by the nurse demonstrates active listening and empathy?

A) "You have to go to therapy, or you will not be able to go home on time."
B) "It sounds like you are feeling exhausted and overwhelmed today."
C) "Why are you refusing to participate in your own recovery plan?"
D) "Everyone feels tired after this surgery; you just need to push through it."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Therapeutic Communication (Active listening/Empathy)
**Section:** Management of Care

**Rationale:** Stating "It sounds like you are feeling exhausted and overwhelmed today" is a therapeutic technique called reflection/validating. It acknowledges the client's feelings without judgment and encourages further conversation. Threatening the client (A), asking "why" questions which can make them defensive (C), or dismissing their feelings by minimizing them (D) are non-therapeutic communication blocks.
</details>

---

### Question 90
A client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit and brings a large amount of cash and expensive jewelry. The client’s family is not present to take the items home. What is the first physical step the nurse should take to secure the client's valuables?

A) Lock the items securely in the client's bedside table drawer.
B) Inventory the items in the presence of the client and have them sign the inventory form.
C) Send the items directly to the hospital security office with a UAP.
D) Place the items in a sealed envelope and leave them unattended at the nurses' station.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Procedural Step (What is the first physical step in a skill?)
**Section:** Management of Care

**Rationale:** When a client brings valuables to the hospital and family cannot take them home, the first procedural step in managing these items is to count and inventory them directly in front of the client. Both the nurse and the client must sign the inventory form to verify the contents before securing them in the hospital safe. Locking them in a bedside drawer (A) or leaving them at the nurses' station (D) risks theft. Sending them with a UAP without following chain-of-custody protocols (C) is inappropriate.
</details>
### Question 91
A client is scheduled for an elective surgical procedure. The surgeon has explained the risks, benefits, and alternatives to the client. What is the nurse's primary role regarding the informed consent document?

A) Explain the potential complications of the surgery.
B) Sign the consent form as a witness to the client's signature.
C) Determine if the procedure is medically necessary.
D) Cancel the surgery if the client feels nervous.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B (Sign the consent form as a witness to the client's signature.)

**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)

**Rationale:** The nurse's primary role in the informed consent process is to witness the client's signature, ensuring the client signed voluntarily and appears competent. Explaining risks and benefits (Option A) and determining medical necessity (Option C) are the responsibilities of the healthcare provider. Feeling nervous is normal and does not require canceling the surgery (Option D) unless the client specifically requests to withdraw their consent.
</details>

---

### Question 92
The registered nurse (RN) is planning morning care for a group of clients. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Administering a scheduled oral stool softener.
B) Assessing a client's heel for skin breakdown.
C) Emptying a client's urinary drainage bag.
D) Teaching a client how to use an incentive spirometer.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C (Emptying a client's urinary drainage bag.)

**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)

**Rationale:** Emptying a urinary drainage bag and recording the output is a standard, routine task that falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. Administering medications (Option A) requires a nursing license. Assessing skin (Option B) and providing client teaching (Option D) are nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

### Question 93
A client is informed that they will be discharged to a rehabilitation facility instead of going home. The client begins to cry and says, "I just want to go to my own house. I hate this." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "It sounds like you are feeling very upset about going to the rehabilitation facility."
B) "Don't worry, the facility has amazing nurses who will take great care of you."
C) "Your doctor believes this is the safest place for you right now."
D) "You will probably only be there for a week or two before you can go home."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A ("It sounds like you are feeling very upset about going to the rehabilitation facility.")

**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)

**Rationale:** Option A uses the therapeutic communication technique of reflection and validation, acknowledging the client's feelings and encouraging further dialogue. Option B provides false reassurance. Option C dismisses the client's feelings by deferring to the doctor's authority. Option D minimizes the client's current distress and makes a prediction the nurse cannot guarantee.
</details>

---

### Question 94
The nurse is teaching a client about advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "My advance directive cannot be changed once I sign it today."
B) "My family can override my medical decisions while I am awake and talking."
C) "My doctor is the only person who needs a copy of this document."
D) "This document tells the healthcare team my wishes if I cannot speak for myself."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D ("This document tells the healthcare team my wishes if I cannot speak for myself.")

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

**Rationale:** An advance directive is a legal document that outlines a client's preferences for medical care if they become unable to make decisions for themselves. Advance directives can be changed at any time by a competent client (Option A). Family members cannot override the decisions of an awake, alert, and competent client (Option B). Copies should be given to the hospital, primary provider, family, and healthcare proxy, not just the doctor (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 95
The nurse is receiving a telephone prescription from a healthcare provider for a client's new medication. What is the mandatory procedural step the nurse must take immediately after writing down the prescription?

A) Administer the medication to the client.
B) Read the prescription back to the provider.
C) Place the written prescription in the client's physical chart.
D) Ask the charge nurse to co-sign the prescription.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B (Read the prescription back to the provider.)

**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)

**Rationale:** The mandatory procedural step when taking a verbal or telephone prescription is to write it down and then "read back" the prescription to the provider to confirm accuracy. Administering the medication (Option A) or filing it in the chart (Option C) cannot happen until the order is verified via read-back. A charge nurse co-signature (Option D) is not a standard requirement for all telephone orders.
</details>
### Question 96
**Lens: DELEGATION**

A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a medical-surgical unit. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to delegate to an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the client's swallow reflex before providing a meal
B) Providing a complete bed bath to a stable client
C) Teaching the client how to use a standard walker
D) Administering a scheduled oral stool softener

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** 
Providing basic hygiene, such as a bed bath to a stable client, falls within the scope of practice for an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Evaluating a client's status (swallow reflex), providing initial client teaching (using a walker), and administering medications are strictly the responsibilities of the licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.

</details>

---

### Question 97
**Lens: LEGAL/ETHICAL**

A nurse receives a phone call from a person claiming to be a client's sister. The caller asks for an update on how the client's surgery went. What is the nurse's appropriate action?

A) Provide a brief, general update stating the client is resting and stable.
B) Ask the caller to provide the client's room number to verify identity.
C) Check the client's medical record for a signed release of information for the sister.
D) Transfer the caller directly to the surgeon for an update on the procedure.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** 
Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), nurses must protect client confidentiality. The nurse must verify that the client has officially authorized the release of health information to the specific individual (in this case, the sister) before sharing any medical details. Even general updates cannot be given without permission.

</details>

---

### Question 98
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING**

A nurse is providing education to a client about advance directives during admission. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I can designate my daughter to make medical decisions for me if I am unable to do so."
B) "Signing this document means I am giving up my right to receive pain medication."
C) "My advance directive can only be legally changed by a judge."
D) "The hospital will automatically make health decisions for me if I don't sign this."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** 
A durable power of attorney for healthcare is a type of advance directive that allows a client to designate a surrogate decision-maker (like a daughter) if they become incapacitated. Advance directives do not mean abandoning comfort measures or pain medication. They can be updated or revoked by the client at any time, and hospitals do not automatically make decisions for clients without one.

</details>

---

### Question 99
**Lens: THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION**

A client who is scheduled to be discharged to a long-term care facility begins to cry and says, "I am so afraid of going to a nursing home. What if they don't take good care of me?" Which response by the nurse is most therapeutic?

A) "Don't worry, the facility has excellent doctors and nurses."
B) "Why do you think they won't take good care of you there?"
C) "You will do just fine once you get unpacked and settled in."
D) "It sounds like you have some concerns about moving to the new facility."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** 
Option D uses the therapeutic communication techniques of reflecting and validating. It acknowledges the client's feelings without judgment and opens the door for further exploration of their fears. Options A and C provide false reassurance, which minimizes the client's feelings. Option B uses "Why," which can make the client feel defensive.

</details>

---

### Question 100
**Lens: EVALUATION**

The nurse arranged a physical therapy referral for a client recovering from a total hip arthroplasty to help with mobility. Which observation best indicates that the collaborative care goal is being met?

A) The client remains on strict bed rest to prevent postoperative falls.
B) The client safely demonstrates transferring from the bed to the chair with a walker.
C) The physical therapist documents the client's refusal to participate in the session.
D) The client routinely requests pain medication immediately prior to physical therapy.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** 
The primary goal of a physical therapy referral following a total hip arthroplasty is to safely improve mobility and restore function. Demonstrating a safe transfer using an assistive device (like a walker) directly shows that this collaborative goal is actively being achieved. Bed rest or refusing participation indicates the goal is not being met. While requesting pain medication before therapy is a good proactive strategy, it is an intervention rather than the outcome showing improved mobility.

</details>
### Question 101
A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a client who requires assistance with activities of daily living. Which task is appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Administering an over-the-counter pain medication for a headache.
B. Assessing the client's skin breakdown during the bath.
C. Assisting the client with a routine bed bath.
D. Showing the client how to use a new walker.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Delegation
Assisting a stable client with a bed bath is a routine, standard task that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Medication administration, physical assessment (like evaluating skin breakdown), and initial client teaching are all nursing responsibilities that cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 102
A client is scheduled for a cholecystectomy. The surgeon has explained the procedure and left the room. The nurse brings the consent form to the client for signature, but the client states, "I still don't understand what they are going to take out." What is the appropriate nursing action?

A. Notify the surgeon that the client needs further explanation.
B. Show the client an anatomy poster to explain the surgery.
C. Have the client sign the form since the provider already spoke with them.
D. Document in the chart that the client refused the procedure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Legal/Ethical
It is the healthcare provider's legal responsibility to explain the procedure, risks, benefits, and alternatives to obtain informed consent. The nurse's role is to witness the signature. If the client indicates they do not understand the procedure, the nurse must halt the consent process and notify the provider to return and clarify.
</details>

---

### Question 103
A client who is scheduled for discharge tomorrow tells the nurse, "I am really nervous about going home because I don't know how I will cook for myself." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "Don't worry, your family can help you cook."
B. "I will cancel your discharge until you feel ready."
C. "Why haven't you figured out a plan for this yet?"
D. "Tell me more about your concerns with managing meals at home."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Therapeutic Communication
"Tell me more..." is an open-ended communication technique that validates the client's feelings and encourages them to elaborate on their concerns. This information is crucial for the nurse to coordinate care, such as initiating a referral to a social worker or case manager for Meals on Wheels or home health assistance.
</details>

---

### Question 104
The nurse is reviewing the health history of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for requiring a referral to a speech-language pathologist (SLP)?

A. A client who has a new prescription for a soft diet due to loose dentition.
B. A client with a history of a stroke who begins coughing every time they drink water.
C. A client who recently had an abdominal surgery and is currently on a clear liquid diet.
D. A client who has a hoarse voice after shouting at a loud concert.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Risk Recognition
Coughing while drinking liquids is a classic sign of dysphagia (difficulty swallowing) and indicates a high risk for aspiration. This requires an immediate referral for an evaluation by a speech-language pathologist to ensure the client's airway remains safe during meals.
</details>

---

### Question 105
The nurse is providing education to a client about advance directives upon admission to the hospital. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "My family can easily override my advance directive if they disagree with my choices."
B. "I must hire a lawyer to write my advance directive so it is legal."
C. "This document allows me to state my wishes regarding life-sustaining treatments."
D. "I only need an advance directive if I am over the age of 65."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Client Teaching
An advance directive is a legal document that allows competent individuals to outline their end-of-life care preferences, including life-sustaining treatment wishes, in case they become unable to communicate. It does not require a lawyer, is appropriate for adults of all ages, and cannot simply be overridden by family members if it is legally executed.
</details>
### Question 106
**Lens: DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)**

A registered nurse (RN) is assigning tasks for the upcoming shift. Which of the following tasks is most appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing a newly admitted client's pressure ulcer.  
B) Administering a scheduled oral pain medication.  
C) Assisting a stable client with a morning bed bath.  
D) Providing discharge instructions regarding a new prescription.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client with activities of daily living (ADLs), such as a bed bath, falls within the scope of practice for unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Assessing wounds, administering medications, and providing client education are solely the responsibility of the licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.

</details>

---

### Question 107
**Lens: LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)**

A client is scheduled for a surgical procedure. The surgeon has just finished explaining the risks, benefits, and alternatives to the client. What is the nurse's primary role regarding the informed consent document?

A) Explaining the surgical risks a second time to ensure complete understanding.  
B) Witnessing the client's signature on the informed consent form.  
C) Suggesting alternative treatments if the client appears hesitant.  
D) Answering detailed questions about the specific surgical technique being used.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** The nurse's legal role in the informed consent process is to act as a witness. This means the nurse verifies the client’s identity, ensures the signature is voluntary, and assesses that the client appears competent to sign. It is the healthcare provider's legal responsibility to explain the procedure, risks, benefits, and alternatives, as well as answer specific medical questions about the surgery.

</details>

---

### Question 108
**Lens: THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)**

A client hospitalized for pneumonia states, "I am tired of being stuck in this hospital room. I want to go home right now." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "Tell me more about what is making you want to leave today."  
B) "You cannot leave until the healthcare provider legally discharges you."  
   C) "I will go get the Against Medical Advice (AMA) forms for you to sign."  
D) "Your insurance company will not pay for your stay if you leave early."  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Open-ended communication is the most therapeutic approach. It encourages the client to express their feelings and frustrations, allowing the nurse to explore and potentially resolve the underlying issue before the client makes a firm decision to leave Against Medical Advice (AMA). Threatening the client with insurance denials or stating they "cannot leave" is coercive and non-therapeutic.

</details>

---

### Question 109
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

A nurse is providing basic education to a newly admitted client regarding advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "My advance directive cannot be changed once it is signed and placed in my chart."  
B) "I must hire a lawyer to make my advance directive legally binding."  
C) "This document will only be used if I become unable to make my own medical decisions."  
D) "My immediate family can override my advance directive if they disagree with it."  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** An advance directive dictates a client's wishes for medical care or designates a healthcare proxy, and it only takes effect when the client loses the capacity to make their own decisions. Advance directives can be changed or revoked by the client at any time, do not require a lawyer to complete, and legally protect the client's wishes so that family members cannot arbitrarily override them.

</details>

---

### Question 110
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/breach?)**

The nurse is working at a busy hospital unit. Which action by a staff member represents the greatest risk for violating the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA)?

A) Shredding printed handover report sheets at the end of the shift.  
B) Logging off the electronic health record before walking away from the computer.  
C) Discussing a client's changing lab results with the primary healthcare provider.  
D) Posting an update about a client's rare condition on a "private" social media page.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Posting any information about a client's condition on social media—even if the page is private and the client's name is not explicitly mentioned—represents a severe risk for a HIPAA violation and a breach of confidentiality. Shredding documents, logging off computers, and discussing care with the provider are all correct, secure practices that protect client information.

</details>
### Question 111
The registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a client who requires assistance with morning routines. According to the principles of safe delegation, which task is appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's risk for skin breakdown over bony prominences.
B. Assisting the stable client with a partial bed bath and oral hygiene.
C. Teaching the client how to properly use an incentive spirometer.
D. Evaluating the client's response to an oral pain medication.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<em>Lens: DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)</em><br>
Assisting a stable client with activities of daily living (ADLs), such as a bed bath and oral hygiene, is a routine, standard task that falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing (Option A), teaching (Option C), and evaluating (Option D) are strictly nursing responsibilities that require the RN's clinical judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

### Question 112
A client is scheduled for an elective surgical procedure. The provider has explained the risks and benefits, and the nurse is preparing to have the client sign the informed consent form. The client states, "I'm not really sure what the doctor is going to do, but I trust him." What is the appropriate legal and ethical action by the nurse?

A. Document that the client trusts the provider and allow them to sign the consent form.
B. Explain the surgical procedure in detail so the client feels comfortable signing.
C. Notify the provider that the client requires further explanation before signing.
D. Ask a family member to explain the procedure to the client in simple terms.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<em>Lens: LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent)</em><br>
Informed consent requires the client to fully understand the procedure, its risks, benefits, and alternatives. The nurse's role in informed consent is to witness the signature and verify the client understands. If the client expresses confusion or a lack of understanding, it is the nurse's legal and ethical duty to notify the provider, as it is the provider's responsibility to explain the procedure (Option C). The nurse should not explain the procedure (Option B) or delegate this to a family member (Option D).
</details>

### Question 113
A client who was recently diagnosed with a terminal illness tells the nurse, "I don't know how I am going to tell my family about this. They are going to be absolutely devastated." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "It sounds like you are feeling overwhelmed about sharing this difficult news."
B. "Don't worry, your family loves you and they will support you no matter what."
C. "You should wait until you feel a little stronger before you try to tell them."
D. "I can call your family and break the news to them if you want me to."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<em>Lens: THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)</em><br>
Option A uses the therapeutic communication technique of reflection and empathy. It validates the client's feelings and encourages further exploration of their concerns without minimizing their emotions. Option B offers false reassurance. Option C gives unwarranted advice. Option D takes away the client's autonomy and crosses professional boundaries.
</details>

---

### Question 114
The nurse is providing education to a client regarding advance directives. Which statement by the client indicates an accurate understanding of the teaching?

A. "Only people over the age of 65 need to have this document in their chart."
B. "My doctor will make all of my medical decisions once this paper is signed."
C. "I must have a lawyer present in the room to legally complete an advance directive."
D. "I have the right to change my mind and revoke my advance directive at any time."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<em>Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)</em><br>
Clients have the legal right to alter, update, or completely revoke their advance directives at any time, provided they are competent to do so. Advance directives are recommended for adults of all ages, not just those over 65 (Option A). The document directs care based on the client's wishes, not the doctor's choices (Option B), and while it requires witnessing or notarization depending on state law, a lawyer is not strictly required to be present to complete one (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 115
The nurse is reviewing the charts of several clients on a medical-surgical unit to initiate safety protocols. Which client factor most strongly indicates a high risk for falls requiring standard fall precautions?

A. The client requires a pureed diet for a mild swallowing impairment.
B. The client is receiving continuous intravenous normal saline hydration.
C. The client has a documented history of two recent falls at home.
D. The client uses a bilateral hearing aid during the daytime.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<em>Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)</em><br>
A history of recent falls is one of the strongest predictive risk factors for future falls. Standard fall risk assessment tools (such as the Morse Fall Scale) give significant weight to a history of falling. While IV lines (Option B) can be a minor tethering hazard, a history of actual falls (Option C) places the client at a definitively high risk requiring targeted fall precautions. Options A and D do not inherently represent a high fall risk.
</details>
### Question 116
A registered nurse (RN) is assigning tasks for the shift. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Performing an admission assessment on a newly admitted client.
B. Administering an oral stool softener to a post-surgical client.
C. Assisting a client with left-sided weakness to transfer from the bed to a chair.
D. Evaluating a client's understanding of their new discharge instructions.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
**Category:** Management of Care

**Reasoning:** UAPs can safely perform routine, standard, unchanging tasks such as assisting with activities of daily living (ADLs), feeding, bathing, and safe transfers. Performing assessments (Option A), administering medications (Option B), and evaluating teaching or providing education (Option D) require the clinical judgment and licensure of a registered nurse and cannot be safely delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 117
A client is scheduled for a routine colonoscopy. The physician explains the risks and benefits and leaves the room. The nurse brings the consent form for the client to sign. The client states, "I don't really understand what the doctor is actually going to do." What is the appropriate action by the nurse?

A. Explain the procedure in simple terms and have the client sign the form.
B. Ask the physician to return and explain the procedure to the client again.
C. Tell the client that they can refuse the procedure if they are unsure.
D. Have a designated family member sign the consent form on the client's behalf.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)
**Category:** Management of Care

**Reasoning:** Informed consent requires the healthcare provider performing the procedure to explain it, discuss risks and alternatives, and ensure the client understands. The nurse's role is to witness the signature and verify that the client understands. If the client expresses confusion or a lack of understanding, the nurse must act as an advocate by notifying the provider to return and clarify the information. The nurse should not explain the procedure themselves in lieu of the physician. 
</details>

### Question 118
A client recently diagnosed with terminal cancer tells the nurse, "I'm not sure if I want to sign a Do Not Resuscitate (DNR) order. It feels like I'm just giving up." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "It sounds like you are struggling with what this decision means for you."
B. "Most people with your diagnosis eventually sign a DNR order."
C. "Why do you feel like signing a DNR means you are giving up?"
D. "You are not giving up; you are just being realistic about your illness."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)
**Category:** Management of Care

**Reasoning:** Reflecting the client's feelings ("It sounds like you are struggling...") is a highly therapeutic technique. It validates the client's emotions, demonstrates active listening, and provides a safe space for the client to explore their thoughts further without judgment. Option B minimizes the client's individual experience. Option C uses "why," which can make the client feel interrogated or defensive. Option D gives an opinion and tells the client how to feel.
</details>

---

### Question 119
The nurse is providing teaching to a newly admitted client about their rights regarding medical records under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA). Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding?

A. "My employer can call and get a copy of my records without my permission."
B. "I have the right to request a physical or electronic copy of my own medical records."
C. "The hospital is legally forbidden to share my records with my health insurance company."
D. "Only the physician can decide who is allowed to see my health information."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Category:** Management of Care

**Reasoning:** Under HIPAA, clients have the specific right to view and request copies of their own medical records. Employers cannot access records without explicit authorization (Option A). Hospitals are permitted to share necessary information with insurance companies for billing and coverage purposes (Option C). The client, not just the physician, has the right to control and authorize who can access their protected health information (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 120
The nurse receives a client transferred from the post-anesthesia care unit (PACU) to the surgical floor following an appendectomy. The handoff report states the client is currently stable. Which finding during the nurse's initial evaluation confirms the client's stability?

A. The client reports a pain level of 9 on a 0-to-10 scale.
B. The client's surgical dressing is saturated with bright red blood.
C. The client is difficult to arouse and breathing 8 times per minute.
D. The client is awake, alert, and oriented to person, place, and time.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** EVALUATION (Is the patient stable after care?)
**Category:** Management of Care

**Reasoning:** Being awake, alert, and oriented is an expected, stable finding for a post-surgical client transferred from the PACU to the surgical floor. Severe pain (Option A), active bleeding/saturated dressing (Option B), and respiratory depression with lethargy (Option C) are all unstable, unexpected findings that require immediate medical intervention. 
</details>
### Question 121
A registered nurse (RN) is working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) on a medical-surgical unit. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the UAP?

A) Assisting a stable client with a morning bed bath
B) Evaluating the effectiveness of a client's pain medication
C) Feeding a client who was just admitted with new-onset dysphagia
D) Teaching a client how to correctly use an incentive spirometer

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
**Rationale:** The RN can delegate routine, standard tasks for stable clients to the UAP, such as assisting with a bed bath, taking routine vital signs, and ambulating. Evaluating pain, feeding a client with new swallowing difficulties (high risk of aspiration), and client teaching are strictly within the RN scope of practice and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 122
A client is scheduled for an elective surgery. The surgeon has explained the procedure, risks, and benefits to the client. The nurse brings the informed consent form for the client to sign. What is the nurse's primary role in this process?

A) Explaining the specific surgical risks to the client
B) Providing a detailed list of medical alternatives
C) Deciding if the client truly needs the surgery
D) Witnessing the client's voluntary signature on the form

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)
**Rationale:** In the informed consent process, the healthcare provider performing the procedure is responsible for explaining the risks, benefits, and alternatives. The nurse's primary role is to witness the signature, ensuring the client is signing voluntarily and appears competent to do so.
</details>

---

### Question 123
The nurse is preparing to transfer a client's medical record to an outpatient long-term care facility. To comply with the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), what is an essential first step the nurse must take?

A) Send a copy of the chart with the client's family member
B) Verify that the client has signed a release of information form
C) Fax the entire medical chart without using a cover sheet
D) Call the receiving facility to discuss the client's financial status

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)
**Rationale:** Under HIPAA, releasing medical information to external facilities or individuals generally requires a signed release of information from the client or their legal representative. Sending charts with family members or faxing without a cover sheet violates information security policies.
</details>

---

### Question 124
A client tells the nurse, "I really don't want to go to a nursing home, but my children are making me do it." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic to advocate for the client's rights?

A) "Your children just want what is best for you in the long run."
B) "Nursing homes are actually very nice places to live these days."
C) "It sounds like you are feeling pressured about this decision."
D) "You have to do what your family thinks is safe for your health."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy)
**Rationale:** Stating "It sounds like you are feeling pressured about this decision" uses the therapeutic technique of reflection and validation. It encourages the client to express their feelings and allows the nurse to advocate for the client's autonomy. The other options are dismissive, offer false reassurance, or take the family's side over the client's expressed wishes.
</details>

---

### Question 125
The nurse is teaching a client about advance directives upon admission to the hospital. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "This document tells the healthcare team my wishes if I cannot speak for myself."
B) "I must have a lawyer present in the room to fill out this paperwork."
C) "My living will designates who will pay my hospital bills after I am discharged."
D) "Once I sign an advance directive, I can never change my mind about it."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Rationale:** Advance directives (such as a living will or durable power of attorney for healthcare) provide instructions regarding the client's medical care preferences if they become incapacitated and unable to communicate. A lawyer is not required to complete them, they do not deal with financial billing, and the client can change or revoke them at any time as long as they are competent.
</details>
### Question 126
The nurse is assigning tasks to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) for a client who had a stroke 3 days ago. Which task is appropriate to delegate to the UAP?

A. Performing the initial swallowing assessment before breakfast.
B. Assisting the client with ambulation to the bathroom using a gait belt.
C. Teaching the client how to use the new bedside commode.
D. Modifying the client's plan of care based on their morning vital signs.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<strong>Lens:</strong> DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
<strong>Rationale:</strong> UAPs can safely assist with routine, standard tasks such as ambulation using assistive devices for stable clients. Assessments (A), patient education (C), and modifying care plans (D) require the nursing judgment of a registered nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 127
A client is scheduled for an elective cholecystectomy. The nurse brings the informed consent form to the client's room. The client states, "I know I am having my gallbladder removed, but I am not sure what the risks are." What is the appropriate nursing action?

A. Explain the specific surgical risks and expected benefits to the client.
B. Have the client sign the form since they already know the name of the procedure.
C. Notify the surgeon that the client needs further explanation regarding the risks.
D. Document in the medical record that the client formally refused the surgery.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<strong>Lens:</strong> LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)
<strong>Rationale:</strong> It is the legal responsibility of the healthcare provider performing the procedure to explain the risks, benefits, and alternatives.
