An aPTT of 65 seconds falls perfectly within this expected therapeutic range, indicating the medication is working effectively without causing an excessive risk of bleeding.
</details>
### Question 206
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

A nurse is reviewing the medication administration records for a group of clients receiving opioid analgesics for pain management. Which client should the nurse identify as being at the highest risk for opioid-induced respiratory depression?

A. A 25-year-old client recovering from a fractured tibia 
B. A 45-year-old client receiving treatment for acute appendicitis 
C. A 78-year-old client with a history of obstructive sleep apnea 
D. A 30-year-old client who had a laparoscopic cholecystectomy 

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Strategy</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong>

<strong>Rationale:</strong> Clients at the highest risk for opioid-induced respiratory depression include the elderly, those with underlying respiratory conditions (such as obstructive sleep apnea, COPD, or asthma), and those who are opiate-naive. The 78-year-old client has two major risk factors: advanced age and obstructive sleep apnea. The other clients do not possess these specific heightened risk factors based on the information provided.

<strong>Nursing Strategy:</strong> Always assess baseline respiratory status before giving opioids. Recognize that age, obesity, and sleep apnea significantly magnify the respiratory-depressant effects of opioids.
</details>

---

### Question 207
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)

A client with atrial fibrillation is prescribed daily oral warfarin. During a medication history interview, the nurse reviews the client's home medications. Which over-the-counter medication should the nurse identify as contraindicated while the client is taking warfarin?

A. Ibuprofen 
B. Calcium carbonate 
C. Acetaminophen 
D. Diphenhydramine 

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Strategy</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong>

<strong>Rationale:</strong> Warfarin is an anticoagulant that thins the blood. Ibuprofen is a nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID) that inhibits platelet aggregation and can irritate the gastrointestinal lining. Taking NSAIDs concurrently with warfarin significantly increases the client's risk for severe bleeding. Acetaminophen (in appropriate doses), calcium carbonate, and diphenhydramine do not carry this same high risk of bleeding when combined with warfarin.

<strong>Nursing Strategy:</strong> Teach clients on anticoagulants to avoid all over-the-counter NSAIDs (like aspirin, ibuprofen, and naproxen) unless specifically directed by their healthcare provider. 
</details>

---

### Question 208
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)

A client who was recently prescribed oral ferrous sulfate for iron deficiency anemia reports having dark, black-colored stools for the past two days. What is the nurse's best interpretation of this finding?

A. An indication of gastrointestinal bleeding requiring immediate intervention 
B. A symptom of iron toxicity that requires administration of an antidote 
C. A sign of a severe allergic reaction to the supplement 
D. An expected, harmless side effect of the medication 

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Strategy</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong>

<strong>Rationale:</strong> Oral iron supplements, such as ferrous sulfate, commonly cause the stool to turn dark green or black. This is a normal, expected, and harmless side effect of unabsorbed iron passing through the gastrointestinal tract. It does not indicate active gastrointestinal bleeding, toxicity, or an allergic reaction. 

<strong>Nursing Strategy:</strong> Anticipatory guidance is key. Always inform clients starting oral iron therapy about the expected change in stool color so they do not become alarmed.
</details>

---

### Question 209
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

A nurse provides discharge teaching to a client newly prescribed a fluticasone metered-dose inhaler for daily management of asthma. Which client statement indicates that the teaching was successful?

A. "I will use this inhaler immediately if I start wheezing." 
B. "I should rinse my mouth with water and spit after using this inhaler." 
C. "I will stop taking this medication as soon as my daily cough goes away." 
D. "I need to take this inhaler on an empty stomach to improve its effect." 

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Strategy</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong>

<strong>Rationale:</strong> Fluticasone is an inhaled corticosteroid used for the long-term control of asthma. Because steroids can suppress local immunity in the mouth, clients are at risk for oral candidiasis (thrush). Rinsing the mouth with water and spitting after use removes the residual medication and prevents this complication. Fluticasone is a maintenance medication, not a rescue inhaler for acute wheezing. It should not be stopped simply because symptoms improve, and taking it on an empty stomach is irrelevant to inhaled medications.

<strong>Nursing Strategy:</strong> Remember the phrase "Rinse and spit after steroids" to prevent oral thrush. Differentiate clearly between maintenance inhalers (steroids) and rescue inhalers (albuterol).
</details>

---

### Question 210
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)

A nurse is preparing to administer a scheduled dose of intravenous potassium chloride to a client. The nurse checks the client's most recent basic metabolic panel and notes a serum potassium level of 4.0 mEq/L (4.0 mmol/L). What is the appropriate nursing action based on this laboratory result?

A. Hold the medication and notify the healthcare provider. 
B. Administer the potassium chloride as prescribed. 
C. Request an order for a STAT electrocardiogram (ECG). 
D. Administer sodium polystyrene sulfonate instead. 

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Strategy</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong>

<strong>Rationale:</strong> The normal range for serum potassium is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 4.0 mEq/L is perfectly within normal limits. Therefore, it is safe and appropriate to administer the prescribed maintenance dose of potassium chloride. Holding the medication, getting an ECG, or giving sodium polystyrene sulfonate (which lowers potassium) would be incorrect and inappropriate actions for a normal lab value.

<strong>Nursing Strategy:</strong> Always verify basic lab values before administering electrolytes. Memorize the normal potassium range (3.5 - 5.0 mEq/L). If the value is normal, proceed with the prescribed maintenance therapy unless there are other contraindications.
</details>
### Question 211
A nurse is reviewing the medical records of several clients receiving intravenous (IV) fluid therapy. Which client is at the highest risk for developing an IV infiltration?

A) A 30-year-old receiving continuous 0.9% sodium chloride at 50 mL/hr
B) A 75-year-old with fragile veins receiving continuous IV fluids 
C) A 45-year-old receiving an intermittent IV antibiotic via a central line
D) A 25-year-old receiving a continuous epidural infusion

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition<br>
Older adult clients often have fragile, thin, and less elastic veins, making them highly susceptible to vein rupture and IV infiltration. A young adult receiving normal saline (Option A) has a lower baseline risk. A central line (Option C) is deep in a large vein and is less likely to infiltrate into surrounding tissue compared to peripheral lines. An epidural (Option D) is not an IV therapy and presents different risks.
</details>

---

### Question 212
A client is receiving intravenous morphine sulfate via a patient-controlled analgesia (PCA) pump. The nurse assesses the client and notes a respiratory rate of 14 breaths per minute and mild drowsiness, though the client is easily arousable. What is the nurse's best action?

A) Document the findings as expected side effects of the medication.
B) Administer the prescribed reversal agent, naloxone, immediately.
C) Stop the PCA pump and notify the healthcare provider.
D) Apply a continuous pulse oximeter and prepare for intubation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected VS Unexpected<br>
A respiratory rate of 14 breaths per minute is within the normal range (12-20 breaths per minute). Mild drowsiness that is easily arousable is an expected and acceptable side effect of opioid analgesics like morphine. Options B, C, and D are emergency interventions that are completely unnecessary for a stable client exhibiting normal, expected effects of the medication.
</details>

### Question 213
The nurse provides teaching to a client prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin for stable angina. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should swallow the pill with a full glass of water."
B) "I will stop taking my daily aspirin while using this medication."
C) "I must keep the tablets in their original, dark glass bottle."
D) "I can chew the tablet if I want it to work faster."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
Nitroglycerin tablets are highly sensitive to light, heat, and moisture. They must be kept in their original, dark glass container to maintain their potency. The medication should be placed under the tongue to dissolve, not swallowed (Option A) or chewed (Option D). There is no contraindication requiring the client to stop their daily aspirin (Option B); in fact, aspirin is standard therapy for coronary artery disease.
</details>

---

### Question 214
A nurse is caring for a client receiving total parenteral nutrition (TPN) via a central venous catheter. Which task can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Verifying the TPN solution ingredients against the provider's order.
B) Assessing the central line insertion site for signs of infection.
C) Adjusting the infusion rate on the electronic pump if it beeps.
D) Measuring and recording the client's daily weight.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
Obtaining and recording daily weights is a routine, non-invasive task that is well within the scope of practice for a UAP. Daily weights are important for clients on TPN to monitor fluid status. Verifying medications (Option A), assessing the insertion site (Option B), and adjusting IV pumps (Option C) require nursing judgment, assessment, and licensure, and therefore cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 215
The nurse is preparing to administer an intramuscular (IM) injection of a vaccine to an adult client. After washing hands, donning gloves, and selecting the deltoid site, what is the next physical step the nurse should take?

A) Inject the medication slowly and steadily into the muscle.
B) Cleanse the injection site with an alcohol swab using a circular motion.
C) Massage the site vigorously to promote medication absorption.
D) Discard the needle directly into the biohazard trash bag.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
Once hand hygiene is performed, gloves are on, and the proper site is identified, the next physical step in the procedure is to cleanse the skin with an antimicrobial swab (such as alcohol) using a circular motion from the center outward to prevent infection. Injecting (Option A) happens after cleansing. Massaging the site (Option C) is contraindicated for many vaccines and IM injections as it can cause tissue damage or alter absorption. Needles should be discarded in a sharps container, not a standard biohazard bag (Option D).
</details>
### Question 216
After verifying the client's identity and donning gloves, the nurse prepares to administer an intravenous (IV) push medication through an existing, compatible continuous IV infusion line. What is the nurse's first physical step in administering the medication?

A. Pinch the IV tubing above the injection port to prevent backflow.
B. Attach the medication syringe securely to the injection port.
C. Flush the IV line with 3 mL of sterile normal saline.
D. Scrub the injection port closest to the client with an alcohol swab.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The first physical step when accessing an existing IV line is to scrub the hub (injection port) with an antimicrobial swab (such as alcohol or chlorhexidine) using friction. This prevents the introduction of microorganisms into the client's bloodstream. After scrubbing, the nurse would attach the flush, pinch the tubing, flush the line, and then administer the medication.
</details>

---

### Question 217
A nurse is teaching a client about how to use a newly prescribed transdermal nitroglycerin patch. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the basic teaching?

A. "I will apply the patch to a hairless area of skin on my chest or upper arm."
B. "I should cut the patch in half if my blood pressure drops too low."
C. "I will leave the same patch on for 3 days before changing it to a new one."
D. "I should apply the new patch over the exact same spot as the old one."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Transdermal patches should be applied to clean, dry, hairless areas of intact skin (often the chest, back, or upper arm) to ensure proper medication absorption. Patches should never be cut (unless specifically directed by the manufacturer, which is rare for nitroglycerin), sites should be rotated daily to prevent skin irritation, and patches are typically removed after 12-14 hours to prevent tolerance, not left on for 3 days.
</details>

---

### Question 218
The nurse is reviewing the medication administration record for a client who has a history of a severe, active gastrointestinal (GI) bleed. Which prescribed over-the-counter medication should the nurse identify as contraindicated for this client?

A. Omeprazole
B. Ibuprofen
C. Acetaminophen
D. Calcium carbonate

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Contraindication<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Ibuprofen is a nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID). NSAIDs inhibit prostaglandins that protect the stomach lining and impair platelet aggregation, which significantly increases the risk of bleeding. Therefore, it is strictly contraindicated in a client with an active gastrointestinal bleed. Omeprazole (a proton pump inhibitor) and Calcium carbonate (an antacid) are often used to treat GI issues, and Acetaminophen does not carry the same bleeding risks as NSAIDs.
</details>

---

### Question 219
A client receiving an oral ferrous sulfate supplement for iron deficiency anemia reports having dark green, almost black stools. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A. It indicates active upper gastrointestinal bleeding caused by the iron.
B. It is a sign of medication toxicity requiring an immediate antidote.
C. It is an expected, harmless side effect of the medication.
D. It is a severe allergic reaction to the medication formulation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected VS Unexpected<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Oral iron supplements, such as ferrous sulfate, commonly cause stools to turn dark green or black. This is an expected and harmless side effect of the unabsorbed iron being excreted from the body. It does not indicate GI bleeding, toxicity, or an allergic reaction.
</details>

---

### Question 220
A client is receiving a continuous intravenous (IV) infusion of normal saline. Which task related to the client's IV therapy is safe for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Pausing the IV pump while the client goes to the bathroom.
B. Checking the IV insertion site for signs of redness or swelling.
C. Notifying the nurse if the client reports pain at the IV site.
D. Adjusting the IV flow rate if the pump repeatedly alarms.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The UAP can be delegated the task of reporting client complaints (such as pain or discomfort at the IV site) or alerting the nurse to pump alarms. Assessing the IV site, adjusting the flow rate, and interacting with the IV pump controls (pausing/starting) require nursing judgment and are outside the UAP's scope of practice.
</details>
### Question 221
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
The nurse is reviewing the medical records of clients receiving continuous intravenous (IV) fluid therapy. Which client characteristic places them at the highest risk for developing an IV infiltration?

A) History of advanced age and fragile peripheral veins
B) Placement of the IV catheter in the forearm
C) Use of a transparent, semi-permeable dressing over the insertion site
D) Receiving a continuous infusion of normal saline (0.9% NaCl)

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Older adults with advanced age often have fragile, thin, and less elastic peripheral veins. This increases their susceptibility to vein rupture and subsequent IV infiltration (fluid leaking into the surrounding tissue). The other options represent normal, safe aspects of IV therapy.
</details>

---

### Question 222
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION
A client is prescribed an over-the-counter medication for a mild tension headache. The nurse reviews the client's medical history and notes a current diagnosis of an active, bleeding gastric ulcer. Which medication is contraindicated for this client?

A) Acetaminophen
B) Calcium carbonate
C) Ibuprofen
D) Diphenhydramine

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Ibuprofen is a nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID). NSAIDs inhibit prostaglandins that protect the stomach lining and can increase the risk of bleeding. Therefore, ibuprofen is strongly contraindicated in a client with an active, bleeding gastric ulcer. Acetaminophen is generally safe for pain relief in clients with gastric ulcers, provided there is no liver disease.
</details>

---

### Question 223
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
A client is taking a prescribed oral iron supplement (ferrous sulfate) for iron-deficiency anemia. During a clinic visit, the client reports having dark, blackish stools for the past week. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) An indication of active, lower gastrointestinal bleeding
B) An expected and harmless side effect of the medication
C) A sign of acute iron toxicity requiring immediate intervention
D) An allergic reaction to the enteric coating of the supplement

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Oral iron supplements commonly cause stools to turn dark green or black. This is an expected, harmless side effect of unabsorbed iron passing through the gastrointestinal tract and does not require intervention. The nurse should reassure the client.
</details>

---

### Question 224
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
The nurse is teaching a client how to apply a newly prescribed transdermal nitroglycerin patch. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will leave the old patch on when I apply the new one."
B) "I should massage the patch firmly into my skin to help it absorb."
C) "I will place the patch on the exact same spot every single day."
D) "I need to choose a clean, hairless area of skin for the patch."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Transdermal patches should be applied to clean, dry, hairless, and intact skin to ensure proper and consistent absorption of the medication. The client should rotate sites (not use the same spot every day), remove the old patch before applying a new one, and avoid rubbing or massaging the patch, as this can alter absorption rates.
</details>

---

### Question 225
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
A client is receiving a continuous intravenous (IV) heparin infusion for the treatment of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The nurse reviews the client's daily laboratory results. Which laboratory value is primarily used to monitor the effectiveness of this specific medication?

A) Prothrombin time (PT)
B) Blood urea nitrogen (BUN)
C) Activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT)
D) White blood cell (WBC) count

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT) is the standard laboratory test used to monitor the therapeutic effect of continuous IV heparin therapy. Prothrombin time (PT) and International Normalized Ratio (INR) are used to monitor warfarin therapy. BUN evaluates kidney function, and WBC evaluates for infection.
</details>
### Question 226
The nurse is reviewing the laboratory results of a client prescribed daily oral digoxin for heart failure. Which finding places this client at the highest risk for developing digoxin toxicity? (Lens 1: Risk Recognition)

A. A sodium level of 138 mEq/L
B. A potassium level of 3.1 mEq/L
C. A fasting blood glucose of 95 mg/dL
D. A calcium level of 9.5 mg/dL

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Hypokalemia (low potassium) significantly increases the risk for digoxin toxicity, even if the digoxin level is within the normal therapeutic range. Normal potassium levels are typically 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L, making a level of 3.1 mEq/L a critical risk factor. The other laboratory values listed are within normal limits and do not directly increase the risk for digoxin toxicity.
</details>

---

### Question 227
A client with erectile dysfunction is prescribed sildenafil. During the medication review, the nurse identifies which current medication as an absolute contraindication to taking sildenafil? (Lens 2: Contraindication)

A. Isosorbide mononitrate
B. Metoprolol tartrate
C. Atorvastatin
D. Omeprazole

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Isosorbide mononitrate is a nitrate medication. The concurrent use of phosphodiesterase-5 (PDE5) inhibitors, such as sildenafil, with any form of nitrates is strictly contraindicated because it can cause a profound, life-threatening drop in blood pressure. The other medications (a beta-blocker, a statin, and a proton pump inhibitor) are not absolutely contraindicated with sildenafil.
</details>

---

### Question 228
A client who started taking an oral ferrous sulfate (iron) supplement three days ago calls the clinic to report dark, blackish-green bowel movements. How should the nurse interpret this finding? (Lens 3: Expected vs Unexpected)

A. It suggests an active upper gastrointestinal bleed.
B. It indicates an allergic reaction to the supplement.
C. It is a sign of iron toxicity requiring immediate intervention.
D. It is a common and expected side effect of oral iron therapy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Black or dark green stools are a harmless and expected side effect of oral iron supplementation. While black, tarry stools can sometimes indicate a gastrointestinal bleed, in the context of a client recently starting iron therapy without other symptoms of bleeding, it is an expected pharmacological finding. The nurse should reassure the client that this is normal.
</details>

---

### Question 229
The nurse is teaching a client how to properly use an albuterol metered-dose inhaler (MDI). Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the basic technique? (Lens 4: Client Teaching)

A. "I should inhale quickly and forcefully when pressing the canister."
B. "I should take a rapid, shallow breath while pressing down on the inhaler."
C. "I need to hold my breath for about 10 seconds after inhaling the medicine."
D. "I will wait 15 seconds before taking a second puff if another is ordered."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** After inhaling the medication from an MDI, the client should hold their breath for about 10 seconds (or as long as comfortable) to allow the medication to deposit deeply into the airways. The client should inhale slowly and deeply (not quickly or shallowly) and should wait at least 1 full minute between puffs of the same medication.
</details>

---

### Question 230
A client is receiving daily oral warfarin to prevent blood clots due to a history of atrial fibrillation. The nurse reviews the client's morning laboratory results and notes an International Normalized Ratio (INR) of 2.5. What does this result indicate regarding the client's pharmacological therapy? (Lens 5: Data Interpretation)

A. The warfarin dose is subtherapeutic and the provider should be notified to increase it.
B. The client's blood is clotting too quickly, placing them at an immediate risk for a stroke.
C. The client is experiencing warfarin toxicity and requires administration of vitamin K.
D. The warfarin level is within the expected therapeutic range for this condition.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** The therapeutic INR range for a client taking warfarin for atrial fibrillation is generally between 2.0 and 3.0. An INR of 2.5 indicates that the medication is effective and working exactly as intended. It is not subtherapeutic, nor does it indicate toxicity or an abnormally high risk for clotting.
</details>
### Question 231
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)**

A nurse is caring for a client receiving a continuous peripheral IV infusion of 0.9% sodium chloride. Which finding indicates the client is at risk for an IV-related tissue injury?

A) The IV transparent dressing is intact and completely dry.  
B) The client's heart rate is 78 beats per minute.  
C) The client reports pain and swelling right at the insertion site.  
D) The IV fluid bag is currently half empty.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Pain, swelling, and coolness or redness at the insertion site are classic signs of infiltration or phlebitis. This indicates the fluid is leaking into the surrounding tissue, placing the client at risk for injury, and requires the IV to be stopped and removed. Intact dressings, normal heart rates, and a half-empty bag are normal, safe findings.
</details>

---

### Question 232
**Lens: CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)**

A healthcare provider prescribes oral amoxicillin for a client with a bacterial respiratory infection. Which information in the client's medical history makes this medication unsafe to administer?

A) A severe anaphylactic reaction to cephalexin.  
B) A mild stomach ache after taking erythromycin.  
C) A family history of seasonal asthma.  
D) A documented allergy to latex products.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Amoxicillin is a penicillin antibiotic. There is a known cross-sensitivity between penicillins and cephalosporins (like cephalexin). A history of a severe anaphylactic reaction to a cephalosporin is a strict contraindication for giving a penicillin due to the high risk of a life-threatening allergic reaction. A mild stomach ache from a macrolide, family history of asthma, and latex allergies do not contraindicate amoxicillin.
</details>

---

### Question 233
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)**

A client is prescribed an oral ferrous sulfate supplement for the treatment of iron-deficiency anemia. Which finding reported by the client is a normal, expected effect of this medication?

A) Yellowing of the sclera  
B) Persistent dry cough  
C) Ringing in the ears  
D) Dark green or black stools  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Oral iron supplements commonly cause gastrointestinal side effects, including turning the stools a harmless dark green or black color. This is an expected, stable finding and does not require stopping the medication. Yellowing of the sclera (jaundice), persistent cough, and ringing in the ears (tinnitus) are not normal side effects of iron supplements.
</details>

---

### Question 234
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

A nurse is teaching a client how to properly use and store sublingual nitroglycerin for angina. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will swallow the pill with a full glass of water."  
B) "I will keep the medication in its original dark glass bottle."  
C) "I can chew the tablet if my chest pain is very severe."  
D) "I should stop taking the medication if I get a headache."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is highly sensitive to light, heat, and moisture. It must be kept in its original dark glass container to prevent it from losing its potency. The medication should be placed under the tongue to dissolve, not swallowed or chewed. A headache is a very common, expected side effect due to vasodilation and is not a reason to avoid taking the medication during an angina attack.
</details>

---

### Question 235
**Lens: DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)**

A client is receiving a daily dose of oral digoxin for heart failure. The nurse reviews the client's morning laboratory results and notes a serum potassium level of 2.8 mEq/L (2.8 mmol/L). What does this basic laboratory value mean for this client?

A) The client is properly absorbing the digoxin.  
B) The client is experiencing acute kidney failure.  
C) The client is at high risk for digoxin toxicity.  
D) The client has a completely normal potassium level.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The normal serum potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.8 mEq/L indicates hypokalemia (low potassium). Low potassium levels dangerously increase a client's sensitivity to digoxin, placing them at a high risk for life-threatening digoxin toxicity and cardiac dysrhythmias.
</details>
### Question 236
The nurse is caring for a client receiving digoxin for heart failure. Which of the following client factors places them at the highest risk for developing digoxin toxicity?

A) Eating a diet high in sodium
B) A serum potassium level of 3.1 mEq/L
C) A resting heart rate of 78 beats per minute
D) A serum digoxin level of 0.8 ng/mL

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) A serum potassium level of 3.1 mEq/L

**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

**Reasoning:** Hypokalemia (a low potassium level) greatly increases the risk of a client developing digoxin toxicity, even if the digoxin dose is within the normal range. A normal potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L, making 3.1 mEq/L abnormally low. The other findings are either normal (heart rate of 78, normal digoxin level of 0.5–2.0 ng/mL) or unrelated to the direct precipitation of digoxin toxicity (high sodium diet).
</details>

---

### Question 237
A client with a history of actively bleeding gastric ulcers is prescribed a medication for joint pain. Which of the following medications is contraindicated for this client?

A) Ibuprofen
B) Acetaminophen
C) Pantoprazole
D) Diphenhydramine

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Ibuprofen

**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)

**Reasoning:** Ibuprofen is a nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID). NSAIDs can irritate the gastric mucosa, decrease protective prostaglandins in the stomach, and inhibit platelet aggregation, which significantly increases the risk of gastrointestinal bleeding. It is strictly contraindicated in clients with active gastric ulcers. Acetaminophen is an analgesic that is generally safe for the stomach and does not increase bleeding risk.
</details>

---

### Question 238
The nurse is assessing a client who started taking oral ferrous sulfate for iron deficiency anemia three days ago. Which of the following findings is an expected effect of this medication?

A) Yellowing of the sclera
B) Ringing in the ears
C) Dark, tarry-looking stools
D) A persistent, dry cough

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Dark, tarry-looking stools

**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)

**Reasoning:** Oral iron supplements (such as ferrous sulfate) commonly cause a client's stools to turn dark green or black and tarry. This is an expected, harmless side effect of the medication and indicates that the iron is passing through the gastrointestinal tract. Yellowing of the sclera (jaundice), ringing in the ears (tinnitus), and a dry cough are not expected side effects of iron therapy. 
</details>

---

### Question 239
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly prescribed levothyroxine for hypothyroidism. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will stop taking the medication once my fatigue goes away."
B) "I should take this medication right before I go to bed."
C) "I can take this with my calcium supplement at breakfast."
D) "I will take this pill every morning on an empty stomach."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) "I will take this pill every morning on an empty stomach."

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

**Reasoning:** Levothyroxine should be taken in the morning on an empty stomach, at least 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast, to maximize its absorption. Taking it with food or certain supplements (like calcium or iron) severely decreases how much of the drug the body absorbs. Levothyroxine is a lifelong medication used to replace missing thyroid hormone, so it should not be stopped simply because symptoms improve.
</details>

---

### Question 240
A client is receiving a continuous intravenous (IV) infusion of heparin for a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Which laboratory value should the nurse monitor to evaluate the therapeutic effectiveness of this medication?

A) Prothrombin time (PT)
B) Activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT)
C) Platelet count
D) International Normalized Ratio (INR)

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT)

**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)

**Reasoning:** The activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT) is the specific laboratory test used to monitor the effectiveness of continuous IV heparin therapy. The nurse adjusts the heparin drip based on the aPTT results to ensure the client stays in a therapeutic range. PT and INR are used to monitor warfarin (Coumadin) therapy, not heparin. While platelet counts are monitored for a complication called heparin-induced thrombocytopenia (HIT), aPTT is the test that directly measures the drug's therapeutic anticoagulation effect.
</details>
### Question 241
A nurse is teaching a client about newly prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin for the management of angina. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the basic medication instructions?

A) "I should keep the pills in the refrigerator to keep them fresh."
B) "I will keep the medication in its original dark glass bottle."
C) "I can take up to five pills within 15 minutes if my chest hurts."
D) "I should swallow the pill with a full glass of water."

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "I will keep the medication in its original dark glass bottle."

**Rationale:** Sublingual nitroglycerin is highly sensitive to light, heat, and moisture, which can cause the medication to lose its potency. It must be stored in its original dark glass container at room temperature. It should not be swallowed (Option D), only a maximum of 3 doses can be taken 5 minutes apart (Option C), and it should not be refrigerated (Option A).

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
</details>

---

### Question 242
The nurse is preparing to administer a mixture of NPH and Regular insulin from multiple-dose vials to a client with diabetes. What is the very first physical step the nurse should take when preparing this injection?

A) Withdraw the Regular insulin into the syringe.
B) Withdraw the NPH insulin into the syringe.
C) Inject air into the NPH insulin vial.
D) Inject air into the Regular insulin vial.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Inject air into the NPH insulin vial.

**Rationale:** When mixing insulins, the correct sequence ensures that the clear, fast-acting insulin is not contaminated by the cloudy, longer-acting insulin. The sequence is: inject air into the cloudy (NPH) vial, inject air into the clear (Regular) vial, withdraw the clear (Regular) insulin, and finally withdraw the cloudy (NPH) insulin. 

**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)
</details>

---

### Question 243
A client receiving daily oral digoxin for heart failure has a routine serum digoxin level drawn. The laboratory reports a level of 1.2 ng/mL. How should the nurse interpret this basic laboratory finding?

A) The level is dangerously high, indicating severe toxicity.
B) The level is subtherapeutic, requiring an increased dose.
C) The level is within the expected therapeutic range.
D) The level is critically low, and the medication should be held.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The level is within the expected therapeutic range.

**Rationale:** The therapeutic range for serum digoxin is generally considered to be 0.8 to 2.0 ng/mL. A level of 1.2 ng/mL is normal and therapeutic, meaning the nurse would continue to administer the medication as prescribed. 

**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)
</details>

---

### Question 244
A parent asks the clinic nurse if it is okay to give over-the-counter chewable aspirin to a 6-year-old child who has a high fever from a viral illness. Which response by the nurse correctly identifies a contraindication?

A) "It is unsafe to give aspirin to a child with a viral illness due to the risk of Reye's syndrome."
B) "Aspirin is safe as long as you give half of the normal adult dose."
C) "It is safe to give aspirin, but only if the fever is above 102°F (38.9°C)."
D) "You can give aspirin, but it must be crushed and mixed with applesauce to prevent stomach upset."

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "It is unsafe to give aspirin to a child with a viral illness due to the risk of Reye's syndrome."

**Rationale:** Aspirin and salicylate-containing products are strongly contraindicated in children and adolescents who are experiencing viral infections (such as influenza or chickenpox). Use of aspirin in this population is linked to Reye's syndrome, a rare but life-threatening condition that causes swelling in the liver and brain. Acetaminophen or ibuprofen should be recommended instead.

**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)
</details>

---

### Question 245
A nurse administers a prescribed dose of intravenous furosemide to a client admitted with heart failure and fluid overload. Which finding best indicates to the nurse that the medication has been effective?

A) The client reports feeling less anxious about their hospitalization.
B) The client's heart rate increases from 80 to 95 beats per minute.
C) The client's blood pressure increases slightly during the shift.
D) The client has a urine output of 600 mL over the next two hours.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) The client has a urine output of 600 mL over the next two hours.

**Rationale:** Furosemide is a loop diuretic used to rapidly pull excess fluid from the body by increasing urine production. A significant increase in urine output (diuresis) directly demonstrates that the medication has been effective in treating the client's fluid overload.

**Lens:** EVALUATION (Is the patient stable/improving after care?)
</details>

# SECTION:Physiological Adaptation 

### Question 246
A nurse is assessing a client's newly created colostomy stoma 24 hours after surgery. Which finding should the nurse identify as an expected outcome at this time?

A) The stoma appears dark purple and dry.
B) The stoma appears beefy red and moist.
C) The stoma is severely retracted into the abdomen. 
D) The stoma is actively bleeding profusely.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The stoma appears beefy red and moist.

**Rationale:** 
- **A is incorrect:** A dark purple, black, or dry stoma is an unexpected and highly concerning finding, indicating ischemia or necrosis of the bowel tissue.
- **B is correct:** A healthy, viable stoma should appear beefy red (or pink) and moist, indicating adequate blood supply and perfusion. This is a normal and expected finding.
- **C is incorrect:** A stoma that is severely retracted into the abdomen is an unexpected complication that can lead to leakage of stool under the skin barrier, causing severe skin breakdown.
- **D is incorrect:** While a small amount of oozing can be normal immediately after surgery, active or profuse bleeding is an unexpected finding that requires immediate medical evaluation.

**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**Concept:** Physiological Adaptation (Illness Management)
</details>

---

### Question 247
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who just had a permanent pacemaker inserted. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I can lift heavy boxes as soon as I get home."
B) "I should avoid standing near a running microwave oven."
C) "I will check my pulse every day to make sure it is not too slow."
D) "I need to take my pulse only when I feel dizzy."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "I will check my pulse every day to make sure it is not too slow."

**Rationale:** 
- **A is incorrect:** Clients should avoid lifting heavy objects or raising the arm on the side of the pacemaker above the shoulder for several weeks to prevent dislodging the pacemaker leads.
- **B is incorrect:** Modern pacemakers are shielded from standard household appliances, including microwave ovens. It is a common myth that they must be avoided.
- **C is correct:** The client should be taught to check their pulse daily at the same time to ensure the pacemaker is firing correctly and that their heart rate has not dropped below the set pacemaker rate.
- **D is incorrect:** The pulse should be checked daily as a routine baseline measure, not just when symptoms like dizziness occur. 

**Lens:** Client Teaching
**Concept:** Physiological Adaptation (Medical Emergencies/Device Management)
</details>

---

### Question 248
A nurse is reviewing the arterial blood gas (ABG) results of a client with a history of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The results show a pH of 7.31 and a PaCO2 of 52 mm Hg. How should the nurse interpret these laboratory findings?

A) Respiratory acidosis
B) Respiratory alkalosis
C) Metabolic acidosis
D) Metabolic alkalosis

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Respiratory acidosis

**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** The normal pH range is 7.35-7.45. A pH of 7.31 indicates acidosis. The normal PaCO2 range is 35-45 mm Hg. A PaCO2 of 52 mm Hg indicates retained carbon dioxide (an acid). Because the primary problem is elevated CO2 matching the acidic pH, this represents respiratory acidosis, which is common in clients with COPD due to air trapping and hypoventilation.
- **B is incorrect:** Respiratory alkalosis would present with a pH greater than 7.45 and a PaCO2 less than 35 mm Hg.
- **C is incorrect:** Metabolic acidosis would present with a low pH but a normal or low PaCO2 (due to respiratory compensation), with a primary deficit in HCO3.
- **D is incorrect:** Metabolic alkalosis would present with an elevated pH (greater than 7.45).

**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**Concept:** Physiological Adaptation (Fluid and Electrolyte Imbalances)
</details>

---

### Question 249
A client with a history of epilepsy is admitted to the medical-surgical unit. The nurse is planning care to ensure the client's safety. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's airway patency during a seizure.
B) Teaching the client about potential seizure triggers.
C) Evaluating the client's postictal level of consciousness.
D) Padding the client's bed rails to prevent physical injury.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Padding the client's bed rails to prevent physical injury.

**Rationale:** 
- **A is incorrect:** Assessing airway patency requires nursing judgment and is outside the scope of practice for a UAP.
- **B is incorrect:** Providing client education is a nursing responsibility that cannot be delegated to a UAP.
- **C is incorrect:** Evaluating a client's neurological status and level of consciousness requires clinical assessment skills and must be performed by the registered nurse.
- **D is correct:** Padding the bed rails is a standard safety measure and a physical task that does not require nursing judgment, making it safe and appropriate to delegate to a UAP.

**Lens:** Delegation
**Concept:** Physiological Adaptation (Illness Management)
</details>

---

### Question 250
A nurse is preparing to administer an intermittent bolus enteral feeding through a client's nasogastric (NG) tube. What physical step should the nurse take first?

A) Flush the NG tube with 30 mL of tap water.
B) Elevate the head of the client's bed to at least 30 degrees.
C) Administer the prescribed formula through the syringe.
D) Clamp the tube to prevent air from entering the stomach.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Elevate the head of the client's bed to at least 30 degrees.

**Rationale:** 
- **A is incorrect:** While flushing the tube is a necessary step, it should only be done *after* positioning the client properly and verifying tube placement.
- **B is correct:** The absolute first physical step to prevent the severe complication of aspiration is to elevate the head of the bed to at least 30 to 45 degrees (Semi-Fowler's position) before any fluids or food are introduced into the NG tube. 
- **C is incorrect:** Administering the formula is the main event of the procedure, which must be preceded by positioning, checking placement, assessing residual, and flushing.
- **D is incorrect:** Clamping the tube is done during the procedure to prevent air entry when syringes are detached, but it is not the first physical step taken before starting the feeding process.

**Lens:** Procedural Step
**Concept:** Physiological Adaptation (Basic Care and Comfort/Alterations in Body Systems)
</details>
### Question 251
A nurse is reviewing the electronic health records of clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the greatest risk for developing a fluid volume deficit?

A) A client with heart failure who receives a daily beta-blocker.  
B) A client with a new ileostomy who has high liquid output.  
C) A client with chronic kidney disease who is currently oliguric.  
D) A client with hypothyroidism who frequently reports constipation.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> An ileostomy bypasses the large intestine, where most water absorption normally occurs. A new ileostomy with high liquid output places the client at a high risk for dehydration (fluid volume deficit) and electrolyte imbalances. The clients with heart failure and oliguric kidney disease are more at risk for fluid volume overload. Constipation does not cause fluid volume deficit.
</details>

---

### Question 252
A nurse is caring for a client diagnosed with a right lower extremity deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Which action by the nurse is contraindicated in the care of this client?

A) Applying warm, moist compresses to the affected leg as prescribed.  
B) Elevating the affected leg when the client is resting in bed.  
C) Massaging the right calf to relieve muscle cramping and pain.  
D) Administering the prescribed subcutaneous low-molecular-weight heparin.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Massaging the affected extremity of a client with a deep vein thrombosis is strictly contraindicated because the mechanical pressure can dislodge the thrombus, leading to a life-threatening pulmonary embolism. Elevation, warm compresses, and anticoagulant therapy are standard, safe interventions for a DVT.
</details>

---

### Question 253
A nurse is assessing a client who is 2 days post-operative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The client has a Jackson-Pratt (JP) drain in place. Which finding requires immediate reporting to the healthcare provider?

A) The drain bulb is compressed and functioning on gentle suction.  
B) The drainage is serosanguineous in color.  
C) The drain output was 20 mL over the last 8 hours.  
D) The drainage suddenly changes from pale pink to bright red.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A sudden change in drainage from pale pink (serosanguineous) to bright red (sanguineous) two days after surgery is an unexpected finding that indicates new, active bleeding. The provider must be notified immediately. Serosanguineous drainage, a compressed bulb, and low output (20 mL/8 hrs) are expected, normal findings at this stage of recovery.
</details>

---

### Question 254
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with type 1 diabetes mellitus. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of how to treat mild hypoglycemia?

A) "I will drink 4 ounces of regular orange juice if I feel shaky and sweaty."  
B) "I should eat a large chocolate candy bar with nuts to raise my blood sugar quickly."  
C) "I will inject my fast-acting insulin as soon as I feel dizzy."  
D) "I need to lie down and rest for an hour before checking my blood sugar."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Mild hypoglycemia should be treated with 15 grams of fast-acting, simple carbohydrates, such as 4 ounces of regular (non-diet) juice or soda. A candy bar with nuts contains fat, which delays gastric emptying and slows the absorption of sugar. Injecting insulin would fatally lower blood sugar further. The client should treat the symptoms immediately, not just rest.
</details>

---

### Question 255
The nurse is reviewing morning laboratory results for a client receiving continuous nasogastric (NG) tube suctioning. The client's serum potassium level is 2.8 mEq/L (2.8 mmol/L). The nurse recognizes this basic lab value indicates which condition?

A) Normal potassium level  
B) Severe hypernatremia  
C) Hypokalemia  
D) Hyperkalemia  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The normal range for serum potassium is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A value of 2.8 mEq/L is critically low, indicating hypokalemia. Clients receiving continuous NG suctioning are at high risk for losing potassium-rich gastric secretions, leading to this imbalance.
</details>
### Question 256
The nurse is reviewing the medical records of four assigned clients. Which client is at the highest risk for developing hypokalemia?

A) A client diagnosed with Addison's disease.
B) A client receiving continuous nasogastric (NG) tube suctioning.
C) A client with end-stage chronic kidney disease.
D) A client receiving a daily potassium-sparing diuretic.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)
<br><br>
<strong>Reasoning:</strong> Continuous nasogastric (NG) suctioning removes gastric secretions, which are rich in potassium, placing the client at a high risk for hypokalemia (low serum potassium). Clients with Addison's disease, chronic kidney disease, or those taking potassium-sparing diuretics are at risk for *hyperkalemia* (elevated serum potassium) due to impaired potassium excretion or retention mechanisms.
</details>

---

### Question 257
A client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with right lower quadrant abdominal pain, nausea, and suspected appendicitis. Which intervention is contraindicated for this client?

A) Keeping the client NPO (nothing by mouth).
B) Administering a prescribed intravenous antiemetic.
C) Applying a heating pad to the abdomen for comfort.
D) Obtaining a prescribed complete blood count (CBC).

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)
<br><br>
<strong>Reasoning:</strong> Applying heat to the abdomen is strictly contraindicated for a client with suspected appendicitis. Heat increases blood flow and inflammation to the area, which can cause the appendix to rupture, leading to life-threatening peritonitis. Keeping the client NPO, administering antiemetics, and drawing blood for a CBC are all safe and expected interventions for this client.
</details>

### Question 258
The nurse is assessing a client who had a total right knee replacement 24 hours ago. Which clinical finding is considered an expected, normal finding at this time?

A) Serosanguineous drainage in the surgical suction drain.
B) An oral temperature of 102.5°F (39.2°C).
C) Sudden, sharp chest pain when taking a deep breath.
D) Complete absence of pedal pulses in the right foot.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)
<br><br>
<strong>Reasoning:</strong> Serosanguineous drainage (pink, watery mixture of blood and serum) is a normal, expected finding in a surgical drain 24 hours postoperatively. An oral temperature of 102.5°F is unexpected and indicates a potential infection. Sudden, sharp chest pain is unexpected and could indicate a pulmonary embolism. The absence of pedal pulses is a sign of severe vascular compromise and is a medical emergency.
</details>

---

### Question 259
The nurse is educating a client newly diagnosed with type 1 diabetes about managing hypoglycemia. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "If I feel shaky and sweaty, I will inject more regular insulin."
B) "I should eat a high-fat chocolate bar if my blood sugar is low."
C) "I will drink 4 ounces of regular fruit juice if I experience a low blood sugar."
D) "I should skip my next scheduled meal if I have an episode of low blood sugar."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
<br><br>
<strong>Reasoning:</strong> Drinking 4 ounces of regular fruit juice provides 15 grams of fast-acting simple carbohydrates, which is the correct initial treatment for hypoglycemia (the "15-15 rule"). Injecting more insulin would dangerously lower the blood sugar further. High-fat foods like chocolate should be avoided for acute hypoglycemia because fat slows down the absorption of sugar. Skipping meals is unsafe and can trigger further hypoglycemic episodes.
</details>

---

### Question 260
The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client experiencing profound fatigue, pallor, and shortness of breath upon exertion. The client's hemoglobin level is 7.5 g/dL (75 g/L). Based on this data, what condition is the client experiencing?

A) Systemic infection
B) Severe dehydration
C) Polycythemia
D) Anemia

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)
<br><br>
<strong>Reasoning:</strong> A normal hemoglobin level is typically 12-16 g/dL for females and 14-18 g/dL for males. A hemoglobin level of 7.5 g/dL is significantly low, indicating anemia, which perfectly explains the client's symptoms of fatigue, pallor, and shortness of breath due to decreased oxygen-carrying capacity. Infection is indicated by an elevated white blood cell count. Dehydration and polycythemia would both cause *elevated* hemoglobin and hematocrit levels.
</details>
### Question 261
A nurse is reviewing the charts of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Based on the principles of physiological adaptation, which client is at the greatest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A 45-year-old client with type 2 diabetes who walks in the hall frequently.
B) A 30-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy yesterday.
C) A 75-year-old client on strict bed rest following a severe ischemic stroke.
D) A 22-year-old client admitted for an acute asthma exacerbation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Risk Recognition
**Rationale:** Deep vein thrombosis (DVT) risk is significantly increased by prolonged immobility, advanced age, and vascular injury. The 75-year-old client on strict bed rest has multiple high-risk factors for DVT due to venous stasis from immobility. The other clients are either younger, mobile, or do not have the same degree of prolonged immobility.
</details>

---

### Question 262
A client is 24 hours postoperative following a total knee arthroplasty. The nurse observes 40 mL of dark red drainage in the Jackson-Pratt (JP) drain over the last 8 hours. What is the most appropriate action by the nurse?

A) Document the finding as an expected outcome.
B) Clamp the drain immediately to prevent further blood loss.
C) Notify the healthcare provider of an active hemorrhage.
D) Irrigate the surgical drain with sterile normal saline.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**Rationale:** Having 40 mL of dark red drainage over an 8-hour period (about 5 mL/hr) 24 hours after a major orthopedic surgery is a normal and expected finding. Dark red blood indicates old bleeding, not an active hemorrhage. The nurse should simply empty the drain, record the output, and document the finding. Clamping, irrigating, or notifying the provider is unnecessary.
</details>

---

### Question 263
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client with heart failure who has a prescribed daily fluid restriction. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I do not need to count beverages containing caffeine in my fluid total."
B) "I will stop taking my daily diuretic medication if I begin to feel thirsty."
C) "I can drink an extra liter of water if I spend time outside in the hot sun."
D) "I will count items that melt at room temperature, like gelatin and ice cream, as fluids."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**Rationale:** When calculating daily fluid intake for a restriction, clients must include any food items that are liquid at room temperature, such as gelatin, ice cream, sherbet, and ice chips. Caffeine must still be counted, diuretics should never be stopped without consulting a provider, and clients with heart failure must strictly adhere to their fluid limits regardless of weather unless instructed otherwise by their provider.
</details>

---

### Question 264
A nurse is caring for a client with end-stage renal disease who just returned to the unit from a hemodialysis treatment. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's arteriovenous (AV) fistula for a bruit and thrill.
B) Obtaining the client's current weight and vital signs.
C) Evaluating the client for signs of post-dialysis disequilibrium syndrome.
D) Teaching the client about post-dialysis dietary fluid restrictions.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Delegation
**Rationale:** The scope of practice for a UAP includes gathering standard, non-invasive data such as obtaining a client's weight and vital signs. Assessing an AV fistula, evaluating for complications (like disequilibrium syndrome), and providing client education all require the clinical judgment and specialized knowledge of a registered nurse (RN) and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 265
A nurse is preparing to administer an intermittent enteral bolus feeding to a client via a nasogastric (NG) tube. After verifying the provider's prescription and identifying the client, what is the first physical step the nurse should take?

A) Flush the NG tube with 30 mL of sterile water.
B) Aspirate the gastric contents to check the residual volume.
C) Elevate the head of the client's bed to at least 30 to 45 degrees.
D) Warm the enteral formula in the microwave for 30 seconds.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**Rationale:** The absolute first physical step before interacting with the NG tube is to elevate the head of the bed to at least 30 to 45 degrees (preferably higher during a bolus feed). This utilizes gravity to prevent aspiration, which is the most critical safety concern. After the client is safely positioned, the nurse will check placement and residual (aspiration), then flush. Formula should never be warmed in a microwave.
</details>
### Question 266
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
The nurse is reviewing a list of clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A 35-year-old client admitted for asthma exacerbation who is ambulating in the hall.
B) A 72-year-old client who is 2 days post-operative from a hip arthroplasty and on strict bedrest.
C) A 50-year-old client with a history of hypertension whose blood pressure is 130/80 mmHg.
D) A 28-year-old client admitted for acute gastroenteritis who is receiving IV fluids.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Deep vein thrombosis (DVT) risk increases with Virchow's triad: venous stasis, endothelial damage, and hypercoagulability. A 72-year-old client who recently underwent major orthopedic surgery (hip arthroplasty) and is immobile on strict bedrest has significant risk factors for developing a DVT. The other clients are either mobile or lack primary DVT risk factors.
</details>

---

### Question 267
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION
A client with a suspected brain tumor is scheduled for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan. Which information from the client's health history is a strict contraindication for this procedure?

A) The client has a history of claustrophobia.
B) The client is allergic to iodine-based contrast dye.
C) The client has a permanent cardiac pacemaker.
D) The client has a titanium joint replacement.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Permanent cardiac pacemakers contain electronic components and metal that can be damaged, deactivated, or displaced by the powerful magnetic field of an MRI machine, making it a strict contraindication. Claustrophobia is a precaution that can be managed with medication. Iodine-based dye is used in CT scans, not MRIs (which use gadolinium). Titanium is a non-ferromagnetic metal and is generally safe for MRIs.
</details>

---

### Question 268
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
The nurse is assessing a client with a newly created arteriovenous (AV) fistula in the left arm for hemodialysis. Which assessment finding is expected and indicates a patent fistula?

A) A palpable thrill over the fistula site.
B) Dependent edema in the left hand.
C) Absence of a bruit upon auscultation.
D) Coolness and pallor in the fingers of the left arm.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** A palpable thrill (a vibrating sensation) and an auscultated bruit (a swishing sound) are expected, normal findings over an AV fistula. They indicate turbulent blood flow, meaning the fistula is patent and functioning properly. Absence of a bruit, edema, coolness, or pallor are unexpected findings that indicate potential complications, such as poor circulation or occlusion.
</details>

---

### Question 269
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD). Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should drink a glass of milk right before I go to bed."
B) "I will eat three large meals a day instead of small snacks."
C) "I can lie down on the couch immediately after eating dinner."
D) "I will wait at least two hours after eating before going to sleep."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Clients with GERD should remain upright for at least 2 to 3 hours after eating to prevent stomach contents from refluxing into the esophagus. Eating large meals, lying down immediately after eating, and drinking milk right before bed can all increase gastric pressure and exacerbate reflux symptoms. 
</details>

---

### Question 270
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
A nurse is reviewing the laboratory results for a client with type 2 diabetes mellitus. The client's Hemoglobin A1c (HbA1c) level is 6.5%. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) The client has had severe hypoglycemia over the past week.
B) The client has demonstrated good long-term glycemic control.
C) The client's blood sugar is dangerously high and requires immediate IV insulin.
D) The client's blood glucose level at the exact time of the blood draw was low.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Hemoglobin A1c measures the average blood glucose control over the past 2 to 3 months, not just the exact moment the blood is drawn. For a client diagnosed with diabetes, an HbA1c of less than 7% generally indicates good long-term glycemic control and adaptation to their treatment plan. 
</details>
### Question 271
The nurse is reviewing the charts of four assigned clients. Which client is at the highest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A. A 20-year-old recovering from a minor ankle sprain
B. A 35-year-old athlete who runs marathons
C. A 70-year-old who is on strict bed rest after a hip replacement
D. A 50-year-old with a recent upper respiratory infection

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)
**Reasoning:** Deep vein thrombosis (DVT) risk is heavily influenced by Virchow's triad: venous stasis, endothelial injury, and hypercoagulability. Advanced age, major orthopedic surgery (like a hip replacement), and strict immobility (bed rest) significantly increase venous stasis and the risk of clot formation. The other clients do not have the same degree of immobility or surgical trauma. 
</details>

---

### Question 272
A client is admitted to the emergency department with suspected appendicitis and complains of severe right lower quadrant abdominal pain. Which intervention is contraindicated and unsafe for this client?

A. Keeping the client NPO (nothing by mouth)
B. Placing a warm heating pad directly on the abdomen
C. Administering prescribed intravenous (IV) fluids
D. Assisting the client into a side-lying position with knees flexed

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)
**Reasoning:** Applying heat to the abdomen of a client with suspected appendicitis is strictly contraindicated. Heat increases blood flow and inflammation to the area, which can cause the inflamed appendix to rupture, leading to life-threatening peritonitis. Keeping the client NPO, giving IV fluids, and positioning for comfort are all safe and expected interventions.
</details>

### Question 273
The nurse is assessing a client who had an open cholecystectomy 12 hours ago. Which assessment finding is considered normal and expected at this time?

A. Bright red blood completely saturating the abdominal dressing
B. A sudden, sharp drop in blood pressure to 78/45 mmHg
C. An oral temperature of 102.8°F (39.3°C)
D. A small amount of serosanguineous drainage on the surgical dressing

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)
**Reasoning:** A small amount of serosanguineous (pinkish/watery blood) drainage is a normal and expected finding on a surgical dressing 12 hours postoperatively. Bright red blood saturating the dressing indicates active hemorrhage, a drop in blood pressure suggests shock, and a high fever suggests severe infection; these are all unexpected and require immediate intervention.
</details>

### Question 274
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client with newly diagnosed heart failure. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of how to monitor their fluid volume status at home?

A. "I should weigh myself every morning after I urinate and before breakfast."
B. "I only need to weigh myself if my shoes start to feel tight."
C. "I will drink at least a gallon of water every day to keep my kidneys flushed."
D. "I should weigh myself at night right before I go to sleep."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Reasoning:** The most accurate way for a client with heart failure to monitor fluid retention is to obtain daily weights at the same time every day, using the same scale, and wearing the same amount of clothing. The best time is in the morning after voiding and before eating. Waiting until swelling occurs is too late, and increasing fluid intake to a gallon is contraindicated as it worsens fluid overload. 
</details>

---

### Question 275
The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving daily furosemide. The client's serum potassium level is 2.8 mEq/L. How should the nurse interpret this data?

A. The client is experiencing hyperkalemia.
B. The client is experiencing hypokalemia.
C. The client's potassium level is within the expected normal range.
D. The client is experiencing hyponatremia.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)
**Reasoning:** The normal serum potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.8 mEq/L is critically low, indicating hypokalemia. Furosemide is a loop diuretic that causes potassium loss through the urine, commonly leading to this electrolyte imbalance. Hyponatremia refers to low sodium, not potassium. 
</details>
### Question 276
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION 
**Scenario:** A nurse is reviewing the medical records of four assigned clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A 25-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy who is ambulating in the halls.
B) A 65-year-old client on strict bed rest following a major hip arthroplasty.
C) A 40-year-old client admitted for an asthma exacerbation who is using an incentive spirometer.
D) A 30-year-old client with viral gastroenteritis receiving continuous intravenous (IV) fluids.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** 
Deep vein thrombosis (DVT) is a serious complication often caused by venous stasis, endothelial injury, and hypercoagulability (Virchow's triad). A 65-year-old client on strict bed rest after major orthopedic surgery (hip arthroplasty) has multiple major risk factors: advanced age, immobility (venous stasis), and recent major surgery (tissue trauma and hypercoagulability). The other clients are either younger, actively ambulating, or do not possess high-risk factors for DVT.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** 
Look for the client with the most risk factors for clot formation. Bed rest + major orthopedic surgery = extremely high risk for DVT.
</details>

---

### Question 277
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION 
**Scenario:** A nurse is caring for a client with poorly controlled diabetes mellitus and severe peripheral neuropathy. The client states their feet are very cold and asks the nurse to provide a heating pad. Which action by the nurse is correct?

A) Provide the heating pad and set it to the lowest possible temperature.
B) Place the heating pad over a thick blanket rather than directly on the client's skin.
C) Explain that a heating pad is unsafe due to decreased sensation and provide warm socks instead.
D) Allow the client to use the heating pad for a maximum of 15 minutes while monitoring.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** 
Applying a heating pad (or any external heat source) to the extremities of a client with severe peripheral neuropathy is contraindicated. Due to nerve damage, the client cannot properly feel the heat and is at a high risk for sustaining severe burns before feeling pain. The safest nursing intervention is to decline the use of the heating pad, explain the safety risks, and provide an alternative such as warm socks or a blanket. 

**Test-Taking Strategy:** 
Identify the safety hazard. Neuropathy = loss of sensation. Loss of sensation + heat source = high risk for burns. Therefore, heat application is contraindicated.
</details>

---

### Question 278
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED 
**Scenario:** A nurse is performing an assessment on a client who had a permanent colostomy created 24 hours ago. Which finding indicates a normal, expected physiological adaptation to the surgery?

A) A beefy red, moist, and slightly edematous stoma.
B) A pale pink, dry stoma with no active output.
C) A dark blue stoma that is retracted below the skin line.
D) A stoma that produces a continuous stream of bright red blood.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** 
In the immediate postoperative period (first 24-48 hours), a healthy, viable stoma should appear beefy red, moist, and slightly edematous (swollen) due to the surgical trauma. A pale, dark blue, or purple stoma indicates impaired perfusion or ischemia and is a medical emergency. Continuous bright red bleeding is abnormal and requires immediate reporting. 

**Test-Taking Strategy:** 
Think about what tissue looks like when it is highly vascularized and freshly surgically manipulated. "Beefy red and moist" is the classic textbook description of a healthy new stoma.
</details>

---

### Question 279
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING 
**Scenario:** A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who just had a permanent pacemaker implanted. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the home care instructions?

A) "I can safely undergo a routine MRI scan if my shoulder hurts."
B) "I will raise the arm on my pacemaker side above my head to stretch the incision."
C) "I should stop taking my prescribed blood pressure medication now."
D) "I will check my heart rate daily and report if it drops below the pacemaker's set rate."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** 
A client with a new pacemaker must know how to check their pulse daily. If the heart rate drops below the rate set by the pacemaker, it indicates the device may be malfunctioning, and the healthcare provider should be notified immediately. Clients should avoid MRIs (unless the device is specifically MRI-compatible and cleared by cardiology), avoid lifting the arm on the operative side above the shoulder until healed (to prevent dislodging the leads), and continue all prescribed medications.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** 
Look for the statement that ensures the client can monitor the primary function of the device. The pacemaker's job is to keep the heart rate from dropping too low; therefore, checking the pulse is a vital self-care skill.
</details>

---

### Question 280
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION 
**Scenario:** A client is receiving a continuous intravenous infusion of regular insulin for the treatment of diabetic ketoacidosis (DKA). The nurse reviews the client's morning basic metabolic panel and notes a serum potassium level of 3.0 mEq/L. What does this laboratory value indicate?

A) The level is dangerously high, increasing the risk of cardiac arrest.
B) The level is below normal, indicating hypokalemia that requires replacement.
C) The level is perfectly normal, meaning the insulin infusion is balanced.
D) The level indicates hemoconcentration and will correct itself with hydration alone.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** 
The normal serum potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 3.0 mEq/L indicates hypokalemia. During the treatment of DKA, continuous insulin pushes potassium back into the cells, frequently leading to a sudden drop in serum potassium levels. The nurse must recognize this low level, as the client will likely need potassium replacement to prevent cardiac dysrhythmias.

**Test-Taking Strategy:**
