Memorize normal lab values. Potassium is 3.5 - 5.0 mEq/L. A value of 3.0 is low (hypokalemia).
</details>
### Question 281
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)**

The nurse is reviewing the medical records of assigned clients. Which client is at the greatest risk for developing hypokalemia?

A) A client with end-stage renal disease who misses dialysis.
B) A client receiving prescribed spironolactone for ascites.
C) A client receiving a daily dose of furosemide for heart failure.
D) A client diagnosed with untreated Addison's disease.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** Furosemide is a loop diuretic that causes the excretion of potassium in the urine, placing the client at a high risk for hypokalemia. The other options describe conditions or medications (end-stage renal disease, spironolactone, Addison's disease) that typically lead to potassium retention and an increased risk of *hyperkalemia*. 
</details>

---

### Question 282
**Lens: CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)**

The nurse is caring for a client with a newly diagnosed deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in the right calf. Which action is contraindicated for this client?

A) Elevating the right leg while resting in bed.
B) Administering a prescribed subcutaneous anticoagulant.
C) Applying a warm, moist compress to the affected leg.
D) Massaging the right calf to relieve muscle aching.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Rationale:** Massaging an extremity with a known deep vein thrombosis (DVT) is strictly contraindicated because the mechanical pressure can dislodge the clot, leading to a life-threatening pulmonary embolism. Elevating the leg, administering anticoagulants, and applying warm, moist compresses are expected and safe interventions for DVT management.
</details>

---

### Question 283
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)**

The nurse is performing a physical assessment on a client with a long history of severe chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). Which clinical finding is an expected characteristic for this client?

A) Sudden onset of sharp, pleuritic chest pain.
B) An anterior-posterior (AP) to transverse chest diameter of 1:1.
C) Blood-tinged, frothy sputum with a cough.
D) A resting respiratory rate of 10 breaths per minute.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** An anterior-posterior to transverse chest diameter of 1:1 (often called a "barrel chest") is an expected finding in chronic COPD due to chronic air trapping and hyperinflation of the lungs. Pleuritic chest pain, frothy sputum (a sign of pulmonary edema), and bradypnea (10 breaths per minute) are abnormal findings that would require further investigation.
</details>

---

### Question 284
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

The nurse is teaching a client with newly diagnosed type 1 diabetes mellitus about managing episodes of hypoglycemia. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "If I feel shaky and sweaty, I will drink 4 ounces of regular fruit juice."
B) "I should eat a high-protein meal, like a piece of steak, as soon as my blood sugar drops."
C) "If my blood sugar is low, I will take an extra dose of my rapid-acting insulin."
D) "I will wait 45 minutes after eating a snack to recheck my blood glucose."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** According to the "Rule of 15" for treating hypoglycemia, the client should consume 15 grams of fast-acting simple carbohydrates, such as 4 ounces (120 mL) of regular fruit juice or regular soda, to quickly raise blood glucose levels. Taking more insulin would worsen the hypoglycemia. Protein does not raise blood sugar quickly enough during an acute episode. The client should recheck their blood glucose 15 minutes after consuming the carbohydrate, not 45 minutes.
</details>

---

### Question 285
**Lens: DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)**

The nurse is reviewing the laboratory results for an adult client admitted with severe gastroenteritis and prolonged vomiting over the last 48 hours. The client's Blood Urea Nitrogen (BUN) is 32 mg/dL, and serum creatinine is 0.9 mg/dL. How should the nurse best interpret these findings?

A) The client is experiencing acute kidney injury.
B) The client has a systemic bacterial infection.
C) The client is experiencing systemic dehydration.
D) The client's liver function is severely impaired.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** An elevated Blood Urea Nitrogen (BUN) level (normal is typically 10-20 mg/dL) alongside a normal serum creatinine level (normal is typically 0.6-1.2 mg/dL) is a classic indicator of dehydration or fluid volume deficit. Prolonged vomiting causes significant fluid loss, concentrating the blood and elevating the BUN, without indicating structural acute kidney injury (which would typically elevate both BUN and creatinine). 
</details>
### Question 286
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

The nurse is assessing clients on a medical-surgical unit for the risk of developing deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Which client has the highest risk for developing a DVT?

A) A 30-year-old client hospitalized for observation after a mild concussion.
B) A 25-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy 12 hours ago.
C) A 65-year-old client on strict bed rest following a hip replacement surgery.
D) A 40-year-old client with asthma receiving respiratory nebulizer treatments.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) A 65-year-old client on strict bed rest following a hip replacement surgery.

**Rationale:** Deep vein thrombosis (DVT) risk factors include venous stasis, endothelial damage, and hypercoagulability (Virchow's triad). Major orthopedic surgery (such as hip replacement) combined with advanced age and strict bed rest places this client at a significantly high risk for DVT. The other clients are either mobile or lack multiple major risk factors for clot formation.
</details>

---

### Question 287
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)

A client is admitted to the emergency department with severe right lower quadrant abdominal pain, nausea, and suspected appendicitis. Which intervention is contraindicated for this client?

A) Applying a heating pad directly to the abdomen to relieve pain.
B) Keeping the client strictly NPO (nothing by mouth).
C) Administering prescribed continuous intravenous (IV) fluids.
D) Assisting the client into a side-lying position with knees flexed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Applying a heating pad directly to the abdomen to relieve pain.

**Rationale:** Applying heat to the abdomen of a client with suspected appendicitis is strictly contraindicated. Heat increases blood circulation to the appendix, which can cause increased swelling and pressure, leading to a high risk of appendix rupture. Keeping the client NPO, giving IV fluids, and placing them in a knee-flexed position for comfort are all safe and expected interventions.
</details>

---

### Question 288
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)

The nurse is assessing a client who had an open cholecystectomy 24 hours ago. The client has a Jackson-Pratt (JP) drain in place. Which assessment finding regarding the drain is considered expected at this time?

A) The drainage fluid has a foul-smelling, thick, and yellow appearance.
B) The drain insertion site has a 4-centimeter area of expanding redness.
C) The collection bulb is completely full of bright red, thick blood.
D) Serosanguineous drainage is present inside the compressed collection bulb.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Serosanguineous drainage is present inside the compressed collection bulb.

**Rationale:** Serosanguineous (pinkish, watery) drainage is an expected and normal finding 24 hours postoperatively. Bright red blood indicates active hemorrhage, foul-smelling yellow drainage indicates infection, and expanding redness at the site indicates localized infection or cellulitis. These unexpected findings would require immediate reporting to the healthcare provider.
</details>

---

### Question 289
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD) regarding dietary modifications. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I can still drink coffee as long as I add plenty of cream to it."
B) "I will make sure to avoid eating peppermint and chocolate."
C) "I should eat a large, heavy meal right before I go to sleep."
D) "I will drink a large glass of orange juice with my breakfast."

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "I will make sure to avoid eating peppermint and chocolate."

**Rationale:** Peppermint, chocolate, caffeine, and acidic foods (like orange juice) decrease lower esophageal sphincter (LES) pressure or irritate the esophageal lining, which exacerbates GERD symptoms. Eating large meals right before bed promotes stomach distention and reflux while lying down. Avoiding peppermint and chocolate demonstrates a correct understanding of dietary management for GERD.
</details>

---

### Question 290
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)

The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving daily furosemide, a loop diuretic. The client's serum potassium level is reported as 3.1 mEq/L (3.1 mmol/L). How should the nurse interpret this basic laboratory value?

A) The client has hyperkalemia.
B) The client has hyponatremia.
C) The client has hypokalemia.
D) The client has a normal potassium level.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The client has hypokalemia.

**Rationale:** The normal therapeutic range for serum potassium is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L (mmol/L). A laboratory value of 3.1 mEq/L falls below the normal range, indicating hypokalemia. This is a common and expected side effect of loop diuretics like furosemide, as they promote the excretion of potassium in the urine.
</details>
### Question 291
A nurse is reviewing the medical records of four assigned clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A 25-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy and ambulating in the halls
B) A 40-year-old client with a history of mild asthma admitted for a respiratory exacerbation
C) A 65-year-old client who is 2 days postoperative from a total hip arthroplasty and remains on bedrest
D) A 50-year-old client admitted with gastroenteritis and mild fluid volume deficit

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION  
Advanced age, major orthopedic surgery (such as a total hip arthroplasty), and prolonged immobility are all major risk factors for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The other clients are either younger, mobile, or lack the combination of significant surgical trauma and immobility that places them at high risk.
</details>

---

### Question 292
A client is admitted to the emergency department with severe right lower quadrant abdominal pain, nausea, and rebound tenderness. The healthcare provider suspects acute appendicitis. Which of the following interventions is contraindicated for this client?

A) Keeping the client NPO (nothing by mouth)
B) Administering a warm tap water enema to relieve constipation
C) Administering prescribed intravenous (IV) fluids
D) Placing the client in a semi-Fowler's position to reduce tension on the abdomen

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION  
Administering enemas, laxatives, or applying localized heat to the abdomen is strictly contraindicated in clients with suspected appendicitis. These interventions increase bowel motility and localized blood flow, which significantly increases the risk of the inflamed appendix rupturing. Keeping the client NPO, giving IV fluids, and placing them in a comfortable position are all expected, safe interventions.
</details>

---

### Question 293
A nurse is assessing a client who has a newly created arteriovenous (AV) fistula in the left arm for hemodialysis. Which assessment finding is an expected, normal finding that indicates the fistula is patent?

A) A palpable thrill over the fistula site
B) Redness, warmth, and swelling around the fistula site
C) An absent radial pulse in the left wrist
D) A capillary refill time of 5 seconds in the left hand

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED  
A palpable thrill (a vibrating sensation felt by the fingers) and an auscultated bruit (a swishing sound heard with a stethoscope) are expected findings over a patent, functioning AV fistula due to the turbulent blood flow. Redness and swelling (signs of infection), an absent pulse, or delayed capillary refill (signs of poor distal perfusion) are unexpected and require immediate medical evaluation.
</details>

---

### Question 294
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD). Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the lifestyle modifications needed?

A) "I will lie down flat immediately after eating a large meal to rest."
B) "I should drink a cup of hot peppermint tea if I experience severe heartburn."
C) "I can eat fried foods as long as I take my prescribed antacid right after."
D) "I will sleep with the head of my bed elevated using pillows or blocks."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING  
Elevating the head of the bed helps utilize gravity to prevent stomach acid from refluxing into the esophagus during sleep. Lying down after meals, consuming peppermint (which relaxes the lower esophageal sphincter), and eating high-fat/fried foods all exacerbate GERD symptoms and indicate a need for further teaching.
</details>

---

### Question 295
A nurse is reviewing the basic laboratory results of a client who has experienced severe diarrhea and vomiting for the past 3 days. Which laboratory finding is most consistent with a fluid volume deficit (dehydration)?

A) Serum potassium 4.0 mEq/L
B) Urine specific gravity 1.005
C) Urine specific gravity 1.035
D) Serum sodium 135 mEq/L

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION  
Normal urine specific gravity ranges from 1.005 to 1.030. An elevated specific gravity (such as 1.035) indicates highly concentrated urine, which is a classic physiological adaptation to fluid volume deficit (dehydration). The kidneys hold onto water, resulting in darker, denser urine. The potassium (4.0 mEq/L) and sodium (135 mEq/L) values listed here are within normal limits.
</details>
### Question 296
A client had a permanent pacemaker inserted yesterday to treat symptomatic bradycardia. Which assessment finding should the nurse identify as an unexpected complication?

A. A heart rate of 72 beats per minute
B. Mild bruising at the insertion site
C. Persistent hiccups occurring with each heartbeat
D. Slight soreness when the arm on the operative side is moved

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Persistent hiccups occurring with each heartbeat

**Rationale:** Persistent hiccups can indicate that the pacemaker lead has become displaced and is stimulating the diaphragm or phrenic nerve instead of the heart. This is an unexpected and potentially dangerous complication that requires immediate provider notification. A heart rate of 72 bpm is a normal, expected finding for a paced rhythm. Mild bruising and slight soreness at the insertion site are expected postoperative findings.
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**Category:** Physiological Adaptation
</details>

---

### Question 297
The nurse is teaching a client newly diagnosed with gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD). Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the basic dietary instructions?

A. "I will avoid eating for at least two hours before I go to sleep."
B. "I should drink a cup of warm peppermint tea after dinner."
C. "I will eat three large meals a day to keep my stomach full."
D. "I can have tomatoes and orange juice as long as I take my antacid."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. "I will avoid eating for at least two hours before I go to sleep."

**Rationale:** To prevent acid reflux, clients with GERD should not eat within 2 to 3 hours of going to bed, as lying down with a full stomach promotes the upward flow of gastric acid. Peppermint, large meals, tomatoes, and citrus juices all lower lower esophageal sphincter (LES) pressure or directly irritate the esophagus, making GERD symptoms worse. 
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**Category:** Physiological Adaptation
</details>

---

### Question 298
A nurse is preparing to empty a Jackson-Pratt (JP) drain for a postoperative client. After washing hands and donning clean gloves, what is the first physical step the nurse should take to empty the drain?

A. Squeeze the bulb flat.
B. Uncap the bulb to release the vacuum.
C. Clean the spout with an alcohol swab.
D. Record the amount of drainage.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Uncap the bulb to release the vacuum.

**Rationale:** The first physical step in emptying a Jackson-Pratt drain is to uncap the bulb, which releases the suction (vacuum) so the fluid can be poured out. After emptying the contents into a measuring cup, the nurse will clean the spout, squeeze the bulb flat to re-establish the vacuum, and recap it. Finally, the nurse will document the amount and characteristics of the drainage.
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**Category:** Physiological Adaptation
</details>

---

### Question 299
A client with end-stage renal disease (ESRD) is experiencing constipation. Which over-the-counter laxative should the nurse recognize as contraindicated for this client?

A. Psyllium husk
B. Docusate sodium
C. Bisacodyl
D. Milk of Magnesia

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Milk of Magnesia

**Rationale:** Milk of Magnesia contains magnesium. Clients with end-stage renal disease cannot properly excrete magnesium through their kidneys, putting them at high risk for life-threatening hypermagnesemia. Psyllium husk (bulk-forming), docusate sodium (stool softener), and bisacodyl (stimulant) do not pose this specific electrolyte risk and are safer options when prescribed by the provider.
**Lens:** Contraindication
**Category:** Physiological Adaptation
</details>

---

### Question 300
A client was admitted with severe dehydration and has been receiving intravenous (IV) normal saline at 125 mL/hr for the past 12 hours. Which assessment finding best indicates to the nurse that the fluid replacement therapy has been effective?

A. The client's heart rate is 112 beats per minute.
B. The client's blood pressure is 86/50 mmHg.
C. The client's urine output is consistently 50 mL/hr.
D. The client's skin turgor shows tenting on the chest.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. The client's urine output is consistently 50 mL/hr.

**Rationale:** Adequate urine output (at least 30 mL/hr in an adult) is one of the best indicators of effective fluid resuscitation and adequate kidney perfusion. A heart rate of 112 bpm (tachycardia), a blood pressure of 86/50 mmHg (hypotension), and skin tenting are all signs that the client is still experiencing a fluid volume deficit.
**Lens:** Evaluation
**Category:** Physiological Adaptation
</details>
### Question 301

A nurse is assessing a client who is 24 hours postoperative following an open appendectomy. Which finding indicates an expected physiological adaptation to the surgery?

A) Purulent green drainage leaking from the incision line
B) Serosanguineous drainage in the surgical wound drain
C) Bright red, spurting blood from the surgical site dressing
D) Sudden, severe, and rigid distension of the abdomen

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Serosanguineous drainage in the surgical wound drain

**Rationale:** Serosanguineous drainage (a mix of clear serum and red blood) is an expected physiological finding 24 hours after a major abdominal surgery as the body begins the healing process. Purulent drainage (A) indicates a developing infection. Bright red, spurting blood (C) indicates active arterial hemorrhage. A rigid, distended abdomen (D) is an unexpected finding that may indicate peritonitis or internal bleeding. 

**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
</details>

---

### Question 302

A nurse is providing dietary teaching to a client with heart failure who has been prescribed furosemide, a loop diuretic. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should limit my daily fluid intake to 4 liters to stay hydrated."
B) "I will stop taking the medication if I urinate too much during the day."
C) "I will include bananas, potatoes, and spinach in my daily diet."
D) "I need to avoid all dietary sodium, including fresh fruits and vegetables."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "I will include bananas, potatoes, and spinach in my daily diet."

**Rationale:** Furosemide is a potassium-wasting loop diuretic. Clients taking this medication are at risk for hypokalemia and should be taught to consume potassium-rich foods, such as bananas, potatoes, and spinach, to maintain normal electrolyte levels. Clients with heart failure typically restrict fluids, not increase them to 4 liters (A). The medication should not be stopped without consulting the provider, as frequent urination is the intended effect (B). Fresh fruits and vegetables are naturally low in sodium and should be encouraged, not avoided (D).

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
</details>

---

### Question 303

A nurse is reviewing the laboratory results for a client who has diabetes mellitus. The client is currently sweating, trembling, and tachycardic. The client's capillary blood glucose level is 42 mg/dL. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) The client has a normal fasting blood glucose level.
B) The client is experiencing diabetic ketoacidosis.
C) The client is experiencing severe hyperglycemia.
D) The client is experiencing hypoglycemia.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) The client is experiencing hypoglycemia.

**Rationale:** A blood glucose level of 42 mg/dL is significantly lower than the normal range (typically 70-99 mg/dL fasting), indicating hypoglycemia. The client's symptoms of sweating (diaphoresis), trembling, and tachycardia are classic physiological responses to a dangerously low blood sugar level. Hyperglycemia (C) and diabetic ketoacidosis (B) are associated with highly elevated blood glucose levels. 

**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
</details>

---

### Question 304

A nurse is caring for a client who is admitted with severe gastroenteritis and dehydration. Which task is appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Recording the volume of the client's emesis and urine output.
B) Assessing the client's skin turgor and mucous membranes for hydration.
C) Teaching the client how to consume oral rehydration solutions.
D) Evaluating the effectiveness of the administered intravenous antiemetic medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Recording the volume of the client's emesis and urine output.

**Rationale:** Documenting intake and output (I&O), including urine and emesis, is a routine, standard task that falls safely within the scope of practice for an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Assessing skin turgor (B), teaching the client (C), and evaluating medication effectiveness (D) all require nursing judgment and must be performed by the registered nurse.

**Lens:** DELEGATION
</details>

---

### Question 305

A nurse enters a client's room and observes the client experiencing a generalized tonic-clonic seizure while in bed. What is the nurse's first physical action?

A) Restrain the client's extremities to prevent them from thrashing.
B) Position the client onto their side to protect their airway.
C) Insert a padded tongue blade into the client's mouth to prevent tongue biting.
D) Leave the room briefly to grab the emergency crash cart from the hallway.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Position the client onto their side to protect their airway.

**Rationale:** During a seizure, the nurse's primary physical action is to protect the client's airway and prevent aspiration. Turning the client onto their side allows saliva or emesis to drain out of the mouth. Restraining a client (A) can cause fractures or soft tissue injury. Inserting anything into the mouth (C) is strictly contraindicated as it can cause broken teeth or airway obstruction. The nurse should never leave a client alone during an active seizure (D).

**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
</details>
### Question 306
The nurse is reviewing the census on a medical-surgical unit. Based on the concept of risk recognition, which client is at the highest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A client who had a laparoscopic appendectomy yesterday and is ambulating in the hallway.
B) A client with a fractured femur who has been on bed rest with traction for three days.
C) A client admitted with community-acquired pneumonia who is receiving IV antibiotics.
D) A client who gave birth vaginally 12 hours ago and is breastfeeding.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)<br>
<strong>Physiological Adaptation Concept:</strong> Alterations in Body Systems / Venous Thromboembolism.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Virchow's triad outlines the primary risk factors for deep vein thrombosis (DVT): venous stasis, endothelial injury, and hypercoagulability. A client with a fractured femur on prolonged bed rest has two major risk factors: severe bone/vessel injury and significant venous stasis from immobility. The other clients are either ambulating or have lower baseline risks for DVT formation.
</details>

---

### Question 307
The nurse is reviewing admission orders for a client presenting with an active upper gastrointestinal (GI) bleed. Which of the following orders should the nurse recognize as a contraindication and question immediately?

A) Administer pantoprazole 40 mg IV push daily.
B) Transfuse 1 unit of packed red blood cells (PRBCs).
C) Administer ibuprofen 400 mg PO every 6 hours for mild pain.
D) Initiate a continuous infusion of 0.9% sodium chloride.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)<br>
<strong>Physiological Adaptation Concept:</strong> Medical Emergencies / Illness Management.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Ibuprofen is a nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID). NSAIDs inhibit platelet aggregation and can cause or exacerbate gastric ulceration and bleeding. It is strictly contraindicated in a client with an active gastrointestinal bleed. Pantoprazole, IV fluids, and blood transfusions are standard, safe, and expected treatments for this condition.
</details>

---

### Question 308
The nurse is assessing a client who had a new colostomy created two days ago. When observing the stoma, which finding is an expected, normal finding that indicates healthy tissue perfusion?

A) The stoma is beefy red and moist.
B) The stoma is pale pink and dry.
C) The stoma is dark purple and swollen.
D) The stoma is black and firm to the touch.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)<br>
<strong>Physiological Adaptation Concept:</strong> Illness Management / Ostomy Care.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A healthy, newly created stoma should appear beefy red and moist, indicating adequate blood supply and tissue perfusion. A pale, dark purple, or black stoma indicates ischemia or necrosis (lack of blood flow) and must be reported to the healthcare provider immediately.
</details>

---

### Question 309
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with type 1 diabetes regarding the management of hypoglycemia. Which statement by the client indicates an accurate understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will lie down and rest for an hour before checking my blood sugar if I feel dizzy."
B) "I should take an extra dose of my fast-acting insulin if I start sweating and trembling."
C) "I will eat a high-protein snack like a piece of cheese if my blood sugar drops."
D) "I will drink 4 ounces of regular fruit juice if my blood sugar is low."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)<br>
<strong>Physiological Adaptation Concept:</strong> Pathophysiology / Diabetes Management.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The "Rule of 15" states that if a client experiences hypoglycemia, they should consume 15 grams of fast-acting, simple carbohydrates, such as 4 ounces of regular fruit juice or regular soda, to rapidly raise their blood glucose level. Protein (cheese) does not raise blood sugar quickly enough. Taking extra insulin would worsen the hypoglycemia, and resting without treating the low blood sugar can lead to a coma.
</details>

---

### Question 310
The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving a daily dose of the loop diuretic furosemide. The client's serum potassium level is reported as 2.8 mEq/L (2.8 mmol/L). How should the nurse interpret this basic laboratory data?

A) The level is elevated, and the client needs sodium polystyrene sulfonate.
B) The level is normal, and no intervention is required for this client.
C) The level is abnormally low, placing the client at high risk for cardiac dysrhythmias.
D) The level is slightly low but represents an expected finding with normal fluid intake.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)<br>
<strong>Physiological Adaptation Concept:</strong> Fluid and Electrolyte Imbalances.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The normal serum potassium range is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.8 mEq/L indicates hypokalemia, which is a common adverse effect of potassium-wasting diuretics like furosemide. Hypokalemia alters the resting membrane potential of the myocardium, placing the client at significant risk for dangerous, potentially life-threatening cardiac dysrhythmias. Immediate intervention is required.
</details>
### Question 311
The nurse is caring for a client who has been vomiting extensively for the past 24 hours due to a gastrointestinal virus. The nurse identifies that this client is at highest risk for which complication when first getting out of bed?

A. Bradycardia  
B. Orthostatic hypotension  
C. Hypertension  
D. Peripheral edema  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Details</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Orthostatic hypotension

**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

**Rationale:** Extensive vomiting leads to a significant fluid volume deficit (dehydration). When a client is dehydrated, they lack adequate circulating blood volume, putting them at high risk for orthostatic (postural) hypotension, dizziness, and falls when changing positions from lying to sitting or standing. 

**NCLEX Category:** Physiological Integrity - Physiological Adaptation  
</details>

---

### Question 312
A client presents to the emergency department with severe right lower quadrant abdominal pain, nausea, and a low-grade fever. The healthcare provider suspects appendicitis. Which intervention is unsafe and contraindicated for this client?

A. Applying a heating pad to the right lower abdomen  
B. Maintaining strict nothing-by-mouth (NPO) status  
C. Administering prescribed intravenous fluids  
D. Placing the client in a comfortable semi-Fowler's position  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Details</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Applying a heating pad to the right lower abdomen

**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)

**Rationale:** Applying heat to the abdomen of a client with suspected appendicitis is strictly contraindicated. Heat increases blood circulation to the area, which can cause the inflamed appendix to swell further and suddenly rupture, leading to life-threatening peritonitis. NPO status, IV fluids, and a comfortable position are all safe, standard interventions.

**NCLEX Category:** Physiological Integrity - Physiological Adaptation  
</details>

---

### Question 313
The nurse is assessing a client who had a bowel resection with the creation of a new colostomy 24 hours ago. Which assessment finding regarding the colostomy stoma is expected and indicates stable, healthy circulation?

A. Dark purple coloration  
B. Pale and dry appearance  
C. Beefy red and moist appearance  
D. Black and necrotic outer edges  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Details</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Beefy red and moist appearance

**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)

**Rationale:** A healthy, newly created stoma should appear beefy red and moist. This indicates adequate blood supply and tissue viability. A stoma that is dark purple, blue, pale, or black indicates impaired circulation (ischemia or necrosis) and must be reported to the healthcare provider immediately.

**NCLEX Category:** Physiological Integrity - Physiological Adaptation  
</details>

---

### Question 314
The nurse is teaching a client how to care for a healing abdominal surgical incision at home. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the basic teaching regarding infection prevention?

A. "I will soak in a warm bath every evening to keep the wound clean."  
B. "I should stop taking my prescribed antibiotics once the redness goes away."  
C. "I will apply rubbing alcohol daily to dry out the wound margins."  
D. "I will wash my hands thoroughly before and after touching my bandage."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Details</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. "I will wash my hands thoroughly before and after touching my bandage."

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

**Rationale:** Hand hygiene is the single most important and basic practice to prevent the introduction of bacteria and the development of a wound infection. Soaking in a bath is typically contraindicated for fresh incisions. Antibiotics must be finished completely to prevent bacterial resistance, and rubbing alcohol is too harsh for healing tissue and will impair the healing process.

**NCLEX Category:** Physiological Integrity - Physiological Adaptation  
</details>

---

### Question 315
The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving continuous diuretic therapy for heart failure. The client's serum potassium level is 3.0 mEq/L (3.0 mmol/L). What does this basic lab value indicate?

A. Normal potassium level  
B. Hypokalemia  
C. Hyperkalemia  
D. Hyponatremia  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Details</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Hypokalemia

**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)

**Rationale:** The normal adult range for serum potassium is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 3.0 mEq/L is below the normal range, indicating hypokalemia (low potassium). This is a common and expected complication of certain diuretic therapies (like loop diuretics) but requires intervention, such as a potassium supplement, to prevent cardiac dysrhythmias.

**NCLEX Category:** Physiological Integrity - Physiological Adaptation  
</details>
### Question 316
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION**
The nurse is reviewing the medical records of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the greatest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A 25-year-old client recovering from a simple laparoscopic appendectomy
B) A 40-year-old client admitted for an acute asthma exacerbation
C) A 65-year-old client on strict bed rest following an ischemic stroke
D) A 50-year-old client ambulating frequently in the halls after gastric bypass surgery

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Prolonged immobility (such as strict bed rest) and older age are major risk factors for venous stasis, which significantly increases the risk of developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). A stroke client with limited mobility is at exceptionally high risk. Early and frequent ambulation (Options A and D) helps prevent DVT. An asthma exacerbation (Option B) does not inherently carry as high a risk for DVT as prolonged bed rest does.
</details>

---

### Question 317
**Lens: CONTRAINDICATION**
A client with severe hypokalemia is prescribed intravenous (IV) potassium chloride. Which action by the nurse is absolutely contraindicated when administering this medication?

A) Administering the medication via an IV push or bolus
B) Diluting the medication in a compatible IV fluid before infusion
C) Monitoring the client's cardiac rhythm continuously during administration
D) Assessing the IV site frequently for signs of redness or phlebitis

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Intravenous potassium chloride must never be administered via IV push, IV bolus, or undiluted. Rapid administration of potassium can cause fatal cardiac arrhythmias and cardiac arrest. It must always be properly diluted (Option B) and infused slowly using an infusion pump. Monitoring the heart (Option C) and the IV site (Option D) are correct and expected nursing actions.
</details>

---

### Question 318
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED**
A client is recovering from a right-sided thoracotomy and has a chest tube connected to a traditional water-seal drainage system. Which observation indicates a normal, expected finding in the water-seal chamber?

A) Continuous, rapid bubbling throughout the respiratory cycle
B) A sudden and complete cessation of all serosanguineous drainage
C) Bright red, bloody drainage filling the chamber entirely within one hour
D) Gentle fluctuation of the fluid level with inspiration and expiration

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Tidaling, which is the gentle fluctuation of fluid in the water-seal chamber with inspiration and expiration, is a normal and expected finding. It indicates that the chest tube is patent and functioning properly. Continuous bubbling in the water-seal chamber (Option A) indicates an air leak. Sudden cessation of drainage (Option B) or heavy bright red bleeding (Option C) are unexpected findings that require immediate investigation.
</details>

---

### Question 319
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING**
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with type 2 diabetes mellitus regarding proper foot care. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will soak my feet in hot water every evening to keep them clean."
B) "I should wear clean, white cotton socks and well-fitting shoes."
C) "I will use a heating pad on my feet when they feel cold at night."
D) "I can walk barefoot in my house, but I should never do it outside."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Clients with diabetes should wear well-fitting shoes and clean, absorbent socks (such as white cotton) to protect their feet and easily spot any bleeding or drainage. Because of the risk of peripheral neuropathy, they should avoid extreme temperatures, meaning no hot soaks (Option A) or heating pads (Option C), as these can cause severe burns. Clients should never walk barefoot, even indoors (Option D), due to the risk of stepping on a sharp object and sustaining an unnoticed injury.
</details>

---

### Question 320
**Lens: DATA INTERPRETATION**
The nurse reviews the basic metabolic panel of a client experiencing muscle cramps, lethargy, and new-onset confusion. The laboratory results show a serum sodium level of 126 mEq/L (126 mmol/L). How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) The client is experiencing significant hyponatremia.
B) The client is experiencing severe hypernatremia.
C) The client has a normal, expected sodium level.
D) The client is experiencing critical hypokalemia.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The normal range for serum sodium is 135 to 145 mEq/L (mmol/L). A value of 126 mEq/L is abnormally low, indicating hyponatremia. This directly correlates with the client's symptoms of confusion, lethargy, and muscle cramps, which are classic signs of low sodium levels affecting the central nervous system.
</details>
### Question 321
The nurse is assessing the fluid status of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Based on the current conditions, which client is at the highest risk for developing a fluid volume deficit?

A) A client with chronic heart failure exacerbation.
B) A client receiving continuous intravenous normal saline at 125 mL/hr.
C) A client with a three-day history of severe nausea, vomiting, and diarrhea.
D) A client with end-stage renal disease who missed their last dialysis session.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) A client with a three-day history of severe nausea, vomiting, and diarrhea.

**Reasoning:** 
* **Lens: Risk Recognition**
* Severe vomiting and diarrhea cause significant loss of water and electrolytes from the gastrointestinal tract, placing the client at a high risk for fluid volume deficit (dehydration). 
* Clients with heart failure (Option A) and end-stage renal disease (Option D) are at risk for fluid volume *overload*, not deficit, due to fluid retention. 
* A client receiving continuous IV normal saline (Option B) is having fluids replenished and is more likely at risk for fluid overload if not monitored carefully.
</details>

---

### Question 322
A client is admitted to the emergency department with suspected acute appendicitis and reports severe right lower quadrant abdominal pain. Which intervention is contraindicated for this client?

A) Applying a warm heating pad to the abdomen to soothe the pain.
B) Administering prescribed intravenous fluids for hydration.
C) Placing the client in a semi-Fowler's position to relieve tension on the abdomen.
D) Keeping the client strictly nothing by mouth (NPO) in preparation for possible surgery.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Applying a warm heating pad to the abdomen to soothe the pain.

**Reasoning:** 
* **Lens: Contraindication**
* The application of heat to the abdomen of a client with suspected appendicitis is strictly contraindicated. Heat increases blood flow to the area, which can increase inflammation and lead to the rupture of the appendix, causing peritonitis.
* Administering IV fluids (Option B) and keeping the client NPO (Option D) are expected standard interventions to prepare the client for potential surgery. 
* Placing the client in a semi-Fowler's position (Option C) is a safe, non-pharmacological comfort measure that relieves tension on the abdominal muscles.
</details>

---

### Question 323
The nurse is performing a physical assessment on a client who has a long-standing history of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). Which anatomical change is considered an expected finding for this client?

A) A resting oxygen saturation of 99% on room air.
B) Inspiratory stridor audible without a stethoscope.
C) Symmetrical chest expansion with a 1:2 anteroposterior diameter.
D) An anteroposterior-to-transverse chest ratio of 1:1.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) An anteroposterior-to-transverse chest ratio of 1:1.

**Reasoning:** 
* **Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**
* Over time, chronic air trapping and hyperinflation of the lungs in COPD lead to physiological adaptations, notably a "barrel chest." This is structurally defined by an anteroposterior-to-transverse (AP:T) ratio of 1:1.
* A normal adult chest has an AP:T ratio of 1:2 (Option C). 
* A resting oxygen saturation of 99% (Option A) is unexpected in severe COPD; clients typically run lower (88-92%). 
* Stridor (Option B) is an unexpected, emergency finding indicating upper airway obstruction, not an expected finding of COPD.
</details>

---

### Question 324
The nurse is reinforcing teaching with a client who has type 2 diabetes mellitus about the management of hypoglycemia. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will wait 30 minutes after eating sugar to recheck my blood glucose."
B) "If I feel shaky and sweaty, I will drink 4 ounces of regular fruit juice."
C) "I should eat a large chocolate candy bar with nuts if my blood sugar is low."
D) "If my blood sugar is less than 70 mg/dL, I will take my fast-acting insulin."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "If I feel shaky and sweaty, I will drink 4 ounces of regular fruit juice."

**Reasoning:** 
* **Lens: Client Teaching**
* The "Rule of 15" states that if a client is experiencing hypoglycemia (blood glucose < 70 mg/dL), they should consume 15 grams of fast-acting simple carbohydrates, such as 4 ounces (120 mL) of regular fruit juice or regular soda. 
* Blood glucose should be rechecked in 15 minutes, not 30 minutes (Option A). 
* Candy bars containing fat and protein (nuts) delay the absorption of sugar and are not recommended for rapid treatment of hypoglycemia (Option C). 
* Taking insulin (Option D) would further lower the blood sugar and is extremely dangerous during a hypoglycemic event.
</details>

---

### Question 325
The nurse is reviewing morning laboratory results for an adult client who receives daily oral furosemide for hypertension. The client's serum potassium level is 2.9 mEq/L (2.9 mmol/L). How should the nurse interpret this basic laboratory finding?

A) The client is experiencing hyperkalemia.
B) The level indicates severe hemoconcentration.
C) The client is experiencing hypokalemia.
D) The level is within the expected normal reference range.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The client is experiencing hypokalemia.

**Reasoning:** 
* **Lens: Data Interpretation**
* The normal reference range for serum potassium is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.9 mEq/L is below normal, indicating hypokalemia. 
* Furosemide is a potassium-wasting loop diuretic, making hypokalemia a common and expected side effect that requires monitoring and potentially potassium supplementation. 
* Hyperkalemia (Option A) would be a level greater than 5.0 mEq/L. 
* This value does not reflect a normal reference range (Option D) or specifically diagnose hemoconcentration (Option B).
</details>
### Question 326
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Scenario:** A client is 12 hours postoperative following an open cholecystectomy. The nurse empties 40 mL of serosanguineous fluid from the client's Jackson-Pratt drain. 
**Question:** How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) It is a sign of an active internal hemorrhage.
B) It is an expected finding during this postoperative period.
C) It indicates a blockage in the drainage tubing.
D) It suggests a postoperative wound infection.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) It is an expected finding during this postoperative period.

**Rationale:** Serosanguineous drainage (a mixture of clear fluid and blood) in small to moderate amounts (like 40 mL) is a normal and expected finding in the first 12 to 24 hours following open abdominal surgery. It does not indicate active hemorrhage, blockage, or infection at this early stage.
</details>

---

### Question 327
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Scenario:** The nurse is teaching a client with a new descending colostomy about pouch care. 
**Question:** Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the basic teaching?

A) "I should empty the pouch when it is about one-third to one-half full."
B) "I will cut the skin barrier opening exactly 2 inches larger than my stoma."
C) "I should wait to empty the pouch until it is completely full."
D) "I will clean the stoma daily using alcohol wipes."

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "I should empty the pouch when it is about one-third to one-half full."

**Rationale:** Emptying the ostomy pouch when it is 1/3 to 1/2 full prevents the weight of the output from pulling the appliance off the skin and causing a leak. Cutting the barrier too large causes skin breakdown, waiting until the pouch is full increases the risk of detachment, and cleaning with alcohol is too harsh and will dry out the stoma.
</details>

---

### Question 328
**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Scenario:** A client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) is receiving oxygen at 2 L/min via nasal cannula. 
**Question:** Which task is safe and appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Adjusting the oxygen flow rate if the client feels short of breath.
B) Assessing the client's skin for irritation around the ears and nose.
C) Reapplying the nasal cannula after assisting the client with bathing.
D) Teaching the client about the importance of wearing the oxygen continuously.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Reapplying the nasal cannula after assisting the client with bathing.

**Rationale:** UAPs can safely reapply a nasal cannula that was temporarily removed for basic care like bathing or changing a gown. Adjusting oxygen flow rates, performing skin assessments, and providing client education are all nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 329
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
**Scenario:** The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client with a history of heart failure who takes a daily loop diuretic (furosemide). The client's serum potassium level is 3.1 mEq/L.
**Question:** What does this basic laboratory result indicate?

A) Hyperkalemia
B) Normal potassium level
C) Hyponatremia
D) Hypokalemia

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Hypokalemia

**Rationale:** The normal serum potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 3.1 mEq/L is below normal, indicating hypokalemia. This is a common and expected side effect of potassium-wasting diuretics like furosemide, requiring the nurse to monitor the client and potentially administer a potassium supplement.
</details>

---

### Question 330
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Scenario:** The nurse is preparing to feed a client who has dysphagia following an ischemic stroke. 
**Question:** What is the first physical step the nurse should take before providing the meal to prevent aspiration?

A) Ensure the client is positioned upright at a 90-degree angle.
B) Instruct the client to tuck their chin when swallowing.
C) Place a suction catheter directly into the client's mouth.
D) Offer the client a large glass of thin water to clear the throat.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Ensure the client is positioned upright at a 90-degree angle.

**Rationale:** The first physical step in preventing aspiration for a client with dysphagia is to position them upright at a 90-degree angle (High Fowler's position) before any feeding begins. Chin tucking is done *during* the swallow, thin liquids are often contraindicated and require evaluation, and suction should be available nearby but not placed into the mouth prior to eating.
</details>
### Question 331
The nurse is reviewing the medication records of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Based on the prescribed medications, which client is at the greatest risk for developing hypokalemia?

A. A client receiving spironolactone for heart failure.
B. A client receiving furosemide for pulmonary edema.
C. A client receiving lisinopril for hypertension.
D. A client receiving heparin for a deep vein thrombosis.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Lens:** Risk Recognition (Who is at risk for injury?)

**Rationale:** Furosemide is a loop diuretic that causes the kidneys to excrete sodium, chloride, water, and potassium. This places the client at a high risk for developing hypokalemia (low potassium). Spironolactone and lisinopril both carry a risk of hyperkalemia (high potassium). Heparin does not typically affect potassium levels.
</details>

---

### Question 332
The nurse is caring for a client with suspected appendicitis who is reporting severe right lower quadrant abdominal pain. Which intervention is contraindicated for this client?

A. Keeping the client strictly nothing by mouth (NPO).
B. Administering prescribed intravenous normal saline.
C. Applying a heating pad to the painful area of the abdomen.
D. Positioning the client with the knees slightly flexed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Lens:** Contraindication (What is unsafe to do/give?)

**Rationale:** Applying heat to the abdomen of a client with suspected appendicitis is strictly contraindicated. Heat increases blood circulation to the area, which can cause the inflamed appendix to swell further and rupture, leading to peritonitis. Keeping the client NPO, administering IV fluids, and positioning them for comfort (such as flexing the knees) are all safe and expected interventions.
</details>

---

### Question 333
The nurse is assessing a client who had a new colostomy created 24 hours ago. Which physical finding regarding the stoma is expected and indicates stable perfusion?

A. Pale pink and dry
B. Dark purple and edematous
C. Black and retracted
D. Beefy red and moist

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected (Is this finding normal/stable?)

**Rationale:** A healthy, well-perfused stoma should appear beefy red or deep pink and moist. Pale pink or dry stomas indicate poor hydration or anemia. Dark purple, blue, or black stomas indicate ischemia or necrosis (lack of blood flow) and must be reported to the healthcare provider immediately.
</details>

---

### Question 334
The nurse is teaching a client with type 1 diabetes mellitus about "sick day rules" to follow when experiencing a mild viral infection. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I will check my blood sugar every 4 hours while I am sick."
B. "I should stop taking my insulin if I cannot eat solid foods."
C. "I will avoid drinking water to prevent feeling nauseous."
D. "I only need to check my urine for ketones if I feel dizzy."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Lens:** Client Teaching (Do they understand the basics?)

**Rationale:** During an illness, the body releases stress hormones that can cause blood glucose levels to rise rapidly. Clients should check their blood glucose every 3 to 4 hours. Insulin should *never* be stopped, even if the client cannot eat solid foods, though the dose may need adjustment by the provider. The client should maintain hydration to flush out excess glucose and ketones, and ketone testing should be done regularly during an illness, not just when dizzy.
</details>

---

### Question 335
The nurse is reviewing the laboratory results for an adult client with a history of type 2 diabetes mellitus. The client's hemoglobin A1c is 6.5%. How should the nurse best interpret this finding?

A. The client is currently experiencing severe hypoglycemia.
B. The client's blood glucose has been well-managed over the past 3 months.
C. The client is in a state of diabetic ketoacidosis (DKA).
D. The client requires an immediate increase in their prescribed insulin dose.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Lens:** Data Interpretation (What does this basic lab mean?)

**Rationale:** Hemoglobin A1c measures the average blood glucose concentration over the lifespan of red blood cells (approximately 2 to 3 months). For most non-pregnant adults with diabetes, an A1c goal of less than 7% indicates good glycemic control. Therefore, a level of 6.5% shows the client's condition is well-managed. A1c does not measure acute states like severe hypoglycemia or DKA.
</details>
### Question 336
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
The nurse is reviewing the medical records of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest physiological risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A 30-year-old client who is walking the halls following a laparoscopic appendectomy yesterday.
B) A 65-year-old client on strictly prescribed bed rest following a severe pelvic fracture.
C) A 45-year-old client receiving intravenous antibiotics for community-acquired pneumonia.
D) A 22-year-old client admitted for acute gastroenteritis and mild dehydration.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Deep vein thrombosis (DVT) risk increases significantly with factors summarized in Virchow's triad: venous stasis (immobility), endothelial injury (trauma/surgery), and hypercoagulability. The 65-year-old client on strict bed rest with a pelvic fracture has multiple high-risk factors, including prolonged immobility, advanced age, and major bone trauma. The other clients are either younger, mobile, or lack the combination of severe risk factors.

</details>

---

### Question 337
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION
The nurse is preparing to administer a scheduled intravenous (IV) infusion of potassium chloride to a client with a serum potassium level of 3.1 mEq/L (3.1 mmol/L). Which physiological assessment finding would cause the nurse to withhold the infusion and notify the healthcare provider immediately?

A) The client's heart rate is 82 beats per minute.
B) The client reports feeling tired and experiencing muscle weakness.
C) The client has not voided in the past 8 hours and has an empty bladder scan.
D) The client's blood pressure is 118/76 mmHg.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Potassium is primarily excreted by the kidneys. If a client is experiencing oliguria or anuria (not voiding for 8 hours with an empty bladder), their kidneys cannot clear potassium properly. Administering IV potassium chloride to a client with impaired urine output is contraindicated because it can rapidly lead to life-threatening hyperkalemia. Muscle weakness is an expected finding of hypokalemia, and the vital signs listed are normal. 

</details>

---

### Question 338
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
A client is 24 hours postoperative following a bowel resection with the creation of a new colostomy. The nurse assesses the stoma. Which assessment finding should the nurse document as a normal, expected physiological response at this time?

A) The stoma is beefy red and slightly swollen.
B) The stoma has dark purple and black edges.
C) The stoma is pale and pinkish-white in color.
D) The stoma is dry and retracted deep into the abdomen.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** In the immediate postoperative period (the first few days), a healthy stoma should appear beefy red, moist, and slightly edematous (swollen) due to the trauma of surgery and establishing adequate blood supply. Dark purple or black tissue indicates necrosis. Pale or pinkish-white tissue indicates ischemia (lack of blood flow). A dry, retracted stoma is also an abnormal finding that requires intervention.

</details>

---

### Question 339
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
A nurse is teaching a client with newly diagnosed type 1 diabetes about managing physiological adaptations to the disease, specifically hypoglycemia. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should inject extra insulin if I start feeling dizzy and weak before lunch."
B) "If my blood sugar drops, I will eat a large chocolate bar to fix it."
C) "I will lie down and take a nap if I get a sudden headache and feel jittery."
D) "If I feel shaky and sweaty, I will eat a piece of hard candy or drink half a cup of juice."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Shakiness and sweating are classic signs of hypoglycemia. The standard "15-15 rule" for treating hypoglycemia dictates consuming 15 grams of fast-acting simple carbohydrates, such as hard candy or 4 ounces (half a cup) of fruit juice. Chocolate contains fat, which slows the absorption of glucose, making it a poor choice for a quick rescue. Injecting extra insulin would worsen the hypoglycemia, and taking a nap delays necessary, potentially life-saving treatment.

</details>

---

### Question 340
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
The nurse is reviewing the basic laboratory results for a client diagnosed with chronic kidney disease (CKD). The client's serum creatinine level is 4.5 mg/dL (398 µmol/L). How should the nurse interpret this laboratory finding based on the client's condition?

A) The client is experiencing an acute, massive infection.
B) The client has severely impaired kidney function.
C) The client's kidneys are filtering waste products normally.
D) The client is severely dehydrated and needs a rapid intravenous fluid bolus.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** The normal range for serum creatinine is generally 0.6 to 1.2 mg/dL. A creatinine level of 4.5 mg/dL is significantly elevated and indicates severely impaired renal function, which is expected but requires careful management in a client with chronic kidney disease (CKD). It does not indicate infection, and giving a rapid IV fluid bolus to a client with CKD could cause dangerous fluid overload. 

</details>

# SECTION: Reduction of Risk Potential 

### Question 341
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)**

A client is scheduled for a percutaneous liver biopsy in the morning. Which of the following daily medications taken by the client places them at the highest risk for a post-procedure complication?

A) Pantoprazole
B) Acetaminophen
C) Warfarin
D) Metoprolol

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Warfarin is an anticoagulant that prolongs clotting time. Because the liver is a highly vascular organ, a liver biopsy carries a significant risk of hemorrhage. Taking warfarin greatly increases this bleeding risk. Acetaminophen, pantoprazole, and metoprolol do not significantly impact coagulation and are not the primary risk factors for bleeding in this procedure.
</details>

---

### Question 342
**Lens: CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)**

A client is preparing for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which finding in the client's medical history is an absolute contraindication for this procedure?

A) Allergy to iodine contrast dye
B) History of claustrophobia
C) Surgical placement of a titanium joint replacement
D) Presence of a permanent cardiac pacemaker

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** An MRI uses powerful magnets that can interfere with the function of electronic devices and pull on ferromagnetic metals. A traditional cardiac pacemaker is an absolute contraindication because the magnet can deactivate it or dislodge the leads, causing life-threatening arrhythmias. Claustrophobia can be managed with medication. Titanium is not magnetic and is generally safe for MRI. Iodine contrast is used for CT scans, while MRI typically uses gadolinium.
</details>

---

### Question 343
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)**

The nurse removes a client's indwelling urinary (Foley) catheter at 0800. At 1000, the nurse assesses the client. Which finding is expected at this time?

A) The client reports severe lower abdominal pain.
B) The client has not voided yet.
C) The client has dribbling of urine continuously.
D) The client has a distended bladder reaching the umbilicus.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** After an indwelling urinary catheter is removed, the client is expected to void within 6 to 8 hours. It is completely normal and expected that the client has not voided only 2 hours after removal, assuming there is no severe distension or pain. Severe pain, a highly distended bladder, or continuous dribbling (incontinence/retention with overflow) are unexpected findings that require intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 344
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

The nurse is teaching a client how to collect a sputum specimen for culture and sensitivity. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the correct procedure?

A) "I will cough deeply and collect the specimen first thing in the morning."
B) "I should rinse my mouth with mouthwash before coughing up the specimen."
C) "I need to spit my saliva into the cup until it is half full."
D) "I will collect the specimen right before I go to sleep."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Sputum specimens should ideally be collected early in the morning because secretions pool in the lungs overnight, providing a more concentrated and accurate sample. The client should cough deeply to bring up sputum from the lungs, not just spit saliva. Mouthwash should be avoided as it can kill the bacteria needed for the culture; the client should rinse their mouth with plain water instead.
</details>

---

### Question 345
**Lens: DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)**

The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving a daily dose of furosemide. The client's serum potassium level is 3.1 mEq/L (3.1 mmol/L). How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) The level is within the expected normal range.
B) The level is low, placing the client at risk for cardiac dysrhythmias.
C) The level is dangerously high and requires immediate insulin administration.
D) The level is slightly elevated, indicating a risk for muscle spasms.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** The normal serum potassium range is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 3.1 mEq/L indicates hypokalemia. Furosemide is a potassium-wasting loop diuretic, making hypokalemia a common complication. Low potassium places the client at significant risk for life-threatening cardiac dysrhythmias, as well as muscle weakness and cramping.
</details>
### Question 346
A nurse is reviewing the assignments for a group of clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for developing aspiration?

A) A client with a newly casted fractured femur.
B) A client with deep vein thrombosis on strict bed rest.
C) A client receiving continuous enteral tube feedings.
D) A client recovering from an uncomplicated laparoscopic appendectomy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<em>Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)</em><br>
A client receiving continuous enteral tube feedings is at a high risk for aspiration because the feeding bypasses the normal swallowing mechanism. If the head of the bed is not elevated or if gastric emptying is delayed, the feeding can regurgitate into the airway. The other clients do not have a specific, direct mechanism placing them at a high risk for aspiration.
</details>

---

### Question 347
A client is scheduled for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain to investigate frequent headaches. Which finding in the client's medical history is a strict contraindication for this procedure?

A) A cardiac pacemaker placed two years ago.
B) A titanium hip joint replacement.
C) An allergy to iodine-based contrast dye.
D) A history of mild claustrophobia.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<em>Lens: CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)</em><br>
Traditional cardiac pacemakers are generally an absolute contraindication for an MRI. The strong magnetic field can disrupt the device's function, alter its settings, or heat the leads, which is highly unsafe for the client. Titanium implants are non-ferromagnetic and typically safe. Iodine allergies are irrelevant to MRIs (which use gadolinium, if contrast is needed), and claustrophobia can be managed with mild sedation.
</details>

---

### Question 348
A nurse is assessing a client who had a colostomy created 24 hours ago. Which assessment finding indicates an unexpected complication requiring immediate intervention from the healthcare provider?

A) The stoma is beefy red and moist.
B) There is a small amount of blood-tinged drainage in the pouch.
C) The stoma is slightly swollen compared to its final expected size.
D) The stoma appears dark purple and dry.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<em>Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)</em><br>
A healthy stoma should be beefy red, moist, and slightly edematous in the immediate postoperative period. A dark purple, black, or dry stoma is an unexpected and urgent finding that indicates compromised blood flow (ischemia or necrosis) to the bowel, requiring immediate surgical evaluation.
</details>

---

### Question 349
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client with a newly applied fiberglass cast on the right arm. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of how to reduce the risk of complications?

A) "I can use a long plastic straw to scratch if my arm itches under the cast."
B) "I will elevate my right arm on pillows above my heart level."
C) "I should expect my fingers to feel cold and numb for the first few days."
D) "I will cover the cast completely with a heavy blanket tonight to keep it warm."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<em>Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)</em><br>
Elevating the casted extremity above the level of the heart promotes venous return, reduces swelling, and helps prevent compartment syndrome. It is unsafe to stick any object inside a cast to scratch, as this can break the skin and cause infection. Cold, numb fingers are a sign of compromised circulation and should be reported immediately, not expected. 
</details>

---

### Question 350
A nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving chemotherapy. The client's platelet count is 45,000/mm³ (45 x 10^9/L). Based on this basic lab finding, what is the primary risk for this client?

A) Risk for spontaneous bleeding.
B) Risk for severe opportunistic bacterial infections.
C) Risk for profound tissue hypoxia.
D) Risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<em>Lens: DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)</em><br>
The normal platelet count is 150,000 to 400,000/mm³ (150 to 400 x 10^9/L). A level of 45,000/mm³ indicates thrombocytopenia, which significantly impairs the blood's ability to clot. This places the client at a high risk for spontaneous bleeding. Infection risk is related to white blood cells, and hypoxia is related to red blood cells/hemoglobin.
</details>
### Question 351
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)**

The nurse is caring for several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for aspiration during meals?

A) A client with a right arm fracture in a cast.
B) A client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy.
C) A client who had a stroke with residual dysphagia.
D) A client with bilateral lower extremity edema.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Dysphagia (difficulty swallowing) frequently occurs after a stroke and significantly increases the client's risk of aspirating food or fluids into the lungs. The other conditions listed do not directly impair the swallowing mechanism or airway protection.
</details>

---

### Question 352
**Lens: CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)**

The nurse is preparing a client for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which client finding is an absolute contraindication for this procedure?

A) A permanent cardiac pacemaker
B) A history of mild claustrophobia
C) An allergy to iodine-based contrast dye
D) Titanium dental implants

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** A permanent cardiac pacemaker is traditionally a strict contraindication for an MRI due to the powerful magnetic field, which can disrupt, heat, or dislodge the device (unless it is explicitly documented as an MRI-conditional pacemaker). Claustrophobia can be managed with anti-anxiety medications. Iodine contrast is used for CT scans, not MRIs (which use gadolinium). Titanium is a non-ferromagnetic metal and is safe in an MRI.
</details>

---

### Question 353
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)**

A client returns to the unit following a lumbar puncture. Which finding should the nurse recognize as expected and not requiring immediate intervention?

A) Clear fluid leaking from the puncture site.
B) Numbness and tingling in the bilateral lower extremities.
C) A sudden increase in oral temperature to 102.1°F (38.9°C).
D) A mild headache when sitting upright.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** A mild to moderate headache is a common and expected finding after a lumbar puncture, often caused by minor cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) shifts. It typically worsens upon sitting up and improves when the client lies flat. Clear fluid leaking (CSF leak), numbness/tingling (nerve damage), and fever (infection) are unexpected complications that require immediate intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 354
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

The nurse is teaching a client how to safely use a standard aluminum walker. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should carry the walker up the stairs instead of using the handrail."
B) "I will place the walker flat on all four legs before taking a step."
C) "I will push myself up from the chair using the walker for leverage."
D) "I need to look straight down at my feet while I am walking."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** The walker must be placed flat on all four legs to ensure stability and support before the client steps forward. Using the walker to pull or push up from a chair can cause it to tip over; clients should use the armrests of the chair to stand. Clients should look forward, not down, to maintain balance, and standard walkers are generally not safe to use on stairs.
</details>

---

### Question 355
**Lens: DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)**

The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client prescribed oral furosemide. The client's serum potassium level is 2.8 mEq/L. What is the most appropriate action for the nurse to take?

A) Administer the scheduled dose of furosemide as ordered.
B) Encourage the client to drink more water with the medication.
C) Document the expected finding in the client's medical record.
D) Withhold the furosemide and notify the healthcare provider.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** A normal serum potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.8 mEq/L indicates severe hypokalemia. Furosemide is a loop diuretic that wastes potassium. Administering the medication would worsen the hypokalemia, placing the client at severe risk for lethal cardiac arrhythmias. The nurse must withhold the medication and notify the provider for further orders, such as a potassium supplement.
</details>
### Question 356
A nurse is reviewing the charts of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Based on the concept of risk recognition, which client is at the highest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A 30-year-old client with asthma using an inhaled bronchodilator.
B) A 45-year-old client recovering from an appendectomy who is frequently ambulating in the hall.
C) A 65-year-old client on strict bed rest following a severe pelvic fracture.
D) A 50-year-old client receiving intravenous antibiotics for community-acquired pneumonia.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** 
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
Strict bed rest (immobility) and a pelvic fracture (trauma/tissue injury) are two major risk factors in Virchow's triad, placing this client at the highest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The other clients are either mobile or do not have significant risk factors for venous stasis or endothelial injury.

</details>

---

### Question 357
The nurse is preparing a client for a scheduled magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) of the brain. Which finding in the client's medical history represents a contraindication for this procedure?

A) The client has an implanted cardiac pacemaker.
B) The client has a history of mild claustrophobia.
C) The client has a reported allergy to shellfish and iodine.
D) The client has a titanium hip replacement.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** 
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION
