An implanted cardiac pacemaker is a strict contraindication for an MRI. The powerful magnetic field of the MRI machine can interfere with the pacemaker's function, potentially causing it to malfunction or stop working, which is life-threatening. Claustrophobia can be managed with medication. Allergy to iodine is irrelevant because MRIs generally use gadolinium contrast, not iodine-based contrast. Titanium is non-ferromagnetic and is generally safe in an MRI.

</details>

---

### Question 358
A client is 12 hours postoperative following a total knee arthroplasty. The nurse empties the client's surgical drain. Which finding is expected at this time?

A) Purulent, foul-smelling drainage.
B) Bright red blood containing large, thick clots.
C) Clear, watery fluid without any red or pink tint.
D) Serosanguineous (pink-tinged) drainage.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** 
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
Serosanguineous drainage (a mixture of serum and red blood cells, appearing pink or pale red) is a normal, expected finding in a surgical drain 12 hours after a procedure. Purulent drainage indicates infection. Bright red blood with large clots suggests active hemorrhage. Clear, watery fluid is not typical for a joint replacement drain at this early stage. 

</details>

---

### Question 359
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly prescribed an antihypertensive medication that can cause orthostatic hypotension. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of how to reduce the risk of falls?

A) "I should stop taking the medication immediately if I feel lightheaded."
B) "I will sit on the edge of the bed for a few minutes before standing up."
C) "I should limit my daily fluid intake to prevent sudden drops in blood pressure."
D) "I will avoid eating leafy green vegetables while taking this medication."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** 
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
Sitting on the edge of the bed (dangling) for a few minutes before standing allows the cardiovascular system time to adjust to position changes, thereby reducing dizziness and the risk of falls from orthostatic hypotension. The client should not stop the medication abruptly without speaking to the provider, should maintain adequate hydration, and does not need to avoid leafy greens for this specific medication effect.

</details>

---

### Question 360
The nurse is reviewing morning laboratory results for a client receiving a daily dose of a loop diuretic (furosemide). The client's serum potassium level is 2.8 mEq/L (2.8 mmol/L). How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) The value is within the normal, expected range.
B) The client is experiencing hyperkalemia.
C) The client is experiencing hypokalemia.
D) The client is experiencing hyponatremia.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** 
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
The normal range for serum potassium is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A value of 2.8 mEq/L indicates hypokalemia (low potassium). Loop diuretics like furosemide are potassium-wasting diuretics, making hypokalemia a common and serious risk that the nurse must monitor and report.

</details>
### Question 361
A nurse is preparing to open a sterile dressing kit to perform wound care. What is the very first procedural step the nurse should take when opening the sterile package to reduce the risk of contamination?

A) Open the outermost flap away from the body.
B) Put on the sterile gloves provided inside the kit.
C) Pour the prescribed cleansing solution over the sterile supplies.
D) Open the side flaps pulling towards the body.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP  
**Rationale:** To maintain a sterile field and reduce the risk of infection, the first step when opening a sterile package is to open the outermost flap away from the body. This prevents the nurse from having to reach their unsterile arm across the sterile field when opening the remaining flaps.
</details>

---

### Question 362
A client returned from an uncomplicated appendectomy 24 hours ago and is preparing for discharge. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) to assist in reducing the risk of complications?

A) Assess the surgical incision for signs of erythema or drainage.
B) Instruct the client on how to splint the abdomen when coughing.
C) Empty and record the volume of the client's urinary drainage bag.
D) Determine if the client needs an additional dose of PRN pain medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** DELEGATION  
**Rationale:** Emptying and recording the output from a urinary drainage bag is a routine, non-invasive task that is safe and appropriate to delegate to a UAP. Assessment, client teaching, and evaluation of medication needs fall strictly within the scope of practice of the Registered Nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 363
A nurse is caring for a client who had a right femoral cardiac catheterization two hours ago. Which assessment finding indicates an unexpected complication requiring immediate intervention to reduce the risk of permanent injury?

A) The client complains of mild tenderness around the puncture site.
B) The right pedal pulse is absent and the foot is cold.
C) A small, dime-sized spot of dried blood is visible on the pressure dressing.
D) The client's blood pressure is 118/74 mmHg and heart rate is 78 bpm.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED  
**Rationale:** An absent pedal pulse and a cold extremity are unexpected findings indicating a loss of arterial blood flow to the limb. This is a severe complication (such as an arterial occlusion or expanding hematoma) following a femoral catheterization and requires immediate intervention. The other findings are normal and expected after this procedure.
</details>

---

### Question 364
The nurse is reviewing the status of four assigned clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for aspiration?

A) A client receiving continuous enteral feedings with the head of the bed elevated to 45 degrees.
B) A client who takes oral medications with thickened liquids due to mild dysphagia.
C) A client receiving continuous enteral feedings who has a gastric residual volume of 40 mL.
D) A client receiving continuous enteral feedings who is lying completely flat in bed while resting.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION  
**Rationale:** Clients receiving continuous enteral feedings must have the head of the bed elevated to at least 30 to 45 degrees at all times to prevent regurgitation and aspiration. Lying completely flat places the client at a high risk for aspirating stomach contents into the lungs.
</details>

---

### Question 365
A client is receiving a daily oral dose of furosemide, a loop diuretic, for heart failure. The nurse reviews the client's morning laboratory results and notes a serum potassium level of 2.9 mEq/L. What does this basic lab value indicate?

A) The client has normal potassium levels.
B) The client is experiencing hyperkalemia.
C) The client is experiencing hypokalemia.
D) The client has an elevated sodium level.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION  
**Rationale:** The normal serum potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.9 mEq/L is below the normal range, indicating hypokalemia. This is a common and potentially dangerous complication of loop diuretics like furosemide, as potassium is excreted in the urine along with fluid.
</details>
### Question 366
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
The nurse is reviewing the census on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for developing a pressure injury?

A) A 25-year-old who is ambulatory and recovering from an appendectomy.
B) A 40-year-old with an asthma exacerbation who uses a bedside commode.
C) A 75-year-old who is bedbound and incontinent of urine.
D) A 60-year-old with hypertension who walks in the hallway twice a day.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** The 75-year-old client has multiple significant risk factors for pressure injuries, including advanced age, immobility (bedbound), and excessive moisture from urinary incontinence. The other clients are either ambulatory or independently able to use a commode, drastically lowering their risk for skin breakdown.
</details>

---

### Question 367
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION
A nurse is completing a safety checklist for a client scheduled for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan. Which finding is a strict contraindication for this procedure?

A) The client has an implanted cardiac pacemaker.
B) The client reports an allergy to iodine contrast.
C) The client has a history of mild claustrophobia.
D) The client has a titanium hip replacement.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** An implanted cardiac pacemaker is generally an absolute contraindication for an MRI because the strong magnetic field can disrupt the pacemaker's function, potentially causing a life-threatening arrhythmia. Iodine is not used for MRI contrast (gadolinium is used). Mild claustrophobia may require sedation but is not an absolute contraindication. Titanium is non-ferromagnetic and is considered safe for MRIs.
</details>

---

### Question 368
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
A client has just returned to the nursing unit following a routine colonoscopy. Which assessment finding is considered normal and expected after this procedure?

A) A rigid, board-like abdomen
B) A new-onset fever of 101.5°F (38.6°C)
C) Large amounts of bright red blood from the rectum
D) Passing flatus and feeling mild abdominal cramping

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Passing flatus and experiencing mild abdominal cramping are expected findings after a colonoscopy because air is pumped into the colon to expand it during the procedure. A rigid abdomen, high fever, or large amounts of bleeding are unexpected and indicate serious complications such as bowel perforation or hemorrhage.
</details>

---

### Question 369
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
The nurse is teaching a postoperative client how to use an incentive spirometer to prevent respiratory complications. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will blow into the mouthpiece as hard as I can."
B) "I will inhale deeply and hold my breath for a few seconds."
C) "I should only use this device when I feel short of breath."
D) "I will use this device once a day right before going to bed."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** The correct way to use an incentive spirometer is to seal the lips around the mouthpiece, inhale slowly and deeply, and hold the breath for 3 to 5 seconds to allow for maximum lung expansion. The client should inhale, not exhale (blow), into the device. It should be used frequently (e.g., 10 times an hour while awake), not just once a day or only when short of breath.
</details>

---

### Question 370
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
The nurse is reviewing morning laboratory results and notes that a client has a platelet count of 30,000/mm³ (30 x 10^9/L). Based on this basic lab value, what is the nurse's primary concern for this client?

A) High risk for spontaneous bleeding
B) High risk for a systemic infection
C) Severe tissue hypoxia
D) Significant fluid volume deficit

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** The normal platelet count ranges from 150,000 to 400,000/mm³. A count of 30,000/mm³ indicates severe thrombocytopenia, placing the client at a critically high risk for bruising and spontaneous bleeding. White blood cells are linked to infection risk, red blood cells/hemoglobin are linked to hypoxia, and hematocrit/BUN/sodium help evaluate fluid volume.
</details>
### Question 371
A nurse is assessing several clients for fall risk on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for experiencing a fall?

A) A 45-year-old receiving intravenous antibiotics for a skin infection.
B) A 78-year-old taking a loop diuretic who is frequently incontinent of urine.
C) A 60-year-old who uses a continuous positive airway pressure (CPAP) machine at night.
D) A 50-year-old recovering from an uncomplicated appendectomy with stable vitals.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) A 78-year-old taking a loop diuretic who is frequently incontinent of urine.

**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential

**Reasoning:** Advanced age, the use of diuretics (which increase urinary frequency and urgency), and incontinence are major risk factors for falls. Clients often rush to the bathroom to avoid accidents, increasing the likelihood of tripping or slipping. The other clients do not present with specific, high-risk fall indicators in their descriptions.
</details>

---

### Question 372
The nurse is preparing to measure a client's tympanic temperature. What is the correct physical technique for positioning the ear canal in an adult client?

A) Pull the pinna up and back.
B) Pull the pinna straight back.
C) Pull the pinna down and back.
D) Leave the pinna in its natural resting position.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Pull the pinna up and back.

**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential

**Reasoning:** For an adult or a child over the age of 3, the pinna must be pulled up and back to properly straighten the ear canal. This ensures an accurate tympanic temperature reading and reduces the risk of trauma from the thermometer probe. Pulling down and back is the correct technique for children under 3 years of age.
</details>

---

### Question 373
A nurse is assessing a client who had a right total knee arthroplasty 24 hours ago. Which finding is considered unexpected and requires immediate intervention to reduce the risk of complications?

A) The client reports a pain level of 4 on a 0-to-10 scale.
B) There is a small amount of serosanguineous drainage on the surgical dressing.
C) The right foot is cool to the touch and lacks a palpable pedal pulse.
D) The client exhibits mild, localized swelling around the right knee joint.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The right foot is cool to the touch and lacks a palpable pedal pulse.

**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential

**Reasoning:** A cool extremity lacking a palpable pulse is an unexpected and abnormal finding indicating potential impaired tissue perfusion or vascular compromise (such as a clot or compartment syndrome). This requires immediate intervention to prevent permanent tissue damage. Mild swelling, moderate pain, and serosanguineous drainage are all expected findings 24 hours postoperatively.
</details>

---

### Question 374
The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving a daily dose of furosemide. The client's serum potassium level is 3.0 mEq/L (3.0 mmol/L). What does this basic laboratory value indicate?

A) The client's potassium level is within the normal expected range.
B) The client has hyperkalemia and is at risk for arrhythmias.
C) The client has hyponatremia and is at risk for seizures.
D) The client has hypokalemia and is at risk for muscle weakness and arrhythmias.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) The client has hypokalemia and is at risk for muscle weakness and arrhythmias.

**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential

**Reasoning:** The normal serum potassium range is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L (mmol/L). A level of 3.0 mEq/L indicates hypokalemia. Furosemide is a potassium-wasting loop diuretic, which places the client at risk for hypokalemia. Low potassium can lead to serious complications such as dangerous cardiac arrhythmias and muscle weakness.
</details>

---

### Question 375
A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a client with diabetes mellitus who requires blood glucose monitoring before meals. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Interpret the blood glucose results to determine the client's insulin dose.
B) Assess the client's fingertips for signs of impaired circulation or bruising.
C) Perform the routine capillary blood glucose test using a glucometer.
D) Teach the client how to properly perform self-monitoring of blood glucose at home.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Perform the routine capillary blood glucose test using a glucometer.

**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential

**Reasoning:** Performing a routine capillary blood glucose test is a standard, repeatable task with a predictable outcome that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing circulation, teaching the client, and interpreting results for medication administration are strict nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>
### Question 376
A nurse is preparing to insert a peripheral intravenous (IV) catheter for a client. What is the very first physical step the nurse should take to reduce the risk of infection after gathering supplies?

A. Apply the tourniquet above the selected site.  
B. Perform hand hygiene.  
C. Clean the insertion site with an antiseptic swab.  
D. Prime the IV extension tubing.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP  
**Rationale:** Performing hand hygiene is the first and most fundamental physical step before initiating any invasive procedure, including IV insertion. This significantly reduces the risk of introducing pathogens. Applying the tourniquet, cleaning the site, and priming the tubing all happen after hand hygiene.
</details>

---

### Question 377
A nurse is assessing a client who had a diagnostic lumbar puncture 2 hours ago. Which finding is considered an expected outcome and not an immediate safety risk?

A. Sudden difficulty voiding.  
B. Clear fluid leaking from the puncture site.  
C. A mild headache when sitting upright.  
D. Numbness and tingling in the lower extremities.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED  
**Rationale:** A mild to moderate headache, often called a "spinal headache," is a common and expected complication following a lumbar puncture, especially when the client sits upright. It is managed with fluids and lying flat. Numbness, clear fluid leakage (CSF leak), and sudden inability to void are unexpected findings that indicate potential nerve damage or a severe CSF leak, requiring immediate intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 378
A nurse is reviewing orders for a client scheduled for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which client finding is a strict contraindication for this procedure?

A. The client has an implanted cardiac pacemaker.  
B. The client has an allergy to iodine-based contrast dye.  
C. The client ate a heavy meal one hour ago.  
D. The client has a history of type 2 diabetes.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION  
**Rationale:** An MRI uses a powerful magnetic field that can disrupt the function of an implanted cardiac pacemaker, making it a strict contraindication. Iodine-based contrast is used for CT scans, not MRIs (MRIs use gadolinium, if contrast is needed). Eating a meal and having diabetes are not contraindications for a standard brain MRI.
</details>

---

### Question 379
A client receiving continuous heparin therapy for a deep vein thrombosis has a drawn partial thromboplastin time (aPTT) result of 60 seconds (control is 30 seconds). How should the nurse interpret this result?

A. The result is critically high and the heparin must be stopped immediately.  
B. The result indicates the heparin dose is subtherapeutic.  
C. The result is dangerously low and indicates an active clot.  
D. The result is within the expected therapeutic range.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION  
**Rationale:** The therapeutic goal for continuous heparin therapy is an aPTT of 1.5 to 2.5 times the normal control value. If the control is 30 seconds, the therapeutic range is 45 to 75 seconds. Therefore, an aPTT of 60 seconds is within the expected therapeutic range and indicates the heparin dose is effective.
</details>

---

### Question 380
A nurse is teaching a client about postoperative care following a cataract extraction. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of how to reduce the risk of increased intraocular pressure?

A. "I should sleep on the same side as my operated eye."  
B. "I will bend at my knees instead of my waist to pick things up."  
C. "I can lift objects up to 25 pounds as long as I do it slowly."  
D. "I should bear down forcefully when having a bowel movement."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING  
**Rationale:** Bending at the waist increases intraocular pressure (IOP) and should be avoided after cataract surgery; bending at the knees is the correct alternative. The client should avoid sleeping on the operative side, avoid lifting objects over 10 pounds, and avoid straining or bearing down during bowel movements, as all of these activities increase IOP.
</details>
### Question 381
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)
The nurse is reviewing the assignment of four clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for developing orthostatic hypotension?

A. A client receiving continuous enteral tube feedings through a gastrostomy tube.
B. A client who has been on strict bed rest for the past three days.
C. A client who ambulates independently to the bathroom multiple times a day.
D. A client with a mild fever who is eating a regular diet.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
Prolonged bed rest causes cardiovascular deconditioning and pooling of blood in the lower extremities, significantly increasing the client's risk for developing orthostatic hypotension when they attempt to stand. The other clients are maintaining regular fluid intake or mobility, which does not inherently pose a high risk for orthostatic hypotension.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Think about the physiological effects of immobility. Bed rest directly impacts blood pressure regulation upon standing, leading to a risk for falls.
</details>

---

### Question 382
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)
The nurse is preparing a client for a scheduled magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which finding in the client's medical history is an absolute contraindication for this diagnostic procedure?

A. Mild claustrophobia
B. Allergy to shellfish
C. Implanted cardiac pacemaker
D. History of migraine headaches

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
An MRI uses exceptionally strong magnetic fields that can disrupt the function of or physically dislodge implanted ferromagnetic metallic devices, such as a cardiac pacemaker. This makes it unsafe and an absolute contraindication. Claustrophobia may require sedation but is not an absolute contraindication. Shellfish allergy is related to iodine contrast (CT scans), not the gadolinium typically used in MRIs.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Associate "MRI" with "magnets." Look for the option containing metal or an electronic device. 
</details>

---

### Question 383
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)
A client is 12 hours post-operative following an uncomplicated open appendectomy. Which assessment finding should the nurse identify as an expected outcome at this time?

A. Hypoactive bowel sounds in all four quadrants
B. Purulent yellow drainage from the incision line
C. A sudden, sharp increase in severe abdominal pain
D. An axillary temperature of 102.5°F (39.2°C)

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
Hypoactive bowel sounds are an expected finding 12 hours after abdominal surgery due to the lingering effects of general anesthesia and bowel manipulation during the procedure. Purulent drainage and high fever indicate a potential infection. A sudden, sharp increase in pain is an unexpected finding that could indicate a complication like a leak or hemorrhage.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Differentiate between expected postoperative recovery signs and signs of complications (infection, hemorrhage). Sluggish bowels are normal right after abdominal surgery.
</details>

---

### Question 384
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
The nurse is teaching a client how to collect a clean-catch midstream urine specimen. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the instructions?

A. "I will pee directly into the cup as soon as I start."
B. "I need to collect all of my urine for the next 24 hours."
C. "I will collect the urine at the very end of my stream."
D. "I will start urinating in the toilet, then catch the urine in the cup."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
For a clean-catch midstream urine specimen, the client must first cleanse the urethral area, initiate voiding into the toilet to flush the urethra, and then catch the "midstream" portion of the urine in the sterile cup. This reduces the risk of contaminating the specimen with normal flora from the skin. 

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Focus on the term "midstream." The client must catch the urine in the *middle* of voiding.
</details>

---

### Question 385
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)
The nurse is reviewing morning laboratory results for a client and notes a serum potassium level of 2.8 mEq/L. The nurse interprets this finding as indicative of which condition?

A. Hyponatremia
B. Hypokalemia
C. Hyperkalemia
D. Hypernatremia

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
The normal serum potassium range is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.8 mEq/L is below the normal range, which is defined as hypokalemia. Hyponatremia and hypernatremia refer to abnormal sodium levels, not potassium. 

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Memorize basic lab values. Potassium (K+) normal range is 3.5 - 5.0 mEq/L. "Hypo-" means low, and "-kalemia" refers to potassium.
</details>
### Question 386
The nurse is reviewing the charts of four clients. Which client is at the highest risk for developing skin breakdown?

A. A client who is incontinent of stool and has limited mobility.
B. A client who independently walks to the bathroom every 2 hours.
C. A client who is eating 100% of their meals and drinks plenty of fluids.
D. A client who has an alert and oriented mental status.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION

**Rationale:** Incontinence of stool exposes the skin to excessive moisture and irritating enzymes, while limited mobility prevents the client from shifting their weight to relieve pressure. Combined, these factors place the client at the highest risk for skin breakdown and pressure injuries. The other options describe factors that promote skin integrity (mobility, good nutrition, and alertness).
</details>

---

### Question 387
The nurse is preparing a client for a computed tomography (CT) scan with intravenous (IV) contrast dye. Which finding in the client's medical record is a contraindication that makes the procedure unsafe?

A. A reported allergy to penicillin antibiotics.
B. A history of a severe allergic reaction to iodine.
C. A recent diagnosis of mild osteoarthritis.
D. A reported allergy to latex products.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION

**Rationale:** A severe allergy to iodine is a significant contraindication for receiving traditional IV contrast dye, as it drastically increases the risk of a life-threatening anaphylactic reaction. The provider must be notified so alternative imaging or premedication can be ordered. Penicillin, latex allergies, and osteoarthritis do not contraindicate the use of IV contrast dye.
</details>

---

### Question 388
A client returned from a colonoscopy 2 hours ago. Which assessment finding should the nurse identify as an expected finding that indicates a normal recovery?

A. A firm, board-like abdomen.
B. Bright red rectal bleeding.
C. Passing flatus (gas).
D. A sudden drop in blood pressure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED

**Rationale:** Passing flatus is a normal and expected finding after a colonoscopy because room air or carbon dioxide is pumped into the colon during the procedure to allow the provider to see the bowel walls. A firm abdomen, bright red bleeding, and a drop in blood pressure are unexpected findings that indicate potential complications such as a bowel perforation or hemorrhage.
</details>

---

### Question 389
The nurse is teaching a postoperative client how to use an incentive spirometer to reduce the risk of respiratory complications. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "I will blow into the mouthpiece as hard as I can."
B. "I should use the device only when I feel short of breath."
C. "I should rapidly pump my breath in and out of the device."
D. "I will inhale deeply and slowly through the mouthpiece."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING

**Rationale:** The correct use of an incentive spirometer involves taking a slow, deep inhalation to fully expand the lungs and prevent atelectasis (collapsed alveoli). Blowing into the device, using it only when short of breath, or taking rapid breaths are incorrect techniques that will not effectively expand the lungs.
</details>

---

### Question 390
The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client who is receiving a continuous heparin infusion. Which laboratory value must the nurse monitor to evaluate the medication's therapeutic effect?

A. Blood urea nitrogen (BUN)
B. Activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT)
C. White blood cell (WBC) count
D. Fasting blood glucose

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION

**Rationale:** The activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT) is the specific laboratory test used to monitor the therapeutic effect of a continuous heparin infusion. It measures how long it takes the blood to clot. BUN evaluates kidney function, the WBC count monitors for infection, and fasting blood glucose measures blood sugar levels; none of these evaluate heparin effectiveness.
</details>
### Question 391
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

A nurse is reviewing the medical records of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for developing fluid volume overload?

A. A client receiving a continuous infusion of 0.9% sodium chloride at 50 mL/hr.  
B. A client with severe heart failure receiving 0.45% sodium chloride at 125 mL/hr.  
C. A client with viral gastroenteritis who is drinking oral rehydration solutions.  
D. A client with a high fever who is sweating profusely.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** B. A client with severe heart failure receiving 0.45% sodium chloride at 125 mL/hr.

**Rationale:** 
- **B is correct:** Heart failure significantly impairs the heart's ability to pump effectively, placing the client at a high risk for fluid volume overload, especially when receiving intravenous fluids at a high rate (125 mL/hr). 
- **A is incorrect:** An infusion of 50 mL/hr is a low "keep vein open" rate and is unlikely to cause fluid overload in a client without cardiac or renal impairment. 
- **C is incorrect:** A client with gastroenteritis is losing fluid through vomiting or diarrhea and needs rehydration; they are at risk for dehydration, not overload. 
- **D is incorrect:** Profuse sweating and fever cause insensible fluid loss, placing this client at risk for fluid volume deficit (dehydration), not overload.
</details>

---

### Question 392
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)

The nurse is preparing a client for a Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which finding in the client's medical history is an absolute contraindication for this procedure?

A. A reported allergy to iodine-based contrast dye.  
B. A history of claustrophobia and anxiety.  
C. The presence of an implanted cardiac pacemaker.  
D. The presence of titanium dental implants.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C. The presence of an implanted cardiac pacemaker.

**Rationale:** 
- **C is correct:** An MRI uses powerful magnets that can severely disrupt or dislodge metal objects containing iron and electronic devices. An implanted cardiac pacemaker is a strict contraindication because the magnet can deactivate the device or alter its programming, causing life-threatening arrhythmias.
- **A is incorrect:** MRIs generally use gadolinium-based contrast, not iodine-based contrast (which is used in CT scans). 
- **B is incorrect:** Claustrophobia is a relative contraindication. It makes the procedure difficult but can be managed safely with anti-anxiety medications or by using an open MRI.
- **D is incorrect:** Titanium is a non-ferromagnetic metal, meaning it is not attracted by the MRI magnet. Titanium dental implants are safe for MRIs.
</details>

---

### Question 393
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)

The nurse is assessing a client who had a thoracentesis performed one hour ago. Which finding is unexpected and should be reported to the healthcare provider immediately?

A. The client demonstrates asymmetrical chest expansion when breathing.  
B. The client reports mild, localized pain at the needle puncture site.  
C. A small amount of dried, dark blood is noted on the sterile dressing.  
D. The client is breathing comfortably at a rate of 16 breaths per minute.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** A. The client demonstrates asymmetrical chest expansion when breathing.

**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** Asymmetrical chest expansion is an unexpected finding that may indicate a pneumothorax (collapsed lung), a known complication of a thoracentesis caused by the needle accidentally puncturing the lung. This requires immediate medical intervention.
- **B is incorrect:** Mild discomfort at the puncture site is an expected and normal finding after a needle insertion.
- **C is incorrect:** A small amount of dried blood on the dressing is expected after a needle puncture and does not indicate active, severe bleeding.
- **D is incorrect:** A respiratory rate of 16 breaths per minute without distress is a normal and expected finding.
</details>

---

### Question 394
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

The nurse is providing instructions to a client scheduled for an electroencephalogram (EEG) the next morning. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the pre-procedure teaching?

A. "I will need to fast and drink only water for 8 hours before the test."  
B. "I am going to drink a strong cup of coffee right before I go in to stay awake."  
C. "I cannot take any of my routine daily blood pressure medications."  
D. "I will wash my hair tonight but avoid using any hair gel or spray."  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** D. "I will wash my hair tonight but avoid using any hair gel or spray."

**Rationale:** 
- **D is correct:** An EEG measures the electrical activity of the brain. The client must have clean hair free of oils, gels, and sprays so the electrodes can stick properly to the scalp and record accurate signals.
- **A is incorrect:** Fasting is contraindicated before an EEG because low blood sugar (hypoglycemia) can alter the brain's electrical activity and invalidate the test results.
- **B is incorrect:** Caffeine is a stimulant that alters brain waves. Clients should avoid caffeine for 12 to 24 hours before an EEG.
- **C is incorrect:** Clients should generally continue to take their routine medications unless specifically told otherwise by the healthcare provider. Blood pressure medications do not typically interfere with an EEG.
</details>

---

### Question 395
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)

The nurse reviews the morning laboratory results for a client receiving daily doses of furosemide, a loop diuretic. The client's serum potassium level is 2.8 mEq/L (2.8 mmol/L). How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A. The level is significantly low, indicating hypokalemia.  
B. The level is within the expected normal range.  
C. The level is slightly elevated, indicating mild hyperkalemia.  
D. The level is dangerously high, requiring immediate dialysis.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** A. The level is significantly low, indicating hypokalemia.

**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** The normal serum potassium range is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.8 mEq/L is significantly low (hypokalemia). Loop diuretics like furosemide cause the body to excrete excess potassium in the urine, making hypokalemia a common complication.
- **B is incorrect:** 2.8 mEq/L is below the normal range of 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L.
- **C is incorrect:** Hyperkalemia is an elevated potassium level (greater than 5.0 mEq/L), not a low level.
- **D is incorrect:** This value represents low potassium, not high potassium. Dialysis is a treatment for severe hyperkalemia or kidney failure, not hypokalemia.
</details>
### Question 396
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

The nurse is reviewing the dietary orders for a client recovering from an ischemic stroke who has right-sided weakness and dysphagia. 

Which dietary item would place this client at the greatest risk for aspiration?

A. Thickened apple juice  
B. Pureed sweet potatoes  
C. Thin chicken broth  
D. Soft scrambled eggs  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Thin chicken broth  
**Rationale:** Clients with dysphagia (difficulty swallowing) after a stroke are at a high risk for aspiration. Thin liquids, such as broth, water, or regular juice, require the most oral motor control and coordination to swallow safely, making them the highest risk for entering the airway. Thickened liquids and pureed or soft foods are much safer and easier to control.
</details>

---

### Question 397
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)

A nurse is preparing to administer an intermittent enteral tube feeding to a client with a nasogastric (NG) tube. 

What is the first procedural step the nurse must take to prevent complications before administering the feeding?

A. Verify the placement of the NG tube.  
B. Flush the tube with 30 mL of sterile water.  
C. Warm the feeding formula to room temperature.  
D. Elevate the head of the bed to 15 degrees.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Verify the placement of the NG tube.  
**Rationale:** The absolute first and most critical step before administering any substance through an NG tube is verifying its placement (e.g., checking aspirate pH or reviewing the most recent X-ray). Administering formula into an improperly placed tube can cause severe respiratory complications, including aspiration pneumonia. The head of the bed should be elevated to at least 30-45 degrees, not 15.
</details>

---

### Question 398
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)

The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving chemotherapy. The client's platelet count is reported as 45,000/mm³ (45 x 10^9/L). 

Based on this laboratory result, what is the most important risk the nurse should monitor for?

A. Sudden cardiac arrhythmias  
B. Opportunistic infections  
C. Deep vein thrombosis  
D. Spontaneous bleeding  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Spontaneous bleeding  
**Rationale:** A normal platelet count is typically between 150,000 and 400,000/mm³. A platelet count of 45,000/mm³ indicates thrombocytopenia. Platelets are essential for blood clotting; therefore, a significantly low count places the client at a high risk for bruising, petechiae, and spontaneous bleeding. The nurse must implement bleeding precautions to reduce risk potential.
</details>

---

### Question 399
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)

A client is resting in the postanesthesia care unit (PACU) two hours after an uncomplicated appendectomy. The nurse performs a routine postoperative assessment. 

Which assessment finding is unexpected and indicates a potential complication requiring intervention?

A. A tympanic temperature of 98.8°F (37.1°C)  
B. An oxygen saturation of 88% on room air  
C. Serosanguineous drainage on the surgical dressing  
D. Reports of incisional pain rated as a 4 on a 0-10 scale  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. An oxygen saturation of 88% on room air  
**Rationale:** An oxygen saturation of 88% indicates hypoxia, which is an unexpected and potentially dangerous finding that requires immediate intervention (such as deep breathing exercises or supplemental oxygen) to reduce the risk of respiratory failure. Mild incisional pain, a normal temperature, and a small amount of serosanguineous (pinkish) drainage are all expected findings in the immediate postoperative period.
</details>

---

### Question 400
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

The nurse is discharging a client who was recently prescribed a new antihypertensive medication. 

Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of how to reduce the risk of injury while taking this medication?

A. "I will restrict my daily fluid intake to prevent swelling in my legs."  
B. "I should double the dose if my blood pressure is high in the morning."  
C. "I will change positions slowly from lying to sitting to standing."  
D. "I need to stop taking the medication immediately if I feel dizzy."  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. "I will change positions slowly from lying to sitting to standing."  
**Rationale:** Antihypertensive medications can cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which makes the client dizzy and increases the risk for falls. Changing positions slowly gives the cardiovascular system time to adjust, safely reducing the risk of injury. Clients should never double doses or abruptly stop blood pressure medications without consulting their healthcare provider.
</details>
### Question 401
The nurse is caring for a client who is receiving continuous enteral nutrition via a nasogastric tube. The nurse walks into the room and finds the client lying completely flat in bed. Which of the following is the client at greatest risk for developing?

A) Pulmonary aspiration  
B) Intestinal obstruction  
C) Gastric ulceration  
D) Fluid volume deficit  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION  
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential  

Clients receiving continuous enteral feedings must have the head of their bed elevated to at least 30 to 45 degrees at all times to reduce the risk of regurgitation and pulmonary aspiration. Lying flat removes the aid of gravity, making it much easier for stomach contents to travel back up the esophagus and into the lungs. Intestinal obstruction, gastric ulceration, and fluid volume deficit are not direct risks caused by the supine position during enteral feeding. 
</details>

---

### Question 402
The nurse is reviewing the health history of a client scheduled for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which finding in the client's medical history is a strict contraindication to this procedure?

A) Allergy to iodine-based contrast dye  
B) A history of claustrophobia  
C) Presence of a cardiac pacemaker  
D) A titanium hip replacement  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION  
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential  

An MRI uses powerful magnets that can interfere with the function of electronic devices like cardiac pacemakers, potentially causing life-threatening arrhythmias or pacemaker failure. Therefore, a traditional pacemaker is a strict contraindication to an MRI. Iodine-based contrast is used in CT scans, not MRIs (which use gadolinium). Claustrophobia is a concern that can be managed with sedation, not an absolute contraindication. Titanium is non-ferromagnetic and generally safe for MRIs.
</details>

---

### Question 403
A client is recovering on the medical-surgical unit two hours after a thoracentesis. Which assessment finding should the nurse identify as an UNEXPECTED complication indicating a potential risk to the client?

A) Blood pressure of 118/76 mmHg  
B) Tracheal deviation to the unaffected side  
C) Symmetrical chest wall expansion  
D) A small amount of serosanguinous drainage on the dressing  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED  
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential  

Tracheal deviation is an unexpected, life-threatening finding that indicates a tension pneumothorax, a known complication of thoracentesis where air enters the pleural space and cannot escape, pushing mediastinal structures to the opposite side. Symmetrical chest expansion, a stable blood pressure, and a small amount of drainage at the puncture site are expected, normal findings after this procedure.
</details>

---

### Question 404
The nurse is teaching a postoperative client how to use an incentive spirometer to reduce the risk of respiratory complications. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will blow into the mouthpiece as hard and fast as I can."  
B) "I should use this device only when I feel short of breath."  
C) "I need to take quick, shallow breaths to keep the little ball at the top."  
D) "I will exhale completely, then inhale slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING  
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential  

To correctly use an incentive spirometer, the client should exhale completely, seal their lips around the mouthpiece, and inhale slowly and deeply to maximize lung expansion and prevent atelectasis. The device is not blown into (like a peak flow meter), it should be used regularly (e.g., 10 times an hour while awake) rather than only when symptomatic, and quick, shallow breaths defeat the purpose of deep alveolar expansion.
</details>

---

### Question 405
The nurse is reviewing the morning laboratory results for a client receiving daily doses of furosemide, a loop diuretic. The client's serum potassium level is 2.8 mEq/L. Based on this data, which complication is the client at greatest risk for developing?

A) Hyperactive deep tendon reflexes  
B) Fluid volume overload  
C) Cardiac dysrhythmias  
D) Acute renal failure  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION  
**Category:** Reduction of Risk Potential  

A normal serum potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.8 mEq/L indicates severe hypokalemia, a common side effect of potassium-wasting diuretics like furosemide. Hypokalemia places the client at high risk for dangerous, potentially fatal cardiac dysrhythmias. Hyperactive reflexes are more commonly associated with hypocalcemia or hypomagnesemia. Fluid volume overload is what the diuretic is treating, not a risk of the low potassium. Renal failure is not a direct result of hypokalemia.
</details>
### Question 406
A nurse is assessing several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which finding places a client at the highest risk for experiencing a fall?

A) The client is 55 years old.
B) The client wears reading glasses.
C) The client has a history of a fall 2 months ago.
D) The client is prescribed a daily multivitamin.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A previous history of falls is one of the strongest predictors of future falls. Recognizing this risk factor is essential for implementing appropriate fall prevention strategies (Reduction of Risk Potential). Age alone (especially 55) and wearing reading glasses are not primary risk factors without other deficits. A daily multivitamin does not increase fall risk.
</details>

---

### Question 407
A client is scheduled for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which finding in the client's medical history is an absolute contraindication for this procedure?

A) A cardiac pacemaker implanted 3 years ago
B) An allergy to iodine-based contrast dye
C) A history of mild claustrophobia
D) An amalgam (silver) dental filling

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CONTRAINDICATION<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> An MRI uses a powerful magnetic field, making the presence of older implantable metallic devices, such as cardiac pacemakers, an absolute contraindication due to the risk of device malfunction and injury. Claustrophobia is a relative contraindication that can be managed with anti-anxiety medications. Allergy to iodine is not relevant, as MRI uses gadolinium contrast if needed. Dental amalgam fillings are safe for MRI. 
</details>

---

### Question 408
A nurse is caring for a client who had a barium enema earlier in the day. Which client statement indicates an expected finding after this procedure?

A) "My heart feels like it is racing."
B) "I have not passed any gas since yesterday."
C) "My abdomen feels very hard and rigid."
D) "I noticed my stool is white and chalky."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> It is completely normal and expected for stools to appear white or chalky for 24 to 72 hours after a procedure involving barium. A rigid abdomen, lack of flatus, or racing heart rate are unexpected findings that could indicate a severe complication like bowel perforation, which requires immediate medical attention.
</details>

---

### Question 409
A nurse is teaching a postoperative client about the purpose and use of sequential compression devices (SCDs). Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should keep these on while I am walking in the hallway."
B) "I should wear these sleeves when I am resting in bed."
C) "The sleeves will massage my legs to prevent muscle loss."
D) "I should take these off if I feel my legs swelling."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> SCDs are used to promote venous return and prevent deep vein thrombosis (DVT) while the client is immobile. They should be worn while the client is resting in bed or sitting in a chair. They must be removed before walking to prevent a fall hazard. They do not prevent muscle loss, and leg swelling should be reported to the nurse rather than simply taking the devices off.
</details>

---

### Question 410
The nurse is reviewing the laboratory results of a client receiving a daily loop diuretic. The client's serum potassium level is 3.0 mEq/L. How should the nurse interpret this laboratory finding?

A) Normal finding
B) Hyperkalemia
C) Hypokalemia
D) Hyponatremia

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DATA INTERPRETATION<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The normal range for serum potassium is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 3.0 mEq/L is below the normal range, indicating hypokalemia. Loop diuretics (like furosemide) are known to deplete potassium, so monitoring this lab value is a crucial part of reducing risk potential.
</details>
### Question 411
A nurse is reviewing the charts of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Based on the documented data, which client is at the highest risk for developing skin breakdown?

A) A 25-year-old client who is ambulating independently after an appendectomy.
B) A 78-year-old client who is incontinent of urine and requires maximum assistance to move in bed.
C) A 40-year-old client who has a cast on the right forearm for a fractured radius.
D) A 60-year-old client who is on a clear liquid diet in preparation for a colonoscopy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Advanced age, severe immobility, and exposure to moisture from urinary incontinence are all major risk factors that significantly increase the likelihood of skin breakdown and pressure injuries. The other clients are highly mobile or lack these compounded risk factors.
</details>

---

### Question 412
A client is prescribed a heating pad to help relieve muscle spasms in the lower back. Which of the following physical findings would make the application of a heating pad unsafe?

A) Decreased sensation in the lower back
B) A history of chronic lower back pain
C) Mild muscle stiffness in the morning
D) Intact skin without redness or breakdown

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Decreased sensation is a strict contraindication for heat therapy. Because the client cannot adequately feel temperature changes, they are unable to recognize if the pad becomes too hot, placing them at an extremely high risk for severe burns.
</details>

---

### Question 413
A client is receiving a continuous intravenous heparin infusion for the treatment of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Which laboratory value should the nurse routinely monitor to ensure the medication is at a safe and therapeutic level?

A) Serum potassium
B) White blood cell (WBC) count
C) Activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT)
D) Prothrombin time (PT)

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT) is the specific laboratory test used to monitor the effectiveness and safety of a continuous heparin infusion. PT/INR is used to monitor warfarin therapy. 
</details>

---

### Question 414
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client who is recovering from abdominal surgery. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's surgical incision for signs of infection.
B) Instructing the client on how to use an incentive spirometer.
C) Evaluating the client's pain level after administering an analgesic.
D) Emptying the client's urinary catheter drainage bag and recording the output.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Emptying a urinary drainage bag and documenting the output is a standard, non-invasive procedure that does not require nursing judgment, making it completely safe to delegate to a UAP. Assessing, teaching, and evaluating are responsibilities that must be done by the RN.
</details>

---

### Question 415
A nurse is preparing to administer an intramuscular (IM) injection to an adult client. After verifying the medication, confirming the client's identity, and providing privacy, what is the first physical step the nurse should take when beginning the procedure?

A) Perform hand hygiene.
B) Cleanse the injection site with an alcohol swab.
C) Insert the needle at a 90-degree angle.
D) Aspirate for blood return.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The very first physical step before initiating contact with the client for any clinical procedure, including medication administration, is performing hand hygiene. This is essential for infection control and reducing the risk of transmitting pathogens.
</details>
### Question 416
A nurse is reviewing the medical records of four clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A) A 25-year-old client who jogs 3 miles a day and is admitted for minor arm trauma.
B) A 40-year-old client recovering from a mild, uncomplicated upper respiratory infection.
C) A 65-year-old client on strict bed rest following major pelvic surgery.
D) A 30-year-old client who drinks 2 liters of water daily and is preparing for discharge.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)<br>
<strong>Reduction of Risk Potential:</strong> Immobility (strict bed rest) and trauma to the pelvic or lower extremity vessels (pelvic surgery) are major risk factors for deep vein thrombosis (DVT) due to venous stasis and endothelial injury. The other clients are active and do not possess major risk factors for clot formation.
</details>

---

### Question 417
A nurse is preparing a client for a scheduled magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which finding in the client's health history is an absolute contraindication for this procedure?

A) The client has an implanted cardiac pacemaker.
B) The client is allergic to iodine-based contrast dye.
C) The client ate a heavy meal one hour ago.
D) The client has a history of claustrophobia.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)<br>
<strong>Reduction of Risk Potential:</strong> Implanted metallic or electronic devices, such as cardiac pacemakers, are absolute contraindications for an MRI because the machine's strong magnetic field can disrupt the device or cause internal thermal injury. Iodine allergy is related to CT scan contrast, not MRI. Claustrophobia is an issue but can be managed with anti-anxiety medications. Eating is not typically a contraindication unless specific contrast or sedation is used requiring NPO status.
</details>

---

### Question 418
A client is 12 hours postoperative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The nurse is emptying the client's Jackson-Pratt (JP) drain. Which type of drainage is an expected, stable finding at this time?

A) Thick, foul-smelling yellow pus
B) Bright red, free-flowing blood
C) Clear, watery fluid that tests positive for glucose
D) Thin, watery, pinkish-red fluid

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)<br>
<strong>Reduction of Risk Potential:</strong> Thin, watery, pinkish fluid (serosanguineous drainage) is a normal and expected finding 12 hours after surgery. Thick yellow pus indicates an infection. Bright red, free-flowing blood indicates an active hemorrhage. Clear fluid testing positive for glucose suggests a cerebrospinal fluid leak, which is unexpected and highly abnormal for abdominal surgery.
</details>

---

### Question 419
A nurse is teaching a client how to use a standard walker safely to reduce the risk of falls at home. Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching has been effective?

A) "I will place the walker a few steps ahead and then hop to catch up to it."
B) "I will move the walker forward about 6 inches, then step forward into the walker."
C) "I should use the walker to pull myself up from a sitting position to standing."
D) "I will carry the walker up the stairs when I need to go to the second floor."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)<br>
<strong>Reduction of Risk Potential:</strong> The correct and safe technique for using a walker is to move it forward a short distance (about 6 to 8 inches) and then step forward into the center of the walker. Pulling oneself up using a walker is unsafe as it can cause the walker to tip over. Standard walkers are not safe for use on stairs.
</details>

---

### Question 420
A nurse is reviewing a client's morning laboratory results. The basic metabolic panel shows a serum potassium level of 2.8 mEq/L. How should the nurse interpret this basic lab finding?

A) It is a normal potassium level requiring no intervention.
B) It indicates hyperkalemia, which puts the client at risk for cardiac dysrhythmias.
C) It indicates hypokalemia, which puts the client at risk for muscle weakness and cardiac dysrhythmias.
D) It indicates hyponatremia, which puts the client at risk for seizures.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)<br>
<strong>Reduction of Risk Potential:</strong> The normal serum potassium level is 3.5 to 5.0 mEq/L. A level of 2.8 mEq/L indicates hypokalemia (low potassium), which increases the client's risk for muscle weakness, cramping, and potentially life-threatening cardiac dysrhythmias.
</details>
### Question 421
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION 
**Topic:** Reduction of Risk Potential (Diagnostic Tests)

A client is scheduled for a routine magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which finding in the client’s medical history should the nurse identify as an absolute contraindication for this diagnostic procedure?

A) History of severe asthma
B) Presence of a cardiac pacemaker
C) Allergy to shellfish and iodine
D) Fear of enclosed spaces (claustrophobia)

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** 
- **B is correct:** An MRI uses a powerful magnetic field. The presence of metal-containing internal devices, such as a traditional cardiac pacemaker, aneurysm clips, or inner ear implants, is typically an absolute contraindication because the magnet can move the device or interfere with its function, causing life-threatening injury. 
- **A is incorrect:** A history of asthma is not a contraindication for an MRI. 
- **C is incorrect:** Shellfish or iodine allergies are relevant for tests using iodine-based contrast (like a CT scan), but standard MRIs do not use iodine contrast (they use gadolinium if contrast is needed). 
- **D is incorrect:** Claustrophobia is a concern that requires management (such as administering a mild sedative before the procedure or using an open MRI), but it is not an absolute physical contraindication.

</details>

---

### Question 422
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Topic:** Reduction of Risk Potential (Post-Procedure Assessment)

The nurse is caring for a client who underwent a barium enema earlier in the day. Which finding should the nurse identify as an expected result of this procedure?

A) Chalky white stools
B) Severe abdominal cramping
C) Absence of bowel sounds
D) Dark black, tarry stools

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** Following a barium enema (or barium swallow), it is completely normal and expected for the client's stool to appear chalky white or light-colored for 24 to 72 hours as the body eliminates the barium contrast.
- **B is incorrect:** While mild discomfort or bloating may occur, severe abdominal cramping is unexpected and could indicate a complication such as a bowel perforation or severe impaction.
- **C is incorrect:** The absence of bowel sounds is an abnormal finding that could indicate a paralytic ileus. 
- **D is incorrect:** Dark black, tarry stools (melena) indicate upper gastrointestinal bleeding and are not an expected finding after a barium enema.

</details>

---

### Question 423
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
**Topic:** Reduction of Risk Potential (Laboratory Values)

A client with a history of atrial fibrillation is prescribed oral warfarin to prevent blood clots. Which laboratory test should the nurse monitor to evaluate the therapeutic effectiveness of this medication?

A) Activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT)
B) Platelet count
C) Prothrombin time and International Normalized Ratio (PT/INR)
D) Erythrocyte sedimentation rate (ESR)

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** 
- **C is correct:** Warfarin is an oral anticoagulant. Its therapeutic effect is measured by monitoring the Prothrombin Time (PT) and the International Normalized Ratio (INR). A typical therapeutic INR range for a client on warfarin is 2.0 to 3.0.
- **A is incorrect:** The activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT) is used to monitor the effectiveness of continuous intravenous heparin therapy, not oral warfarin.
- **B is incorrect:** Platelet count is monitored to assess for thrombocytopenia, but it does not measure the therapeutic anticoagulant effect of warfarin.
- **D is incorrect:** The erythrocyte sedimentation rate (ESR) is a marker of inflammation in the body and has no relationship to anticoagulant therapy.

</details>

---

### Question 424
**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Topic:** Reduction of Risk Potential (Safe Delegation)

The nurse is caring for a client receiving continuous bladder irrigation (CBI) following a transurethral resection of the prostate (TURP). Which task can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Adjust the flow rate of the irrigation fluid.
B) Assess the client's abdomen for bladder spasms.
C) Evaluate the patency of the urinary catheter.
D) Empty the urinary drainage bag and record the output amount.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** 
- **D is correct:** Emptying the urinary drainage bag and accurately recording the output is a routine, standard procedure that falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. 
- **A is incorrect:** Adjusting the flow rate of the CBI requires clinical judgment based on the color and characteristics of the drainage. This is a nursing responsibility and cannot be delegated.
- **B is incorrect:** Assessing for bladder spasms requires nursing assessment skills and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
- **C is incorrect:** Evaluating catheter patency requires clinical judgment and nursing assessment, which are outside the UAP's scope of practice.

</details>

---

### Question 425
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Topic:** Reduction of Risk Potential (Infection Control/Procedures)

The nurse is preparing to perform a sterile dressing change for a client with a central venous catheter. What is the most appropriate first physical step the nurse should take to reduce the risk of infection?

A) Open the sterile dressing kit on a clean bedside table.
B) Don sterile gloves before touching the client.
C) Perform thorough hand hygiene.
D) Remove the old dressing using clean gloves.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** 
- **C is correct:** Performing thorough hand hygiene is always the first and most crucial physical step in any procedure to reduce the risk of infection. Handwashing removes transient flora before interacting with the client or gathering/handling supplies.
- **A is incorrect:** Opening the sterile kit is a later step, which must only be done *after* hand hygiene and preparing the client.
- **B is incorrect:** Donning sterile gloves happens later in the procedure, after hand hygiene, removal of the old dressing, and setting up the sterile field. 
- **D is incorrect:** Removing the old dressing is an early step, but it must be preceded by performing hand hygiene and donning clean gloves.

</details>

# SECTION: Safety and Infection Control

### Question 426
**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Scenario:** A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a client requiring airborne precautions due to active pulmonary tuberculosis. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the client's adherence to keeping the negative-pressure isolation door closed.
B) Restocking N95 respirator masks and gloves in the supply cart outside the client's room.
C) Showing the client how to cough into a tissue and dispose of it properly in the trash.
D) Educating the client's visiting family members about the reasons for airborne precautions.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Restocking isolation supplies is a routine, non-clinical task that does not require nursing judgment or assessment, making it entirely safe and appropriate for a UAP. Education (options C and D) and evaluation of adherence (option A) are strictly the responsibility of the registered nurse and cannot be delegated.

</details>

---

### Question 427
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Scenario:** The nurse is preparing to remove personal protective equipment (PPE) after caring for a client on contact precautions for Methicillin-resistant *Staphylococcus aureus* (MRSA). Which physical step should the nurse perform first?

A) Remove the gown by pulling it away from the neck and shoulders.
B) Perform hand hygiene using an alcohol-based rub or soap and water.
C) Untie the mask and pull it away from the face holding only the strings.
D) Grasp the outside of one glove with the opposite gloved hand and peel it off.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** According to standard CDC guidelines for PPE removal, gloves are considered the most contaminated piece of equipment. The first physical step is to remove the gloves to prevent cross-contamination to the skin, hair, or clothes while removing the rest of the PPE. Hand hygiene is the final step in the process.

</details>

---

### Question 428
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Scenario:** A nurse is discharging an older adult client with a new prescription for a cane. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of basic home safety to prevent falls?

A) "I will place the cane on my weaker side when I am walking around the house."
B) "I should wear my slip-on backless slippers around the house for easy walking."
C) "I need to make sure my stairways are well-lit and completely free of clutter."
D) "I will keep decorative throw rugs in the hallway so I don't slip on the wood."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Ensuring that stairways and walkways are well-lit and free of clutter is a fundamental home safety measure to prevent falls. A cane should be held on the strong (unaffected) side, not the weaker side. Backless slippers and unsecured throw rugs are major tripping hazards and should be avoided.

</details>

---

### Question 429
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Scenario:** The nurse is assessing a client's peripheral intravenous (IV) insertion site after 48 hours of continuous fluid therapy. Which finding at the insertion site is an expected, normal finding?

A) The transparent dressing is intact, and the skin is its normal color.
B) The client reports a burning sensation when the fluids are running.
C) The skin immediately around the IV catheter is warm and red.
D) The area surrounding the insertion site appears swollen and pale.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** An intact transparent dressing with normal skin color indicates a healthy IV site free of complications. Redness and warmth indicate phlebitis or infection. Swelling and paleness indicate infiltration. A burning sensation is unexpected and warrants stopping the infusion and assessing for complications.

</details>

---

### Question 430
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
**Scenario:** A nurse walks past a computer terminal at the nurses' station and notices that another staff member has walked away while logged into a client's electronic health record (EHR). What is the safest and most appropriate action for the nurse to take?

A) Leave the computer as is, assuming the staff member just stepped away for a moment.
B) Log out of the system immediately to protect the client's confidentiality.
C) Look around the station to see if the staff member is returning soon.
D) Write a note in the client's chart stating the terminal was left unattended.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Under HIPAA regulations, protecting client health information is a primary ethical and legal duty. Logging off an unattended computer prevents unauthorized individuals from accessing protected health information (PHI). Leaving it open, even for a moment, violates confidentiality and safety protocols.

</details>
### Question 431
A nurse is reviewing the census on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for a fall?

A) A 45-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic cholecystectomy.
B) A 65-year-old client with a history of osteoporosis.
C) A 72-year-old client who recently received an IV dose of morphine.
D) A 50-year-old client with hypertension taking a daily beta-blocker.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION

**Rationale:** Intravenous narcotics, such as morphine, alter a client's sensorium, causing central nervous system depression, drowsiness, and dizziness. This significantly increases the risk of a fall. While osteoporosis (Option B) increases the risk of severe injury (like a fracture) *if* a fall occurs, it does not inherently cause falls. Options A and D have a lower immediate fall risk compared to a client receiving IV opioids.
</details>

---

### Question 432
A nurse is applying bilateral soft wrist restraints to a client who is agitated and attempting to pull out their nasogastric tube. Which action by the nurse is unsafe and contraindicated?

A) Securing the restraint ties directly to the bed's side rails.
B) Tying the restraints using a quick-release knot.
C) Securing the restraint ties to the movable part of the bed frame.
D) Ensuring two fingers can comfortably fit under the secured restraint.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION

**Rationale:** Restraints must *never* be secured to the side rails of a bed. If the side rail is lowered, it will pull the client's limb and could cause severe injury or dislocation. Restraints should always be secured to the movable portion of the bed frame (Option C) using a quick-release knot (Option B) to ensure they move with the client and can be removed rapidly in an emergency. Ensuring two fingers fit under the restraint (Option D) ensures adequate circulation and is a correct safety practice.
</details>

---

### Question 433
The nurse is teaching a client's family about infection control measures for a client diagnosed with *Clostridioides difficile* (C. diff). Which statement by a family member indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I only need to wear a mask when I am inside the room."
B) "I will use an alcohol-based hand rub before leaving the room."
C) "I do not need to wear gloves unless I am directly touching the patient."
D) "I must wash my hands with soap and water after visiting."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING

**Rationale:** *Clostridioides difficile* (C. diff) requires strict contact precautions, and hand hygiene must be performed with soap and water. Alcohol-based hand sanitizers are ineffective at killing the hardy spores produced by C. diff (Option B). Visitors should don gowns and gloves upon entering the room, regardless of whether they plan to physically touch the client, because spores can live on environmental surfaces (Option C). A mask is not standard for contact precautions unless there is a risk of splashing (Option A).
</details>

---

### Question 434
A client with influenza is on droplet precautions. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's lung sounds and respiratory rate.
B) Refilling the client's water pitcher while wearing a surgical mask.
C) Educating the client on how to appropriately cover their cough.
D) Administering an oral over-the-counter cough syrup.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** DELEGATION

**Rationale:** Refilling a water pitcher is a basic, routine care task that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. The UAP must follow isolation precautions, which means wearing a surgical mask when entering a droplet precaution room. Assessing the client (Option A), providing education (Option C), and administering medications (Option D) are strictly nursing responsibilities and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 435
A nurse discovers a small fire in a trash can in a client's room. According to the RACE acronym for fire safety, what is the nurse's *first* action?

A) Rescue the client and move them to a safe area.
B) Activate the nearest fire alarm system.
C) Close the doors and windows to confine the fire.
D) Extinguish the fire using a designated fire extinguisher.

<details>
