<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP

**Rationale:** The RACE acronym dictates the order of actions during a fire: **R**escue anyone in immediate danger, **A**larm (activate the fire alarm/call for help), **C**onfine the fire (close doors and windows), and **E**xtinguish the fire if it is safe to do so. Therefore, the very first physical step is to rescue the client and get them to safety.
</details>
### Question 436
A nurse is reviewing the census of a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for sustaining a fall?

A) A 45-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy.
B) A 30-year-old client receiving intravenous antibiotics for pneumonia.
C) A 78-year-old client with nocturia and newly prescribed zolpidem.
D) A 55-year-old client with a fiberglass cast on the left arm.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
**Client Need:** Safety and Infection Control

Advanced age, urinary frequency at night (nocturia), and the use of sedative-hypnotic medications (such as zolpidem) are all major risk factors for falls. A client waking up in the dark to use the restroom while under the influence of a sleep aid is at a very high risk for tripping or losing balance. The other clients do not possess as many compounding fall risk factors.
</details>

---

### Question 437
A client is scheduled for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which finding in the client's medical history is an absolute safety contraindication for this procedure?

A) A titanium hip replacement placed 5 years ago.
B) A documented history of severe claustrophobia.
C) A severe allergic reaction to iodine contrast dye.
D) An implanted traditional cardiac pacemaker.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION
**Client Need:** Safety and Infection Control

An MRI utilizes a massive, powerful magnetic field. Devices containing ferromagnetic metals or complex electronics, such as a traditional implanted cardiac pacemaker, are contraindicated because the magnet can severely disrupt the device's function, heat the metal, or dislodge it, causing fatal injury to the client. Titanium (Option A) is non-ferromagnetic and generally safe. Claustrophobia (Option B) can be managed with sedatives and is a relative, not absolute, contraindication. MRI uses gadolinium, not iodine contrast (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 438
A nurse is teaching a client about basic infection control measures to use at home. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I only need to wash my hands after using the bathroom."
B) "I should wash my hands with soap and water for at least 20 seconds."
C) "Hand sanitizer is much better than soap and water when my hands are visibly dirty."
D) "I should dry my hands by wiping them on my clothes to avoid touching dirty towels."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Client Need:** Safety and Infection Control

Proper hand washing requires vigorous scrubbing with soap and water for at least 20 seconds to effectively mechanically remove and destroy pathogens. Hand sanitizer is ineffective if hands are visibly soiled (Option C). Hands should be washed frequently throughout the day, not just after using the bathroom (Option A). Hands should be dried on a clean towel or paper towel, never on clothing, which harbors bacteria (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 439
A client is placed on Contact Precautions for a methicillin-resistant *Staphylococcus aureus* (MRSA) infection in a leg wound. Which task can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Providing a routine daily bed bath for the client.
B) Teaching the client's family about how to properly wear an isolation gown.
C) Assessing the client's leg wound for signs of granulation and healing.
D) Determining if the client still needs to remain on Contact Precautions.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Client Need:** Safety and Infection Control

The UAP can safely perform basic hygiene tasks, such as bathing, for clients on isolation precautions, provided they are instructed on standard and specific isolation requirements (e.g., wearing a gown and gloves). Assessing wounds, teaching family members, and evaluating the need for precautions are all tasks that require the nursing process and critical thinking; therefore, they must be performed by the registered nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 440
A nurse is preparing to exit the room of a client who is on Contact and Droplet Precautions. The nurse is wearing a gown, gloves, a surgical mask, and a face shield. According to standard CDC guidelines, what is the first physical step the nurse should take when removing the personal protective equipment (PPE)?

A) Take off the face shield.
B) Untie the gown at the waist.
C) Grasp the outside of one glove at the wrist to peel it off.
D) Remove the surgical mask.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Client Need:** Safety and Infection Control

According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), the most contaminated items should be removed first to prevent the spread of pathogens. Gloves are considered the most contaminated piece of PPE. The first step in the traditional PPE removal sequence is to grasp the outside of one glove at the wrist and peel it away from the hand, turning the glove inside out. (Alternatively, gown and gloves can be removed together, but peeling the glove is always the initial physical action regarding the hands). This is followed by removing the face shield/goggles, then the gown, and finally the mask or respirator.
</details>
### Question 441
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION**
The nurse is reviewing the health history of a newly admitted adult client. Which finding places the client at the greatest risk for experiencing a fall during their hospital stay?

A) A history of type 2 diabetes managed with diet
B) A daily intake of a multivitamin and a probiotic
C) A recent episode of orthostatic hypotension
D) An allergy to latex and penicillin

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Orthostatic hypotension causes a sudden drop in blood pressure when changing positions (such as standing up), which can lead to dizziness or fainting. This significantly increases the client's risk for falls. Dietary management of diabetes, taking vitamins, and allergies do not directly increase fall risk.
</details>

---

### Question 442
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING**
The nurse provides teaching to a client with a weakened immune system about preventing infection at home. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will wash my hands with soap and water before preparing food."
B) "I should share my bath towels only with my immediate family."
C) "I can stop taking my antibiotics once my fever completely goes away."
D) "I will use hot water to wash my hands to kill more bacteria."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Proper handwashing with soap and water before handling food is a fundamental and highly effective way to prevent infection. Towels should never be shared by an immunocompromised client, antibiotics must be taken for the full prescribed course even if symptoms resolve, and warm (not hot) water is recommended to prevent skin breakdown and drying.
</details>

---

### Question 443
**Lens: DELEGATION**
The nurse is caring for a client who is in bilateral soft wrist restraints to prevent the pulling of medical devices. Which of the following tasks can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's skin integrity under the restraints.
B) Providing range-of-motion exercises when the restraints are temporarily removed.
C) Determining if the client's behavior indicates the restraints can be discontinued.
D) Explaining the legal reason for the restraints to the client's visiting family.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The UAP can perform basic, routine care tasks such as providing range-of-motion exercises, assisting with feeding, and taking vital signs while restraints are temporarily removed. Assessing skin integrity, evaluating the ongoing need for restraints, and providing education are entirely nursing responsibilities and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 444
**Lens: LEGAL/ETHICAL**
A visitor approaches the nurses' station and asks for the room number of a specific client. The client has requested to be a "no-information" patient, meaning no information should be shared with the public. What is the most appropriate response by the nurse?

A) "If you are an immediate family member, I can give you the room number."
B) "Let me go ask the client if they want to see you today."
C) "The client has requested that we not share their room number with anyone."
D) "I cannot confirm whether or not that individual is a patient here."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Under HIPAA guidelines, if a client opts out of the facility directory (a "no-information" or "do not announce" status), the staff cannot acknowledge that the person is in the facility. Stating "I cannot confirm whether or not that individual is a patient here" or "I have no information on a patient by that name" maintains the client's legal right to privacy. Option C violates privacy by confirming the client is indeed admitted.
</details>

---

### Question 445
**Lens: PROCEDURAL STEP**
The nurse enters a client's room and discovers a small fire in the trash can next to the bed. Using the RACE protocol, what is the nurse's very first physical action?

A) Pull the fire alarm handle located in the hallway.
B) Remove the client from the room immediately.
C) Close the room door to contain the smoke and fire.
D) Use a nearby fire extinguisher to put out the fire.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The RACE acronym stands for Rescue, Alarm, Confine, and Extinguish. The very first step when a fire is discovered is to Rescue (remove) any clients in immediate danger. After the client is safe, the nurse should pull the alarm, confine the fire by closing doors, and attempt to extinguish it if it is small and safe to do so.
</details>
### Question 446
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION 
A nurse is reviewing the medical records of four clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for falls?

A. A 45-year-old client with pneumonia who is taking oral antibiotics
B. A 30-year-old client with a fractured arm in a fiberglass cast
C. A 75-year-old client with nocturia who recently started taking a sedative
D. A 60-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic cholecystectomy

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer: C**

**Rationale:** Advanced age, frequent need to use the restroom at night (nocturia), and the use of central nervous system depressants (sedatives) are all major risk factors for falls. This combination severely increases the client's risk of tripping or losing balance while attempting to get to the bathroom in the dark. The other clients do not possess as many compounding fall risk factors.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Look for the client with compounding risk factors. Age > 65 + urgency to ambulate (nocturia) + altered mental status/drowsiness (sedative) equals a classic high fall risk scenario.
</details>

---

### Question 447
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING 
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who will be using continuous home oxygen therapy. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the safety instructions?

A. "I will check the electrical cords on my oxygen equipment for fraying."
B. "I can use petroleum jelly on my lips if they get dry from the oxygen."
C. "I should keep my oxygen tank at least 2 feet away from the space heater."
D. "I will use a wool blanket on my bed to keep warm at night."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer: A**

**Rationale:** Oxygen supports combustion, making fire safety a priority. Clients must regularly inspect electrical equipment for frayed cords to prevent sparks that could ignite a fire. Petroleum jelly is oil-based and highly flammable; water-soluble lubricants should be used instead. Oxygen tanks must be kept at least 5 to 10 feet away from heat sources or open flames. Wool and synthetic blankets can generate static electricity, which can spark a fire; cotton blankets should be used.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Remember the fire triad: oxygen, heat, and fuel. Oxygen makes things burn faster and hotter. Eliminate options that introduce a spark risk (wool, frayed cords) or a flammable fuel source (petroleum jelly, proximity to space heaters).
</details>

---

### Question 448
**Lens:** DELEGATION 
A nurse is assigning care for a client who is on strict contact precautions for a *Clostridioides difficile* (*C. diff*) infection. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's skin for breakdown around the perineal area
B. Teaching the client's family about the purpose of contact precautions
C. Determining which type of isolation supplies are needed outside the room
D. Emptying the client's bedside commode and recording the output

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer: D**

**Rationale:** Emptying a bedside commode and recording intake/output are standard, routine tasks that fall within the UAP's scope of practice, provided they wear the appropriate personal protective equipment (PPE). Assessing skin (A), teaching families (B), and making clinical decisions about infection control supplies (C) require the nursing process and must be performed by the registered nurse.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Use the "T.A.C.E." rule for delegation—do not delegate Teaching, Assessment, Clinical judgment, or Evaluation. Emptying a commode is a routine task without required clinical judgment.
</details>

---

### Question 449
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
A nurse is caring for a client who is on airborne precautions for suspected tuberculosis. A visitor stops the nurse in the hallway and asks, "Why does my friend have a sign on the door saying everyone needs to wear a mask?" Which response by the nurse is the most appropriate?

A. "The client has tuberculosis, so we must wear masks to prevent spreading it."
B. "You will need to ask the client directly about the reason for the precautions."
C. "We are waiting for some test results, so it is just a hospital policy."
D. "The client has a severe respiratory infection that is highly contagious."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer: B**

**Rationale:** Under HIPAA, the nurse cannot disclose the client's medical diagnosis, condition, or reasons for treatment to visitors without the client's explicit permission. Directing the visitor to ask the client protects the client's privacy and confidentiality while remaining professional. Options A and D violate confidentiality. Option C is evasive and still implies a medical workup without the client's consent.

**Test-Taking Strategy:** When faced with a visitor asking about a client's health status, always protect the client's privacy. The safest legal and ethical action is to direct the visitor to speak with the client.
</details>

---

### Question 450
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP 
A nurse discovers a small fire in a trash can in the staff breakroom. The nurse has already moved everyone out of immediate danger and activated the fire alarm. The nurse retrieves a fire extinguisher. What is the first physical step the nurse should take to operate the extinguisher?

A. Aim the nozzle at the base of the fire
B. Pull the locking pin on the extinguisher
C. Sweep the nozzle from side to side
D. Squeeze the handle to release the extinguishing agent

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer: B**

**Rationale:** To operate a fire extinguisher, the nurse should use the PASS acronym: **P**ull the pin, **A**im at the base of the fire, **S**queeze the handle, and **S**weep from side to side. Therefore, the very first step to operate the device is pulling the locking pin. 

**Test-Taking Strategy:** Recall the acronyms for fire safety. RACE dictates the overall response (Rescue, Alarm, Confine, Extinguish), while PASS dictates the physical operation of the extinguisher (Pull, Aim, Squeeze, Sweep).
</details>
### Question 451
A nurse is conducting admission assessments on an adult medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for falls?

A. A 45-year-old recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy with mild incisional pain
B. A 30-year-old client admitted for intravenous antibiotics for a skin infection
C. A 78-year-old client with nocturia, a history of osteoarthritis, and poor vision
D. A 60-year-old taking daily over-the-counter vitamin C and a daily probiotic

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)
**Rationale:** Advanced age, nocturia (frequent trips to the bathroom at night), mobility issues from osteoarthritis, and visual impairments all significantly increase a client's risk for experiencing a fall. The other clients lack these compounding high-risk factors. 
</details>

### Question 452
A nurse enters a client's room and discovers a small fire in the bedside trash can. According to the RACE acronym for fire safety, what is the nurse's first physical action?

A. Confine the fire by closing the room door
B. Rescue the client by moving them to a safe area
C. Extinguish the fire using the proper extinguisher
D. Activate the facility's fire alarm system

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)
**Rationale:** The RACE acronym stands for Rescue, Alarm, Confine, Extinguish. The absolute first step when encountering a fire is to "Rescue" or remove any clients, visitors, or staff in immediate danger. After securing their safety, the nurse should pull the alarm.
</details>

### Question 453
A client with acute confusion is currently in bilateral soft wrist restraints to prevent the pulling of a newly placed nasogastric (NG) tube. Which task is safe and appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Explaining the reason for the restraints to the client's anxious family
B. Assessing the client's radial pulses and skin integrity under the restraints
C. Evaluating if the client's cognitive status has improved enough to remove the restraints
D. Assisting the client with range-of-motion exercises when the restraints are temporarily removed

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)
**Rationale:** Unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) can perform basic, standard care tasks such as assisting with range-of-motion exercises, hygiene, and feeding. Assessing skin integrity, evaluating cognitive status, and educating families fall under the RN's scope of practice and cannot be delegated.
</details>

### Question 454
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client with a weakened immune system about food safety and infection control at home. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "I should wash my hands with warm water and soap for at least 20 seconds before eating."
B. "It is completely safe to thaw frozen raw meats on the kitchen counter overnight."
C. "I only need to wash my hands after using the bathroom, not before I cook."
D. "I can save time by using the same cutting board for raw chicken and fresh salad vegetables."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Rationale:** Proper hand hygiene—washing hands with warm water and soap for at least 20 seconds—is a primary defense against infection, which is crucial for immunocompromised clients. Thawing meat on the counter, failing to wash hands before cooking, and sharing cutting boards between raw meat and vegetables all promote bacterial growth and cross-contamination.
</details>

### Question 455
A client is scheduled for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain to evaluate chronic headaches. Which finding in the client's medical history is an absolute contraindication for this procedure?

A. A known allergy to iodine-based contrast dye
B. A surgical history of an uncomplicated cholecystectomy
C. The presence of an implanted cardiac pacemaker
D. A documented history of mild claustrophobia

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)
**Rationale:** An MRI uses an extremely powerful magnetic field. Implants containing metal or electronics, such as a cardiac pacemaker, are absolute contraindications because the magnet can severely disrupt the device's function, heat the metal, or dislodge it, causing fatal injury. Iodine allergies are relevant for CT scans, not standard MRIs. Claustrophobia is a concern but can be managed with anti-anxiety medications. A previous cholecystectomy is not a contraindication.
</details>
### Question 456
A nurse is assessing a group of clients for fall risk on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for a fall?

A. A 45-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy
B. A 60-year-old client taking a daily multivitamin and calcium supplement
C. A 78-year-old client with nocturia who is taking a loop diuretic
D. A 30-year-old client receiving intravenous antibiotics for cellulitis

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition<br>
Advanced age combined with nocturia (waking up at night to urinate) and the use of a loop diuretic significantly increases the risk for falls. The client is likely to get out of bed frequently, often in a hurried state or in the dark, which presents a major safety hazard. The other clients lack these compounding fall risk factors.
</details>

### Question 457
A nurse is teaching a client with an infected surgical wound about preventing the spread of infection at home. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I should wash my hands with soap and water before and after changing my dressing."
B. "I can leave the soiled bandages on the nightstand until trash day."
C. "I only need to wash my hands if they look visibly dirty."
D. "I will share my bath towels with my family to save on laundry."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
Hand hygiene before and after wound care is the single most effective way to prevent the transmission of microorganisms. Leaving soiled bandages exposed, sharing bath towels, and neglecting routine hand hygiene before medical care demonstrate a lack of understanding and increase the risk of spreading the infection.
</details>

### Question 458
A nurse is caring for a client with soft wrist restraints to prevent the pulling of a nasogastric tube. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Evaluating the client's continuous need for the restraints
B. Explaining the reason for the restraints to the client's family
C. Assessing the client's radial pulse and capillary refill
D. Assisting the client with range-of-motion exercises when the restraints are temporarily removed

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
The UAP can safely perform basic, standard care tasks, such as assisting with range-of-motion exercises, feeding, and basic hygiene when restraints are temporarily removed for standard care. Assessing circulation, evaluating the ongoing medical need for restraints, and providing education are core nursing responsibilities that require a license and cannot be delegated.
</details>

### Question 459
A nurse is working on a busy medical unit. A visitor approaches the nurse's station and asks, "What room is my friend, John Smith, in?" Which action should the nurse take first to comply with safety and privacy protocols?

A. Give the visitor the room number if the client is visibly stable.
B. Check the facility directory to see if the client has opted out of having their information shared.
C. Escort the visitor directly to the client's room to ensure they are a safe visitor.
D. Tell the visitor that nurses are never allowed to give out room numbers.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Legal/Ethical<br>
Under HIPAA regulations, healthcare facilities maintain a patient directory that allows staff to give basic information (like a room number) to visitors who ask for the patient by name. However, patients have the legal right to opt out of this directory. The nurse must first verify the client's privacy status in the system before confirming their presence or releasing any information.
</details>

---

### Question 460
A nurse is preparing to enter the room of a client on Contact Precautions for MRSA. The nurse gathers a disposable gown and a pair of clean gloves. What is the first physical step the nurse should take before putting on the protective equipment?

A. Apply the clean gloves.
B. Tie the gown at the waist.
C. Put on the disposable gown.
D. Perform hand hygiene.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
The very first step in donning (putting on) personal protective equipment (PPE) is to perform hand hygiene. This ensures that the nurse's hands are clean before touching and applying the clean gown and gloves, adhering to strict infection control standards.
</details>
### Question 461
A nurse is screening clients on a medical-surgical unit to determine their risk for injury. Which client factor represents the highest risk for falls?

A) Use of a hearing aid in the right ear
B) A daily prescription for a multivitamin
C) A diet high in dietary fiber and fluids
D) A history of falling twice in the past month

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** According to risk recognition principles, a recent history of falls is one of the strongest and most reliable predictors for future falls. The nurse must implement fall precautions immediately for this client. Hearing aids, daily vitamins, and a high-fiber diet do not inherently increase a client's fall risk.
**Lens:** Risk Recognition
</details>

---

### Question 462
A nurse is caring for a client who requires wrist restraints to prevent them from pulling out an intravenous (IV) line. Which action by the nurse is unsafe and contraindicated?

A) Removing the restraints every 2 hours to perform skin care and range of motion
B) Tying the restraint straps using a quick-release slip knot
C) Securing the restraint straps directly to the movable side rails of the bed
D) Verifying that two fingers can fit between the restraint and the client's wrist

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Restraints must never be tied to the bed's side rails. If the side rail is lowered, it can pull on the client's extremity and cause severe injury. Restraints should always be secured to the movable part of the bed frame. The other options are safe and expected nursing practices for restraint use.
**Lens:** Contraindication
</details>

---

### Question 463
A nurse is teaching a client about basic infection control practices at home after a surgical procedure. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of proper hand hygiene?

A) "I only need to wash my hands if I can physically see dirt on them."
B) "I should wash my hands with soap and warm water for at least 20 seconds."
C) "Hand sanitizer is always better to use than soap and water, even if my hands are soiled."
D) "I will just wash my hands once a day in the morning to prevent infection."

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Washing hands with soap and water for at least 20 seconds is the standard and most effective recommendation for basic hand hygiene. Hand sanitizer is not effective when hands are visibly soiled, and washing only once a day or only when visible dirt is present is insufficient to prevent infection.
**Lens:** Client Teaching
</details>

---

### Question 464
A registered nurse (RN) is working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) on an oncology floor. Which task related to safety and infection control is appropriate for the RN to delegate to the UAP?

A) Emptying a Foley catheter drainage bag into a graduated cylinder and recording the amount
B) Assessing a newly admitted client's risk for skin breakdown and pressure injuries
C) Determining if a client with a cough requires droplet transmission precautions
D) Teaching a client how to use a new wheeled walker safely in the hallway

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Emptying a catheter bag and recording the output is a standard, routine task that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing risk, determining the need for isolation precautions, and providing initial client teaching all require nursing judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
**Lens:** Delegation
</details>

---

### Question 465
A nurse is preparing to lift a heavy box of medical supplies from the floor to a waist-high medication room counter. What is the first physical step the nurse should take to ensure proper body mechanics and prevent injury?

A) Bend forward at the waist to quickly grasp the box
B) Twist the back while lifting to easily transfer the weight to the counter
C) Position the feet wide apart to establish a stable base of support
D) Hold the box at arm's length while carrying it to the counter

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Establishing a wide base of support by positioning the feet shoulder-width apart is the first and most crucial procedural step in proper body mechanics before attempting to lift any heavy object. Bending at the waist, twisting the spine, and holding items away from the body increase the risk of back injury and are incorrect practices.
**Lens:** Procedural Step
</details>
### Question 466
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

A nurse is conducting a safety assessment on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest identified risk factor for experiencing a fall?

A) A 55-year-old client who takes a daily multivitamin.
B) A 72-year-old client with a history of a recent fall at home.
C) A 40-year-old client with completely intact vision and hearing.
D) A 60-year-old client who walks one mile every day.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** A history of a previous fall is one of the strongest and most reliable predictors of future falls. The other options represent factors that do not significantly increase fall risk or are associated with generally good health and mobility.
**Lens:** Risk Recognition - Identifying which client possesses known, established risk factors for an injury or adverse event.
</details>

---

### Question 467
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

A nurse is teaching a client about basic infection control and hand hygiene at home. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will wash my hands with soap and water for at least 20 seconds."
B) "I only need to wash my hands when they look visibly dirty."
C) "Using hand sanitizer is better than soap and water if my hands have dirt on them."
D) "I should share my bath towel with my family to help build our immunity."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Proper hand hygiene requires washing hands with soap and warm water for a minimum of 20 seconds. Hand sanitizer is ineffective if hands are visibly soiled, hands should be washed at multiple points throughout the day (not just when visibly dirty), and sharing towels can spread pathogens rather than safely build immunity.
**Lens:** Client Teaching - Evaluating if the client understands fundamental, foundational health practices.
</details>

---

### Question 468
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)

The nurse is assigning tasks to an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) for a group of clients. Which task related to safety and infection control is most appropriate to delegate to the UAP?

A) Assessing a newly admitted client's risk for skin breakdown.
B) Evaluating the effectiveness of a client's fall prevention plan.
C) Emptying a client's Foley catheter drainage bag into a graduated cylinder.
D) Teaching a client how to properly use a wheeled walker.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Emptying a Foley catheter drainage bag is a routine, standard task that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing risk, evaluating care plans, and providing client teaching are strictly nursing responsibilities that require the clinical judgment of a licensed nurse.
**Lens:** Delegation - Identifying routine, standard, and safe tasks that do not require nursing judgment.
</details>

---

### Question 469
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)

A nurse receives a phone call from a person claiming to be a client's sister. The caller asks for an update on the client's medical condition. What is the nurse's most appropriate action to maintain information security?

A) Give the sister a brief, general update on the client's current status.
B) Transfer the call directly to the client's healthcare provider.
C) Ask the caller to provide her full name and then disclose the information.
D) Verify if the caller is listed as an authorized contact in the client's medical record.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Under HIPAA and client confidentiality regulations, the nurse must verify that a caller is an authorized contact (often using a specific code or checking the documented consent list) before sharing any protected health information (PHI). Simply getting a name or transferring the call does not protect the client's privacy rights.
**Lens:** Legal/Ethical - Applying fundamental rules of confidentiality and protected health information (PHI).
</details>

---

### Question 470
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)

A nurse is preparing to insert an indwelling urinary catheter for a client. What is the very first physical action the nurse should take before gathering the sterile supplies?

A) Don sterile gloves.
B) Cleanse the client's perineal area with an antiseptic solution.
C) Perform thorough hand hygiene.
D) Open the sterile catheter kit on the bedside table.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Hand hygiene is the primary and most foundational step before initiating any nursing procedure, especially prior to handling sterile supplies. This basic infection control measure drastically reduces the risk of healthcare-associated infections. Hand hygiene must be completed before touching the kit, donning gloves, or cleansing the client.
**Lens:** Procedural Step - Recognizing the absolute first, chronological physical action in standard nursing procedures.
</details>
### Question 471
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
A nurse is completing an admission assessment on a client admitted to the medical-surgical unit. Which client factor indicates the highest risk for a fall?

A) The client takes a daily over-the-counter multivitamin.
B) The client has a history of a recent fall at home.
C) The client uses prescription reading glasses.
D) The client has a documented mild allergy to latex.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The client has a history of a recent fall at home.

**Rationale:** 
A history of a previous fall is one of the strongest, most significant predictors of future falls. Identifying this risk factor is a key component of basic fall risk assessments (such as the Morse Fall Scale or Hendrich II Fall Risk Model). Using reading glasses, taking vitamins, or having a latex allergy do not inherently place the client at an immediate, high risk for falls.
</details>

---

### Question 472
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client about basic infection control practices at home. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "Hand sanitizer is always better than using soap and water."
B) "I only need to wash my hands when they visibly look dirty."
C) "I should wash my hands with soap and water after using the restroom."
D) "Sharing towels with my family is safe as long as they aren't coughing."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "I should wash my hands with soap and water after using the restroom."

**Rationale:** 
Washing hands with soap and water after using the restroom is a fundamental infection control practice that physically removes pathogens and prevents the fecal-oral transmission of disease. Hand sanitizer is effective in many situations but is not "always" better, especially if hands are visibly soiled or if exposed to certain pathogens like *C. difficile*. Hands should be cleaned frequently, not just when visibly dirty. Towels should not be shared to prevent cross-contamination.
</details>

---

### Question 473
**Lens:** DELEGATION
The nurse is planning care for a stable client placed on contact precautions for Methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus (MRSA). Which task is appropriate to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Emptying the client's urinary catheter drainage bag.
B) Assessing the client's skin under the medical tape.
C) Teaching the client's family about how to put on isolation gowns.
D) Deciding when the client can be safely taken off contact precautions.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Emptying the client's urinary catheter drainage bag.

**Rationale:** 
Emptying a urinary catheter drainage bag is a routine, standard task that falls well within the UAP's scope of practice, provided they adhere to the required contact precautions (wearing a gown and gloves). Assessing skin, teaching the family, and making clinical decisions regarding the discontinuation of isolation precautions all require nursing judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 474
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
A family member points to the "Contact Precautions" sign on a client's door and asks the nurse, "Why is my sibling in isolation? What infection do they have?" What is the most appropriate response by the nurse?

A) "Your sibling has a contagious skin infection that we are treating right now."
B) "Your sibling requested that no visitors be allowed inside the room."
C) "You will have to ask the doctor about their specific diagnosis."
D) "I cannot share their medical information, but I can show you how to put on the required protective equipment."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) "I cannot share their medical information, but I can show you how to put on the required protective equipment."

**Rationale:** 
Under HIPAA (Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act), the nurse must maintain client confidentiality. The nurse cannot disclose the client's diagnosis or medical condition to visitors without the client's explicit permission. Providing instructions on how to don the necessary Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) protects the visitor and maintains infection control standards while upholding the ethical and legal boundaries of client privacy.
</details>

---

### Question 475
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
The nurse is preparing to enter the room of a client on airborne precautions for suspected pulmonary tuberculosis. What is the very first step the nurse must perform before donning Personal Protective Equipment (PPE)?

A) Secure the ties on the back of the isolation gown.
B) Perform thorough hand hygiene.
C) Put on a pair of clean gloves.
D) Check the seal of the N95 respirator mask.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Explanation</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Perform thorough hand hygiene.

**Rationale:** 
The fundamental first step in any donning or doffing procedure for Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) is hand hygiene. Cleaning the hands prevents the transfer of microorganisms to the clean PPE items (such as the gown, N95 mask, and gloves) before they are placed on the body. Putting on the gown, applying the N95 mask, and donning gloves follow hand hygiene.
</details>
### Question 476
A nurse is assessing several clients on a medical-surgical unit for fall risk. Which client factor represents the strongest predictor that the client is at risk for injury from a fall?

A) Having an indwelling urinary catheter
B) Receiving intravenous fluids via a peripheral line
C) Using a standard walker to ambulate down the hall
D) A history of a previous fall within the last 3 months

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

**Rationale:** A history of falling is one of the strongest and most significant predictors for future falls. While using assistive devices, having tethers (like IV lines or catheters), and impaired mobility also increase risk, a previous fall is a primary indicator that necessitates strict fall precautions. 
</details>

---

### Question 477
A nurse is preparing to apply a prescribed physical restraint to a client's wrists to prevent the removal of a breathing tube. Which action by the nurse is unsafe and contraindicated?

A) Securing the restraint tie to the movable part of the bed frame.
B) Tying the restraint directly to the bed rails using a square knot.
C) Padding the client's wrists before applying the restraint.
D) Ensuring two fingers can easily fit between the restraint and the client's skin.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)

**Rationale:** Tying a restraint to the bed rails is strictly contraindicated because lowering the rail can cause severe injury to the client. Furthermore, restraints should always be secured with a quick-release knot, never a square knot, so they can be removed rapidly in an emergency. The restraint should be tied to the movable part of the bed frame. Padding the wrists and leaving a two-finger gap are safe and expected practices.
</details>

---

### Question 478
A nurse is caring for a client with active pulmonary tuberculosis who is placed on airborne precautions. Which observation by the nurse indicates an expected and correct implementation of these infection control precautions?

A) The client is placed in a negative-pressure airflow room.
B) The nurse wears a standard surgical mask when entering the room.
C) The client's door is kept propped open to promote air circulation.
D) Visitors are required to wear a gown and gloves at all times.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)

**Rationale:** It is an expected and correct infection control measure to place a client with active pulmonary tuberculosis in a negative-pressure airflow room. This prevents contaminated air from escaping into the hallway. The nurse should wear an N95 respirator, not a standard surgical mask. The door must remain closed, and airborne precautions primarily require respiratory protection for visitors, not necessarily a gown and gloves unless contact with body fluids is expected.
</details>

---

### Question 479
The nurse is teaching a client who will be using continuous oxygen therapy at home. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of basic home oxygen safety?

A) "I can use petroleum jelly on my lips if they get dry from the oxygen."
B) "I will keep my oxygen concentrator at least 3 feet away from my space heater."
C) "I should wear cotton clothing rather than synthetic materials."
D) "It is okay to smoke in the same room as long as I turn the oxygen off first."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

**Rationale:** Wearing cotton clothing is a safe practice because synthetic or wool materials can generate static electricity, which could ignite in an oxygen-rich environment. Petroleum jelly is flammable and contraindicated; a water-based lubricant should be used. Oxygen equipment should be kept at least 8 to 10 feet away from open flames or heat sources. Smoking is strictly prohibited in a home where oxygen is used, even if the machine is turned off.
</details>

---

### Question 480
A nurse reviews the laboratory results for a client receiving chemotherapy. The client's white blood cell (WBC) count is 2,000/mm³ (2.0 x 10^9/L). Based on this basic lab finding, what is the most appropriate infection control safety intervention?

A) Place the client in a negative-pressure room.
B) Encourage the daily consumption of unpeeled fresh fruit.
C) Instruct visitors who are feeling mildly ill to wear a surgical mask.
D) Restrict the presence of fresh flowers or live plants in the room.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic lab mean?)

**Rationale:** A normal WBC count is 5,000 to 10,000/mm³. A count of 2,000/mm³ indicates leukopenia/neutropenia, putting the client at a high risk for infection. The most appropriate safety intervention is initiating neutropenic precautions, which include restricting fresh flowers and live plants due to the risk of fungal and bacterial spores. Unpeeled fresh fruits should be avoided, and sick visitors should not visit the client at all, even with a mask. Negative-pressure rooms are for airborne infections, not neutropenia (which sometimes requires positive-pressure airflow).
</details>
### Question 481
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
A nurse is reviewing the health records of several clients on a medical-surgical unit to determine fall risk. Which factor places a client at the highest risk for experiencing a fall?

A. A history of a previous fall within the last 3 months
B. Taking a daily over-the-counter multivitamin
C. Having an indwelling urinary catheter removed yesterday
D. Using a hearing aid in the left ear

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** A history of a recent fall is the single strongest predictor that a client is at high risk for falling again. The nurse must recognize this risk and implement strict fall precautions. The other options do not inherently pose a high risk for falls.
</details>

---

### Question 482
**Lens:** DELEGATION
A nurse is caring for a client who is on contact precautions due to a Methicillin-resistant *Staphylococcus aureus* (MRSA) infection. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Educating the client's family about how to properly put on a gown and gloves.
B. Emptying the client's urinary drainage bag while wearing appropriate personal protective equipment (PPE).
C. Assessing the client's skin for new MRSA lesions during the morning bath.
D. Applying a prescribed topical antibiotic ointment to the client's wound.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Emptying a urinary drainage bag is a routine, standard task that falls within the UAP's scope of practice, provided they wear the correct PPE for contact precautions. Educating the family (A), assessing the skin (C), and administering medications (D) are strictly nursing responsibilities and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 483
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client who will be using continuous home oxygen therapy. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of basic oxygen safety?

A. "I should use a petroleum-based lip balm if my lips get dry from the oxygen."
B. "I can use a synthetic blanket on my bed to keep warm at night."
C. "I will place 'No Smoking' signs on the front door of my house."
D. "I should turn up the oxygen flow if I feel a little short of breath."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Oxygen supports and violently accelerates combustion. Placing "No Smoking" signs is a critical safety measure to prevent fires. Petroleum-based products (A) are flammable and should be avoided; water-based lubricants should be used instead. Synthetic blankets (B) can create static electricity, which is a fire hazard. The client should never adjust the oxygen flow rate (D) without a healthcare provider's prescription.
</details>

---

### Question 484
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
A nurse discovers a small trash can fire in the breakroom and grabs the nearest fire extinguisher. Using the PASS acronym, what is the very first physical step the nurse should take when operating the extinguisher?

A. Aim the nozzle at the base of the fire.
B. Sweep the nozzle from side to side.
C. Squeeze the handle to release the extinguishing agent.
D. Pull the pin out of the extinguisher handle.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** When using a fire extinguisher, the nurse should follow the PASS acronym: **P**ull the pin, **A**im at the base of the fire, **S**queeze the handle, and **S**weep from side to side. Therefore, pulling the pin is the very first physical step.
</details>

---

### Question 485
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION
A nurse is preparing to apply soft wrist restraints to a client who is confused and repeatedly pulling at their intravenous (IV) lines. Which action by the nurse is strictly contraindicated?

A. Fastening the restraint straps to the bed side rails.
B. Tying the restraint using a quick-release slip knot.
C. Securing the restraints to the movable portion of the bed frame.
D. Allowing enough room to fit two fingers between the restraint and the client's wrist.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Restraints must never be tied to the bed's side rails. If the side rail is suddenly lowered, it will severely pull on the client's extremity, potentially causing dislocation or severe injury. Restraints should always be tied to the movable portion of the bed frame (C) using a quick-release knot (B), with enough space for two fingers to ensure adequate circulation (D).
</details>
### Question 486
**Lens:** Delegation 
A nurse is assigning tasks for the upcoming shift. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) regarding a client on contact precautions for Methicillin-resistant *Staphylococcus aureus* (MRSA)?

A) Evaluating the client's skin under their medical devices for breakdown.  
B) Assisting the client with a bed bath while wearing a gown and gloves.  
C) Educating the client's visiting family members on proper hand hygiene.  
D) Changing the sterile dressing on the client's infected surgical wound.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client with basic hygiene, such as a bed bath, is a routine task that falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. The UAP must simply follow the established contact precautions (gown and gloves). Evaluating skin, providing education, and performing sterile dressing changes are nursing responsibilities that cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 487
**Lens:** Procedural Step 
A nurse is preparing to lift a heavy box of intravenous fluids from the floor to a storage shelf. To prevent back injury, what is the first physical step the nurse should take?

A) Keep the feet close together to maintain a strong center of gravity.  
B) Bend at the waist while keeping the knees straight to grasp the box.  
C) Position the feet shoulder-width apart to create a wide base of support.  
D) Hold the box as far away from the body as possible while lifting.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** The first step in proper body mechanics when lifting a heavy object is establishing a wide base of support by keeping the feet shoulder-width apart. This increases stability. Bending at the knees (not the waist), keeping the load close to the body, and avoiding twisting are also key steps to prevent injury.
</details>

---

### Question 488
**Lens:** Client Teaching 
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to an older adult client about fall prevention in the home environment. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the basic safety measures?

A) "I will keep a small nightlight on in the hallway and bathroom."  
B) "I will wear loose slippers to keep my feet comfortable while walking."  
C) "I will walk around the house in my socks to avoid slipping in hard shoes."  
D) "I will use small throw rugs to cover up slippery spots on my kitchen floor."  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** Ensuring adequate lighting, especially at night, is a primary intervention to prevent falls in the home. Loose slippers, walking in socks, and using throw rugs are all significant tripping and slipping hazards that increase the risk of a fall. 
</details>

---

### Question 489
**Lens:** Contraindication 
A nurse is caring for a client who is confused and requires bilateral soft wrist restraints to prevent the removal of a nasogastric tube. Which action by the nurse is unsafe and contraindicated?

A) Tying the restraints using a quick-release slip knot.  
B) Assessing the client's skin integrity and circulation every 2 hours.  
C) Securing the restraint ties to the movable part of the bed frame.  
D) Securing the restraint ties directly to the side rails of the bed.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** D  
**Rationale:** Restraints must *never* be secured to the side rails of a bed. If the side rail is lowered, it can pull violently on the restraint, causing severe injury to the client. Restraints must always be secured to the movable part of the bed frame using a quick-release knot, and the client must be assessed at least every 2 hours.
</details>

---

### Question 490
**Lens:** Legal/Ethical 
A nurse finds a client sitting on the floor after attempting to get out of bed unassisted. After assessing the client for injuries and safely returning them to bed, the nurse completes an incident report. Which action regarding the incident report is legally and ethically correct?

A) Place a copy of the incident report in the client's room for the family to read.  
B) Document in the nursing notes that an incident report was completed.  
C) Submit the completed incident report to the facility's risk management department.  
D) File the original incident report directly into the client's permanent medical record.  

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** Incident reports are internal documents used by a healthcare facility's risk management department for quality improvement and to identify safety hazards. They are legally privileged documents and should *not* be placed in the client's medical record, nor should the nurse document in the chart that an incident report was filed, as this could open the report to legal discovery. Only the objective facts of the fall and the client's condition are documented in the medical record.
</details>
### Question 491
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

The nurse is reviewing the census on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for experiencing a fall?

A. A 30-year-old client admitted for an asthma exacerbation who is receiving inhaled albuterol.
B. A 68-year-old client with pneumonia who has a continuous IV infusion and a history of nocturia.
C. A 45-year-old client recovering from an appendectomy who ambulates in the hallway with assistance.
D. A 55-year-old client with primary hypertension who has a baseline blood pressure of 124/82 mmHg.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Fall risk increases with advanced age, the presence of continuous medical equipment (like an IV pole/tubing), and frequent needs to use the restroom (nocturia). This combination makes the older adult client with nocturia and a continuous IV infusion the highest risk for a fall. The other clients lack multiple compounding risk factors for falling.
</details>

---

### Question 492
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)

The nurse is preparing a client for a magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan of the brain. Which client finding is a strict safety contraindication for this procedure?

A. The client has an implanted cardiac pacemaker.
B. The client has a documented allergy to shellfish and iodine.
C. The client reports a severe history of claustrophobia.
D. The client is currently wearing prescription contact lenses.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** An MRI uses a very powerful magnet. Any ferromagnetic metal or electronic devices inside the body, such as an implanted cardiac pacemaker, are strict contraindications because the magnet can deactivate the device or pull it out of place, causing life-threatening harm. Iodine allergy is related to CT contrast, not MRI. Claustrophobia may require sedation but is not a strict physical contraindication. Contact lenses are generally safe but may need to be removed depending on facility policy; they are not a strict contraindication.
</details>

---

### Question 493
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

The nurse is providing home safety teaching to a client who has been prescribed continuous oxygen therapy. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of oxygen safety precautions?

A. "I will apply a petroleum-based jelly to my lips if they get dry from the oxygen."
B. "I should keep my oxygen concentrator at least 3 feet away from the fireplace."
C. "I will replace my synthetic fleece blankets with 100% cotton blankets."
D. "I can continue smoking inside the house as long as I turn the oxygen off first."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Oxygen supports combustion. Cotton blankets should be used instead of synthetic or wool blankets to prevent the buildup of static electricity, which could cause a spark. Petroleum jelly is combustible and should not be used on the face or lips while using oxygen; water-based lubricants are required. Oxygen equipment must be kept at least 10 feet away from open flames. Smoking is strictly prohibited anywhere in a home where oxygen is used, even if turned off, as oxygen saturates the environment and clothing.
</details>

---

### Question 494
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)

The nurse is caring for a client in bilateral soft wrist restraints to prevent the pulling of medical devices. Which task is safe to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's radial pulses and capillary refill.
B. Evaluating the client's ongoing behavioral need for the wrist restraints.
C. Explaining the reason for the restraints to the client's visiting family members.
D. Providing range-of-motion exercises when the restraints are temporarily removed.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** The UAP may perform basic care tasks, such as assisting with activities of daily living, repositioning, and providing passive or active range-of-motion exercises when the nurse temporarily removes the restraints. Assessing circulation (pulses, capillary refill), evaluating the ongoing need for restraints, and educating the family are all nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 495
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)

The nurse discovers a small fire in a client's bedside wastebasket. According to the RACE acronym for fire safety, what is the nurse's first physical action?

A. Pull the nearby fire alarm to alert the facility.
B. Extinguish the fire using the nearest ABC fire extinguisher.
C. Move the client out of the room to a safe area.
D. Close the door to the client's room to confine the fire.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** The RACE acronym stands for Rescue, Alarm, Confine, Extinguish. The very first step is "R" - Rescue or Remove clients in immediate danger. Therefore, the nurse's first physical action is to move the client out of the room to a safe area. Only after ensuring the client is safe should the nurse activate the alarm, confine the fire by closing doors, and attempt to extinguish it if safe to do so.
</details>
### Question 496
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)

A nurse is completing an admission assessment on a newly admitted adult client. Which finding places the client at the highest risk for a fall during their hospital stay?

A) The client uses reading glasses when looking at menus.
B) The client takes a daily over-the-counter multivitamin.
C) The client has a history of a previous fall at home last month.
D) The client ambulates independently with a steady gait.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** A history of a previous fall is one of the strongest, most well-documented predictors of future falls. Using reading glasses corrects a visual deficit (improving safety), taking a multivitamin does not inherently increase fall risk, and a steady gait indicates a lower risk of falling.

</details>

---

### Question 497
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

A nurse is teaching a client about basic hand hygiene and preventing the spread of infection at home. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should wash my hands with soap and water for at least 20 seconds."
B) "Hand sanitizer is always better than soap and water, even if my hands are visibly dirty."
C) "I only need to wash my hands after using the bathroom."
D) "I can share my bath towel with family members as long as I do not feel sick."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) recommends scrubbing hands with soap and water for at least 20 seconds to effectively remove germs. Hand sanitizer is not effective when hands are visibly soiled. Hands should be washed frequently throughout the day (before eating, after coughing, etc.), and sharing personal items like towels can easily spread pathogens.

</details>

---

### Question 498
**Lens:** DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)

A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for the shift and working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Which task related to safety and infection control can the RN safely delegate to the UAP?

A) Assessing a client's sacrum for early signs of skin breakdown.
B) Emptying urine from a client's Foley catheter drainage bag.
C) Teaching a newly admitted client how to properly use an incentive spirometer.
D) Evaluating a client's verbalized pain level 30 minutes after medication administration.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Emptying a Foley catheter drainage bag and recording output is a routine, standard procedure that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing, teaching, and evaluating are all parts of the nursing process and must be performed by the registered nurse.

</details>

---

### Question 499
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality)

A nurse receives a phone call at the nursing station from an individual claiming to be a client's sister, asking for an update on the client's medical condition. The client has not provided a privacy code or explicit permission to share health information. What is the nurse's best action?

A) Provide a brief, general update stating that the client is currently resting and stable.
B) Ask the caller for their phone number so the physician can call them back later.
C) Transfer the call directly to the client's room without verifying the caller's identity.
D) State that no information can be provided without the client's explicit permission.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), a nurse cannot disclose Protected Health Information (PHI) to any unauthorized person. The nurse must firmly but politely explain that no information can be released without the client's consent or a valid privacy code. 

</details>

---

### Question 500
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)

A nurse enters a client's room and discovers a small fire burning in the trash can next to the bed. According to the RACE mnemonic for fire safety, what is the very first physical step the nurse must take?

A) Confine the fire by closing the client's room door.
B) Activate the nearest building fire alarm system.
C) Remove the client from the room to a safe area.
D) Extinguish the fire using the appropriate fire extinguisher.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** The standard fire safety mnemonic is RACE: Rescue, Alarm, Confine, Extinguish. The very first step is to **R**escue (remove) any clients in immediate danger from the fire. Afterward, the nurse should activate the alarm, confine the fire by closing doors, and finally attempt to extinguish it if safe to do so.

</details>
### Question 501
**Lens: Risk Recognition**

A nurse is reviewing the census on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for developing a healthcare-associated infection (HAI)?

A) An adolescent who is recovering from a closed fracture of the radius.  
B) A middle-aged adult receiving intravenous fluids for dehydration.  
C) An older adult client who has an indwelling urinary catheter in place.  
D) A young adult who is ambulatory on postoperative day two after an appendectomy.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** Invasive devices, such as indwelling urinary catheters, significantly bypass the body's natural defense mechanisms, placing the client at a high risk for catheter-associated urinary tract infections (CAUTIs), which are among the most common healthcare-associated infections. Intact skin and the absence of long-term invasive devices in the other clients put them at a much lower risk for HAIs.

</details>

---

### Question 502
**Lens: Procedural Step**

A nurse is preparing to enter the room of a client on strict Contact Precautions for *Clostridioides difficile*. What is the nurse's appropriate first step when preparing to interact with the client?

A) Put on a particulate respirator (N95).  
B) Perform thorough hand hygiene.  
C) Don a pair of clean, nonsterile gloves.  
D) Secure the isolation gown at the neck and waist.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** Hand hygiene is the critical first step before donning any personal protective equipment (PPE) and before entering any client's room. After washing hands with soap and water (required for *C. difficile* spores), the nurse would then don the gown followed by the gloves. An N95 respirator is not required for contact precautions.

</details>

---

### Question 503
**Lens: Delegation**

A nurse is assigning tasks for the shift for a client who is on bed rest and has a high risk for skin breakdown. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Reposition the client in bed every two hours.  
B) Assess the client's sacral skin for nonblanchable redness.  
C) Teach the client about the importance of moving frequently.  
D) Determine the appropriate pressure-relieving mattress for the bed.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** Repositioning a stable client is a basic care task that falls safely within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing skin, providing client education, and determining the appropriate medical equipment (like a specialized mattress) require the critical thinking and clinical judgment of the registered nurse and cannot be delegated.

</details>

---

### Question 504
**Lens: Client Teaching**

The nurse is teaching a client how to safely use a straight cane to assist with left-sided leg weakness. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will hold the cane on my weaker, left side."  
B) "I will move the cane and my right leg forward at the same time."  
C) "I will keep the cane about 12 inches out to the side of my foot."  
D) "I will hold the cane on my stronger, right side."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Rationale:** To provide the best balance and support, a cane should always be held on the stronger, unaffected side (COAL: Cane Opposite Affected Leg). The cane should be kept about 4 to 6 inches to the side of the foot, not 12 inches. The client should move the cane forward concurrently with the weaker leg, not the stronger leg.

</details>

---

### Question 505
**Lens: Legal/Ethical**

A nurse is completing their shift and has printed handoff reports containing clients' medical diagnoses, names, and laboratory values. Which action by the nurse strictly complies with the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA) regulations?

A) Shredding the documents in a designated secure disposal bin.  
B) Placing the reports in the regular trash bin under the nurse's desk.  
C) Tearing the papers in half before throwing them into the wastebasket.  
D) Leaving the reports at the nurse's station counter for the oncoming shift.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** According to HIPAA guidelines, any document containing protected health information (PHI) must be properly destroyed, typically by placing it in a secure, locked shredding bin. Tearing them in half or placing them in regular trash violates privacy laws, as the information can still be retrieved and read. Leaving them openly on the counter exposes PHI to unauthorized individuals.

</details>
### Question 506
The nurse is reviewing the charts of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for sustaining a fall?

A. A 45-year-old client recovering from an appendectomy who reports a pain level of 1 out of 10.
B. A 30-year-old client admitted for observation after a mild concussion with a normal CT scan.
C. A 78-year-old client with a history of osteoarthritis who takes a prescribed loop diuretic.
D. A 60-year-old client admitted for an elective hernia repair who takes a daily multivitamin.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Advanced age (over 65), impaired mobility due to joint issues (osteoarthritis), and the use of diuretics (which cause urinary urgency and frequency) are all major, cumulative risk factors for falls. The other clients lack this combination of significant physiological and pharmacological risk factors.
</details>

---

### Question 507
The nurse provides teaching to a client with a localized staphylococcal skin infection about practicing hand hygiene at home. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I should wash my hands with soap and water for at least 20 seconds."
B. "I only need to wash my hands if I can actually see dirt on them."
C. "Using hand sanitizer is the only way to truly kill the bacteria."
D. "I should use cold water to wash my hands so my skin doesn't dry out."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Proper hand hygiene requires scrubbing the hands with soap and water for at least 20 seconds to effectively remove pathogens. Hands should be washed before and after direct contact with wounds, not just when visibly soiled. Hand sanitizer is effective but not the "only" way, and warm water is generally recommended over cold water to help create an effective lather without causing excessive skin irritation.
</details>

---

### Question 508
The nurse is caring for a client who is in bilateral soft wrist restraints to prevent the pulling of medical devices. Which task is safe and appropriate to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's circulation, motion, and pulse in the wrists.
B. Repositioning the client and offering fluids every 2 hours.
C. Explaining the medical necessity of the restraints to the client's family.
D. Deciding when the client is calm enough to have the restraints removed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<strong>Lens:</strong> DELEGATION
<strong>Rationale:</strong> UAPs can perform basic, routine care for a client in restraints, such as turning, repositioning, offering fluids, and assisting with toileting every 2 hours. Assessing circulation, providing medical education to the family, and evaluating the ongoing need for restraints all require the clinical judgment of the registered nurse and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 509
A visitor approaches the nurse's station and asks for the room number of a specific client, stating they are a close family friend. The client previously requested to opt out of the facility directory and asked that no information be given to visitors. What is the nurse's best action?

A. Ask the visitor to provide photo identification before giving out the room number.
B. Tell the visitor the room number but ask them to keep the visit brief.
C. Confirm the client is admitted to the unit but refuse to provide the exact room number.
D. State that no information can be provided about the presence of the client.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<strong>Lens:</strong> LEGAL/ETHICAL
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), clients have the right to opt out of the facility directory. When this occurs, healthcare staff cannot acknowledge that the client is in the facility, nor can they provide any information, including room numbers, to callers or visitors.
</details>

---

### Question 510
The nurse walks into a client's room and discovers a small fire burning in the bedside trash can. Following the RACE protocol, what is the nurse's *first* physical action?

A. Aim a fire extinguisher at the base of the flames.
B. Pull the nearest fire alarm handle in the hallway.
C. Move the client out of the room to a safe area.
D. Close the door to the client's room to contain the smoke.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<strong>Lens:</strong> PROCEDURAL STEP
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The RACE acronym outlines the standard procedural steps for a fire emergency: Rescue/Remove, Alarm, Confine, Extinguish. The very first physical step is to rescue and remove any clients in immediate danger from the fire. Afterward, the nurse should activate the alarm, confine the fire by closing doors, and attempt to extinguish it if safe to do so.
</details>

# SECTION: Basic Care and Comfort 

### Question 511
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION**
The nurse is assessing clients on a medical-surgical unit for their risk of developing impaired skin integrity. Which client is at the highest risk for developing a pressure injury?

A) A client who walks independently but requires a cane.
B) A client who wears bilateral compression stockings during the day.
C) A client who is completely bedbound and has urinary incontinence.
D) A client who uses a bedside commode with minimal assistance.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) A client who is completely bedbound and has urinary incontinence.

**Rationale:** 
Prolonged immobility combined with moisture from urinary incontinence significantly increases the risk of skin breakdown and pressure injuries (maceration and friction). Clients who are mobile or only require minimal assistance have a much lower risk because they can shift their weight and relieve prolonged pressure. Compression stockings alone do not pose the highest risk as long as they are applied correctly and removed periodically for skin assessment.

</details>

---

### Question 512
**Lens: PROCEDURAL STEP**
The nurse is preparing to provide oral care to an unconscious client. What is the *first* physical action the nurse should take to ensure client safety during the procedure?

A) Turn the client into a side-lying position.
B) Insert a bite block between the client's teeth.
