C) Brush the client's tongue using a soft-bristled toothbrush.
D) Suction the client's mouth with a Yankauer catheter.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Turn the client into a side-lying position.

**Rationale:** 
When providing oral care to an unconscious client, the absolute priority is preventing aspiration. The first step is to turn the client into a side-lying (lateral) position or turn their head to the side so that fluids naturally drain out of the mouth rather than pooling in the back of the throat. Using a bite block, brushing, and suctioning are subsequent steps performed *after* the client is positioned safely.

</details>

---

### Question 513
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED**
The nurse is evaluating the sleep patterns of a 78-year-old client. Which finding is considered an expected, normal age-related change in sleep for this client?

A) Requiring a daily prescription sleep aid to rest.
B) Sleeping 12 uninterrupted hours every night.
C) Falling asleep immediately upon lying down.
D) Waking up more frequently during the night.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Waking up more frequently during the night.

**Rationale:** 
As individuals age, their sleep architecture changes. Older adults typically spend less time in deep sleep (Stages 3 and 4) and REM sleep, making them more easily aroused. Therefore, waking up more frequently during the night is an expected age-related change. Sleeping 12 hours uninterrupted, falling asleep instantly (which can indicate severe sleep deprivation), or needing daily prescription sleep aids are not considered expected or normal physiological changes of aging.

</details>

---

### Question 514
**Lens: CONTRAINDICATION**
A client reports a dull, aching pain in their right calf after being on bed rest for three days. The client asks the nurse to vigorously massage the leg to relieve the ache. Why is massaging the client's calf contraindicated?

A) It may cause severe muscle spasms in the lower extremity.
B) It could dislodge a potential deep vein thrombosis (DVT).
C) It will decrease arterial circulation to the affected foot.
D) It might increase localized swelling in the ankle joint.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) It could dislodge a potential deep vein thrombosis (DVT).

**Rationale:** 
Aching pain in the calf of an immobilized client is a classic warning sign of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Massaging the calf is strictly contraindicated because the mechanical pressure could break off the blood clot, turning it into an embolus that could travel to the lungs, causing a life-threatening pulmonary embolism. It does not primarily decrease arterial circulation, cause spasms, or increase ankle swelling.

</details>

---

### Question 515
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING**
The nurse is teaching a client who experiences frequent constipation about basic dietary modifications to promote regular bowel movements. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will increase my intake of fresh fruits and whole grains."
B) "I will limit my fluid intake to 1 liter per day."
C) "I should drink a cup of coffee right before every meal."
D) "I need to eat more cheese and dairy products."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "I will increase my intake of fresh fruits and whole grains."

**Rationale:** 
Increasing dietary fiber through foods like fresh fruits, vegetables, and whole grains adds bulk to the stool, promoting regular bowel movements and preventing constipation. Limiting fluid intake to 1 liter per day can worsen constipation by making stools hard and dry. Eating more cheese and dairy can also contribute to constipation. While caffeine can stimulate the bowels, drinking coffee before every meal is not a foundational dietary recommendation for preventing chronic constipation compared to increasing fiber.

</details>
### Question 516
A nurse is checking the gastric residual volume of a client receiving continuous enteral tube feedings. 

Which finding is expected and indicates the feeding can safely continue at the current rate?

A) A gastric residual volume of 600 mL
B) The client complaining of severe, sudden nausea
C) The client's abdomen feeling firm and distended
D) A gastric residual volume of 100 mL

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
A gastric residual volume of less than 250 mL (often up to 500 mL depending on facility policy) is considered an expected finding, indicating the client is properly digesting and tolerating the continuous feeding. Large volumes (like 600 mL), nausea, and a firm, distended abdomen are unexpected findings that indicate delayed gastric emptying or intolerance.

</details>

---

### Question 517
A nurse is planning morning care for a client who is on strict bed rest due to a fractured pelvis. 

Which basic care task is safe to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Administering a PRN oral pain medication before the client's bath
B) Applying the client's prescribed anti-embolism stockings
C) Assessing the client's sacrum and heels for pressure injuries
D) Teaching the client how to use the incentive spirometer

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** DELEGATION
Applying anti-embolism stockings is a standard, routine procedure that does not require nursing assessment or clinical judgment, making it safe to delegate to the UAP. Assessing skin integrity, providing initial teaching, and administering medications fall exclusively under the registered nurse's scope of practice.

</details>

---

### Question 518
A nurse is preparing to provide oral hygiene to a client who is unconscious. 

What is the first physical step the nurse should take to prevent complications during this procedure?

A) Pour an alcohol-based mouthwash into a small cup
B) Insert a padded tongue blade into the client's mouth
C) Position the client in a side-lying position
D) Apply a water-soluble lubricant to the client's lips

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
Positioning the unconscious client in a side-lying (lateral) position with the head of the bed slightly elevated is the first and most crucial physical step. This allows oral secretions and cleaning fluids to drain out of the mouth, effectively preventing the dangerous complication of aspiration.

</details>

---

### Question 519
A nurse is providing education to an older adult client on basic lifestyle modifications to prevent constipation. 

Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will make sure to drink at least 8 glasses of water every day."
B) "I should try to eat more low-fiber foods like white rice and cheese."
C) "I will limit my daily physical activity to let my digestive tract rest."
D) "I will take an over-the-counter stimulant laxative every single morning."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
Increasing fluid intake is a fundamental non-pharmacological intervention to keep stool soft and prevent constipation. Decreasing fiber (white rice and cheese) and limiting physical activity will actually worsen constipation. Taking a daily stimulant laxative is unsafe because it can cause the bowel to become dependent on the medication.

</details>

---

### Question 520
A client who was recently admitted to the medical-surgical unit tells the nurse, "I haven't been able to sleep at all since I got here. The noises are making me so anxious."

Which response by the nurse demonstrates therapeutic communication?

A) "You should try to ignore the monitors; they are just part of the hospital."
B) "It must be really difficult to rest with all the unfamiliar noises around you."
C) "I will ask the doctor to prescribe you a strong sleeping pill right now."
D) "Everyone has a hard time sleeping on their first night in the hospital."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
This response uses empathy and validation, which are key components of therapeutic communication. It acknowledges the client's feelings and situation without minimizing them (as seen in options A and D) or immediately relying on a pharmacological fix without further exploring the issue (as seen in option C).

</details>
### Question 521
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)**

The nurse is assessing clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for developing a pressure injury?

A) A client who requires minimal assistance to ambulate to the bathroom.
B) A client who is eating 100% of their prescribed meals and snacks.
C) A client who is bedbound and experiences frequent bowel incontinence.
D) A client who uses a continuous positive airway pressure (CPAP) machine at night.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) A client who is bedbound and experiences frequent bowel incontinence.

**Rationale:** Immobility (being bedbound) and prolonged exposure to moisture and bacteria (bowel incontinence) are major risk factors for skin breakdown and pressure injuries. Ambulating, adequate nutrition, and standard respiratory equipment do not present the same severe, combined risk for generalized pressure injuries.
</details>

---

### Question 522
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

The nurse is providing education on sleep hygiene to a client experiencing insomnia. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should keep my bedroom temperature cool and the room dark."
B) "I will drink a cup of black tea right before going to bed."
C) "I should exercise vigorously for an hour right before I try to sleep."
D) "I will leave the television on to help me fall asleep faster."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "I should keep my bedroom temperature cool and the room dark."

**Rationale:** A cool, dark, and quiet environment promotes optimal sleep hygiene. Caffeine (found in black tea), vigorous exercise immediately before bed, and the blue light/stimulation from a television are known to disrupt the sleep cycle and should be avoided.
</details>

---

### Question 523
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)**

The nurse is providing routine hygiene and stoma care for a client with a newly established colostomy. Which assessment finding is expected and indicates healthy tissue?

A) The stoma appears pale and dry.
B) The stoma is dark purple and dusky.
C) The stoma is flush with or retracted below the skin.
D) The stoma is beefy red, moist, and slightly edematous.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) The stoma is beefy red, moist, and slightly edematous.

**Rationale:** A healthy, well-perfused stoma should be beefy red, moist, and may be slightly swollen (edematous) in the postoperative period. A pale, dry, dark purple, or dusky stoma indicates impaired blood flow (ischemia) and is a complication requiring immediate intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 524
**Lens: CONTRAINDICATION (What is unsafe to do/give?)**

The nurse is preparing to provide oral hygiene to a client who is unconscious. Which action is unsafe and contraindicated for this client?

A) Placing the client in a side-lying (lateral) position.
B) Pouring a cup of water directly into the client's mouth to rinse.
C) Having a Yankauer suction catheter available during the procedure.
D) Applying a water-soluble lubricant to the client's lips afterward.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Pouring a cup of water directly into the client's mouth to rinse.

**Rationale:** Pouring water directly into an unconscious client's mouth is strictly contraindicated due to the high risk of aspiration, as the client lacks an intact swallow or gag reflex. The nurse should use a moistened sponge or suction toothbrush. Placing the client in a side-lying position and using suction are safe and necessary interventions.
</details>

---

### Question 525
**Lens: PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)**

The nurse is performing female perineal care for a client who is on bed rest. Which physical step demonstrates the correct technique?

A) Wiping from the anal area toward the pubic area (back to front).
B) Wiping from the pubic area toward the anal area (front to back).
C) Reusing the same section of the washcloth for multiple strokes.
D) Scrubbing the perineum back and forth vigorously to remove debris.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Wiping from the pubic area toward the anal area (front to back).

**Rationale:** When performing female perineal care, the nurse must always wipe from front to back (cleanest to dirtiest) to prevent introducing fecal bacteria into the urethra, which can cause a urinary tract infection. The nurse should use a clean section of the washcloth for every stroke and avoid vigorous scrubbing, which can damage delicate tissues.
</details>
### Question 526
A nurse is preparing to provide oral care to a client who is unconscious. What is the **first** physical step the nurse should take to ensure client safety during this procedure?

A) Place the client in a supine position with the head flat.
B) Apply a thick layer of petroleum jelly to the client's lips.
C) Position the client in a side-lying position with the head turned to the side.
D) Use a firm-bristled toothbrush to deeply scrub the back of the tongue.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Position the client in a side-lying position with the head turned to the side.

**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Category:** Basic Care and Comfort

**Rationale:** When providing oral care to an unconscious client, the greatest risk is aspiration of fluids since the client cannot swallow or protect their own airway. The very first procedural step is to place the client in a side-lying position (Sims' position) or turn their head completely to the side. This allows secretions and fluids to drain out of the mouth by gravity. Placing the client supine increases aspiration risk. Petroleum jelly may be applied after care, and a soft-bristled brush is preferred over a firm one to prevent tissue trauma.
</details>

---

### Question 527
The registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a group of clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client's mealtime care is safe and appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) A client who frequently chokes on liquids and needs a specialized pureed diet.
B) A client with newly diagnosed dysphagia who requires a bedside swallow evaluation.
C) A client who is receiving continuous enteral tube feedings via a nasogastric tube.
D) A client with bilateral hand tremors who requires assistance holding utensils.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) A client with bilateral hand tremors who requires assistance holding utensils.

**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Category:** Basic Care and Comfort

**Rationale:** The UAP can safely feed and assist clients who have physical limitations (like hand tremors) but have an intact, normal swallowing mechanism. Clients with a high risk for aspiration, such as those who frequently choke or have newly diagnosed dysphagia, require feeding and assessment by the RN or a speech-language pathologist (SLP). Managing and monitoring continuous enteral tube feedings is a nursing responsibility and cannot be delegated to the UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 528
A nurse is teaching a client about basic sleep hygiene to help manage mild insomnia. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should avoid drinking caffeinated tea or coffee in the late afternoon and evening."
B) "I will take a brisk 30-minute walk right before I get into bed to tire myself out."
C) "I will stay in bed and watch television if I cannot fall asleep after 20 minutes."
D) "I should drink a large glass of water right before bedtime to stay hydrated."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "I should avoid drinking caffeinated tea or coffee in the late afternoon and evening."

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Category:** Basic Care and Comfort

**Rationale:** Good sleep hygiene includes avoiding stimulants such as caffeine (found in coffee, tea, and soda) in the late afternoon and evening. Vigorous exercise right before bed can stimulate the body and make it harder to fall asleep; it should be done earlier in the day. If a client cannot fall asleep within 20 minutes, they should get out of bed and do a relaxing activity, rather than watching television in bed (the bed should be reserved for sleep and intimacy). Drinking a large glass of water immediately before bed may lead to nocturia, waking the client up to use the restroom.
</details>

---

### Question 529
A client who is recovering from a severe motor vehicle accident requires a complete bed bath. The client looks away, blushes, and says, "I am so embarrassed that I need someone to wash me like a baby." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "Don't worry, we do this for dozens of patients every single day."
B) "It can be difficult to rely on others for your personal care. Let's talk about how you are feeling."
C) "I will work as fast as I can so you do not have to be embarrassed for long."
D) "You should be glad you have people here in the hospital who are willing to help you."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "It can be difficult to rely on others for your personal care. Let's talk about how you are feeling."

**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
**Category:** Basic Care and Comfort

**Rationale:** Therapeutic communication involves acknowledging and validating the client's feelings and encouraging them to express themselves. Option B validates the client's feelings of vulnerability and loss of independence, opening the door for active listening. Option A minimizes the client's feelings by making it about the nurse's routine. Option C focuses on rushing the task rather than addressing the emotional distress. Option D is dismissive, judgmental, and guilt-inducing.
</details>

---

### Question 530
A nurse is evaluating a client two hours after the removal of an indwelling urinary catheter. Which finding is considered an **expected** outcome that the nurse should simply document?

A) Mild burning during the first time the client urinates.
B) Complete inability to void for 10 hours after the catheter is removed.
C) Frank, dark red blood with large clots in the toilet.
D) Continuous, uncontrolled dribbling of urine into the undergarments.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Mild burning during the first time the client urinates.

**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Category:** Basic Care and Comfort

**Rationale:** After an indwelling urinary catheter is removed, it is completely normal and expected for the client to experience mild dysuria (burning) during the first void due to urethral irritation from the catheter. The nurse should reassure the client and document the finding. An inability to void for 10 hours indicates urinary retention, which is unexpected and requires intervention. Frank red blood with clots is unexpected and indicates trauma or bleeding. Continuous dribbling of urine indicates incontinence or overflow retention, which is an unexpected and abnormal finding.
</details>
### Question 531
The nurse is preparing to transfer a client who has right-sided weakness from the bed to a wheelchair. After bringing the wheelchair into the room, what is the first physical step the nurse should perform regarding the wheelchair to ensure safety?

A. Lock the brakes on both wheels of the wheelchair.
B. Place the wheelchair on the client's weaker side.
C. Fold up the footrests before positioning the chair.
D. Ensure the wheelchair is facing the head of the bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> PROCEDURAL STEP<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> When transferring a client to or from a wheelchair, the most critical first physical step regarding the equipment is to ensure the brakes are locked on both wheels. This prevents the chair from sliding out from under the client, which could cause a fall. The wheelchair should be placed on the client's stronger side, not the weaker side. While adjusting footrests is important, it is secondary to locking the brakes.
</details>

---

### Question 532
The nurse is caring for a client who requires assistance with basic activities of daily living. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Evaluating the client's swallowing ability during breakfast.
B. Administering a prescribed medicated barrier cream to an intact perineal area.
C. Assessing the client's skin integrity during a bed bath.
D. Providing oral care for a client who is conscious and stable.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DELEGATION<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Providing routine oral care to a conscious, stable client is a standard hygiene task that falls well within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing skin integrity and evaluating swallowing are nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated. Administering medications, including medicated creams, is also generally outside the UAP's scope of practice.
</details>

---

### Question 533
The nurse provides teaching to a client diagnosed with frequent constipation. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of basic bowel management?

A. "I will decrease my intake of high-fiber foods."
B. "I should limit my daily physical activity to rest my bowels."
C. "I should drink at least 2 to 3 liters of fluid every day."
D. "I will wait to use the restroom until I have a very strong urge."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Adequate fluid intake (typically 2 to 3 liters per day, unless contraindicated) helps keep the stool soft and promotes regular, comfortable bowel movements. Decreasing high-fiber foods, limiting physical activity, and delaying defecation when the urge occurs will all exacerbate constipation, not relieve it.
</details>

---

### Question 534
A nurse is emptying a client's urinary catheter drainage bag at the end of the shift. Which finding should the nurse identify as an expected, normal characteristic of urine?

A. Strong, foul-smelling odor.
B. Thick, cloudy appearance with sediment.
C. Dark amber color with pink streaks.
D. Pale yellow color with clear transparency.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Normal, expected urine is pale yellow to amber in color and clear or transparent, indicating adequate hydration and the absence of infection. A foul-smelling odor, thick or cloudy appearance, and pink streaks (which indicate hematuria or bleeding) are all unexpected findings that could suggest a urinary tract infection or other complications. 
</details>

---

### Question 535
The nurse is reviewing the health records of clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for developing obstructive sleep apnea (OSA)?

A. A 25-year-old client with a body mass index (BMI) of 19.
B. A 40-year-old client with a history of hyperthyroidism.
C. A 55-year-old male client with obesity and a large neck circumference.
D. A 65-year-old client who routinely consumes a high-protein diet.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Major risk factors for obstructive sleep apnea (OSA) include obesity, a large neck circumference (which narrows the airway), male gender, and increasing age. The 55-year-old male with obesity and a large neck circumference perfectly matches the high-risk profile for this sleep disorder. A BMI of 19 (normal weight), hyperthyroidism, and a high-protein diet are not primary risk factors for OSA.
</details>
### Question 536
The nurse is preparing to administer an enema to a client for constipation. What is the first physical step the nurse should take when positioning the client for this procedure?

A) Place the client in the left Sims position.
B) Elevate the head of the bed to a high Fowler's position.
C) Position the client supine with the knees flexed.
D) Assist the client to the right lateral recumbent position.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> PROCEDURAL STEP<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The left Sims position is the optimal position for enema administration. It follows the natural anatomical curve of the lower intestine and allows gravity to help the solution flow into the sigmoid colon and rectum.
</details>

---

### Question 537
The nurse is caring for a client who is on strict bed rest due to a fractured pelvis. Which task related to the client's basic care and comfort is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the client's skin integrity over the sacrum.
B) Assisting the client with eating their meals.
C) Teaching the client how to perform deep breathing exercises.
D) Assessing the client's pain level after a bed bath.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> DELEGATION<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Assisting a stable client with activities of daily living, such as feeding, falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. Evaluating skin integrity, patient teaching, and assessing pain are strictly nursing responsibilities.
</details>

---

### Question 538
A nurse is teaching a client about promoting healthy sleep habits. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will take a long nap every afternoon to catch up on my sleep."
B) "I will exercise right before bed so I feel tired enough to sleep."
C) "I should try to go to bed and wake up at the same time every day."
D) "Watching television in bed will help me fall asleep faster."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> CLIENT TEACHING<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Maintaining a consistent sleep-wake schedule helps regulate the body's internal clock and promotes better sleep quality. Napping late in the day, vigorous exercise before bed, and using screens in bed (like watching TV) can disrupt sleep patterns and make it harder to fall asleep.
</details>

---

### Question 539
A client with an indwelling urinary catheter has been drinking adequate fluids throughout the day. Which finding regarding the client's urine output should the nurse recognize as expected?

A) Pale yellow, clear urine draining into the bag.
B) Small blood clots present in the drainage tubing.
C) Cloudy, thick urine collecting in the bag.
D) Dark amber urine with a strong, foul odor.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Normal, expected urine for a well-hydrated client is pale yellow and clear. Dark amber urine, foul odors, cloudiness, or the presence of blood clots are unexpected findings that may indicate dehydration, infection, or trauma.
</details>

---

### Question 540
The nurse is assessing the skin integrity of several clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client has the highest risk for developing impaired skin integrity?

A) A 30-year-old client recovering from a laparoscopic appendectomy.
B) A 65-year-old client who ambulates independently in the hallway.
C) A 40-year-old client eating a high-protein, high-calorie diet.
D) A 78-year-old client who is incontinent of urine and bedbound.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> RISK RECOGNITION<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A client who is older, bedbound (experiencing prolonged pressure), and incontinent (experiencing moisture and chemical irritation) possesses multiple high-risk factors for developing pressure injuries and moisture-associated skin damage. 
</details>
### Question 541
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury?)**

A nurse is reviewing the assigned clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which client is at the highest risk for developing a pressure injury?

A) A young adult client who is day one postoperative and walking with assistance.
B) An older adult client who is immobile and incontinent of urine. 
C) A client who is ambulatory on crutches for a fractured ankle.
D) A client who receives total parenteral nutrition and uses a bedside commode.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Immobility and constant moisture from urinary incontinence are two of the primary risk factors for skin breakdown and pressure injuries. The other clients are either ambulatory or able to use a commode, meaning they are moving and thus at a much lower risk for impaired skin integrity. 
</details>

---

### Question 542
**Lens: PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)**

The nurse is preparing to administer a complete bed bath to a client who requires total assistance. What is the first physical step the nurse should take to ensure safety and proper body mechanics before starting the bath?

A) Wash the client's face with soap and warm water.
B) Remove the client's hospital gown completely.
C) Place a dry towel under the client's head.
D) Raise the bed to a comfortable working height. 

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Before beginning any physical care such as a bed bath, the nurse must ensure proper ergonomics to prevent back injury. Raising the bed to a comfortable working height is the first essential physical step. The other steps happen later in the bathing process.
</details>

---

### Question 543
**Lens: DELEGATION (What is safe to give to a UAP?)**

The nurse is planning care for a client who had a stroke three days ago. The client has right-sided weakness but a normal swallow evaluation and no dysphagia. Which task related to nutrition is most appropriate to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Assisting the client with feeding their lunch.
B) Assessing the client's swallowing ability during the meal.
C) Teaching the client how to use adaptive utensils.
D) Modifying the client's diet to pureed textures if they cough.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Feeding a stable client without swallowing difficulties (dysphagia) is a routine, basic care task that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing swallowing, teaching the use of adaptive equipment, and modifying a diet require nursing judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 544
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

The nurse is teaching a client about basic sleep hygiene practices to improve nighttime rest. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will drink a cup of coffee right before bed to help me relax."
B) "I should keep my bedroom very warm and brightly lit."
C) "I will use my bed only for sleep and intimacy."
D) "I should take a two-hour nap every afternoon to catch up on sleep."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Good sleep hygiene involves training the brain to associate the bed with sleep and intimacy, not with awake activities like watching TV or working. Caffeine before bed, bright/warm environments, and long daytime naps interfere with normal sleep patterns and are contraindicated for good sleep hygiene.
</details>

---

### Question 545
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)**

A client is receiving a daily oral iron supplement for iron deficiency anemia. The nurse asks the client about their elimination patterns. Which finding is an expected, normal effect of the iron supplement?

A) Pale, clay-colored stool
B) Dark green or black stool
C) Loose, watery diarrhea
D) Bright red blood in the stool

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Oral iron supplements commonly cause the stool to turn dark green or black. This is an expected, harmless side effect of the medication. Pale/clay-colored stool indicates biliary issues, bright red blood indicates lower GI bleeding, and severe watery diarrhea is not a standard expected side effect of iron (though mild constipation is common).
</details>

# SECTION:  Psychosocial Integrity

### Question 546
**Lens: Risk Recognition**

A nurse is reviewing the histories of several clients admitted to an inpatient mental health unit. Which client has the highest documented risk factor for suicide?

A. A client who recently joined a community bereavement support group
B. A client who expresses mild anxiety about returning to college
C. A client who has a history of a previous suicide attempt
D. A client who recently started a new job in a different city

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** A history of a previous suicide attempt is one of the strongest and most significant risk factors for future suicidal behavior. While life transitions (like starting a new job or returning to college) can cause stress, they are not inherently high-risk factors for suicide. Joining a support group is a protective factor, as it indicates the client is seeking help and building a support network.
</details>

---

### Question 547
**Lens: Therapeutic Communication**

A client whose spouse recently passed away begins crying during the admission assessment and states, "I just don't know how I am going to live without him." What is the most therapeutic response by the nurse?

A. "It is going to take time, but you will eventually feel better."
B. "This must be a very difficult time for you. Tell me more about how you are feeling."
C. "You should try to focus on the happy memories you shared together."
D. "I know exactly how you feel; my uncle passed away last year."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** This response uses the therapeutic techniques of empathy and open-ended questioning. It acknowledges the client's pain without minimizing it and invites them to share their feelings. Option A offers false reassurance. Option C gives non-therapeutic advice and dismisses the client's current pain. Option D shifts the focus away from the client and onto the nurse. 
</details>

---

### Question 548
**Lens: Legal/Ethical**

A voluntary client on a psychiatric unit refuses to take their prescribed morning dose of an oral antidepressant. What is the appropriate action by the nurse?

A. Document the refusal in the chart and explore the client's reasons for refusing.
B. Crush the medication and mix it into the client's applesauce without their knowledge.
C. Inform the client that they will be discharged if they do not comply with the medication regimen.
D. Call security to help hold the client so the medication can be safely administered.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** All clients, including voluntary psychiatric clients, have the legal and ethical right to refuse treatment, including medications. The nurse's role is to respect this right, explore the client's reasoning (e.g., side effects, lack of understanding), provide education, and document the refusal. Options B, C, and D are unethical and illegal, representing battery, coercion, and assault/battery, respectively.
</details>

---

### Question 549
**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**

A nurse is assessing a client who was unexpectedly laid off from their job two weeks ago. Which finding is an expected psychosocial response to this situational loss?

A. The client states they have created a detailed plan to end their life.
B. The client has not eaten or drank any fluids for the last four days.
C. The client reports hearing voices telling them they are worthless.
D. The client expresses feelings of sadness and frustration about the situation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Experiencing sadness, frustration, and mild-to-moderate anxiety are normal and expected psychological responses to a recent job loss or situational crisis. Options A, B, and C describe severe, unexpected psychological impairments (suicidal ideation, severe physiological neglect, and psychosis) that require immediate psychiatric intervention and are not considered expected grieving responses.
</details>

---

### Question 550
**Lens: Client Teaching**

A nurse is teaching a client about basic, healthy stress management techniques. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I will drink a few glasses of wine before bed to help me relax."
B. "I will stop spending time with my friends so I can focus on my stress."
C. "I will take slow, deep breaths when I start to feel overwhelmed."
D. "I should try to ignore my problems until they eventually go away."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Deep breathing is a healthy, adaptive coping mechanism that helps activate the parasympathetic nervous system to reduce stress and anxiety. Option A relies on a maladaptive coping mechanism (substance use). Option B involves social isolation, which can worsen stress. Option D represents denial and avoidance, which do not effectively manage or resolve the underlying stress.
</details>
### Question 551
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended)

A client recently diagnosed with a terminal illness is crying quietly in their room. What is the nurse's best response to support the client psychosocially?

A) "I know exactly how you feel; it is normal to be sad."
B) "Everything will be okay once we get your pain managed."
C) "Would you like me to sit quietly with you for a while?"
D) "You need to try to stay strong for your family."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Offering self by sitting quietly with the client is a highly effective therapeutic communication technique. It conveys empathy, availability, and support without minimizing the client's feelings or offering false reassurance.<br>
<strong>Lens Focus:</strong> Therapeutic Communication. This question asks the nurse to select the response that best demonstrates active listening, empathy, and patient-centered support.
</details>

---

### Question 552
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality/Rights)

A client is voluntarily admitted to the psychiatric unit for the treatment of severe depression. Two days later, the client walks up to the nurse's station and demands to leave immediately. What is the nurse's understanding of this client's rights?

A) The client can leave the facility immediately without notifying the healthcare provider.
B) The client retains the right to request a discharge against medical advice (AMA).
C) Voluntary clients legally lose the right to refuse prescribed psychiatric medications.
D) The client must be placed on an involuntary 72-hour hold immediately upon asking to leave.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Clients who are voluntarily admitted to a psychiatric facility retain all of their civil rights, including the right to request discharge against medical advice (AMA). The provider will evaluate the client to ensure they are not an imminent danger to themselves or others before they leave, but the right to make the request remains intact.<br>
<strong>Lens Focus:</strong> Legal/Ethical. This question tests the nurse's basic understanding of a voluntary psychiatric client's civil rights.
</details>

---

### Question 553
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

A client is preparing for discharge after an inpatient alcohol detoxification program and agrees to attend Alcoholics Anonymous (AA) meetings. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching regarding AA?

A) "I must find a sponsor before I am allowed to go to my first meeting."
B) "I will be cured of my alcoholism once I finish the 12 steps."
C) "I will graduate from the program after 12 weeks of perfect attendance."
D) "The primary goal of this program is completely abstaining from alcohol."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Alcoholics Anonymous (AA) is a self-help group where the primary goal is total abstinence from alcohol, taking it one day at a time. It is a lifelong process, not a 12-week course, and clients are never considered "cured." A sponsor is helpful but not a requirement to attend meetings.<br>
<strong>Lens Focus:</strong> Client Teaching. This evaluates whether the client comprehends the foundational purpose and structure of a common psychosocial support group.
</details>

---

### Question 554
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?)

The nurse is reviewing the charts of several older adult clients at a community health clinic. Which client factor places an older adult at the highest risk for elder abuse?

A) The client requires total assistance with activities of daily living (ADLs) due to advanced dementia.
B) The client lives alone in a single-story home in a rural neighborhood.
C) The client participates in a local senior center bingo night twice a week.
D) The client receives a monthly retirement pension and manages their own finances.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> High dependency needs, such as requiring total care for ADLs due to cognitive or physical impairment (like advanced dementia), significantly increase caregiver strain. This high level of vulnerability and dependency makes the client at the highest risk for elder abuse or neglect.<br>
<strong>Lens Focus:</strong> Risk Recognition. This requires the nurse to identify the psychosocial and environmental risk factors that lead to elder abuse.
</details>

---

### Question 555
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)

A client diagnosed with acute schizophrenia tells the nurse, "I hear the television telling me that I need to stay in my room all day." Which interpretation of this assessment finding is correct?

A) This is an unexpected finding indicating medication toxicity.
B) This is an expected finding demonstrating an auditory hallucination.
C) This is an expected finding demonstrating a somatic delusion.
D) This is an unexpected finding indicating impending physical violence.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Hearing voices or sounds that are not actually present (such as the TV speaking directly to them with instructions) is an auditory hallucination. This is a common and expected positive symptom in clients experiencing acute schizophrenia.<br>
<strong>Lens Focus:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected. This tests the nurse's ability to recognize basic psychiatric symptoms and determine if they are an expected part of the client's established disease process.
</details>
### Question 556
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION 

A client with a new diagnosis of major depressive disorder says, "I don't think I'll ever feel like myself again. It's just hopeless." What is the most therapeutic response by the nurse?

A. "It sounds like you are feeling overwhelmed right now."
B. "Why do you feel hopeless? We just started your treatment."
C. "Don't worry, the medication will start working in a few weeks."
D. "Many people feel this way, but they get better eventually."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. "It sounds like you are feeling overwhelmed right now."

**Rationale:** 
Validating the client's feelings and using active listening is the core of therapeutic communication. By reflecting the client's emotions, the nurse demonstrates empathy and encourages further open communication. Option B is incorrect because asking "why" questions can make the client feel defensive. Option C uses false reassurance ("Don't worry"), which dismisses the client's feelings. Option D minimizes the client's unique experience by comparing them to "many people."

</details>

---

### Question 557
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING

The nurse is teaching a client about basic stress management techniques to help reduce mild daily anxiety. Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching was effective?

A. "I will drink a large cup of coffee right before bed to help me relax."
B. "I will wait until I am having a severe panic attack to practice my coping skills."
C. "I need to avoid all stressful situations at work and home completely."
D. "I should try to take slow, deep breaths when I feel my heart starting to race."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. "I should try to take slow, deep breaths when I feel my heart starting to race."

**Rationale:** 
Deep breathing exercises are a proven, effective stress management technique that helps lower the heart rate and promote relaxation. Option A is incorrect because caffeine is a stimulant that can increase anxiety and disrupt sleep. Option B is incorrect because coping skills should be practiced daily when the client is calm so they are ready to use during acute stress. Option C is incorrect because it is impossible and unrealistic to avoid all stressors in life; the goal is to manage the response to stress.

</details>

---

### Question 558
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL

The nurse receives a phone call from someone claiming to be the employer of a client currently admitted to the inpatient psychiatric unit. The caller asks if the client is a patient at the facility. What is the nurse's best action?

A. Confirm the client is there but refuse to give any medical details.
B. State that no information can be given without the client's written consent.
C. Ask the client if they want the employer to know they are admitted while the caller waits.
D. Transfer the call to the unit social worker to handle the employer.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. State that no information can be given without the client's written consent.

**Rationale:** 
Under HIPAA and strict psychiatric confidentiality laws, the nurse can neither confirm nor deny the presence of a client in the facility without explicit, documented consent from the client. Option A violates confidentiality by confirming the client's presence. Option C is inappropriate as the nurse should not acknowledge the client's presence to the caller in any capacity before checking for pre-existing consent. Option D does not solve the confidentiality issue and improperly passes the task to someone else.

</details>

---

### Question 559
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION

The nurse is performing a psychosocial assessment on a newly admitted client. Which specific factor in the client's history represents the most significant risk for a future suicide attempt?

A. A recent job loss
B. Occasional alcohol use
C. A previous suicide attempt
D. A family history of generalized anxiety

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. A previous suicide attempt

**Rationale:** 
A past suicide attempt is the single strongest predictor and highest risk factor for future suicidal behavior. While a recent job loss (Option A), substance use (Option B), and a family history of mental health issues (Option D) can contribute to stress and depression, they do not carry the same high level of imminent risk as a known history of a prior suicide attempt. 

</details>

---

### Question 560
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED

A client whose spouse passed away unexpectedly three weeks ago tells the nurse, "I keep thinking I hear his voice calling my name in the house, even though I know he is gone." How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A. An indication that the client is experiencing severe medication toxicity.
B. An unexpected sign of a new-onset psychotic disorder.
C. An expected manifestation of the normal grieving process.
D. A warning sign that the client is developing schizophrenia.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. An expected manifestation of the normal grieving process.

**Rationale:** 
In the acute stages of grief (especially within the first few weeks or months), it is normal for individuals to experience brief auditory or visual illusions, such as hearing the deceased person's voice or sensing their presence. The client acknowledges reality ("even though I know he is gone"), which indicates intact reality testing. Options A, B, and D misinterpret a normal grief response as a severe psychiatric or medical pathology.

</details>
### Question 561
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
A client whose spouse recently passed away tells the nurse, "I just don't know how I am going to live without him. Everywhere I look, everything reminds me of him." Which of the following is the most therapeutic response by the nurse?

A) "Time heals all wounds; it will get easier for you soon."
B) "You should try packing up his belongings so you aren't reminded as much."
C) "It sounds like you are feeling incredibly overwhelmed right now."
D) "Are you making sure to eat enough and get plenty of sleep?"

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Reflecting the client's feelings ("It sounds like you are feeling incredibly overwhelmed right now") is a highly effective therapeutic communication technique. It demonstrates empathy, active listening, and validates the client's emotional state, encouraging further discussion. Option A is a cliché and minimizes the client's grief. Option B offers unsolicited advice, which is non-therapeutic. Option D changes the subject away from the client's feelings to basic physical needs, cutting off emotional expression.
</details>

---

### Question 562
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
A nurse is reviewing the health history of a client recently diagnosed with major depressive disorder. Which finding in the client's history represents the most significant risk factor for suicide?

A) A previous suicide attempt two years ago.
B) A recent diagnosis of generalized anxiety disorder.
C) A report of sleeping up to 10 hours a night.
D) Expressing feelings of sadness over a recent job loss.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** A previous suicide attempt is the single strongest predictor and most significant risk factor for future, potentially completed, suicides. While anxiety, hypersomnia (sleeping 10 hours), and sadness over job loss are associated with distress or depression, a past attempt elevates the client's immediate and long-term risk profile above the other factors.
</details>

---

### Question 563
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
A client is admitted to the inpatient psychiatric unit. The unit phone rings, and the caller identifies themselves as the client's employer, asking to confirm if the client is currently hospitalized. What is the nurse's most appropriate action?

A) Confirm the client is admitted but refuse to provide the medical diagnosis.
B) Ask the employer to explain why they need this specific information.
C) Tell the employer that the client is asleep and cannot take calls right now.
D) State that no information can be given out regarding who is a client at the facility.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA) and strict psychiatric confidentiality laws, the nurse must not confirm or deny the presence of a client in the facility without the client's explicit, written consent. Confirming admission (Option A) violates the client's right to privacy. Option B is inappropriate because the employer's reasoning does not bypass the law. Option C indirectly confirms the client is admitted, which is a breach of confidentiality.
</details>

---

### Question 564
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
The nurse is teaching a client about basic stress management and coping techniques. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should try to completely avoid any situation that causes me stress."
B) "I will practice taking slow, deep breaths when I start feeling anxious."
C) "Having a couple of alcoholic drinks each night will help me unwind safely."
D) "I should ignore my stressful feelings, and they will eventually go away."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Deep breathing is a healthy, effective, and basic coping mechanism for managing stress and reducing acute anxiety. Option A is unrealistic, as it is impossible to completely avoid all stressors in life. Option C describes a maladaptive coping mechanism (substance use) that can lead to addiction or worsen mental health. Option D is also maladaptive, as ignoring or suppressing feelings usually exacerbates stress rather than resolving it.
</details>

---

### Question 565
**Lens:** DELEGATION
A nurse is caring for an older adult client with dementia who is experiencing mild confusion and wandering. Which psychosocial intervention is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's current level of orientation and anxiety.
B) Explaining the daily routine and unit rules to the client.
C) Sitting with the client to look at a picture book and provide companionship.
D) Administering a prescribed PRN oral antianxiety medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Providing basic companionship and distraction, such as looking at a picture book, is a safe, routine task that falls well within the scope of practice for a UAP. Option A requires nursing assessment. Option B involves client teaching, which is a nursing responsibility (and may not be effective for a confused client without specific nursing strategies). Option D involves medication administration, which is strictly the responsibility of licensed nursing staff.
</details>
### Question 566
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
A client with severe depression says to the nurse, "I don't think I'll ever feel better. There is just no point in trying anymore." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "You have so much to live for, you really shouldn't say that."
B) "It sounds like you are feeling very hopeless right now."
C) "I'm sure you will feel differently after the medication kicks in."
D) "Why do you feel like there is no point in trying?"

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Stating "It sounds like you are feeling very hopeless right now" utilizes the therapeutic communication technique of reflection and empathy. It acknowledges the client's feelings and encourages them to elaborate without judgment. Option A minimizes the client's feelings and is invalidating. Option C offers false reassurance. Option D uses the word "why," which can make the client feel defensive and is generally non-therapeutic in psychiatric nursing.

</details>

---

### Question 567
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
A client is prescribed fluoxetine, a selective serotonin reuptake inhibitor (SSRI), for generalized anxiety disorder. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the basic medication teaching?

A) "I should stop taking this medication as soon as my anxiety goes away."
B) "I can expect to feel completely better by tomorrow morning."
C) "I will avoid eating foods that are high in tyramine, like aged cheese."
D) "It may take up to four weeks for me to feel the full effects of this medication."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** SSRIs typically take 2 to 4 weeks (sometimes up to 6 weeks) to reach their full therapeutic effect. Clients must understand this so they do not stop taking the medication prematurely. Option A is incorrect because stopping psychiatric medications abruptly can cause withdrawal symptoms or a relapse of the condition. Option B is incorrect because SSRIs do not work overnight. Option C refers to dietary restrictions for MAOIs, not SSRIs.

</details>

---

### Question 568
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
A person calls the psychiatric inpatient unit and says, "Hi, I am looking for my sister, Mary Jane. Is she a patient on your floor?" Which response by the nurse legally protects the client's rights?

A) "I cannot confirm or deny whether that person is a patient here."
B) "Yes, she is here, but I cannot give you any medical updates over the phone."
C) "Hold on, I will go ask the patient if she wants to talk to you."
D) "Please call back during official visiting hours for that information."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), a client's presence in a psychiatric facility is strictly confidential. The nurse must not acknowledge whether the individual is admitted unless the client has explicitly signed a release of information authorizing the staff to share this with the specific caller. Providing any information (even just confirming they are there) violates the client's right to privacy.

</details>

---

### Question 569
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
A nurse is assessing a client diagnosed with major depressive disorder. Which action or statement by the client represents a classic warning sign and high-risk factor for suicide?

A) The client states they have been crying more frequently than usual.
B) The client reports a decrease in appetite over the last two weeks.
C) The client has suddenly started giving away their prized possessions.
D) The client complains of having difficulty falling asleep at night.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Giving away favorite or prized possessions, sudden changes in a will, or suddenly making peace with loved ones are classic, high-risk warning signs that a client may have formulated a suicide plan. While crying (Option A), decreased appetite (Option B), and insomnia (Option D) are common expected symptoms of depression, they do not indicate an immediate risk of a suicide attempt as strongly as giving away belongings does.

</details>

---

### Question 570
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
A client with bipolar disorder is admitted to the unit in an acute manic episode. Which assessment finding is expected for a client experiencing mania?

A) Complaining of severe lethargy and a lack of energy.
B) Talking rapidly and frequently jumping from one topic to another.
C) Sleeping for 10 to 12 hours without interruption.
D) Speaking softly and avoiding eye contact with the nursing staff.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Strategy</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Mania is characterized by an elevated mood, increased energy, and hyperactive behavior. Pressured speech (talking rapidly) and "flight of ideas" (jumping from topic to topic) are classic, expected findings during a manic episode. Options A, C, and D are characteristics typically associated with depression, not mania.

</details>
### Question 571
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
A client recently diagnosed with a terminal illness is crying and says to the nurse, "I don't know how I'm going to tell my children about this. It is going to break their hearts." Which response by the nurse is the *most* therapeutic?

A) "Don't worry, children are incredibly resilient and bounce back quickly."
B) "You should wait until you have processed it yourself before telling them."
C) "This sounds like a very overwhelming and painful situation for you."
D) "Why do you think it will be so hard for you to tell them the news?"

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** 
- **C is correct:** This is a therapeutic response that utilizes empathy and reflection. It acknowledges the client's feelings and validates their emotional distress, encouraging further communication.
- **A is incorrect:** This provides false reassurance and minimizes the client's valid concerns about their children.
- **B is incorrect:** This offers unwanted advice, which takes away the client's autonomy and shuts down their expression of feelings.
- **D is incorrect:** Asking "why" questions is nontherapeutic. It can make the client feel defensive and interrogated rather than supported.

</details>

---

### Question 572
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
The nurse is reviewing the psychological history of a client whose spouse recently passed away. Which client factor places them at the *highest* risk for developing complicated grieving?

A) The spouse died after a prolonged, anticipated illness.
B) The client had a highly dependent relationship with the spouse.
C) The client is actively involved in a supportive religious community.
D) The client and spouse had an open, communicative marriage.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** 
- **B is correct:** Complicated grieving is more likely to occur when the survivor was highly dependent on the deceased, had an ambivalent relationship with them, or lacked a support system. 
- **A is incorrect:** An anticipated death often allows for "anticipatory grieving," which typically reduces the risk of complicated grief.
- **C is incorrect:** Having a strong, supportive community is a protective factor against complicated grieving, not a risk factor.
- **D is incorrect:** A healthy, communicative marriage leaves fewer unresolved issues, which generally decreases the risk of complicated grieving.

</details>

---

### Question 573
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
The nurse is teaching a client who is newly prescribed a selective serotonin reuptake inhibitor (SSRI) for depression. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the medication education?

A) "I can stop taking this medication as soon as I feel my depression lifting."
B) "I should avoid eating foods containing tyramine, like aged cheese."
C) "I will restrict my sodium and water intake while on this pill."
D) "It may take up to a month for me to feel the full effects of this drug."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** 
- **D is correct:** SSRIs typically take 2 to 4 weeks (sometimes up to 6 weeks) to reach their full therapeutic effect. The client needs to understand this so they do not become discouraged and stop the medication early.
- **A is incorrect:** Antidepressants should never be stopped abruptly, even if the client feels better, as this can cause discontinuation syndrome or a relapse of depression.
- **B is incorrect:** Tyramine restriction is necessary for monoamine oxidase inhibitors (MAOIs), not SSRIs.
- **C is incorrect:** Sodium and water restriction are not required for SSRIs. Maintaining consistent sodium intake is a teaching point for lithium therapy, not SSRIs.

</details>

---

### Question 574
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL
An adult client is voluntarily admitted to the psychiatric unit for the treatment of severe depression. The next day, the client requests to leave the hospital against medical advice (AMA). What is the nurse's *best* understanding of this situation regarding client rights?

A) The client retains the right to request discharge because the admission was voluntary.
B) The client loses the right to leave because of the severe depression diagnosis.
C) The nurse must immediately administer a chemical restraint to prevent elopement.
D) The client must sign a permanent waiver of confidentiality before leaving the facility.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** Clients admitted voluntarily retain their civil rights, including the right to request discharge. The provider will evaluate them for safety, but they legally have the right to make the request.
- **B is incorrect:** A diagnosis of depression alone does not strip a voluntarily admitted client of their rights. Only if they are deemed an imminent danger to themselves or others would a hold be legally initiated.
- **C is incorrect:** Administering a chemical restraint without an immediate, acute safety threat is illegal and constitutes false imprisonment and battery.
- **D is incorrect:** Leaving AMA does not require the client to waive their right to medical confidentiality.

</details>

---

### Question 575
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
The nurse is assessing a client diagnosed with schizophrenia. The provider's note mentions the client is experiencing prominent "negative symptoms." Which finding should the nurse *expect* to observe?

A) Flat affect and lack of motivation (avolition)
B) Auditory and visual hallucinations
C) Flight of ideas and pressured speech
D) Delusions of persecution and paranoia

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** Negative symptoms of schizophrenia are the absence or reduction of normal behaviors. These include flat or blunted affect, avolition (lack of motivation), alogia (poverty of speech), and anhedonia (inability to experience pleasure).
- **B is incorrect:** Hallucinations are "positive symptoms" (the presence of problematic behaviors).
- **C is incorrect:** Flight of ideas and pressured speech are more commonly associated with mania, and disorganized speech is considered a positive symptom of schizophrenia.
- **D is incorrect:** Delusions are "positive symptoms," representing an excess or distortion of normal functioning.

</details>
### Question 576
A client who was recently diagnosed with a terminal illness begins to cry silently in their hospital room. Which action by the nurse demonstrates the best therapeutic communication technique?

A) "Don't cry; everything happens for a reason."
B) "I will just sit here with you for a while." 
C) "Why are you crying when you knew this was a possibility?"
D) "Let me call the chaplain to help you pray."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** B

**Lens:** Therapeutic Communication 
**Why:** Offering self and using silence are highly effective therapeutic communication techniques. Sitting quietly with the client conveys empathy, presence, and acceptance without minimizing their feelings. 

**Distractor Rationale:**
- A: This is false reassurance and uses a cliché, which invalidates the client's feelings.
- C: Asking "why" questions is non-therapeutic because it can make the client feel defensive. 
- D: While a chaplain might be helpful later, offering one immediately deflects the nurse's responsibility to provide emotional support in the moment and makes assumptions about the client's spiritual needs.
</details>

---

### Question 577
A client is admitted to the inpatient psychiatric unit. A person claiming to be the client's sibling calls the nurse's station and asks how the client is doing. The client has not signed a release of information form. What is the appropriate legal and ethical response by the nurse?

A) Confirm the client's presence but refuse to give medical details.
B) Ask the caller to provide the client's date of birth before sharing information.
C) State that you cannot confirm or deny the presence of any client on the unit.
D) Tell the caller to hang up and wait for the client to call them back.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C

**Lens:** Legal/Ethical
**Why:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), a nurse cannot acknowledge whether a client is admitted to a psychiatric facility (or any facility) without the client's explicit, documented consent. Stating that the facility can neither confirm nor deny the client's presence protects the client's privacy and confidentiality.

**Distractor Rationale:**
- A: Confirming the client is on the unit is a direct violation of HIPAA.
- B: Knowing the client's date of birth does not override the need for a signed release of information.
- D: Telling the caller to wait for the client to call back implies that the client is indeed at the facility, which violates confidentiality.
</details>

---

### Question 578
A nurse is assessing a client with major depressive disorder. Which statement by the client indicates the highest immediate risk for self-harm?

A) "I have been having a lot of trouble falling asleep lately."
B) "I feel really sad when I think about my recent divorce."
C) "I sometimes wish I could just go to sleep and never wake up."
D) "I gave my prized guitar collection to my neighbor this morning."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** D

**Lens:** Risk Recognition
**Why:** Giving away prized possessions is a classic behavioral warning sign that a client may have made a decision to commit suicide and is preparing for death. This requires immediate intervention and safety precautions.

**Distractor Rationale:**
- A: Insomnia is a common, expected symptom of depression but does not inherently indicate immediate self-harm risk.
- B: Expressing sadness regarding a difficult life event is an expected psychosocial response and does not directly indicate imminent self-harm.
- C: Passive suicidal ideation (wishing to not wake up) is a concern, but giving away possessions (an active preparatory behavior) represents a higher, more immediate risk of a suicide attempt.
</details>

---

### Question 579
A client is newly prescribed fluoxetine, a selective serotonin reuptake inhibitor (SSRI), for the treatment of generalized anxiety and depression. Which statement should the nurse include in the client teaching?

A) "It may take 4 to 6 weeks before you notice a significant improvement in your mood."
B) "You should stop taking the medication as soon as you feel happy again."
C) "Take this medication only on days when you are feeling extremely depressed."
D) "You will experience immediate relief of your depressive symptoms after the first dose."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** A

**Lens:** Client Teaching
**Why:** SSRIs typically take several weeks (often 4 to 6 weeks) to reach their full therapeutic effect. It is crucial for clients to understand this timeline so they do not become discouraged and stop taking the medication prematurely.

**Distractor Rationale:**
- B: Stopping an SSRI abruptly or discontinuing it just because symptoms improve can lead to a relapse of depression and discontinuation syndrome. 
- C: Antidepressants must be taken consistently every day, not on an "as-needed" (PRN) basis.
- D: SSRIs do not provide immediate relief of symptoms; this is a false statement.
</details>

---

### Question 580
The nurse is caring for a client experiencing moderate anxiety. The nurse guides the client through deep breathing exercises. Which observation indicates that the intervention was effective?

A) The client begins pacing back and forth in the hallway.
B) The client's respiratory rate increases to 26 breaths per minute.
C) The client states, "My heart isn't racing anymore, and I feel calmer."
D) The client repeatedly asks the nurse if the doctor has arrived yet.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C

**Lens:** Evaluation
**Why:** Evaluation focuses on determining if the care provided achieved the desired outcome. Deep breathing exercises are designed to activate the parasympathetic nervous system, reducing physiological and psychological signs of anxiety. A decrease in heart rate and a subjective report of feeling calmer indicate the intervention was successful.

**Distractor Rationale:**
- A: Pacing is an indication of continued psychomotor agitation and unresolved anxiety.
- B: An increased respiratory rate (tachypnea) suggests ongoing or worsening anxiety, not an improvement.
- D: Repeated questioning is a sign of hypervigilance and continuous anxiety.
</details>
### Question 581
A client with major depressive disorder tells the nurse, "I don't see the point in trying anymore. Nothing ever gets better." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "Let's talk about the activities you enjoyed doing yesterday."
B. "You have so much to live for; things will definitely get better."
C. "It sounds like you are feeling very overwhelmed and hopeless right now."
D. "Why do you feel like nothing is going to improve?"

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended)
**Rationale:** Option C uses the therapeutic technique of reflection and empathy. It acknowledges and validates the client's feelings without judgment. Option A changes the subject, which invalidates the client's current distress. Option B offers false reassurance, which is non-therapeutic. Option D uses a "why" question, which can make the client feel defensive.
</details>

---

### Question 582
A client with schizophrenia is admitted voluntarily to the psychiatric unit. After two days, the client becomes upset and demands to leave the hospital immediately. What is the nurse's understanding of this client's legal rights?

A. The client has the right to request discharge because they were admitted voluntarily.
B. The client must remain on the unit for at least 72 hours regardless of admission status.
C. The client loses the right to refuse treatment upon entering a psychiatric facility.
D. The client can only be discharged if a family member agrees to take responsibility.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality/Rights)
**Rationale:** Clients who are voluntarily admitted to a psychiatric facility retain all their civil rights, including the right to request release/discharge. The provider will evaluate the client to ensure they are not an imminent danger to themselves or others before finalizing the discharge, but the right to request it is absolute. Involuntary holds (like a 72-hour hold) only apply if the client meets specific legal criteria for involuntary commitment.
</details>

---

### Question 583
The nurse is reviewing the charts of several clients on a mental health unit. Which client observation places the client at the highest risk for carrying out a suicide attempt?

A. A client with generalized anxiety disorder who frequently seeks reassurance from staff.
B. A client with severe depression who has suddenly become very calm and energetic.
C. A client with bipolar disorder in a manic phase who is pacing the hallways.
D. A client with borderline personality disorder who demands a different room assignment.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?)
**Rationale:** A sudden shift from deep depression to a calm, energetic, or seemingly happy state is a massive red flag for suicide risk. This often indicates that the client has finally made the decision to end their life (bringing relief and calmness) and now has the physical energy to carry out the plan. The other clients display expected symptoms of their respective conditions but do not show immediate, high-risk signs of suicide.
</details>

---

### Question 584
The nurse is teaching a client who has just been diagnosed with a mild anxiety disorder about healthy coping mechanisms. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I will drink a glass of wine every evening to help me relax."
B. "I should avoid all situations that make me feel slightly nervous."
C. "I will try ignoring my anxious feelings until they completely go away."
D. "I plan to go for a 30-minute walk when I start feeling stressed."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Rationale:** Exercise, such as walking, is a positive, healthy coping mechanism that helps reduce stress and anxiety. Using alcohol (Option A) is a maladaptive coping mechanism that can lead to substance use disorders. Avoiding all stressful situations (Option B) is unrealistic and promotes avoidance behavior, which can worsen anxiety. Ignoring feelings (Option C) is suppression or repression, which does not resolve the underlying anxiety.
</details>

---

### Question 585
A client with a history of alcohol use disorder has been participating in a rehabilitation program and attending group therapy for the past month. Which client behavior best indicates that the current psychosocial treatment plan is effective?

A. The client blames their former boss for causing their drinking problem.
B. The client acknowledges that their drinking has negatively impacted their family.
C. The client states they can safely drink one beer on special occasions.
D. The client sits quietly in group therapy without making eye contact.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** EVALUATION (Is the patient stable/improving after care?)
**Rationale:** A key milestone in recovery from substance use disorder is breaking through denial and taking personal responsibility for the addiction and its consequences.
