Acknowledging the negative impact of drinking on their family shows insight and improvement. Blaming others (Option A) shows projection and denial. Believing they can drink safely (Option C) shows a lack of insight into the chronic nature of addiction. Sitting quietly without engagement (Option D) does not demonstrate active progress or insight.
</details>
### Question 586
A client with severe anxiety is pacing the hallway and says to the nurse, "I feel like I'm going to jump out of my skin. I can't calm down!" Which response by the nurse is most therapeutic?

A) "Why do you feel so anxious right now?"
B) "You are safe here; let's walk together for a few minutes."
C) "Try to sit down in your room so you don't exhaust yourself."
D) "Everyone feels nervous when they first arrive at the hospital."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended)
**Rationale:** Offering self ("let's walk together") and providing reassurance of safety are highly therapeutic techniques for a client experiencing severe anxiety. "Why" questions (Option A) can make the client defensive. Telling the client to sit down (Option C) ignores their immediate need to expend anxious energy. Option D belittles the client's feelings by generalizing them.
</details>

---

### Question 587
A nurse is reviewing the histories of several clients attending a community support group. Which client has the highest risk for developing complicated grief?

A) A client whose spouse died suddenly in a tragic motor vehicle accident.
B) A client whose elderly parent passed away after a ten-year decline from dementia.
C) A client who is sad after losing their beloved pet dog of 15 years.
D) A client who is experiencing sorrow after sending their youngest child off to college.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?)
**Rationale:** Complicated (or prolonged) grief is more likely to occur when a loss is sudden, violent, traumatic, or unexpected. While all losses cause grief, anticipating a death (such as after a long illness like dementia) often allows for anticipatory grieving, which can reduce the risk of complicated grief.
</details>

---

### Question 588
The nurse is teaching a client about using deep breathing exercises for stress management. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will practice deep breathing only when I am actively having a panic attack."
B) "I should breathe in through my mouth and exhale quickly out of my nose."
C) "I will inhale slowly through my nose and exhale slowly through my mouth."
D) "Deep breathing works best if I hold my breath for 30 seconds between each breath."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Rationale:** The correct technique for diaphragmatic deep breathing is to inhale slowly and deeply through the nose, and exhale slowly through the mouth (often with pursed lips). Practicing regularly (not just during panic attacks) helps maintain baseline calmness. Holding the breath for 30 seconds can cause hypoxia and increase anxiety.
</details>

---

### Question 589
A client voluntarily admitted to the psychiatric unit for major depressive disorder packs their belongings and states, "I feel fine now, and I want to leave immediately." What is the nurse's legal responsibility?

A) Place the client in a locked room until the psychiatrist evaluates them.
B) Immediately administer an as-needed sedative to calm the client down.
C) Tell the client they are legally required to stay until the doctor discharges them.
D) Notify the healthcare provider and explore the client's reasons for wanting to leave.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality/Rights)
**Rationale:** Clients who are voluntarily admitted retain the right to demand and obtain release. The nurse should notify the provider, explore the client's feelings, and if the client still wishes to leave, provide an Against Medical Advice (AMA) form. False imprisonment (locking them in a room) or chemical restraint without an emergency safety indication violates the client's rights.
</details>

---

### Question 590
A client with anorexia nervosa has been receiving inpatient treatment for two weeks. Which finding best indicates to the nurse that the psychosocial treatment plan is currently effective?

A) The client consumes 80% of their meals without needing prompts.
B) The client wears oversized, baggy clothing to group therapy sessions.
C) The client exercises vigorously in their room immediately after eating.
D) The client hides portions of their food in a napkin during lunchtime.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** EVALUATION (Is the patient stable/improving after care?)
**Rationale:** Consuming a significant portion of meals without prompts is a positive outcome, indicating an improvement in eating behaviors and a decrease in food restriction. Hiding food, exercising vigorously after meals (purging behavior), and wearing baggy clothing (to hide weight loss) are all negative behaviors associated with eating disorders.
</details>
### Question 591
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION 
A nurse enters the room of a client who is crying quietly after being informed of a terminal cancer diagnosis. What is the most therapeutic response by the nurse?

A) "You should try to stay strong for your children."
B) "Everything happens for a reason; you just have to believe."
C) "I can see that you are very upset. I am going to sit here with you."
D) "Please do not cry; there are still experimental treatments available."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Offering self (sitting with the client) and acknowledging the client's feelings ("I can see that you are very upset") are powerful therapeutic communication techniques. They demonstrate empathy, acceptance, and a willingness to support the client without minimizing their pain. Options A, B, and D use non-therapeutic techniques such as giving advice, using clichés, and offering false reassurance, which invalidate the client's feelings.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Therapeutic Communication (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended) - This question tests the nurse's ability to select the response that best fosters a supportive and empathetic environment for a client in distress.
</details>

---

### Question 592
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL 
A client is admitted to the inpatient psychiatric unit for major depressive disorder. A person calls the nursing station, states they are the client's employer, and asks if the client is currently admitted. Which action by the nurse is correct?

A) Confirm that the client is admitted but refuse to provide a room number.
B) Ask the employer to state the client's exact diagnosis before confirming their presence.
C) Transfer the phone call directly to the client's room without answering the question.
D) State that no information can be provided without the client's explicit consent.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA) and ethical principles of confidentiality, the nurse cannot confirm or deny a client's presence in a healthcare facility (especially a psychiatric unit) without the client's explicit consent. Options A, B, and C all violate the client's right to privacy and confidentiality.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Legal/Ethical (Informed consent/Confidentiality/Rights) - This question focuses on the legal rules surrounding patient privacy and the ethical duty to protect mental health records.
</details>

---

### Question 593
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING 
The nurse is teaching a client about healthy coping strategies for mild, everyday anxiety. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will practice deep breathing exercises when I feel my muscles get tense."
B) "I will make sure to avoid any situation that might cause me stress."
C) "I will have a glass of wine to relax my nerves in the evenings."
D) "I will stop taking my prescribed anxiety medication as soon as I feel better."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Deep breathing, relaxation techniques, and mindfulness are healthy, adaptive coping mechanisms for managing mild anxiety. Avoiding all stress (Option B) is unrealistic and maladaptive. Using alcohol to cope (Option C) is maladaptive and can lead to substance use disorders. Stopping medications abruptly (Option D) is dangerous and non-compliant with medical treatment.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching (Do they understand the basics?) - This question assesses if the client has grasped the fundamental, safe coping strategies taught by the nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 594
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION 
A client with a history of severe major depressive disorder has suddenly become highly energetic and is giving away cherished personal belongings. The nurse recognizes that this client is at highest risk for which of the following?

A) Developing delirium
B) A suicide attempt
C) Medication noncompliance
D) A manic episode

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A sudden lifting of mood or increase in energy in a severely depressed client, combined with giving away prized possessions, is a major warning sign for an impending suicide attempt. The client now has the energy to carry out a suicide plan and may feel "at peace" because they have made the decision to end their life.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?) - This question tests the nurse's ability to identify crucial psychosocial warning signs that place a client at immediate risk of self-harm.
</details>

---

### Question 595
**Lens:** EVALUATION 
A client with moderate dementia is pacing the hallways, wringing their hands, and appears highly agitated. The nurse plays soft, familiar music and dims the harsh overhead lights in the dayroom. Which observation best indicates that the intervention was effective?

A) The client begins yelling at the nursing staff.
B) The client continues to walk rapidly up and down the hallway.
C) The client sits quietly in a comfortable chair and looks at a picture book.
D) The client repeatedly attempts to open the locked exit doors.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens Explanation</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Decreasing environmental stimuli (dimming lights, playing soft music) is a standard intervention to reduce agitation in clients with dementia. The intervention is effective if the client displays calm, relaxed behavior, such as sitting quietly with a book. Yelling, rapid pacing, and exit-seeking behaviors (Options A, B, and D) indicate continued or escalating agitation.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Evaluation (Is the patient stable/improving after care?) - This question evaluates the outcome of a non-pharmacological nursing intervention aimed at altering an agitated client's psychosocial behavior.
</details>
### Question 596
A client recently diagnosed with a terminal illness is crying and says, "Why is this happening to me? It is just not fair." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "Everything happens for a reason, even if we do not understand it."
B. "This must be incredibly difficult for you. I am here to listen."
C. "You should talk to the hospital chaplain about how you are feeling."
D. "Many people go through this and find a way to cope eventually."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: B**
* **Lens: THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION**
* **Psychosocial Integrity:** Active listening and offering self are foundational therapeutic communication techniques. Validating the client's feelings and offering an empathetic presence encourages the client to openly express their grief and fears.
* **Incorrect Options:**
    * **A:** This is a cliché and minimizes the client's genuine distress, acting as a communication block.
    * **C:** While a chaplain may be a helpful resource later, this response passes the buck and fails to address the client's immediate emotional needs.
    * **D:** This minimizes the client's unique experience and utilizes false reassurance, which halts further communication.

</details>

---

### Question 597
The nurse is reviewing the charts of clients in a community health clinic. Which client is at the highest risk for experiencing complicated grief?

A. A 20-year-old whose childhood pet dog died peacefully of old age.
B. A 30-year-old whose grandfather passed away after a decade-long battle with dementia.
C. A 45-year-old who is sad because a close friend recently moved to another country.
D. A 60-year-old whose spouse of 40 years died suddenly in a motor vehicle accident.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: D**
* **Lens: RISK RECOGNITION**
* **Psychosocial Integrity:** Complicated grief is prolonged, intensely painful, and debilitating. Risk factors for complicated grief include sudden, unexpected, or traumatic deaths, the loss of a spouse or child, and heavy dependence on the deceased. The 60-year-old who lost their long-term spouse suddenly fits this high-risk profile.
* **Incorrect Options:**
    * **A, B, C:** These clients are experiencing loss, but expected deaths (old age, chronic terminal illness) and non-death losses (moving) typically lead to normal, uncomplicated grief processes rather than putting the client at the highest risk for complicated grief.

</details>

---

### Question 598
A nurse is providing education to a client prescribed a new selective serotonin reuptake inhibitor (SSRI) for depression. Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching was successful?

A. "I will feel completely better by tomorrow morning."
B. "I should stop taking this medication as soon as I feel happy."
C. "It may take several weeks before I notice an improvement in my mood."
D. "I must avoid eating aged cheese and cured meats while on this drug."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: C**
* **Lens: CLIENT TEACHING**
* **Psychosocial Integrity:** SSRIs (like sertraline, fluoxetine, or citalopram) typically take 2 to 4 weeks (sometimes up to 6 weeks) to reach therapeutic levels and produce a noticeable improvement in depressive symptoms. Educating the client on this timeline prevents them from stopping the medication prematurely due to a perceived lack of efficacy.
* **Incorrect Options:**
    * **A:** Antidepressants do not work overnight; establishing realistic expectations is crucial.
    * **B:** Antidepressants should not be abruptly discontinued, even when symptoms improve, as this can lead to relapse or withdrawal syndrome.
    * **D:** Dietary restrictions of tyramine (aged cheeses, cured meats) are required for Monoamine Oxidase Inhibitors (MAOIs), not SSRIs.

</details>

---

### Question 599
A client who was voluntarily admitted to the inpatient psychiatric unit states, "I changed my mind. I want to leave the hospital right now." Which statement represents the nurse's best understanding of this client's legal rights?

A. The client has the right to request a discharge since they were admitted voluntarily.
B. The client must stay for a mandatory 72 hours before requesting to leave.
C. The client loses all right to refuse treatment once admitted to the unit.
D. The client can only leave if a family member signs them out of the facility.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: A**
* **Lens: LEGAL/ETHICAL**
* **Psychosocial Integrity:** A client who is voluntarily admitted to a psychiatric facility retains all their civil rights, including the right to demand and obtain release. They must formally request discharge, at which point the provider evaluates them to ensure they are not an imminent danger to themselves or others before allowing them to leave.
* **Incorrect Options:**
    * **B:** A mandatory 72-hour hold applies to *involuntary* admissions (often called a 5150 or emergency hold), not voluntary admissions.
    * **C:** Voluntary clients retain the right to refuse treatment, including medications.
    * **D:** An adult, competent client does not need a family member's permission to be discharged.

</details>

---

### Question 600
A client with severe anxiety has been practicing deep breathing exercises and guided imagery for the past week. Which finding indicates to the nurse that these interventions have been effective?

A. The client is pacing the hallway while rapidly wringing their hands.
B. The client reports sleeping continuously for 16 hours a day.
C. The client states they are still completely unable to concentrate on conversations.
D. The client reports feeling relaxed and is actively participating in group therapy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: D**
* **Lens: EVALUATION**
* **Psychosocial Integrity:** The goal of relaxation techniques (like deep breathing and guided imagery) is to reduce anxiety to a manageable level. Feeling relaxed and participating in therapeutic activities (like group therapy) demonstrates improved coping and a decrease in severe anxiety.
* **Incorrect Options:**
    * **A:** Pacing and hand-wringing are physical manifestations of severe anxiety or psychomotor agitation.
    * **B:** Hypersomnia (sleeping excessively) can be a symptom of depression or an avoidance mechanism, not an indicator of healthy anxiety reduction.
    * **C:** An inability to concentrate is a classic symptom of severe anxiety and indicates the interventions have not yet been fully effective.

</details>
### Question 601
**Lens: THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended)**

A client with a new diagnosis of major depressive disorder says to the nurse, "I just feel so empty, like I have absolutely nothing left to give." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "Why do you feel like you have nothing to give?"
B. "It sounds like you are feeling completely drained right now."
C. "You will feel a lot better once the antidepressants start working."
D. "Everyone feels sad sometimes; this feeling will pass."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Restating and reflecting ("It sounds like you are feeling completely drained right now") is a therapeutic communication technique that shows empathy and helps the client feel heard and validated. Asking "Why" (Option A) can make the client defensive. Option C provides false reassurance, and Option D minimizes and belittles the client's feelings.
</details>

---

### Question 602
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)**

The nurse is teaching a client about a new prescription for fluoxetine, a selective serotonin reuptake inhibitor (SSRI). Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I should notice my mood improving by tomorrow morning."
B. "I will stop taking this medication immediately if I experience mild nausea."
C. "It may take up to four weeks for me to feel the full effect of this medicine."
D. "I need to avoid eating any aged cheeses or cured meats while on this pill."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** SSRIs take time to build up in the system, typically taking 2 to 4 weeks (sometimes up to 6 weeks) for the client to feel the full therapeutic effect. Clients should be taught not to expect immediate results (Option A) and should not stop taking the medication abruptly for mild side effects (Option B). Dietary restrictions involving tyramine (aged cheeses, cured meats) are required for Monoamine Oxidase Inhibitors (MAOIs), not SSRIs (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 603
**Lens: LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality/Rights)**

A client voluntarily admitted to the psychiatric unit for generalized anxiety requests to leave against medical advice (AMA). Which action should the nurse take?

A. Provide the client with the AMA form to sign and notify the provider.
B. Inform the client that a voluntary admission automatically requires a 72-hour hold.
C. Place the client in seclusion until the healthcare provider arrives.
D. Refuse the request because the client has a documented mental health diagnosis.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** A voluntary admission means the client retains all civil rights, including the right to refuse treatment and leave against medical advice (AMA), as long as they are not an imminent danger to themselves or others. The nurse should follow facility protocol, which includes explaining the risks, notifying the provider, and having the client sign the AMA form.
</details>

---

### Question 604
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?)**

The nurse is reviewing the charts of several clients on an inpatient mental health unit. Which client is at the greatest risk for injury?

A. A client with generalized anxiety disorder who reports feeling overwhelmed at work.
B. A client with obsessive-compulsive disorder who washes their hands 20 times a day.
C. A client with bipolar disorder currently in the manic phase who hasn't slept in 12 hours.
D. A client with schizophrenia who is hearing a voice telling them to harm themselves.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Command hallucinations (voices instructing the client to perform specific acts, such as harming themselves or others) represent a psychiatric emergency. This puts the client at an immediate, high risk for suicide or severe injury. The other clients require care and intervention but are not displaying immediate life-threatening symptoms.
</details>

---

### Question 605
**Lens: EVALUATION (Is the patient stable/improving after care?)**

A client with anorexia nervosa has been participating in an inpatient treatment program for two weeks. Which finding best indicates that the client's psychosocial interventions are effective?

A. The client exercises for two hours in their room after meals.
B. The client sits with peers in the dining room and completes 75% of their meal.
C. The client refuses to look in the mirror while getting dressed in the morning.
D. The client hides small portions of food in their pockets during lunch.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Completing a significant portion of a meal in a social setting (the dining room) demonstrates compliance with the treatment plan, a decrease in meal-related anxiety, and positive social interaction. Hiding food, excessive exercising, and severe body image avoidance (refusing to look in the mirror) are signs of ongoing pathology and indicate that the interventions are not yet fully effective.
</details>
### Question 606
A client with severe anxiety is pacing the room and says to the nurse, "I just can't stop worrying about my job. I feel like I'm going to lose it and everything is ruined." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "You should try to find a new job if this one is causing you so much stress."
B) "Everything will work out fine once you calm down and get some rest."
C) "It sounds like you are feeling very overwhelmed right now."
D) "Why do you think your boss wants to fire you?"

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended)

**Rationale:** Option C is an empathetic reflection of the client's feelings, which shows active listening and encourages the client to continue sharing without judgment. Option A gives unwanted advice, which is non-therapeutic. Option B offers false reassurance, which minimizes the client's feelings. Option D asks a "why" question, which can make the client feel defensive.

</details>

---

### Question 607
The nurse is assessing a group of clients for their risk of experiencing a situational crisis. Which client is at the highest risk?

A) A young adult preparing to graduate from college next month.
B) A client who recently experienced the unexpected death of their spouse.
C) A teenager experiencing significant mood swings related to puberty.
D) An older adult retiring after 40 years of working for the same company.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?)

**Rationale:** A situational crisis arises from an external, unexpected, and often sudden event, such as the sudden death of a loved one, loss of a job, or a severe illness. Options A, C, and D are examples of maturational (developmental) crises, which occur during normal, expected life transitions across the lifespan.

</details>

---

### Question 608
A client is admitted to the psychiatric unit for major depressive disorder. The client's neighbor calls the nurses' station and asks, "Is John doing okay? I noticed he wasn't home, and I want to send him some flowers." Which response by the nurse is correct?

A) "He is currently resting, but I cannot give out specific medical information."
B) "John is doing well but cannot come to the phone right now."
C) "I can only tell you that he is stable and resting comfortably."
D) "I cannot confirm or deny whether that person is a patient here."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** LEGAL/ETHICAL (Informed consent/Confidentiality/Rights)

**Rationale:** Under the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), the nurse must protect the client's privacy and confidentiality. The nurse cannot acknowledge that the client is admitted to the facility without the client's explicit consent. Options A, B, and C are direct breaches of confidentiality because they confirm the client's presence at the facility.

</details>

---

### Question 609
The nurse is teaching a client about healthy coping mechanisms to manage daily stress. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will take a 30-minute walk outside when I feel overwhelmed."
B) "I will ignore the things that stress me out until they go away."
C) "I will have a glass of wine every night to help me unwind."
D) "I will stop spending time with my friends so I can focus on my worries."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

**Rationale:** Taking a walk is a healthy, adaptive coping mechanism that helps reduce stress and improve mental well-being through physical activity. Option B represents avoidance, Option C represents reliance on substances (maladaptive coping), and Option D represents social isolation, all of which worsen stress and mental health over time.

</details>

---

### Question 610
A client with severe depression has been receiving treatment on the inpatient psychiatric unit for one week. Which observation best indicates to the nurse that the client's condition is improving?

A) The client sleeps 14 hours a day in their room.
B) The client initiates a conversation with another peer in the dayroom.
C) The client eats less than 25% of their meals.
D) The client refuses to attend group therapy sessions.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** EVALUATION (Is the patient stable/improving after care?)

**Rationale:** Initiating social interaction with peers indicates an improvement in mood, energy levels, and social engagement, which are common goals in the treatment of depression. Sleeping excessively (hypersomnia), poor appetite, and social isolation/refusing therapy are all ongoing symptoms of depression, indicating that further intervention is needed.

</details>

# SECTION: Health Promotion and Maintenance 

### Question 611
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
A nurse is assessing several older adult clients at a community health center. Which client has the highest risk for sustaining an injury from a fall?

A) A 70-year-old who walks daily for cardiovascular exercise.
B) A 68-year-old who occasionally uses reading glasses for a newspaper.
C) A 75-year-old taking a daily loop diuretic and a nightly sedative.
D) A 72-year-old who recently installed grab bars in the bathroom shower.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Taking multiple medications (polypharmacy) significantly increases the risk of falls in older adults. Diuretics can cause urinary urgency (prompting the client to rush to the bathroom), and sedatives can cause drowsiness, dizziness, and impaired coordination. The other options describe healthy habits or safety modifications that actually decrease fall risk.
</details>

---

### Question 612
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to new parents about sudden infant death syndrome (SIDS) prevention. Which statement by the parents indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "We bought a soft, plush crib bumper to prevent our baby from getting head bumps."
B) "We will cover the baby with a thick quilt to keep them warm during the night."
C) "We will put the baby to sleep on their stomach immediately after feeding."
D) "We will place our baby on their back every time they go to sleep."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The most critical step in preventing SIDS is placing the infant on their back (supine) for all sleep times. Placing a baby on their stomach (prone), using soft bedding, heavy quilts, or crib bumpers all significantly increase the risk of suffocation and SIDS.
</details>

---

### Question 613
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
A nurse is assessing an 18-month-old toddler during a routine well-child visit. Which of the following findings is considered a normal, expected developmental milestone for a child of this age?

A) The child can build a tower of three to four blocks.
B) The child can independently ride a small tricycle.
C) The child speaks in full, complex five-word sentences.
D) The child can tie their own shoelaces without assistance.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
At 18 months of age, an expected fine motor milestone is the ability to build a tower of three to four blocks. Riding a tricycle is expected around age 3; speaking in complex sentences typically develops between ages 3 and 4; and tying shoelaces is usually achieved around age 5.
</details>

---

### Question 614
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
A client at a primary care clinic tells the nurse, "I know I should quit smoking to improve my health, but I've tried three times and always failed. It feels impossible." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "You just need to have more willpower this time around."
B) "Quitting is very difficult. Tell me more about what happened the other times you tried."
C) "Why do you think you weren't able to quit during your previous attempts?"
D) "Don't worry, there are plenty of medications that will make it easy for you."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
This response is therapeutic because it validates the client's feelings and acknowledges that quitting is hard (empathy), while using an open-ended statement to encourage the client to share their specific barriers. Asking "why" (Option C) can sound confrontational or judgmental. Giving unsolicited advice (Option A) or false reassurance (Option D) shuts down meaningful communication.
</details>

---

### Question 615
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
A nurse is teaching a young adult client how to perform a breast self-examination (BSE) at home for routine health maintenance. What is the first physical step the client should take when beginning the examination?

A) Palpate the underarm area for enlarged lymph nodes.
B) Lie down comfortably with a pillow placed under the right shoulder.
C) Look at the breasts in a mirror with the arms resting at the sides.
D) Gently squeeze each nipple to check for unexpected, clear discharge.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The first step in a breast self-examination is visual inspection. The client should begin by looking at their breasts in a mirror with their arms at their sides to check for normal symmetry, dimpling, redness, or skin changes. Palpation and checking for discharge occur after the visual inspection is complete.
</details>
### Question 616
The nurse is assessing a healthy 2-month-old infant during a well-child visit. Which finding places the infant at the highest risk for Sudden Infant Death Syndrome (SIDS)?

A. The infant sleeps on a firm mattress in a crib.
B. The infant is put to sleep in a prone position.
C. The infant uses a pacifier at bedtime.
D. The infant sleeps in the parents' room in a separate bassinet.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
**Category:** Health Promotion and Maintenance

Placing an infant to sleep in a prone (on the stomach) position is a major, modifiable risk factor for Sudden Infant Death Syndrome (SIDS). To promote health and safety, infants should always be placed on their backs (supine) to sleep. Options A, C, and D are all considered protective factors or safe sleep practices that actually reduce the risk of SIDS.
</details>

---

### Question 617
A nurse is reviewing the immunization record of a healthy client who is 12 weeks pregnant. Which vaccine is contraindicated for this client?

A. Inactivated influenza vaccine
B. Tetanus, diphtheria, and pertussis (Tdap) vaccine
C. Measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine
D. Recombinant hepatitis B vaccine

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CONTRAINDICATION
**Category:** Health Promotion and Maintenance

The MMR vaccine is a live attenuated virus vaccine and is strictly contraindicated during pregnancy due to the theoretical risk of transmitting the virus to the developing fetus, which could cause congenital anomalies. The inactivated influenza, Tdap, and Hepatitis B vaccines are safe and often recommended during pregnancy to promote maternal and fetal health. 
</details>

---

### Question 618
The nurse is assessing an 82-year-old client during a routine physical examination. Which of the following is considered an expected, age-related physiological change?

A. Increased sense of thirst
B. Decreased resting heart rate to 45 beats per minute
C. New-onset urinary incontinence
D. Decreased skin elasticity and thinning of the epidermis

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Category:** Health Promotion and Maintenance

Decreased skin elasticity, loss of subcutaneous fat, and thinning of the epidermis are all expected, normal physiological changes of aging. An increased sense of thirst is unexpected (older adults typically have a decreased sense of thirst). A resting heart rate of 45 bpm is bradycardia and requires further evaluation. New-onset urinary incontinence is not a normal part of aging and indicates an underlying issue that needs assessment.
</details>

---

### Question 619
A nurse is teaching a 30-year-old female client about breast health and awareness. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of when to perform a breast self-examination?

A. "I will examine my breasts 3 to 5 days after my period starts."
B. "I should check my breasts on the first day of my menstrual bleeding."
C. "The best time to do the exam is right before my period begins."
D. "I need to perform the exam every day while taking a shower."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Category:** Health Promotion and Maintenance

The ideal time to perform a breast self-examination (BSE) is 3 to 5 days after the menstrual period begins, or right after the period ends. During this time, the breasts are less tender, swollen, and lumpy due to lower hormone levels, making it easier to detect any abnormal changes. Examining them right before or during the first day of menstruation can lead to false concern due to normal hormonal swelling.
</details>

---

### Question 620
During a routine wellness screening, a 45-year-old client's height and weight are measured, resulting in a Body Mass Index (BMI) of 22.5 kg/m². How should the nurse interpret this data?

A. Underweight
B. Normal weight
C. Overweight
D. Obese

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
**Category:** Health Promotion and Maintenance

A Body Mass Index (BMI) of 22.5 kg/m² falls into the "Normal weight" category. The standard BMI categories are: Underweight (less than 18.5), Normal weight (18.5 to 24.9), Overweight (25 to 29.9), and Obese (30 or greater). Recognizing normal baselines is a key component of health promotion and maintenance.
</details>
### Question 621
A nurse is teaching the parent of a 6-month-old infant about the introduction of solid foods. Which statement by the parent indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should mix rice cereal into the baby's bottle at night to help them sleep longer."
B) "I will introduce one new food at a time and wait 3 to 5 days before trying another."
C) "I can start giving my baby small amounts of regular cow's milk with meals."
D) "I should offer solid foods first before giving breast milk or formula."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Scientific Rationale:** When introducing solid foods to an infant, parents should introduce only one new, single-ingredient food at a time and wait 3 to 5 days before introducing another. This allows the parent to easily identify any food allergies or intolerances.
A is incorrect because mixing cereal in a bottle is a choking hazard and does not promote healthy sleep habits. C is incorrect because cow's milk should not be introduced until 12 months of age due to the risk of intestinal bleeding and iron deficiency. D is incorrect because breast milk or formula should remain the primary source of nutrition at this age, and offering them first ensures the infant gets adequate calories and nutrients.

</details>

---

### Question 622
A nurse is reviewing the health history of a young adult client during an annual wellness exam. The nurse calculates the client's Body Mass Index (BMI) to be 22.5. How should the nurse interpret this data?

A) The client is classified as underweight.
B) The client is classified as overweight.
C) The client is at a normal, healthy weight.
D) The client is classified as obese.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
**Scientific Rationale:** A Body Mass Index (BMI) between 18.5 and 24.9 is considered a normal, healthy weight for adults. Therefore, a BMI of 22.5 falls squarely within the expected healthy range.
A is incorrect because underweight is defined as a BMI less than 18.5. B is incorrect because overweight is defined as a BMI between 25.0 and 29.9. D is incorrect because obesity is defined as a BMI of 30.0 or higher.

</details>

---

### Question 623
A nurse is conducting health screenings at a community center. Which client should the nurse recognize as being at the highest risk for developing osteoporosis?

A) A 65-year-old postmenopausal woman who smokes and leads a sedentary lifestyle.
B) A 40-year-old man who runs marathons and eats a high-protein diet.
C) A 30-year-old pregnant woman who takes daily prenatal vitamins.
D) A 50-year-old man who lifts weights three times a week and consumes dairy products.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
**Scientific Rationale:** Osteoporosis is a disease characterized by low bone mass and structural deterioration of bone tissue. Risk factors include advanced age, female gender, postmenopausal status (due to a drop in estrogen), sedentary lifestyle, smoking, and inadequate calcium/vitamin D intake. The 65-year-old woman has multiple compounding risk factors.
Weight-bearing exercise (such as running or weightlifting) and adequate nutrition (like dairy and prenatal vitamins) are protective factors against osteoporosis, making options B, C, and D lower-risk profiles.

</details>

---

### Question 624
A client visiting an outpatient clinic expresses a desire to quit smoking but tells the nurse, "I've tried quitting three times before, and I always end up starting again. It feels useless." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "You just need to find the right nicotine patch and stick with it this time."
B) "Most people fail a few times before they finally quit for good."
C) "Why do you think you keep going back to smoking when you try to quit?"
D) "It sounds like you are feeling discouraged. What were the challenges you faced during your previous attempts?"

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
**Scientific Rationale:** Therapeutic communication involves active listening, showing empathy, and using open-ended questions to explore the client's feelings and experiences. Option D validates the client's feelings of discouragement and gently opens a dialogue to identify barriers, which is essential for developing a successful smoking cessation plan.
A is dismissive and gives unwarranted advice. B minimizes the client's personal frustration. C uses the word "why," which can sound accusatory and make the client defensive.

</details>

---

### Question 625
A nurse is performing a routine physical assessment on a 2-day-old, full-term newborn of African American descent. Which of the following findings should the nurse identify as an expected variation?

A) A resting heart rate of 85 beats per minute while awake.
B) Sternal retractions and nasal flaring during breathing.
C) A flat, bluish-gray pigmented area on the lower back and buttocks.
D) A yellowish tint to the skin and sclera that appeared at 12 hours of life.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Scientific Rationale:** A flat, bluish-gray pigmented area on the lower back and buttocks is known as a Mongolian spot (or slate grey nevus). This is a completely benign, expected variation commonly seen in newborns of African, Asian, Hispanic, and Native American descent. It requires no treatment and usually fades over time.
A is unexpected (normal newborn heart rate is 110-160 bpm; 85 is bradycardia). B is unexpected as it indicates respiratory distress. D is unexpected because jaundice appearing within the first 24 hours of life is considered pathologic and requires immediate medical evaluation.

</details>
### Question 626
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?)

A nurse is conducting a home assessment for an older adult client to promote safety and independence. Which finding in the home environment presents the greatest risk for a fall?

A. Throw rugs placed over the hardwood floors in the living room
B. Grab bars securely installed in the shower and near the toilet
C. Small nightlights placed along the hallway connecting the bedroom and bathroom
D. A sturdy handrail running the entire length of the staircase

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** Throw rugs are a major tripping hazard for older adults, especially those with unsteady gaits or who use assistive devices. To promote health and safety in the home, throw rugs should be removed or securely taped down. Grab bars, nightlights, and handrails are all excellent safety modifications that decrease the risk of falls.

</details>

---

### Question 627
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)

A nurse is teaching a new parent about safe sleep practices for their newborn to prevent Sudden Infant Death Syndrome (SIDS). Which statement by the parent indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "I should place a soft, warm blanket over the baby while they sleep."
B. "I will place the baby flat on their back to sleep."
C. "I can put a small stuffed animal in the crib to keep the baby calm."
D. "I will use a padded crib bumper to protect the baby's head from the rails."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** To reduce the risk of SIDS, infants should always be placed on their backs to sleep on a firm mattress. Soft bedding, blankets, stuffed animals, and crib bumpers (Options A, C, and D) pose suffocation and strangulation hazards and should never be placed in a newborn's sleep environment.

</details>

---

### Question 628
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic assessment/lab mean?)

A nurse is assessing a 6-month-old infant during a routine well-child visit. Based on normal growth and development, which milestone does the nurse expect the infant to demonstrate?

A. Walking while holding onto the edge of furniture
B. Speaking in two-to-three-word sentences
C. Rolling over from back to front and sitting with support
D. Building a tower of four to six blocks

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** By 6 months of age, infants typically develop the gross motor skills to roll over in both directions (front to back, back to front) and can sit up with support. Walking while holding onto furniture (cruising) occurs closer to 10-12 months. Speaking in short sentences and building block towers are toddler milestones (18-24 months).

</details>

---

### Question 629
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended)

A client discussing lifestyle modifications tells the nurse, "I know I should quit smoking, but I’ve tried to stop before and it is just too hard for me." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "You really need to quit soon if you want to avoid severe lung problems."
B. "Why do you think it is so hard for you to stick to quitting?"
C. "Don't worry, many people fail the first few times they try to quit."
D. "Let's talk about what worked and didn't work when you tried to quit before."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Rationale:** Option D is an open-ended, non-judgmental response that encourages the client to reflect on past experiences, empowering them to find a successful strategy this time. Option A uses scare tactics. Option B uses "Why," which can make the client feel defensive. Option C minimizes the client's feelings and uses the negative word "fail."

</details>

---

### Question 630
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP (What is the first physical step in a skill?)

A nurse is teaching a client how to perform a routine monthly breast self-examination (BSE). What is the first physical step the client should take when beginning the exam?

A. Palpate the breasts using the pads of the three middle fingers
B. Stand in front of a mirror and visually inspect the breasts with arms at the sides
C. Gently squeeze the nipples to check for any clear or bloody discharge
D. Lie down flat on a bed and place a small pillow under the right shoulder

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** The first step of a breast self-examination is visual inspection. The client should stand in front of a mirror with their arms relaxed at their sides to look for any changes in symmetry, shape, skin dimpling, or nipple changes. Palpation (Options A, C, and D) comes after the visual inspection step.

</details>
### Question 631
A nurse is educating a 45-year-old female client about breast cancer screening guidelines. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I only need a mammogram if I find a lump during my self-exam."
B) "I should have a clinical breast exam every 5 years."
C) "I should schedule a screening mammogram every year."
D) "I do not need any screenings because I have no family history."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Rationale:** According to general health promotion guidelines (such as the American Cancer Society), women aged 45 to 54 should get mammograms every year. Screening is recommended for all women, regardless of family history, to detect breast cancer early before symptoms or lumps appear.
</details>

---

### Question 632
A nurse is assessing a 2-day-old newborn and notes tiny, white, pinhead-sized cysts on the infant's nose and chin. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) This is a normal finding called milia that will resolve on its own.
B) This indicates an allergic reaction to the hospital formula.
C) This is a sign of a bacterial skin infection requiring antibiotics.
D) This suggests the newborn has neonatal herpes simplex virus.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)
**Rationale:** Milia are small, white sebaceous cysts commonly found on a newborn's nose, chin, and forehead. They are an expected, normal finding that requires no treatment and will disappear spontaneously within a few weeks.
</details>

---

### Question 633
A home health nurse is assessing the living environment of an 82-year-old client. Which finding places the client at the greatest risk for a fall?

A) A brightly lit hallway leading to the bathroom.
B) Grab bars installed securely inside the shower stall.
C) Electrical cords secured flat against the baseboards.
D) Small, unsecured throw rugs placed over the hardwood floors.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?)
**Rationale:** Unsecured throw rugs are a major tripping hazard for older adults and significantly increase the risk of falls in the home. The home environment should be cleared of clutter and loose rugs to promote safety and mobility. The other options are positive safety measures.
</details>

---

### Question 634
A nurse is reviewing the immunization record of a healthy 2-month-old infant. The infant received the Hepatitis B vaccine at birth. Based on the recommended schedule, which vaccine is appropriate to administer at this visit?

A) Measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR)
B) Diphtheria, tetanus, and acellular pertussis (DTaP)
C) Varicella (chickenpox)
D) Human papillomavirus (HPV)

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic assessment/lab mean?)
**Rationale:** The DTaP vaccine is part of the standard 2-month immunization schedule. MMR and Varicella are live vaccines typically administered at 12 to 15 months of age. The HPV vaccine is recommended starting at 9 to 11 years of age.
</details>

---

### Question 635
A 50-year-old client who was recently told they need to lose weight to manage their blood pressure says to the nurse, "I've tried every diet out there, and I always just gain the weight back. It's hopeless." What is the most therapeutic response by the nurse?

A) "It sounds like you are feeling really frustrated with your past weight loss attempts."
B) "You just need to find the right diet and stick to it this time."
C) "Why do you think the other diets didn't work for you?"
D) "Don't worry, your doctor will prescribe a medication to help you."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended)
**Rationale:** Option A uses reflection and validates the client's feelings of frustration. This builds trust and encourages the client to communicate more openly. Asking "why" can make the client defensive, offering false reassurance is unhelpful, and giving advice blocks therapeutic communication.
</details>
### Question 636
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Topic:** Health Promotion and Maintenance - Newborn Assessment

A nurse is performing a routine assessment on a 2-day-old newborn. Which of the following findings is considered an expected, normal variation for a newborn?

A) A respiratory rate of 75 breaths per minute at rest
B) Central cyanosis around the lips and mucous membranes
C) Tiny white bumps across the nose and cheeks
D) Complete absence of the Moro (startle) reflex

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Tiny white bumps across the nose and cheeks

**Rationale:** 
Tiny white bumps on a newborn's nose and cheeks are called milia. These are exposed sebaceous glands and are an expected, harmless finding that resolves without treatment. A resting respiratory rate of 75 is tachypnea (normal is 30-60 breaths per minute). Central cyanosis is an unexpected sign of hypoxia. The absence of the Moro reflex is an unexpected neurological finding.
</details>

---

### Question 637
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Topic:** Health Promotion and Maintenance - Prenatal Care

A nurse is providing health promotion teaching to a pregnant client who has been prescribed an oral iron supplement. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will take my iron supplement with a glass of orange juice."
B) "I should take the iron pill with a large glass of milk."
C) "I will stop taking the iron if my bowel movements turn dark."
D) "I can crush the pill and mix it into a cup of hot black tea."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "I will take my iron supplement with a glass of orange juice."

**Rationale:** 
Vitamin C, which is abundant in orange juice, enhances the absorption of dietary and supplemental iron. Taking iron with milk (calcium) or black tea (tannins) can decrease its absorption. Dark or black stools are an expected, harmless side effect of iron supplementation, so the client should not stop taking the medication if this occurs.
</details>

---

### Question 638
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Topic:** Health Promotion and Maintenance - Disease Management

The nurse is instructing an older adult client on how to obtain accurate daily weights at home to monitor fluid status. What is the most important procedural step the client should follow?

A) Weigh yourself wearing heavy shoes to ensure the scale registers.
B) Weigh yourself right after eating your largest meal of the day.
C) Weigh yourself only on the days you feel short of breath.
D) Weigh yourself at the same time every morning after emptying your bladder.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Weigh yourself at the same time every morning after emptying your bladder.

**Rationale:** 
To obtain an accurate daily weight trend, clients should weigh themselves at the same time each day (preferably in the morning), using the same scale, after voiding, and while wearing similar clothing. This ensures the weight reflects true fluid status rather than variations in clothing, food intake, or a full bladder.
</details>

---

### Question 639
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
**Topic:** Health Promotion and Maintenance - Health Screening

A nurse is reviewing vital signs and health screening data for a 45-year-old client during an annual physical. The client has a Body Mass Index (BMI) of 22. How should the nurse interpret this data?

A) The client is considered underweight.
B) The client is within a healthy, normal weight range.
C) The client is considered overweight.
D) The client is considered obese.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The client is within a healthy, normal weight range.

**Rationale:** 
A BMI between 18.5 and 24.9 is classified as a normal, healthy weight. A BMI less than 18.5 is considered underweight. A BMI between 25 and 29.9 is considered overweight, and a BMI of 30 or greater is classified as obese. 
</details>

---

### Question 640
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION
**Topic:** Health Promotion and Maintenance - Aging Process

A 52-year-old client tells the clinic nurse, "I am really struggling with getting older and going through menopause. I feel like my body is completely failing me." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "Every woman goes through menopause eventually, so try not to worry."
B) "Why do you think your body is failing you right now?"
C) "It sounds like you are feeling overwhelmed by the changes in your body."
D) "You should try exercising more; it will make you feel a lot younger."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "It sounds like you are feeling overwhelmed by the changes in your body."

**Rationale:** 
This statement uses the therapeutic technique of reflection and empathy. It acknowledges the client's feelings and opens the door for further exploration without judgment. Option A minimizes the client's feelings. Option B uses "Why," which can make the client defensive. Option D gives unsolicited advice, which blocks therapeutic communication.
</details>
### Question 641
The nurse is conducting a community health screening. Which client is at the highest risk for developing osteoporosis?

A. A 65-year-old postmenopausal female with a petite frame.
B. A 40-year-old male who jogs 3 miles every morning.
C. A 25-year-old female who takes daily prenatal vitamins.
D. A 55-year-old male who is overweight and plays golf.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION

**Rationale:** Postmenopausal females, especially those with small, petite frames, are at the highest risk for developing osteoporosis due to a decrease in protective estrogen levels and a naturally lower peak bone mass. Weight-bearing exercises, such as jogging, actually help strengthen bones and prevent osteoporosis. While being overweight increases the risk for osteoarthritis, it places more stress on the bones, which generally leads to higher bone density and lowers the risk of osteoporosis. Daily vitamins containing calcium and vitamin D promote bone health.
</details>

---

### Question 642
The nurse is assessing a 2-day-old full-term newborn. Which finding should the nurse document as an expected, normal variation?

A. A resting heart rate of 78 beats per minute.
B. A resting respiratory rate of 80 breaths per minute.
C. Bluish discoloration of the hands and feet.
D. A yellowish tint to the skin that appeared 6 hours after birth.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED

**Rationale:** Acrocyanosis (bluish discoloration of the hands and feet) is a normal, expected finding in newborns during the first 24 to 48 hours of life due to immature peripheral circulation. A resting heart rate of 78 bpm is considered bradycardia in a newborn (normal is 110–160 bpm). A respiratory rate of 80 breaths per minute is tachypnea (normal is 30–60 breaths per minute). Jaundice appearing within the first 24 hours of life is pathological and requires immediate medical evaluation.
</details>

---

### Question 643
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to the parents of a healthy newborn regarding car seat safety. Which statement by the parents indicates correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "The car seat can be placed in the front passenger seat if the airbag is turned on."
B. "We should turn the car seat forward-facing when our baby turns 6 months old."
C. "We can add a thick, fluffy blanket under the harness straps to keep the baby warm."
D. "We will place the car seat rear-facing in the back seat of the vehicle."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING

**Rationale:** Infants and toddlers must ride in a rear-facing car seat in the back seat of the vehicle until they reach the highest weight or height allowed by the car seat manufacturer (typically at least 2 years of age). Placing a rear-facing car seat in the front seat with an active airbag is extremely dangerous and can cause fatal injuries. Thick blankets should never be placed under the harness straps because they can compress during a crash, making the straps too loose to properly restrain the baby.
</details>

---

### Question 644
A client tells the clinic nurse, "I know I need to quit smoking, but I've tried three times before and always end up starting again." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "You just need to find the right nicotine patch and stick with it."
B. "Quitting smoking is very hard. What made you start smoking again in the past?"
C. "If you don't quit soon, you are at a very high risk for developing lung cancer."
D. "Why do you think you failed the last three times?"

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION

**Rationale:** Option B acknowledges the client's struggle and validates their feelings without judgment, while using an open-ended question to explore barriers to quitting. Option A provides unwarranted advice and diminishes the client's struggle. Option C uses fear tactics and is non-therapeutic. Option D uses the word "Why" and the word "failed," which can make the client feel defensive and attacked.
</details>

---

### Question 645
The nurse is evaluating the nutritional choices of a client who is trying to become pregnant. Which lunch choice indicates that the client has successfully met the goal of increasing dietary folic acid?

A. A cheeseburger with lettuce and tomato and a side of french fries.
B. A grilled chicken sandwich on white bread with a side of potato chips.
C. A spinach salad topped with lentils and a glass of fortified orange juice.
D. A bowl of clam chowder with saltine crackers and a glass of whole milk.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** EVALUATION

**Rationale:** Folic acid (Vitamin B9) is crucial before and during pregnancy to prevent neural tube defects. Excellent dietary sources of folic acid include dark leafy greens (such as spinach), legumes (such as lentils and beans), and fortified products like citrus juices and enriched cereals. The other meal choices do not contain high amounts of naturally occurring or fortified folic acid.
</details>
### Question 646
A nurse is teaching new parents about safe sleep practices to prevent Sudden Infant Death Syndrome (SIDS). Which statement by the parents indicates that the teaching has been effective? 

A) "We will place our baby on their stomach to sleep."
B) "We can put a soft blanket in the crib to keep the baby warm."
C) "We will make sure the baby sleeps on a firm mattress on their back."
D) "It is safe to let the baby sleep in our bed if we don't roll over."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
To reduce the risk of SIDS, infants should always be placed on their backs to sleep on a firm mattress. Soft bedding, blankets, stuffed animals, and co-sleeping (bed-sharing) are dangerous and increase the risk of suffocation and SIDS. Prone (stomach) sleeping is also contraindicated. 
</details>

---

### Question 647
A 55-year-old client at a routine physical reports needing to hold the newspaper at arm's length to read the text clearly. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) It is a sign of early macular degeneration.
B) It is an expected age-related change called presbyopia.
C) It indicates a sudden risk for retinal detachment.
D) It is an unexpected finding that requires immediate surgery.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
Presbyopia is a common, expected age-related change where the lens of the eye loses its elasticity, making it difficult to focus on close objects. It typically begins in middle age. It is not an emergency, nor is it a sign of macular degeneration or retinal detachment. 
</details>

---

### Question 648
The nurse is educating a young adult male client on how to perform a routine testicular self-examination (TSE). What is the best environment or time for the client to perform this exam?

A) Right after a warm shower or bath.
B) Immediately upon waking up in the morning.
C) Before engaging in strenuous exercise.
D) Right after applying a cold compress to the groin.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
A testicular self-examination (TSE) is best performed during or immediately after a warm shower or bath. The warm temperature relaxes the scrotal sac, making it easier to feel the testicles and identify any unusual lumps or changes. Cold temperatures will cause the scrotum to contract.
</details>

---

### Question 649
The nurse is reviewing the charts of several clients in a primary care clinic. Which client is at the highest risk for developing osteoporosis?

A) A 30-year-old male who weight-lifts regularly.
B) A 45-year-old premenopausal female who runs marathons.
C) A 50-year-old male with a high body mass index.
D) A 65-year-old postmenopausal female with a small body frame.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition<br>
Risk factors for osteoporosis include advanced age, female gender, postmenopausal status (due to a decrease in estrogen), and having a small, thin body frame. Weight-bearing exercise (like running or weight-lifting) actually helps strengthen bones and prevents osteoporosis.
</details>

---

### Question 650
A nurse is preparing a healthy client for their annual wellness physical. Which health promotion or screening task can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Provide education on a low-sodium diet.
B) Evaluate the client's understanding of breast self-examination.
C) Measure and record the client's height and weight.
D) Assess the client's readiness to quit smoking.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
Measuring routine vital signs, height, and weight are within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing readiness to change, evaluating understanding, and providing education are nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated.
</details>
### Question 651
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION**

A nurse is completing a home safety assessment for an older adult client who lives alone. Which finding places the client at the greatest risk for falls?

A) Grab bars installed inside the shower
B) Small, unsecured throw rugs in the hallway
C) A nightlight plugged into the bathroom wall
D) Nonskid backing on the client’s house slippers

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Small, unsecured throw rugs in the hallway

**Rationale:** Small, unsecured throw rugs are a major tripping hazard and significantly increase the risk of falls, especially in older adults who may have gait or balance issues. Grab bars, nightlights, and nonskid slippers are all safety measures that help *prevent* falls and promote health maintenance in the home environment. 
</details>

---

### Question 652
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED**

A nurse is assessing a healthy 2-day-old newborn during a routine postpartum check. Which assessment finding should the nurse recognize as an expected, normal variation?

A) Central cyanosis around the lips
B) Yellowing of the sclera
C) A resting respiratory rate of 80 breaths/min
D) Small white bumps scattered across the nose

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Small white bumps scattered across the nose

**Rationale:** Small white bumps on the newborn's nose are called milia. These are exposed sebaceous glands that are a completely benign, normal finding and will resolve on their own without treatment. Central cyanosis, jaundice in the first 24-48 hours, and a resting respiratory rate of 80 breaths/min (normal is 30-60) are unexpected findings that require further evaluation.
</details>

---

### Question 653
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING**

A nurse is teaching a pregnant client about taking prescribed oral iron supplements to prevent anemia. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will take my iron with a glass of milk to protect my stomach."
B) "I should stop taking the supplement if my stool turns dark black."
C) "I will take my iron with a glass of orange juice."
D) "I should take the iron on a full stomach right after a large meal."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "I will take my iron with a glass of orange juice."

**Rationale:** Vitamin C (ascorbic acid), found in orange juice, greatly enhances the absorption of iron. Iron should optimally be taken on an empty stomach for best absorption (not after a large meal), and dairy products (milk) inhibit iron absorption. Dark green or black stools are a harmless, expected side effect of oral iron therapy, so the client should not stop taking the medication.
</details>

---

### Question 654
**Lens: DATA INTERPRETATION**

A nurse is reviewing the health record of a 35-year-old client who has a Body Mass Index (BMI) of 22. How should the nurse interpret this data?

A) Normal weight
B) Underweight
C) Obese
D) Overweight

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Normal weight

**Rationale:** A BMI of 22 falls within the normal, healthy weight range. The standard BMI categories are: Underweight (less than 18.5), Normal weight (18.5 to 24.9), Overweight (25 to 29.9), and Obese (30 or greater). 
</details>

---

### Question 655
**Lens: PROCEDURAL STEP**

A nurse is teaching a young adult male client how to perform a testicular self-examination (TSE) for early cancer detection. Which instruction should the nurse provide regarding the physical steps of this examination?

A) "Perform the exam immediately after vigorous exercise."
B) "Perform the exam during or immediately after a warm shower."
C) "Perform the exam first thing in the morning before getting out of bed."
D) "Perform the exam right before going to sleep at night."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "Perform the exam during or immediately after a warm shower."

**Rationale:** The best time to perform a testicular self-examination is during or immediately after a warm bath or shower. The warm water relaxes the scrotal sac, making it much easier to palpate the testicles for any lumps, thickening, or changes in shape.
</details>
### Question 656
A nurse is teaching a new parent about safe sleep practices for a newborn to prevent Sudden Infant Death Syndrome (SIDS). Which statement by the parent indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will place the baby on their stomach to prevent them from choking."
B) "I should keep a soft, heavy blanket in the crib to keep the baby warm."
C) "I will position my baby flat on their back for every sleep."
D) "I need to use a bumper pad to protect my baby's head from the crib rails."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING (Do they understand the basics?)
**Rationale:** To reduce the risk of SIDS, infants should always be placed supine (on their backs) on a firm, flat sleep surface. The crib should be free of soft objects, toys, loose bedding, and bumper pads, as these pose suffocation hazards.
</details>

---

### Question 657
A nurse is assessing an older adult client during an annual wellness visit. Which finding should the nurse recognize as an expected age-related physiological change?

A) Sudden onset of confusion
B) Decreased sense of thirst
C) Resting heart rate of 115 bpm
D) Painful urination

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED (Is this finding normal/stable?)
**Rationale:** A decreased sense of thirst is an expected, normal physiological change associated with aging, which puts older adults at a higher risk for dehydration. Sudden confusion, tachycardia, and dysuria are unexpected findings that indicate an acute problem (such as an infection or dehydration) and require prompt evaluation.
</details>

---

### Question 658
A community health nurse is conducting a health screening clinic. Which client should the nurse identify as being at the highest risk for developing osteoporosis?

A) A 65-year-old postmenopausal woman who smokes daily
B) A 30-year-old man who weight-lifts four times a week
C) A 40-year-old woman who consumes dairy products regularly
D) A 25-year-old female who runs marathons

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION (Who is at risk for injury/crisis?)
**Rationale:** Risk factors for osteoporosis include advanced age, postmenopausal status (due to decreased estrogen), female sex, smoking, heavy alcohol use, and a sedentary lifestyle. Weight-bearing exercises (like running or weight-lifting) and a diet rich in calcium and vitamin D are protective factors against osteoporosis.
</details>

---

### Question 659
A nurse is observing a 2-year-old toddler during a well-child checkup. Which observed motor skill indicates that the child is meeting appropriate developmental milestones for their age?

A) Riding a tricycle using the pedals
B) Tying their own shoelaces securely
C) Kicking a ball forward without falling
D) Using a pincer grasp to pick up a small raisin

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION (What does this basic assessment/lab mean?)
**Rationale:** By 2 years of age, a toddler should possess the gross motor skill of kicking a ball forward without losing balance. Riding a tricycle is a milestone typically reached around age 3. Tying shoelaces is a fine motor skill expected around age 5. The pincer grasp is developed much earlier, typically around 9 to 10 months of age.
</details>

---

### Question 660
A 19-year-old client visits the college health clinic requesting a pregnancy test. The client is pacing the room, wringing her hands, and appears highly anxious. Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "Don't worry, many young women go through this exact same situation."
B) "Why didn't you come to the clinic sooner to get this tested?"
C) "You should have been using birth control if you didn't want to get pregnant."
D) "You seem very upset. Can you tell me more about what you are feeling?"

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** THERAPEUTIC COMMUNICATION (Active listening/Empathy/Open-ended)
**Rationale:** "You seem very upset. Can you tell me more about what you are feeling?" uses the therapeutic communication techniques of making observations and asking open-ended questions. This validates the client's emotional state and encourages her to express her concerns. The other options are non-therapeutic because they provide false reassurance, ask "why" questions (which can be perceived as accusatory), or are judgmental.
</details>
### Question 661
**Lens: RISK RECOGNITION**

The nurse is reviewing the health records of several clients in a primary care clinic. Which client has the highest risk for developing osteoporosis?

A) A 30-year-old woman who runs marathons and eats a high-calcium diet.  
B) A 65-year-old postmenopausal woman with a history of smoking.  
C) A 50-year-old overweight man with well-controlled type 2 diabetes.  
D) A 40-year-old man who works a physically demanding job in construction.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** Risk factors for osteoporosis include advanced age, female sex, postmenopausal status (due to decreased estrogen), smoking, a sedentary lifestyle, and low calcium or vitamin D intake. A 65-year-old postmenopausal woman who smokes has multiple major risk factors. Weight-bearing exercises (like running or construction work) and a high-calcium diet actually help protect against bone density loss.
</details>

---

### Question 662
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED**

A nurse is assessing a 4-month-old infant during a routine well-child visit. Which finding should the nurse identify as an expected developmental milestone for this age?

A) The infant sits unsupported on the floor.  
B) The infant says "mama" and "dada" with specific meaning.  
C) The infant rolls from their stomach to their back.  
D) The infant pulls up to a standing position using furniture.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** Rolling from the stomach to the back is an expected gross motor developmental milestone for a 4-month-old infant. Sitting unsupported typically occurs around 6 to 8 months. Pulling to a standing position usually occurs around 9 to 10 months. Saying specific words with meaning, such as "mama" or "dada," generally begins around 10 to 12 months.
</details>

---

### Question 663
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING**

A nurse is providing education to a 25-year-old female client regarding breast self-awareness. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "The best time to check my breasts is right before my menstrual period begins."  
B) "I will schedule a screening mammogram every year starting right now."  
C) "I should perform a thorough breast exam every single day while taking a shower."  
D) "I should report any new lumps, skin dimpling, or nipple discharge to my doctor."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
