**Rationale:** Breast self-awareness involves knowing what is normal for one's own breasts and reporting any abnormal changes (such as new lumps, dimpling, or spontaneous discharge) to a healthcare provider. If a client chooses to do self-exams, the best time is 5 to 7 days *after* menstruation begins, when the breasts are least swollen and tender. Daily exams are not recommended, and annual screening mammograms for average-risk women typically begin at age 40 or 45, not 25.
</details>

---

### Question 664
**Lens: EVALUATION**

A nurse evaluates a client's adherence to a newly prescribed Dietary Approaches to Stop Hypertension (DASH) diet. Which food log entry indicates that the client is successfully following the diet?

A) A grilled chicken breast with a side of steamed broccoli and quinoa.  
B) Sliced deli turkey on white bread with pickles and mayonnaise.  
C) A double cheeseburger with a side of deep-fried onion rings.  
D) A large bowl of canned soup served with salted crackers.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** The DASH diet is designed to lower blood pressure by emphasizing lean proteins, whole grains, vegetables, and fruits, while strictly limiting sodium, saturated fats, and processed foods. Grilled chicken, steamed broccoli, and quinoa align perfectly with these guidelines. Canned soups, deli meats, pickles, and fast food are typically very high in sodium and/or saturated fat and represent poor adherence to the diet.
</details>

---

### Question 665
**Lens: PROCEDURAL STEP**

The nurse is preparing to administer an annual influenza vaccine intramuscularly (IM) to an adult client. After verifying the correct patient and drawing up the medication, what is the first physical step the nurse should take when preparing the chosen injection site?

A) Pinch the skin to elevate the subcutaneous tissue.  
B) Aspirate the syringe for 5 seconds to check for blood return.  
C) Cleanse the deltoid site with an alcohol swab using a circular motion.  
D) Quickly insert the needle at a 45-degree angle.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** After identifying the correct site (the deltoid muscle for an adult IM vaccine), the first physical step in preparing the skin is to cleanse the area with an antimicrobial swab (such as alcohol) using a firm, circular motion moving outward from the center. Pinching the skin is generally used for subcutaneous injections. Aspiration is no longer recommended for routine vaccine administration, and IM injections are administered at a 90-degree angle, not 45 degrees.
</details>
### Question 666
The nurse is conducting a community health screening. Which client is at the highest risk for developing osteoporosis?

A) A 40-year-old male who jogs three miles daily  
B) A 65-year-old female who smokes and lives a sedentary lifestyle  
C) A 30-year-old pregnant female taking daily prenatal vitamins  
D) A 50-year-old male who swims laps twice a week  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION  
**Rationale:** Risk factors for osteoporosis include advancing age, female biological sex, smoking, and a sedentary lifestyle. Weight-bearing exercises (like jogging) actually help build and maintain bone density, making the other clients at a lower risk for developing osteoporosis.
</details>

### Question 667
The nurse is teaching the parents of a newborn about safe sleep practices to prevent Sudden Infant Death Syndrome (SIDS). Which statement by the parents indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "We will place our baby on their stomach to sleep so they don't choke."  
B) "We should keep a soft blanket and a few stuffed animals in the crib."  
C) "We will place our baby on their back to sleep on a firm mattress."  
D) "We can use a soft mattress for the crib as long as we use a tightly fitted sheet."  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING  
**Rationale:** To reduce the risk of SIDS, infants should always be placed on their backs to sleep on a firm, flat surface. The crib should be completely free of soft objects, including stuffed animals, pillows, and loose blankets, to prevent suffocation.
</details>

---

### Question 668
A nurse is assessing a client who is 32 weeks pregnant during a routine prenatal visit. Which of the following findings is an expected, normal change at this stage of pregnancy?

A) Painless, irregular uterine tightening  
B) Blurred vision and a severe headache  
C) Vaginal bleeding accompanied by abdominal pain  
D) Dysuria and foul-smelling urine  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED  
**Rationale:** Painless, irregular uterine contractions (Braxton Hicks contractions) are an expected, normal finding during the second and third trimesters as the uterus prepares for labor. Blurred vision and headaches are unexpected signs of preeclampsia; bleeding is a sign of placental abruption or previa; and dysuria indicates a urinary tract infection.
</details>

---

### Question 669
The nurse is reviewing the growth chart of a healthy 6-month-old infant whose birth weight was 7 lbs (3.1 kg). Based on normal growth and development milestones, what is the expected weight of this infant?

A) 9 lbs (4.0 kg)  
B) 12 lbs (5.4 kg)  
C) 21 lbs (9.5 kg)  
D) 14 lbs (6.3 kg)  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION  
**Rationale:** According to normal infant growth and development, an infant's birth weight is expected to double by 6 months of age. Therefore, a baby born at 7 lbs should weigh approximately 14 lbs at 6 months. Birth weight typically triples by 12 months of age.
</details>

### Question 670
A nurse is evaluating a client's ability to choose foods appropriate for a newly prescribed low-sodium diet to help manage hypertension. Which lunch choice by the client indicates that the dietary teaching has been effective?

A) Baked chicken breast with steamed broccoli  
B) Canned chicken noodle soup with saltine crackers  
C) Deli turkey sandwich with a large dill pickle  
D) Frozen television dinner with meatloaf and gravy  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** EVALUATION  
**Rationale:** Baked chicken breast and steamed fresh vegetables (like broccoli) are naturally low in sodium. Canned soups, deli meats, pickles, and frozen pre-packaged meals are all highly processed and contain very high amounts of sodium, which the client must avoid on a low-sodium diet. Selecting the baked chicken indicates the teaching was effective.
</details>
